# Executive Synthesis and Integrated Investment View

**Decisive call: DO-NOTHING / WATCHLIST for fresh capital at US$409.74/ADS (2026-07-16 close).** Not a fresh buy, not a short. Existing exposure: hold, against the monitoring state machine in Sections II.7 and III.10.

**One-line thesis.** TSMC is the single most structurally advantaged business in the global technology supply chain — an input-priced toll booth on leading-edge logic and advanced packaging, collecting on the order of 30–50× the combined gross profit of every merchant rival [II.1] — but at ~US$2.1 trillion the price already underwrites roughly an 18% earnings-per-ADS compound annual growth rate through 2030 at a 20× terminal multiple [II.6], on gross margins that sit ~8–11 percentage points above the company's own mid-cycle band [II.3], with the load-bearing assumption — no AI-capex air pocket through 2028 — honestly unknowable from the current evidence [II.7]. Price has caught up to a franchise that remains under-appreciated in kind but is no longer under-appreciated in degree.

---

### The six findings that matter

**1. The 67.7% gross margin is three different margins stacked, and only one of them is permanent.** The margin ledger [II.3, audited in II.7]: a franchise layer worth ~57–60% mid-cycle (the technology monopoly plus node mix — durable); a shortage/allocation-rent layer of ~2.5–4pp (2026 price hikes of 3–10%+ into >100% utilization and booked-out CoWoS packaging — reverting on dated supply clocks); a largely unrecognized **depreciation-holiday layer of ~3–4pp** — FY2025 depreciation grew +3.8% against revenue +31.6% because the 2022–24 capex plateau met the 5-year equipment life, an artifact that mechanically reverses as the 2025–26 capex surge (US$40.9B → US$60–64B guided) enters service, with D&A dollars up an estimated +22–28% in FY2027 [II.3, agent-inferred]; and ~0.6–0.7pp of currently favorable FX. Management's "56%-and-higher" long-term floor is best read as a sandbagged mid-cycle marker, honest point-estimate ≈58%. The forward margin path is therefore *down* from here even in the bull case — the only question is the slope. (The governance pass's counterpoint — TSMC's 5-year depreciation life *understates* margins ~4–5pp versus peers' 8-year lives — is cross-sectional, valid only for peer comparisons; it does not offset the intertemporal holiday on TSMC's own books [II.7].)

**2. The price sets an excellence burden the evidence meets only through ~2027.** A transparent reverse-DCF at a 10% discount rate: US$409.74 requires ~18.4% EPS CAGR 2026→2030 at a 20× terminal P/E — TSMC's all-time-best five-year run is 21.9% — and the implied 2030 configuration stacks record growth on record margins on an undisturbed Taiwan [II.6]. Consensus FY2026 (~$15.91/ADS) is actually 4–7% *too low* against the raised "slightly above 40%" guide — the near-term earnings momentum is real and probably still under-modeled — but consensus 2027 (~$20/ADS derived) embeds ~51% net margin and no normalization at all, against management's own disclosed dilution stack (overseas fabs 2–3pp widening to 3–4pp, N2 ramp, the depreciation wave) [II.6]. The three-year return profile from $409.74: bull +17.8%/yr, base **+2.1%/yr**, mediocrity −15.0%/yr, bear −27.0%/yr, with the conflict tail carried separately (residual value $40–80/ADS) rather than blended [II.6]. A mere *pause* in the AI buildout — not a bust — costs roughly a third of the position. That asymmetry, not any defect in the franchise, is the call.

**3. The moat is real, quantified, and durable to ~2030 — and it is already the most expensive line in the price.** The replication arithmetic [II.2]: Intel's external foundry revenue ratio versus TSMC is ~400:1 ($307M vs $122.4B FY2025) on a −$10.3B operating loss; Samsung's foundry ran ~KRW 6T FY2025 losses and reached its first monthly profit in three years in June 2026 with yields a generation behind; ~$35B+ of cumulative challenger losses accrued while TSMC's share *rose* from ~60% to 72.3% (and ~90%+ of leading-edge, effectively 100% of leading-edge merchant capacity [III.2]). Process-plus-packaging leadership is defensible into ~2030 at ≥80% confidence, with dated markers that would shorten it (a committed top-10 fabless customer on Intel 14A in the 2H26–1H27 decision window; Samsung Taylor AI5 at volume 2H27) [II.2]. But the market is paying 3.7–5.1× replacement cost — ~75–80% of enterprise value is capitalized moat [II.6] — so moat confirmation from here is worth little, while moat *dents* are expensive.

**4. The Taiwan discount is thin-to-fair, not fat — there is no stigma-alpha to harvest in the wrapper.** Reverse-engineering the price gap to consensus fair value implies the market charges only ~1.0–1.25%/yr of expected geopolitical loss against ~2.3%/yr actuarially warranted at published expert base rates [II.5, agent-inferred]. The ADS itself trades at a +6.9% *premium* to the underlying 2330 ordinaries (conversion friction plus index plumbing), meaning the discount lives in the business value, not the instrument — the inverse of the classic frozen-wrapper pattern, and the premium doubles as a live fragility gauge. In stress the wrapper is the weak link: a blockade scenario prices to a realizable-value multiplier of roughly 0.22× (the ADS keeps trading; converting to ordinaries would be value-destructive, the inverse of Russia-2022 mechanics), and invasion is absorbing (~0.02–0.06×) [II.5]. Verdict: investable-with-haircut, jurisdiction gate PASS, with pre-committed wrapper-event and controller-incentive kill triggers — and the Berkshire 2022–23 round-trip stands as the canonical precedent that the deepest quality investors price this tail as a veto, not a discount [III.9].

**5. The control stack extracts in kind, not in cash — and the $265B U.S. program is the toll.** The de-facto controllers (Taiwan's National Development Fund at 6.38% with a board seat, the statutory N-2 regime keeping the newest two nodes onshore, and U.S. policy leverage via tariffs/quotas/CHIPS/an unresolved export-control matter) do not skim the income statement: dividends per share are ramping NT$14 → NT$18 → ≥NT$23 on a share count frozen at 25,932M (zero dilution across a self-funded ~$167B buildout) [II.4]. What the states take is *capacity location*: the U.S. commitment ratcheted $65B → $165B → **$265B announced on the Q2 call (2026-07-16)** — best modeled not as one NPV event (~$25–60B drag net of subsidies and price recapture, ~1–3% of market cap [II.4]) but as a **recurring, face-saving settlement currency** in a mutual-hostage relationship that never fully closes [III.5]. Earnings quality is otherwise high (grade A−): strip the NT$63.2B Vanguard disposal gain and 2Q26 clean EPS is **US$3.93/ADS versus $4.31 reported** (+61.5% clean YoY versus +77.4% headline); 1H26 core EPS ≈ NT$46.8 versus NT$49.33 reported; clean FY2026E ≈ **$16.5/ADS core** (consensus-floor build $15.5) [II.4, II.7, III.5].

**6. The tape is not signaling what it appears to signal — in either direction.** The −14% drawdown from the June 30 all-time high is fully explained by sector beta plus the round-trip of a quarter-end EM-flow markup (semis index −17.2% over the identical window; the TSM-specific residual ≈ 0 until a 1–2pp margin-guide nitpick on print day), so the pullback is not "the market knows something" [III.7]. Equally, the record print is not "all clear": the cycle-dating table scores 2026 at ~8/10 on supply-side late-cycle markers (record margins, capex/revenue ~39%, allocation economics, inventory days 76→87) but only ~1/6 on demand-rollover markers — **"priced and margined like a peak, ordered like a mid-cycle"** [III.7]. The forward-looking language machinery agrees: TSMC's guidance archaeology shows the sandbag floor holds mid-cycle (+3.9pp mean final-vs-initial, 2018–25) but fails at cycle turns as multi-step cut sequences, with capex-restraint language leading formal cuts by ~1 quarter and revenue down-years by 2–4 quarters in 3-of-3 precedents — and today's calls contain **zero Tier-1 warning markers** (`guidance_cut_pressure = +400 @ 0.60`) [III.3]. Two quieter counter-signals earn a watch: customer prepayments *peaked* in 2024 (NT$291B) and fell to NT$190B in 2025 [III.4], and the ADS premium's collapse from ~19–26% to +6.9% after Taiwan's April-2026 fund-cap change means the marginal holder has rotated from New York to Taipei [III.8]. And the non-AI two-thirds of the business is quietly worth more than the market's attention implies: smartphone silicon-content growth alone adds an estimated +$13–17B of revenue by 2029 with near-zero unit growth — the shock absorber under the digestion scenario [III.1].

The formal evidence-matrix and hypothesis-arena treatment [III.11] independently reproduces this shape: across 34 credibility-weighted claims, 47% are refute-rows against some leg — no leg's refute mass exceeds its support today, but the demand leg's support is time-boxed to ~2027 while the price owns 2030. The arena also registered a genuinely live third alternative the market narrative skips: the **range-bound compounder** — earnings compound 15–20%/yr while the multiple compresses 15–20%, netting a roughly flat ADS for two years — which is the single hypothesis most consistent with a stock that fell 2.3% on an 11.7% earnings beat. Eight dated prediction locks (from the 2026-10-15 print through 2027-02-28) are registered in Section III.11 so this report's calibration can be scored, not debated, in arrears. And the substrate/overhang formalism [III.13] closes the loop on why DO-NOTHING is structural rather than timid: of the four independent paths to value, only one (packaging-rent deepening) is both surviving and uncharged at $409.74 — below the two-path bar a fresh commitment requires — whereas at the $280–330 review zone three paths clear.

### The decision card

- **Flip trigger → BUY (deadline 2027-02-28):** the big-4 hyperscalers' aggregate CY2027 capex guidance on their Q4'26 calls comes in **≥ +20% YoY**. That resolves the one load-bearing unknown (demand duration) in the long's favor while the margin ledger is still intact; the multiple can be paid once the 2027 revenue leg is funded, not inferred. *Companion condition [III.11]: the trigger should fire together with confirmation that the December-2026 wafer-pricing round proceeded as reported — funded demand plus intact pricing power, not either alone.*
- **Flip trigger → SELL/AVOID (any window through 2027-12-31):** TSMC **monthly revenue YoY < +15% for two consecutive prints** — the two-stage rule: <+15% ×2 = deceleration alarm (this trigger); <+10% ×2 = air-pocket confirmation. TSMC's monthly cadence is the fastest public series in the entire AI supply chain [III.6]; it dates the short case, which today is real but undated [II.7].
- **Price-based re-entry review** (not a trigger, a standing order): forward P/E <15× on an unchanged through-cycle guide — roughly **$280–330/ADS** on the clean FY26–27 builds — the zone where the mediocrity case stops costing money and the Graham question re-opens [II.6, III.9; Part I's $300–330 band agrees].
- **Tail discipline:** pre-committed exits on wrapper events (Taiwan repatriation controls or TWSE halt >3 days; ADS premium inverting to a persistent discount; cross-strait interdiction >72h) and controller events (a U.S. equity demand on TSMC; N-2 tightening paired with NDF selling) [II.5]. These are absorbing-tail rules, deliberately insensitive to valuation.
- Next scheduled evidence, in order: July monthly revenue (~2026-08-10), big-4 hyperscaler Q2 calls (late July 2026), ASML/semicap prints (leading TSMC capex by ~2–3 quarters), **3Q26 print 2026-10-15** (GM ≥65% and 4Q guide ≥$48B keep the bull path; GM <64% breaks the normalization floor early), December 2026 cluster (2027 price-hike round, Nanjing license renewal, ART ratification, ITC 337-TA-1443 final), January 2027 FY27 capex/depreciation guide [II.7, III.10].

**Why DO-NOTHING is the honest output rather than a hedge:** every pass that could have manufactured conviction was run — the falsification-first arena, the ten-claim refutation ledger, the lens library, the short-side inversion — and each returned the same shape: *the business survives every attack; the price survives none of the demand-pause scenarios* [II.7, III.9]. The long at $409.74 requires knowing 2027–2028 hyperscaler capex, which is unknowable today but becomes knowable on dated prints between now and February 2027. The short requires paying carry against a sandbagging serial-beater compounding ~30%/yr with $78–83B of net cash — undated cycle shorts on monopolies are how short books die. The edge here is not directional; it is *preparedness*: the flip triggers are pre-committed, the monitoring machine is built, and the analysis will not need to be redone when the tape moves — only consulted.

**What would make this report wrong, in one paragraph.** Bullish wrongness: the margin ledger over-normalizes — if pricing power keeps ratcheting (2027 hike round already reported in planning) and the N2/A16 ladder mixes up faster than depreciation lands, mid-cycle GM could prove 61–63%, not 57–60%, and today's price is then merely fair for a 25%+ compounder and the +20% capex trigger will fire anyway (cost: entry ~10–15% higher — acceptable). Bearish wrongness: the tail probability is regime-dependent, not actuarial — if deterrence is decaying on a 5–8 year half-life as U.S. fabs scale [II.5], the "thin-to-fair" discount read flips to "thin against a rising hazard," and the correct posture would be structural avoidance at any multiple; the wrapper kill triggers, not valuation, are the guard on that error. Process wrongness: the whole demand edifice rests on public prints with no proprietary channel work — the report's largest honest gap [III.10].

## Synthesized Investment Analysis — TSM (Taiwan Semiconductor Manufacturing Company Limited, NYSE ADS)

**Analyst (internal)** | Report date basis: 2026-07-17 | Price basis: US$409.74/ADS close 2026-07-16

---

### 1. Decisive Call, Thesis, and Flip Triggers

**Call: DO NOTHING / WATCHLIST at $409.74/ADS.** Not a fresh BUY, not a SHORT/AVOID.

**One-line thesis:** TSMC is the highest-quality, most structurally advantaged business in the entire semiconductor value chain — an effective monopoly on leading-edge logic manufacturing riding the AI-compute buildout — but at a ~$2.13 trillion market cap (ADS up from a 52-week low of $221.18 to a high of $479.00) and a forward 2026 P/E of roughly 23–24x on margins that are already at or above through-cycle normal, the stock is priced for continued near-perfection, leaving a thin margin of safety for new capital even though the underlying franchise is not overvalued in an absolute sense.

**Flip triggers (exactly two, atomic, time-bounded):**

1. **BUY trigger:** At the Q3 2026 earnings release (expected ~mid-October 2026, consistent with TSMC's historical Jan/Apr/Jul/Oct cadence), if reported gross margin comes in **≥66%** (above the low end of the company's own 65–67% guide) **and** management's forward commentary reiterates or raises the multi-year capex/revenue growth trajectory (i.e., no signal of a 2027 AI-capex "digestion year") — this would confirm the current cycle is not yet peaking, and would justify committing new capital despite the rich multiple.
2. **SHORT/AVOID trigger:** By the Q4 2026 print (expected ~mid-January 2027), if **YoY USD revenue growth decelerates below 25%** **or** the **Q1 2027 gross-margin guidance drops below 60%** — either would be the first hard evidence that the AI-accelerator capex supercycle is entering a digestion/correction phase, which — combined with rising customer concentration (top-10 customers = 78% of FY2025 revenue) and highly fixed, node-specific capacity — would flag a capacity-cycle peak requiring a de-rating of the multiple, not just of the earnings base.

Existing holders: no action required — hold. New capital: wait for either trigger, or for a pullback into the **$300–$330/ADS** range (≈17–19x 2026E EPS of ~$17.4 [agent-inferred]), which would restore a genuine margin of safety without requiring the bull case to be right.

---

### 2. Consensus vs. Counter-Consensus Framing

**What the market appears to believe:** The Q2 2026 print (6-K filed 2026-07-16, accession 0001046179-26-000451) delivered records across almost every headline metric — revenue +36.0% YoY, gross margin 67.7%, operating margin 60.3%, net margin 55.6%, ROE 45.9% annualized, EPS +77.4% YoY — and management reiterated "2026 revenue to increase by slightly above 40%" in USD terms. Yet the ADS fell from a prior close of $419.48 to $409.74 (-2.3%) on elevated volume (24.9M shares), with an intraday low of $403.50, on the day of the print itself.[¹] This is a market that is not rewarding a beat — it is looking for confirmation that the *next* data point (Q3 guide: 65–67% GM, 56–58% OM, both sequentially **below** the just-reported 2Q26 actuals) does not represent the first crack in the cycle. In other words: consensus has moved from "will TSMC beat?" to "how long can this last?" — the market is pricing a monopoly-quality compounder near the top of a historically unprecedented margin structure, and treats any deceleration in the *rate of acceleration* as a sell signal.

**Where this analysis agrees:** The business quality read is not in dispute — sole/leading position in advanced-node logic (2nm/3nm), essential CoWoS advanced-packaging capacity for AI accelerators, disciplined balance sheet, clean earnings, no governance red flags. The market is correct that current margins (67.7% GM) sit well above TSMC's own long-run historical target band (management has historically cited a long-term gross-margin target closer to ~53%+ in prior cycles) and are not a "new normal" that should be capitalized into perpetuity without a normalization discount.[²]

**Where this analysis diverges (modestly):** The market's -2.3% reaction to a beat-and-raise appears to overweight a single sequential margin data point (Q3 guide vs. 2Q26 actual) that is fully explained by (a) an FX-assumption change (32 NT$/US$ in the Q3 guide vs. 31.60 realized average in 2Q26) and (b) 2-nanometer ramp costs as that node scales from just 3% of 2Q26 wafer revenue toward much higher volume — a normal, disclosed cost-of-ramp dynamic, not evidence of demand softening. The customer-prepayment evidence in the 20-F ("customers have entered into agreements ... to pay temporary receipts in order to retain specified capacity") is real, costly, hard-to-fake evidence of a genuinely supply-constrained (allocation) market, not a vendor-financed or channel-stuffed narrative.[³] The counter-consensus view here is therefore narrower than "the market is wrong" — it is "the market's one-day reaction was noisy relative to the underlying evidence," while the **valuation** itself (not the day's price action) is closer to fair than cheap.

---

### 3. Business Overview and Corporate History

Taiwan Semiconductor Manufacturing Company Limited was founded in 1987 as a joint venture among the R.O.C. government and private investors, incorporated in Taiwan on February 21, 1987. TSMC pioneered the pure-play foundry business model — manufacturing semiconductors to customer-owned designs rather than designing and selling its own chips — and has been the world's leading dedicated foundry ever since. Common shares have traded on the Taiwan Stock Exchange (2330.TW) since September 5, 1994; ADSs have traded on the NYSE (1 ADS = 5 common shares) since October 8, 1997.[⁴]

TSMC operates one 150mm wafer fab, six 200mm wafer fabs, nine 300mm wafer fabs, and seven advanced backend (packaging/test) fabs. Corporate headquarters and nine fabs sit in Hsinchu Science Park; additional fabs are in the Central Taiwan Science Park (Taichung), Southern Taiwan Science Park, and Kaohsiung. International capacity includes TSMC Washington (Camas, WA, existing), TSMC Arizona (Fab 21, under major expansion), TSMC China (Shanghai, Fab 10), TSMC Nanjing (Fab 16), JASM in Kumamoto, Japan (Fab 23), and ESMC in Dresden, Germany (Fab 24, under construction).[⁵] The 20-F discloses 305 distinct process technologies deployed and 12,682 products manufactured for 534 customers in 2025.

Leadership: Chairman & CEO C.C. Wei; CFO Wendell Huang (Senior Vice President).

### 5. Segment Economics and Unit Economics

#### 5.1 Platform (end-market) mix — FY2023–FY2025 (20-F, Item 5)

| Internet of Things | 8% | 6% | 5% |
| Automotive | 6% | 5% | 5% |
| Digital Consumer Electronics | 2% | 1% | 1% |
| Other | 3% | 2% | 2% |
| **Total net revenue** | NT$2,161,736mn | NT$2,894,308mn | **NT$3,809,054mn** |

The revenue increase from 2024→2025 was driven overwhelmingly by HPC (+NT$716,040mn, +48% YoY) versus Smartphone (+NT$105,686mn, +11% YoY) — the AI/HPC platform mix-shift is the dominant structural story in the P&L, not a one-quarter phenomenon.[⁶]

#### 5.2 Wafer revenue by process node

| Node | FY2023 | FY2024 | FY2025 | 2Q26 (deck) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 3-nanometer | 6% | 18% | 24% | 30% |
| 5-nanometer | 33% | 34% | 36% | 33% |
| 7-nanometer | 19% | 17% | 14% | 11% |
| 2-nanometer | — | — | — | 3% |
| **Advanced (7nm and below)** | 58% | 69% | **74%** | **77%** |

Advanced-node share of wafer revenue has risen every year and continued rising intra-2026, even as legacy nodes (28nm, 40/45nm, 65nm, 0.11–0.25 micron) shrink as a share of the mix.[⁷] 2-nanometer entered volume production in 2025 and is still in the earliest phase of its ramp (3% of 2Q26 wafer revenue) — the single largest swing factor in the next 2–3 years of gross-margin trajectory, since new nodes typically carry higher unit costs (lower yield, new equipment depreciation) before scaling.

#### 5.3 Customer concentration — the central unit-economics risk

Per the 20-F: *"our ten largest customers in 2023, 2024 and 2025 accounted for approximately 70%, 76% and 78% of our net revenue"* respectively; *"our largest customer in 2023, 2024 and 2025 accounted for 25%, 22% and 19%"*; *"our second largest customer... accounted for 11%, 12%, and 17%"* respectively.[⁸] This is a structurally **rising** concentration, explicitly attributed by the company to "the changing dynamics of the electronics industry with the structural shift to HPC" — i.e., the same AI-driven mix shift that is powering revenue growth is also concentrating the customer base (implicitly Nvidia/AMD-class HPC customers and Apple). Wafer fabrication represents ~86% of total net revenue; the balance is packaging/testing, mask-making, design, and royalty income.

#### 5.4 Capacity and capital intensity

Annual capacity exceeded 17 million 12-inch-equivalent wafers in 2025 (flat vs. 2024's ~17 million — implying 2025 revenue growth came overwhelmingly from ASP/mix, not unit volume expansion). Capital expenditures: FY2023 NT$949,817mn, FY2024 NT$956,007mn, FY2025 **NT$1,272,411mn** (US$40,895mn); **2026 guidance: US$52–56 billion**, a ~30–37% YoY step-up, explicitly earmarked for 2nm/3nm capacity (Fab 20/21/22), specialty/advanced-packaging capacity (Fab 23/24, including CoWoS/SoIC), and continued R&D.[⁹]

### 6. Valuation

**Basis notes (mandatory per instructions):** All per-share figures below are stated **per ADS** (1 ADS = 5 common shares) unless explicitly marked "per common share." All NT$ figures are translated to US$ at the rate disclosed in the relevant filing (FY2025 20-F used NT$31.37/US$1.00; the Q3 2026 guidance explicitly assumes NT$32.00/US$1.00; 2Q26 actual average was NT$31.60/US$1.00) — a meaningfully different rate can move reported USD figures by low single digits.

#### 6.1 Market capitalization (ADS-and-common reconciled)

- Common shares outstanding: 25,932,524,521 (as of 2026-02-28, per 20-F)
- ADS-equivalent shares: 25,932,524,521 / 5 = 5,186,504,904
- ADS close, 2026-07-16: **$409.74**
- **Implied market capitalization ≈ $409.74 × 5,186,504,904 ≈ US$2.125 trillion** [agent-inferred arithmetic on cited inputs]

#### 6.2 Trailing multiple

- FY2025 net income: NT$1,697,604mn (US$54,116mn per 20-F FX)
- FY2025 EPS: NT$1,697,604mn / 25,932.5mn shares = NT$65.46/common share × 5 = NT$327.3/ADS ÷ 31.37 = **US$10.43/ADS**
- **Trailing P/E ≈ 409.74 / 10.43 ≈ 39.3x**

#### 6.3 Forward 2026 estimate build [agent-inferred — not company guidance beyond the stated growth-rate and margin ranges]

Using disclosed Q1 2026 (NT$572.48bn NI) and Q2 2026 (NT$706.56bn NI) actuals, the Q3 2026 guidance range (revenue $44.6–45.8bn; GM 65–67%; OM 56–58%; FX 32 NT$/US$), and management's full-year color ("2026 revenue to increase by slightly above 40%" in USD, implying ~$170bn), a straight-line extension of margin ratios implies:

| | Q1 2026 | Q2 2026 | Q3 2026E | Q4 2026E | FY2026E |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Revenue (US$bn) | 35.9 (actual) | 40.2 (actual) | ~45.2 (guided midpt) | ~49 [agent-inferred] | **~170.3** |
| Net income (US$bn) | 18.1 (actual) | 22.4 (actual) | ~23.8 [agent-inferred] | ~26 [agent-inferred] | **~90.3** |

- **FY2026E EPS ≈ $90.3bn / 5,186.5mn ADS ≈ $17.41/ADS** [agent-inferred]
- **Forward 2026 P/E ≈ $409.74 / $17.41 ≈ 23.5x** [agent-inferred]

At face value, ~23–24x forward earnings for ~40%+ USD revenue growth and >50% net margins is not an expensive multiple in isolation — but it is being paid on a margin base that is already **above** TSMC's own historically-cited long-run gross-margin target (~53%+), which is the crux of the "priced for continued excellence" read.

#### 6.4 Peer/multiple sanity check

There is no true like-for-like public comp at TSMC's combination of scale, leading-edge share, and margin structure. Samsung Foundry and Intel Foundry Services are the nearest conceptual peers but are both sub-scale at the leading edge and materially less profitable — neither was independently pulled with a comparable multiple in this session (hfdt's `peers suggest`/`comps` surfaces were not run; this is a data-quality gap, see §9). The valuation call below therefore leans on an internal (through-cycle normalized-margin) anchor rather than a cross-sectional peer multiple, consistent with the semiconductor-sector discipline against capitalizing peak-cycle margins.

#### 6.5 Bear / Base / Bull / Mediocrity scenarios [all agent-inferred; 2–3 year horizon]

| Scenario | Key assumption | Implied ADS value | Logic |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Bear** | AI-capex "digestion year" in 2027 (analogous to 2019/2022–23 industry corrections); revenue growth decelerates to single digits; GM reverts toward high-50s%; multiple compresses to mid-teens forward P/E on a lower earnings base | **~$220–260** | Roughly back to the 52-week low; a genuine cyclical peak-to-trough re-rating, not a business-quality impairment |
| **Mediocrity** | "Merely good, not great": 2027 revenue growth decelerates to ~20–25% (still very strong); GM normalizes to ~60–62% (above historical target, below current peak); multiple settles at ~20x forward earnings | **~$420–460** | Roughly flat to modestly up from today — the base case for a company executing competently but not living up to the current run-rate of records |
| **Base** | 2026 guide is delivered in full; 2027 growth moderates to ~25–30% as 2nm scales; margins stay in the mid-60s% as node mix offsets ramp costs; multiple holds near 23–24x | **~$480–520** | Modest upside, consistent with continued execution at the currently-guided trajectory |
| **Bull** | No AI-capex digestion year; 2027–28 growth sustains 30%+; GM structurally re-rates toward high-60s% on pricing power and packaging-bottleneck economics; multiple expands to ~28–30x | **~$650–750** | Requires the AI buildout to continue accelerating without a pause — the "dream" case |

**The mediocrity case roughly clears (flat-to-modest-upside), which is the necessary gate before a BUY call — but it does not clear by a wide enough margin to justify committing fresh capital at $409.74 given the bear case is a genuine ~35–45% drawdown risk on a well-evidenced historical analog (semiconductor capex cycles have overshot and corrected before).** This asymmetry — modest upside in the base/mediocrity cases against a real, precedented downside in the bear case — is why the call is DO NOTHING/WATCHLIST rather than BUY, and also why it is not a SHORT (the business itself shows no signs of deterioration today, and shorting dominant infrastructure without a dated break mechanism is explicitly against sound practice).

#### 7.1 Balance sheet (2Q26, earnings deck; NT$ billions)

| | 2Q26 | 1Q26 | 2Q25 |
|---|---|---|---|
| Cash & marketable securities | 3,518.01 (37.5%) | 3,383.60 (39.0%) | 2,634.43 (37.6%) |

| Net PP&E | 4,302.88 (45.9%) | 3,954.68 (45.7%) | 3,386.21 (48.3%) |
| **Total assets** | **9,375.65** | **8,660.95** | **7,006.35** |
| Current liabilities | 1,857.76 (19.8%) | 1,714.25 (19.8%) | 1,377.31 (19.7%) |
| Long-term interest-bearing debt | 864.27 (9.2%) | 901.02 (10.4%) | 883.67 (12.6%) |

Net cash positive; long-term debt is a modest ~9.2% of assets and falling as a share of the balance sheet even as absolute capex accelerates — the capex ramp is being funded by internally generated cash flow, not by leveraging up.

#### 7.2 Cash flow bridge (earnings deck; NT$ billions)

| | 2Q26 | 1Q26 | 2Q25 |
|---|---|---|---|
| Cash from operating activities | 783.36 | 698.97 | 497.07 |
| Capital expenditures | (496.00) | (350.76) | (297.22) |
| **Free cash flow (OCF − capex)** | **287.36** | **348.21** | **199.85** |
| Cash dividends | (155.59) | (129.66) | (116.70) |

FY2025 full-year: operating cash flow NT$2,274,976mn, capex NT$1,272,411mn, implied FCF ≈ NT$1,002,565mn (~US$32bn) — comfortably positive even in a year where capex rose 33% YoY.[¹⁰] Note the capex acceleration is heavily back-half-loaded intra-2026 (Q2 capex NT$496bn vs. Q1's NT$350.76bn), consistent with the 2026 full-year guide of $52–56bn requiring a much larger H2 spend.

#### 7.3 Share count, dilution, SBC

- Total common shares outstanding: 25,932,524,521 (unchanged 2025-12-31 → 2026-02-28)
- Authorized share capital: NT$280.5bn / 28.05bn shares, of which 500mn reserved for employee stock options; **no employee stock options were outstanding** as of 2025-12-31 or 2026-02-28
- Restricted share award (RSA) reclaims in March 2026: 32,060 + 81,394 + 41,000 shares — immaterial (<0.001% of shares outstanding)
- Share-based compensation expense (from XBRL, FY2024 vintage — see §9 staleness caveat): ~NT$1.65bn against FY2024 revenue of NT$2,894bn — **SBC/revenue ≈ 0.06%**, dramatically lower than typical US large-cap tech peers (often mid-single-digit-to-double-digit % of revenue). This is a material earnings-quality positive: reported EPS is not being inflated by an under-recognized dilution/compensation expense the way it is at many high-multiple tech names.
- No common-stock buyback program was identified in the financing-activities disclosures reviewed; capital return runs through a growing quarterly cash dividend instead (NT$7.00/share for Q1 2026, ex-dividend 2026-09-16, distribution 2026-10-08).

#### 7.4 Ownership and control structure

- **No controlling shareholder.** Largest holder is Taiwan's National Development Fund, Executive Yuan (a state entity), at **6.38%** of common shares as of 2026-02-28. Directors and executive officers as a group hold only **0.23%**.[¹¹]
- One-share-one-vote; no dual-class structure. The 20-F states TSMC "is not aware of any arrangement that may at a subsequent date result in a change of control."
- ADS mechanics: as of 2026-02-28, 5,313,450,863 common shares (≈20.5% of shares outstanding) were represented by 1,062,690,167 ADSs registered in the name of a nominee of Citibank, N.A. (the depositary), held of record by Cede & Co. and 173 other registered holders. Non-R.O.C. persons holding common shares directly (not via ADS) must generally do so through R.O.C.-licensed custodians.
- **Related-party positions:** TSMC owns ~27.6% of Vanguard International Semiconductor (VIS) — a legacy 1994 JV, now a dedicated foundry, from which TSMC purchased only NT$878mn (US$28mn) in 2025, ~0.1% of total cost of revenue — immaterial economically. TSMC **sold 8.1%** of its VIS stake on 2026-05-15 per the Q2 earnings-deck "recent major events" list; deal terms and counterparty were **not found** in the 20-F (a subsequent event) and should be confirmed against the underlying 6-K before being treated as fully cleared (flagged, not resolved, in this pass). TSMC also holds 38.8% of Systems on Silicon Manufacturing Company (SSMC, Singapore), a JV with NXP (61.2%) that manufactures exclusively for TSMC/NXP — a captive, non-arm's-length-by-design but economically minor structure. Neither relationship shows evidence of value transfer away from public shareholders.

#### 7.5 Foreign/cross-border claim-enforceability read

TSMC is an R.O.C.-incorporated foreign private issuer; U.S. holders own either ADSs (standard Citibank depositary mechanics, ordinary convertibility, no sanctions or capital-control impairment currently evident) or common shares held through R.O.C. custodians. Governing law for common-share holder rights is the R.O.C. Company Act and Securities and Exchange Act; dividends, capital changes, and preemptive rights require R.O.C. FSC/Ministry of Economic Affairs involvement. **The wrapper mechanics themselves look intact and unimpaired** — this is not a VIE, not a sanctioned jurisdiction, and not a capital-controls regime with a history of trapping foreign capital. The residual and genuinely material cross-border risk is **not instrument-level enforceability but geopolitical/cross-strait tail risk**, which the 20-F discloses in careful, non-alarmist language ("risk of outbreak of war or hostilities," "military conflicts") as a factor that could affect "the market price and liquidity of our ADSs and common shares" given that the majority of principal executive offices and production facilities sit in Taiwan. This risk is real, extremely difficult to price probabilistically from public disclosure alone, and is best treated as a fat-tail overlay on the valuation range above rather than a base-case adjustment.

---

### 8. Key Risks, Catalysts, and Next Dates

#### 8.1 Risks (ranked)

1. **Capacity-cycle / AI-capex-digestion risk (highest-weighted).** Current gross margin (67.7% 2Q26) is well above TSMC's own historically-cited long-run target (~53%+), and the 2026 capex ramp ($52–56bn, funding overwhelmingly advanced-node-specific, hard-to-repurpose capacity) is a large bet that AI-accelerator demand keeps accelerating. Prior semiconductor cycles (2018–19, 2022–23) have overshot and corrected; TSMC has historically been more resilient than commodity-memory peers due to its leading-edge monopoly, but a "digestion year" in hyperscaler capex would still compress utilization and margin off a very high base.
2. **Customer concentration, structurally rising.** Top-10 customers = 78% of FY2025 revenue (up from 70% in 2023); single largest customer 19–25% depending on year. A single large customer's design win loss, in-sourcing decision, or diversification to a rival foundry is a whole-P&L-scale risk, not a footnote.
3. **US tariff / export-control policy risk.** A live, still-unresolved policy stack: an April 2025 IEEPA tariff struck down by the Supreme Court in February 2026; a replacement Section 122 tariff; March 2026 Section 301 investigations into manufacturing overcapacity and forced labor targeting Taiwan among others; a concluded Section 232 investigation resulting in a 25% ad valorem tariff proclamation on certain advanced-computing-chip imports (with a US-domestic-use exemption and a tariff-offset program); and a January 2026 US-Taiwan trade framework capping reciprocal tariffs at ≤15% with preferential Section 232 treatment for Taiwanese producers investing in US capacity. **The company itself states the interaction of these mechanisms is "still unclear."** Duration/cliff risk is genuinely open — there is no single expiry date, but a live policy stack that could tighten or loosen materially over the next 12–24 months.
4. **Taiwan / cross-strait geopolitical risk** (see §7.5) — low-probability, high-severity, essentially unpriceable from public filings.
5. **Geographic-diversification margin dilution.** Arizona (Fab 21), Japan (JASM), and Germany (ESMC) fabs are all smaller-scale than the Taiwan base and — per general industry commentary, not independently quantified from TSMC's own primary disclosures in this pass — likely run at lower margins during ramp. This is a real but currently unquantified drag risk (flagged as a data-quality gap, §9).
6. **Technology execution risk.** The 16-angstrom node's "risk production" is slated for 2026; any slippage would push out the next leg of the technology roadmap.

#### 8.2 Catalysts / next dates

| Date | Event |
|---|---|
| ~10th–13th of each month | Monthly consolidated revenue report (6-K) |
| 2026-09-16 | Ex-dividend date, Q1 2026 cash dividend (NT$7.00/share) |
| 2026-09-22 | Dividend record date |
| 2026-10-08 | Dividend distribution date |
| **~mid-October 2026** | **Q3 2026 earnings release** (historical cadence: mid-Jan/Apr/Jul/Oct) — tests both flip triggers in §1 |
| Ongoing, undated | 16-angstrom risk-production milestone (2026, no specific date disclosed) |
| Ongoing, undated | Resolution of March-2026 Section 301 investigations (Taiwan among named economies); further clarity on US-Taiwan Section 232 preferential-treatment mechanics |
| **~mid-January 2027** | **Q4 2026 / FY2026 earnings release** — the second flip-trigger checkpoint |

This is precisely the "live-stamped-but-stale" trap the source-discipline reference warns about; **no FY2025 or FY2026 figure in this report was sourced from this surface** — all such figures trace to the as-filed 20-F text or the 6-K press release/deck instead.

This is the most material data-quality gap: the valuation section's forward-EPS build is entirely agent-inferred from the company's own guidance ranges, not cross-checked against consensus.

# PART II — Deep-Dive Analyses (evidence pack + full-depth skill application)


> Provenance: seven analyst passes over a six-file primary-evidence pack (SEC filings, TSMC IR, hfdt live pulls, and ~200 dated web citations), each pass applying named library skills and reference modules in full; every analytical move carries a `[skill: … / module: …]` attribution, every fact a source, every estimate an `[agent-inferred]` mark. Section II.7's adversarial-refutation ledger audited the other sections' load-bearing claims; its ordered corrections have been applied throughout.


## Section II.1 — Business Architecture, Wafer Unit Economics, and the AI Value-Chain Profit Pool

**TSMC (NYSE: TSM ADS = 5 common shares; TWSE 2330; CIK 0001046179). As of 2026-07-17.** TSM ADS closed $409.74 on 2026-07-16 (Q2'26 print day, −2.32%) [stockanalysis.com; hfdt prices fetch TSM, polygon live]. NT$ figures TIFRS consolidated; FX ≈ 31.6 (2Q26 actual) to 32.0 (3Q26 guide) NT$/US$ [TSMC 2Q26 press release ("2Q26 PR"), 2026-07-16, SEC 6-K acc. 0001046179-26-000451]. Computed figures show arithmetic; anything not directly sourced is marked **[agent-inferred]**.

---

### 1. What TSMC actually is economically

The market label — "semiconductor company," or even "foundry" — misclassifies the exposure unit. TSMC does not sell chips and does not bear end-chip average-selling-price (ASP) risk; it sells **metered manufacturing capacity priced per input unit: the wafer** (front-end logic wafers, and increasingly advanced-packaging wafers — CoWoS, Chip-on-Wafer-on-Substrate, is also priced per wafer). Decomposed by true exposure unit [skill: economic-exposure-map, Stage 2 exposure-unit decomposition]:

1. **Price/mix unit — the node-tier wafer ASP ladder.** Advanced technologies (≤7nm) were 77% of 2Q26 wafer revenue (2nm 3%, 3nm 30%, 5nm 33%, 7nm 11%) [2Q26 PR, 2026-07-16]; each tier prices materially above the last (see §2). This — not units — is the dominant owner-earnings driver: wafer shipments in 2025 (15,022 kpcs, thousand 12-inch-equivalent wafers) were *below* 2022 (15,253 kpcs), while US$ revenue rose 61% ($75.88B→$122.42B) [TSMC 4Q22/4Q25 management reports; evidence file 01 §7]. The entire 2022–25 revenue expansion was price/mix, zero net units [computed].
2. **Volume/utilization unit.** 4,336 kpcs shipped in 2Q26 (+16.6% YoY) [2Q26 mgmt report]; 3nm lines reported running above 100% utilization [BigGo call coverage, 2026-07-16 — REPORT]; annual capacity >17M wafers [FY2025 20-F, filed 2026-04-16].
3. **FX — a transaction margin lever, not translation noise** [skill: economic-exposure-map / module: CURRENCY_REVENUE_COST_MISMATCH.md]. Revenue is ~100% US$-linked, the cost base largely NT$: "every 1% appreciation of NT against US dollars will reduce our reported NT revenue by 1%… gross margin will come down by about 40 basis points" (CFO, 2Q25 call, 2025-07-17). The May-2025 TWD surge (USD/TWD 32.13→29.67 in two sessions) [CNBC, 2025-05-06] drove the 2Q25 gross-margin (GM) trough of 58.6% (−180bp stated FX effect) [2Q25 transcript]; TWD −9.47% y/y by July 2026 [tradingeconomics, 2026-07-16] is a tailwind now. Module output rule: **include FX in the driver model at 40bp/1%; do not capitalize the current weak-TWD tailwind into normalized margin.**
4. **Control/policy unit.** Export controls (7nm-and-below China AI ban since Nov-2024, unlifted as of mid-2026), the Jan-2026 25% Section 232 tariff with Taiwan-deal quota exemptions, and Taiwan's N-2 statute (leading edge stays two generations ahead at home, in force end-2025) [evidence file 04 §§1–2, 5] gate where the toll booth may operate, not whether it collects.

**Profit-pool isolation** [skill: economic-exposure-map / module: PROFIT_POOL_ISOLATION_AND_SURPLUS_CONCENTRATION.md]: the cash engine is leading-edge logic (≤7nm, 77% of wafer revenue) plus advanced packaging ("slightly over 10%" of 2025 revenue, guided to low-teens % in 2026; CoWoS wafer ASP reported nearing 7nm-wafer levels) [4Q25 call, 2026-01-15; TrendForce, 2026-04-28]. Mature nodes (≥16nm, ~23% of wafer revenue) are capacity absorbers being actively trimmed (6"/8" fabs shutting by end-2027) [4Q25 call; Astute, 2026]. At the *industry* layer, TSMC captures effectively the entire foundry profit pool: 2Q26 TSMC gross profit = 67.7% × $40.2B ≈ **$27.2B/quarter** vs UMC ~$0.56B (29.2% GM × $1.93B), GlobalFoundries ~$0.45B (27.6% × $1.63B), Samsung foundry ~breakeven (first *monthly* profit in ~3 years, June 2026), Intel Foundry −$10.3B operating loss on FY2025 [UMC 6-K Q1'26; GFS Q1'26 release; SamMobile/TechTimes, 2026-06; Intel 10-K FY2025]. TSMC holds 72.3% of foundry *revenue* (1Q26) [TrendForce, 2026-06-12] but effectively the **entire foundry-layer operating profit pool** [computed; caveat: Samsung's foundry returned to marginal monthly profitability in June 2026, so the literal ">100%" reading of 2025 is stale — the accurate 2026 statement is that TSMC earns on the order of 25–30× the COMBINED gross profit of UMC, GlobalFoundries, and Samsung foundry (still ~15–20× including SMIC)] — the textbook signature of a layer with the right to tax the chain [skill: profit-pool-and-channel-power / module: PROFIT_POOL_PRIVILEGE_MAP.md, privilege-ranking logic].

**One-line valuation instruction** [skill: economic-exposure-map, Stage 5 verdict]: *Value TSMC as a metered, capacity-rationed toll road on leading-edge compute — wafer volume × node-tier ASP ladder × utilization, with **gross profit per 12"-equivalent wafer** as the core metric and FX as a dated 40bp/1% overlay — not as a "semiconductor company" on blended-EPS comps against its own fabless customers (who bear end-ASP risk TSMC does not) or against commodity foundries (whose 27–29% GMs share nothing with TSMC's 67.7% except the word "foundry").*

### 2. Wafer and node unit economics

#### 2.1 The node-tier ASP ladder (reported prices — all UNOFFICIAL; TSMC does not disclose pricing)

[skill: tech-hardware-and-semiconductors / module: NODE_TIER_ASP_LADDER_MIX_AS_A_STACKABLE_GROWTH_COMPONENT.md]

| Node tier | Reported wafer price | Step vs prior tier | Source (unofficial) |
|---|---|---|---|
| N7 (7nm) | no direct 2026 print in pack; CoWoS packaging-wafer ASP is reported "nearing 7nm levels" — used below only as a relative anchor | — | TrendForce, 2026-04-28 |
| N5/N4 family | between N7 and N3 [agent-inferred interpolation; no direct print in pack] | — | — |
| N3 (3nm) | ~$18–20k (one line of reporting) vs ~$25–27k (another); conflicting | — | wccftech, 2026; TechNode, 2025-10-09; Tom's Hardware (China Times) |
| N2 (2nm) | ~$30,000 | +10–20% vs the $25–27k N3 figure; ~+50–66% vs the $18k figure | TechNode, 2025-10-09; Tom's Hardware |
| A16/A14 | rumored up to ~$45,000 | +~50% vs N2 | China Times via Tom's Hardware |

Triangulating the conflict [agent-inferred, arithmetic shown]: 2Q26 revenue $40.20B less ~13% packaging (management: low-teens % of 2026 revenue) → wafer-only revenue ≈ $35.0B; 3nm = 30% ≈ $10.5B/quarter. At $18–20k/wafer that implies 525–583k 3nm wafers/quarter ≈ **175–194k wafers-per-month (wpm)** — matching TrendForce's ~180k wpm 2026 3nm capacity estimate at the reported >100% utilization [TrendForce, 2026-04-27; BigGo, 2026-07-16]. At $25–27k it implies only ~130–140k wpm, inconsistent with a sold-out 180k wpm line. **The lower N3 figure (~$18–20k) triangulates better; treat $25–27k as list-flavored — large customers pay below list** [module rule: cross-check modeled ASP vs rumored list]. Same check on N2: 3% × $35.0B ≈ $1.05B ÷ $30k ≈ 35k wafers ≈ 12k wpm in its first revenue quarter — sensible one quarter into high-volume manufacturing (HVM began 4Q25) [4Q25 call, 2026-01-15].

#### 2.2 Blended revenue per wafer — level and trajectory [all computed: US$ revenue ÷ 12"-equivalent shipments; both inputs company-reported]

| Period | US$ revenue | Shipments (kpcs) | **Rev/wafer** | GM | **Gross profit/wafer** |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2019 | $34.63B | 10,068 | **$3,440** | 46.0% | $1,582 |
| 2020 | $45.51B | 12,398 | $3,671 | 53.1% | $1,949 |
| 2021 | $56.82B | 14,179 | $4,008 | 51.6% | $2,068 |
| 2022 | $75.88B | 15,253 | $4,975 | 59.6% | $2,965 |
| 2023 | $69.30B | 12,002 | $5,774 | 54.4% | $3,141 |
| 2024 | $90.08B | 12,910 | $6,978 | 56.1% | $3,915 |
| 2025 | $122.42B | 15,022 | $8,149 | 59.9% | $4,881 |
| 1Q26 | $35.90B | 4,174 | $8,601 | 66.2% | $5,694 |
| **2Q26** | **$40.20B** | **4,336** | **$9,271** | **67.7%** | **$6,276** |

Sources: annual figures per TSMC 4Q management reports 2019–2025 (evidence file 01 §7); quarterlies per 1Q26/2Q26 releases [SEC 6-Ks, 2026-04-16 and 2026-07-16]. All rev/wafer and GP/wafer cells **[agent-inferred: computed]**; note the numerator includes packaging/other revenue (~10–13%), so this is revenue per shipped wafer, i.e., it deliberately captures packaging attach.

Read-through: **revenue per wafer rose 2.7x from 2019 to 2Q26 ($3,440→$9,271) and gross profit per wafer 4.0x ($1,582→$6,276)** [computed]. YoY 2Q26 rev/wafer: 2Q25 = $40.20B/1.337 = $30.07B over 4,336/1.166 = 3,719 kpcs → $8,086; growth = **+14.7% YoY** and +7.8% QoQ [computed] — the QoQ jump driven by 2nm's first 3%, 3nm 25%→30%, and HPC (high-performance computing) 61%→66% of revenue [2Q26 mgmt report]. Critically, the ladder held in the downcycle: **2023 shipments fell 21% while rev/wafer rose 16%** ($4,975→$5,774) and GM fell only 5.2pp [computed from file 01 §7] — hard evidence that TSMC can shed volume and hold price, the defining volume-refusal test for pricing power [skill: profit-pool-and-channel-power / module: VOLUME_REFUSAL_AND_PRICING_POWER.md — "volume decline accepted with stable or improving gross profit dollars"].

#### 2.3 Mix-up-the-ladder as a stackable growth line

Model three separable engines, not one blended ASP [skill: tech-hardware-and-semiconductors / module: NODE_TIER_ASP_LADDER…]: (i) **units** (+16.6% YoY in 2Q26); (ii) **base-price hikes** (reported +3–10% across advanced nodes effective 2026, differentiated smartphone ~+5% / CPU ~+7% / HPC-AI up to +10%, with annual hikes planned through ~2029; a further ~15% 3nm hike reported for 2H26 — all supply-chain reports, not company-confirmed) [TrendForce, 2025-12-29; Tom's Hardware, 2026; cryptobriefing, 2026]; (iii) **tier migration** (≤7nm: 69% of 2024 wafer revenue → 74% 2025 → 77% 2Q26; 3nm 24%→30%; 2nm 0→3% and "a bigger node than 3nm from the start," tape-outs ~4x N3 at the same stage) [FY25 20-F; 2Q26 PR; CFO, 2026-01-15; DigiTimes, 2026-07-16]. The migration line is the falsifiable KPI: it compounds even at flat units and flat list prices, and it is observable quarterly in the node-mix table. The offset to model against it: N2 dilutes 2026 GM ~2–3pp full-year (company; same-day 2Q26 coverage cited 3–4pp for 2H26 — verify against the official transcript), overseas fabs dilute 2–3pp early widening to 3–4pp, and depreciation grows high-teens % in 2026 on a 5-year equipment life [4Q25 call, 2026-01-15; 20-F PP&E policy; Investing.com 2Q26 coverage, 2026-07-16].

#### 2.4 The unit earns its capital [skill: unit-economics, Stages 2–5]

The "unit" here is a wafer of capacity. Denominator integrity check [module discipline: rebuild the denominator before accepting ROI]: capex per 1k wpm of N2 is "substantially higher" than N3, and A14 higher still (company, no figure) [4Q25 call], so the ladder must out-run capital intensity. It does, so far: depreciation per shipped wafer was NT$50.6k ≈ **$1,575 (FY24: NT$653.6B ÷ 12,910 kpcs ÷ 32.13)** [computed from XBRL companyfacts + mgmt report] against 2Q26 gross profit per wafer of $6,276. Crude program-level payback [agent-inferred]: 2024–26E capex ≈ $29.8B + $40.9B + $62B(mid) ≈ $133B; incremental annual gross profit 2026E vs 2024 ≈ (0.66×$171B) − (0.561×$90.1B) ≈ $62B → **≈2.1 years of incremental gross profit pays back the entire three-year capex program** (2026E revenue = $122.42B × 1.40, per the raised "slightly above 40%" guide [2Q26 call via Investing.com, 2026-07-16]). Corroborating returns: ROE 35.4% FY25, 45.9% annualized in 2Q26; management through-cycle floor: GM "56% and higher," ROE high-20s% [4Q25 call; 2Q26 presentation, 6-K]. Grade A unit economics — with the explicit caveat that the marginal unit (N2/A16 wafer, Arizona wafer) is costlier than the blended historical unit, the classic marginal-vs-average trap [skill: unit-economics / module: NEW_UNIT_RETURN_DECAY_INTO_SECONDARY_MARKETS.md, applied to nodes/geographies rather than stores].

### 3. The AI value-chain profit-pool map

#### 3.1 Gross-profit dollars per accelerator across the chain

[skill: profit-pool-and-channel-power, Stage 1 value-chain map; skill: tech-hardware-and-semiconductors / module: VALUE_CHAIN_TAKE_RATE_BRIDGE.md]

Per high-end merchant accelerator (~$30–40k end ASP frame; Blackwell-class dual-die package). Every row below is **[agent-inferred]** estimation anchored to pack facts, arithmetic shown:

| Chain layer | Revenue per accelerator | Gross profit per accelerator | Basis |
|---|---|---|---|
| Merchant GPU designer (NVIDIA) | $30–40k | ~$21–30k at ~70–75% GM [agent-inferred margin] | End ASP frame; NVIDIA FY26 revenue $215.9B, Q1 FY27 DC $75.0B [NVIDIA 8-K/PRs, 2026-02-25, 2026-05] |
| **TSMC — front-end wafer** | ~$500–700 | included below | ~800mm² die → ~60 gross/50–58 good die/wafer → ~25–29 dual-die GPUs per wafer; N4/N5-class wafer $14–18k [agent-inferred; N3 anchor $18–20k] |
| **TSMC — CoWoS + SoIC + test** | ~$800–1,300 | included below | 5.5-reticle CoWoS package (company, Apr-2026 symposium) → ~10–13 packages/packaging wafer; CoWoS wafer ASP "nears 7nm levels" [TrendForce, 2026-04-28] ≈ ~$10k [agent-inferred] |
| **TSMC total** | **~$1,300–2,000 (≈4–6% of end ASP)** | **~$900–1,350 at 67.7% GM** | [computed] |
| HBM supplier (high-bandwidth memory, ~8 stacks/192GB) | ~$2,500–3,500 | ~$1,300–2,000 [agent-inferred HBM margins 50–60%] | [agent-inferred; memory pricing volatile] |
| Litho-tool maker (ASML), amortized | ~$11–19 flow-through | captured at tool sale, not per unit | Total TSMC depreciation $1,575/wafer (FY24, computed §2.4); litho ~20–30% of equipment [agent-inferred] → ~$315–470/wafer ÷ 25–29 GPUs |

**Top-down cross-check** [module requirement: bottom-up bridge must reconcile with top-down]: NVIDIA was TSMC's largest customer at ~19% of 2025 revenue ≈ $23.4B [FY25 20-F customer-A 19%; press math, DigiTimes/wccftech, 2026-01]; against NVIDIA FY26 revenue of $215.9B that is a **~10.8% take-rate of NVIDIA's entire revenue** [computed; calendar mismatch flagged — NVIDIA FY26 ends 2026-01-25]. The gap vs the ~4–6% per-flagship-GPU figure reconciles honestly: NVIDIA's TSMC bill also covers gaming/pro silicon (higher silicon-content share of lower ASPs), Grace CPUs, NVLink switches and NICs in rack-scale systems, and CoWoS/base-die work — system-level TSMC content per "GPU sold" is roughly 2x die-level content [agent-inferred]. At the market level: TSMC's AI-accelerator revenue was "high-teens %" of 2025 revenue ≈ ~$21B [4Q25 call], growing at a guided mid-to-high-50s% CAGR (2024–29) → ~$32B 2026E [computed], against ~$450–525B of AI-related hyperscaler capex (one compilation puts AI-related at ~$450B; ~75% of the $700–725B big-4 2026 total would imply ~$525–545B) [Tom's Hardware/creditsights compilations, 2026] → **TSMC directly captures roughly 5–7 cents of each AI-infrastructure dollar, at 67.7% gross margin, without bearing end-ASP risk** [computed].

#### 3.2 Why TSMC's capture EXPANDS as AI silicon commoditizes

[skill: tech-hardware-and-semiconductors / module: PRICE_PER_WAFER_DECOUPLES_CAPTURE_FROM_END_CHIP_ASP.md]

TSMC prices the **input unit** (wafer); NVIDIA prices the **finished unit** (chip/rack) at a scarcity-inflated ASP. Per the module's regime table, peak scarcity (very high end-ASP, constrained units) is the *worst* regime for an input-priced supplier — few wafers move per dollar of end-market revenue. As AI silicon commoditizes — merchant ASPs fall, custom ASICs (application-specific ICs) proliferate, volumes scale — wafer consumption per dollar of AI spend **rises**, and so does TSMC's take-rate. The ASIC math makes it concrete [agent-inferred]: a hyperscaler ASIC at perhaps $3–8k end-cost carries similar-order silicon+packaging content (~$1.0–1.5k) to a $30–40k GPU → TSMC's share of each end-dollar is ~15–30% on ASICs vs ~4–6% on flagship GPUs — a take-rate uplift per dollar of compute that shifts from merchant GPU to custom ASIC — plausibly on the order of **3–5x [agent-inferred; the DIRECTION is well-evidenced, the magnitude rests on unofficial content estimates and should not be load-bearing]**. And that shift routes almost entirely through TSMC: Google TPU (Ironwood, Nov-2025), AWS Trainium3 (N3, GA Dec-2025, >1M chips deployed), Microsoft Maia 200 (N3, Jan-2026), Meta MTIA (four more generations roadmapped), OpenAI's Broadcom co-designed XPU (N3 end-2026, gen-2 on A16) are all TSMC-fabbed; the only exceptions are Huawei Ascend (SMIC) and the *reported* Maia 2 move to Intel 18A-P [evidence file 03 §5]. This makes TSMC a structurally different — and arguably safer — claim on AI than its customers: it is long **units × silicon content**, they are long **price × share**. The module's falsifier disciplines the claim: the thesis breaks only if compute shifts to a *substitute input that bypasses TSMC's wafers* (SMIC's DUV 5nm at ~20% yields serving China; Intel 18A/14A winning hyperscaler ASICs at scale) — see kill signals.

#### 3.3 The inverse test: what if end-chip ASPs crash?

Three cases [skill: profit-pool-and-channel-power, Stage 2 gate — do not celebrate share without stress]:
- **Commoditization crash (ASP down, units up):** tailwind per §3.2. NVIDIA's ~$25k/unit gross-profit pool compresses; TSMC's wafer pull grows.
- **Demand air pocket (units down):** the real exposure. HPC is 66% of revenue [2Q26 PR]; depreciation ≈51% of cost of goods sold (FY24: NT$653.6B ÷ NT$1,270B COGS) [computed from XBRL] is fixed, so decrementals are steep. Empirical anchor: 2023 — shipments −21%, US$ revenue −8.7%, GM −5.2pp to 54.4%, still ~2x peer GMs [file 01 §7]. A 30% cut in AI wafer starts ≈ −6–8% revenue direct [agent-inferred: ~20% AI-accelerator share × 30%, plus CoWoS] before utilization deleverage; management's through-cycle floor is GM ≥56% [4Q25 call]. Survivable; the multiple, not the franchise, carries this risk.
- **Bypass crash (ASP down because a non-TSMC substrate wins):** bounded today — Intel 14A has **zero committed external customers** (two prospects; commitments expected 2H26–1H27; Intel's 10-K warns it may pause 14A absent a customer) [Tom's Hardware, 2026-01; Intel 10-K FY2025]; Samsung SF2 yields reported mid-50s% vs TSMC N2's 60–70% [phonearena/wccftech, 2026]; SMIC is export-walled and yield-limited (~20% at 5nm; Huawei's TSMC die bank exhausted early 2026) [SemiAnalysis, 2026]. The scenario to date-stamp, not dismiss (§5).
