### 4. Customer decision-point ethnography — who decides, and at what absurdity threshold

[skill: customer-decision-point-ethnography, Stages 1–3, 5]

**Decision venue and chooser.** The decision is made 2–4 years before revenue, at design-start: a silicon-engineering leadership team (Apple, NVIDIA, AMD, Qualcomm, MediaTek; for hyperscalers, the silicon team *plus* the ASIC design partner — Broadcom or Alchip/Marvell — who physically owns the foundry interface) selects a process design kit (PDK), ports IP, and commits a tape-out. The payer is the product P&L (for hyperscalers, an AI-capex budget funded by *expected* cloud revenue); the beneficiary is the end customer of compute [module: PAYER_CHOOSER_BENEFICIARY_MAP applied]. The chooser optimizes performance-per-watt, yield-adjusted schedule certainty, and packaging access — not wafer price.

**The real alternatives at the decision moment (mid-2026):** Samsung SF2 — real but second-tier: Exynos 2600 shipping, Tesla AI5 taped out at Taylor on SF2 with volume realistically mid-to-2H-2027, yields reported ~mid-50s% [Korea Times, 2026-07-13; TechTimes, 2026-07-14]. Intel 18A — real for Intel's own tiles (Panther Lake launched 2026-01-05) and for a thin external base (Microsoft, AWS fabric chip, reported Maia 2); 14A external commitments still pending [file 03 §2]. SMIC — unavailable to Western customers (export controls). For flagship AI/mobile silicon, the observed choice set at the venue collapses to one proven option; N2's ~15 committed customers and 4x-N3 tape-out count are the revealed-preference tally [TrendForce, 2025-09-22; DigiTimes, 2026-07-16].

**Switching-cost stack** [module: SWITCHING_COST_STACK.md]: technical (full PDK/EDA re-qualification, IP re-port, re-timing closure — a $50–100M+, 12–24-month program per flagship die [agent-inferred]); workflow (co-optimization with TSMC packaging roadmaps — >90% of CoWoS output is TSMC's [BigGo, 2026-07-16], so even a defector may still need TSMC packaging); peak-stress reliability (missing an AI product cycle at NVIDIA's $75B/quarter data-center run-rate is a career-and-company event — the worst-hour cost dwarfs any wafer saving); economic (see threshold below).

**The customer absurdity threshold** [module: CUSTOMER_ABSURDITY_TEST.md — name the decision, the alternative, the numeric threshold]. The wafer is a *small-invoice, high-consequence* input: front-end silicon is ~$500–700 of a $30–40k accelerator (≈2%) [§3.1], so a +20% wafer hike moves the end-unit bill of materials ~0.3–0.4% [computed]. In smartphones: a ~100mm² N3 die ≈ $30–35 of silicon on an ~$1,100 phone; a +5% hike ≈ **$1.60 per phone** [agent-inferred arithmetic]. The savings-vs-failure inequality [SWITCHING_COST_STACK worksheet] essentially never clears against an *unproven* alternative: rational tolerance for a TSMC premium at the flagship tier is plausibly **>30–50% sustained wafer-price premium versus a proven-yield competitor, and effectively unbounded versus an unproven one** [agent-inferred]. The pack brackets the revealed tolerance directly: AMD's CEO publicly accepted **5–20% more for Arizona-fabbed wafers** purely for supply-chain resilience — same supplier, same node, zero performance gain [Tom's Hardware, 2026] — while the *true* Arizona cost premium is ~10% [TechInsights, 2025] and quoted Arizona prices run 25–30% over Taiwan [financialcontent, 2026-04-15]. Customers volunteering to pay above cost for geography is the strongest available ethnographic read of the tolerance band.
**Observed defections define the boundary, not the center:** Intel insourcing 80–90% of Nova Lake compute tiles to 18A (from a 60–70%-to-TSMC plan) is a customer who *owns a fab* exercising a make-buy option as its yields improved [TrendForce, 2026-07-15] [skill: profit-pool-and-channel-power / module: CUSTOMER_MAKE_BUY_AND_PAIN_ECONOMICS.md]; Tesla dual-sourcing AI5/AI6 across TSMC and Samsung is a price-and-capacity-motivated buyer with in-house architectural control and a $16.5B anchor deal [Tom's Hardware, 2026-04; file 03 §3]; reported Maia 2 → 18A-P is an internal-deployment chip where absolute leadership is non-essential. Apple (>half of initial N2 capacity), NVIDIA (lead A16 customer), AMD's data-center flagships, and every Broadcom-designed hyperscaler ASIC stayed. The pattern: **defection happens where the defector owns a fab, owns the architecture, or doesn't need the frontier — never yet at the flagship tier.**

---

### 5. Contract-repricing clocks — both directions, dated

[skill: contract-repricing-and-customer-power-clock, Stages 2–4]

**TSMC's forward clock (supplier-favorable).** Wafer agreements reprice on an annual cadence [agent-inferred industry practice]; the reported schedule: **2026-01**: +3–10% across advanced nodes (smartphone ~+5%, CPU ~+7%, HPC/AI up to +10%), covering ~74% of wafer revenue [TrendForce, 2025-12-29; Tom's Hardware, 2026 — REPORT]; **2H26**: additional ~15% on 3nm reported [cryptobriefing — low confidence]; **N2 at ~$30k** = +10–20% embedded tier step [TechNode, 2025-10-09]; **annually through ~2029**: hikes planned "four consecutive years" [TrendForce/wccftech — REPORT]. Realized capture shows in the guidance ratchet: FY26 US$ growth "close to 30%" (2026-01-15) → "above 30%" (2026-04-16) → **"slightly above 40%"** (2026-07-16), with GM printing 67.7% vs a 65.5–67.5% guide [6-Ks; call coverage]. Management's own anchor moved: long-term GM floor restated from "53% and higher" (2Q25 call) to **"56% and higher"** (4Q25 call), framed as "our pricing will remain strategic, not opportunistic, to earn our value" [CFO, 2026-01-15] and "we earn our value… our gross margin is enough for our long-term sustaining expansion" [2Q26 call via Investing.com, 2026-07-16]. Customer prepayments/long-term capacity agreements cushion the cycle [agent-inferred from TSMC's long-running capacity-deposit ("temporary receipts") practice; verify balance in the FY25 20-F]. Reporting risk: **the entire hike schedule is supply-chain press, not company disclosure** — the auditable trace is the node-mix table, GM prints, and rev/wafer [§2.2].

**The reverse clock — when customer power can reassert** [module: SWITCHING_CALENDAR_AND_HYBRID_INSOURCING.md — the binding date is the last practical implementation date, not the contract date; module: CONTRACT_VINTAGE_DEMAND_REGIME_MISMATCH.md — 2026-vintage pricing assumes scarcity that may not exist at the 2028 reset]:

| Date window | Event | Mechanism |
|---|---|---|
| 2H26–1H27 | Intel 14A external commitment decisions (two prospects running test chips; Intel: capacity only against firm commitments) [Tom's Hardware, 2026-01] | First credible leading-edge second source *announcement*; production impact 2028–29. Conversely, no commitment ≈ Intel's 10-K contemplates pausing 14A — the reverse clock stops. |
| End-2026 → 2027 | CoWoS supply-demand gap narrows ~20%→~10% (TrendForce, 2026-06-15); TSMC ~90k→~120–140k wpm + OSAT adds | Packaging scarcity rent fades first; watch OSAT pricing and the ~240–270k wafers/yr outsourced to Amkor/SPIL |
| Mid-to-2H 2027 | Tesla AI5 volume on Samsung SF2 at Taylor; SF2P+ line 2027 [Korea Times, 2026-07-13; TechTimes, 2026-05-31] | First at-scale non-TSMC 2nm-class AI silicon; a working benchmark for the 2028 design cycle |
| Jan–Feb 2027 | Hyperscaler FY27 capex guides | The funding-line test [module: CUSTOMER_QUALITY_AND_SUPPLIER_SURPLUS funding-line logic]: TSMC's AI revenue sits inside customers' capex budgets funded by *expected* AI revenue; the capex-to-revenue gap is already flagged [Forbes, 2026-06-02]. Deceleration from +77% 2026 growth resets the 2027–28 wafer negotiation regime |
| 2028 design-start cycle | If Samsung yields reach TSMC-minus-a-few-points AND Intel 18A/14A is externally proven, anchor customers gain a real BATNA for 2029–30 products | The through-2029 hike plan is a plan, not a contract — its final years are hostage to this window [CONTRACT_VINTAGE… applied] |
| Continuous | ASIC shift | Reprices *NVIDIA*, not TSMC (§3.2) — a customer-power clock running against TSMC's customer, to TSMC's take-rate benefit |

Net verdict [module: REPRICING_CLOCK discipline]: through the 2027 reset, every clock hand points TSMC's way (sold-out leading edge, 4x tape-outs, no proven alternative). The first date at which a counterparty can credibly start a negotiation with leverage is the **2028 design cycle, for 2029–30 revenue** — and only if at least two of {Intel 14A committed externally, Samsung SF2/SF2P proven at scale, AI demand air pocket} have occurred by then.

---

### (a) Key judgments

| # | Judgment | Conf. | What would falsify it |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | TSMC is an input-priced (per-wafer) toll booth on leading-edge compute; value it on wafer volume × node-ASP ladder × utilization with GP/wafer as the core metric, not as a chip company on blended comps | H | Evidence of value-share/finished-unit pricing (e.g., revenue contractually indexed to customer chip ASPs) — none found in pack |
| 2 | Mix-up-the-ladder is a stackable growth line worth ~7–15%/yr independent of units (rev/wafer +14.7% YoY 2Q26; +64% 2022→25 on flat units) | H | Two consecutive quarters of YoY decline in blended rev/wafer not explained by FX; N2 share failing to reach ~10% of wafer revenue by 4Q27 [agent-inferred benchmark] |
| 3 | TSMC captures ~4–6% of a flagship GPU's end value (~$1.3–2.0k content, ~$0.9–1.4k gross profit) and ~5–7% of AI-infrastructure capex; this share RISES as AI silicon commoditizes/ASIC-shifts (3–5x take-rate on ASIC dollars) | M (estimates), H (direction) | ASIC volumes migrating to Intel 18A/14A or Samsung at scale (bypass); ASIC content estimates proving materially lower on teardown |
| 4 | Anchor-customer switching threshold exceeds any plausible price premium (>30–50% vs proven alternative; 5–20% premium already volunteered for geography alone); flagship defection risk before 2028 is de minimis | H (to 2027), M (2028+) | A top-5 customer committing a *flagship* product to a competing node; Samsung/Intel yield parity reports sustained across two quarters |
| 5 | Pricing power is currently one-sided: annual hikes through ~2029 reported, GM floor restated 53%→56%, 2023 precedent shows price holds even at −21% volume | M (hike schedule is unofficial press) | 4Q26–1Q27 reports of the 2027 hike round suspended/rolled back; GM guide reset below 60% absent FX |
| 6 | The dominant fragility is a demand air pocket (HPC 66% of revenue, top customer 19%, top-10 ≈78%), not margin competition; GM trough in that case ≈ 54–56%, not peer levels | M | Hyperscaler 2027 capex guides flat/down AND monthly revenue YoY <+15% for 2 consecutive prints (alarm; <+10% x2 = confirmation); GM printing below 56% floor |

### (b) Kill signals (dated thresholds)

1. **By 2027-06-30:** any top-5 TSMC customer publicly commits a flagship (not second-tier/internal) AI or CPU product to Intel 14A or Samsung SF2P → kills the switching-moat leg (judgment 4); begin repricing the 2029+ ladder assumptions immediately.
2. **By end-2027:** CoWoS supply-demand gap ≤0 / OSAT CoWoS price cuts reported (TrendForce-class sourcing) → packaging scarcity rent ending; cut the packaging-attach growth line.
3. **Any two consecutive months (watch monthly prints, 10th of each month):** revenue YoY below +15% (vs +35.6% 1H26) → alarm; below +10% for a further two consecutive prints → confirmed air-pocket onset; 2023 playbook applies (volume −20%, price holds).
4. **4Q26–1Q27 negotiation season:** credible reports that the 2027 advanced-node hike round is suspended or reversed → pricing-clock inversion two years early.
5. **Two consecutive quarters:** blended rev/wafer down YoY with FX contribution <half the decline [computed per §2.2 method] → the ladder itself is broken (mix-down or discounting), the single most direct falsifier this section owns.
6. **FX regime:** sustained USD/TWD below ~29 (the 3Q25 stress level) → ~−4pp GM vs the 32.0 planning rate at 40bp/1%; margin model must be rebased (this is a model reset, not a thesis kill).

### (c) Handoffs

**To valuation:** (i) value on **gross profit per wafer × shipment trajectory** plus a separately-modeled packaging line — not blended EPS comps vs fabless names; (ii) normalized GM band 56% (company floor) to ~68% (current), with dilution stack: overseas fabs −2–3pp→−3–4pp, N2 ramp −2–3pp FY26, depreciation +high-teens% 2026, FX ±40bp per 1%; (iii) FY26 company frame: revenue >$171B ($122.42B×1.40+), capex $60–64B; (iv) QoE adjustments: 2Q26 net income carries NT$63.2B one-off VIS disposal gain (non-operating NT$95.8B) — use operating income (+65.4% YoY) as the run-rate line; net cash ≈ NT$2,654B ≈ US$83B; (v) pick one listing basis: ADS-implied cap ≈ $2.12T vs local ≈ $1.98T (≈7% ADS premium, non-synchronous closes).
**To red-team:** attack, in order of leverage — (1) the AI funding line (customers' capex vs their AI revenue; TSMC's §3 take-rate math survives commoditization but not a units bust); (2) the unofficial pricing schedule (all hikes are supply-chain press — build the falsification path via node-mix/GM prints, §2.2); (3) marginal-unit economics (N2/A16/Arizona capex-per-k-wpm rising; test whether GP/wafer growth continues to outrun depreciation/wafer); (4) the 2028 second-source window (judgments 4–5 expire there, not in 2026); (5) concentration mechanics (19%/17%/78% top-customer structure per FY25 20-F) interacting with the Section 232/tariff regime. Geopolitical tail risk (Taiwan strait, N-2 statute, $265B US buildout economics) is owned by the geopolitics section; this section's interface to it is the Arizona 25–30%-quoted vs ~10%-cost premium — resilience pricing that is currently margin-accretive.

*All claims sourced to the evidence pack (files 01–06, compiled 2026-07-16/17) or marked [agent-inferred]; unofficial pricing/capacity figures are labeled reports, not company disclosures.*


## Section II.2 — Moat Arithmetic and the Competitive Threat Map

**TSMC (NYSE: TSM, ADS = 5 common shares; TWSE: 2330) — as of 2026-07-17; ADS $409.74 (2026-07-16 close)**

*Method note.* This section runs the sector skill's replication ladder, anchor-customer flywheel, socket book, ASIC-shift, and roadmap-cadence modules, then overlays a dated structural-change threat map and a per-share compounding grade. Facts carry source + date from the evidence pack (compiled 2026-07-16/17) with its quality labels ([COMPANY], [REPORT], [PRESS], [ANALYST]); analytical estimates are marked **[agent-inferred]**. Acronyms: EUV = extreme-ultraviolet lithography; High-NA = high-numerical-aperture EUV; DUV = deep-ultraviolet; GAA = gate-all-around transistor; HVM = high-volume manufacturing; wpm = wafers per month; PPA = power/performance/area; PDK = process design kit; ASP = average selling price; ASIC = application-specific integrated circuit; OSAT = outsourced assembly and test; CoWoS = Chip-on-Wafer-on-Substrate (TSMC 2.5D packaging); SoIC = System-on-Integrated-Chips (3D stacking); CoPoS = Chip-on-Panel-on-Substrate (panel-level); HBM = high-bandwidth memory; GM = gross margin.

---

### 1. Replication arithmetic: the fab-buildout feasibility ladder

[skill: tech-hardware-and-semiconductors / module: FAB_BUILDOUT_FEASIBILITY_ARITHMETIC_LADDER.md]

The module's discipline: a "cannot replicate" claim is underwritable only when written as four numbers — capex-for-competitive-throughput, ramp time at leader yields, implied lag scaled by the revenue ratio, and the economics haircut of the #2 alternative. Anti-pattern to avoid: modeling the challenger's catch-up at the leader's ramp pace.

**The moving target first.** TSMC FY2026 capital budget: **US$60–64B**, raised mid-year from $52–56B (2Q26 call, 2026-07-16 [SECONDARY, transcript pending]; 4Q25 call 2026-01-15 for the original), with ~70–80% for advanced process = **~$42–51B/yr of leading-edge capex alone**. Cumulative 2021–2025 capex was **US$167B plus ~$30B of R&D** (CFO, 4Q25 call, 2026-01-15). Capacity exceeded 17M 12"-equivalent wafers/yr in 2025 (FY2025 20-F, filed 2026-04-16); CoWoS is estimated at ~90K wpm mid-2026 heading to ~120–140K wpm by end-2026 [REPORT: TrendForce 2026-06-15; call coverage 2026-07-16]. Foundry revenue share: **72.3% in 1Q26** (TrendForce, 2026-06-12), up ~11pts in nine quarters.

#### 1a. Intel

| Ladder step | Number | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| 1. Capex for competitive throughput | TSMC-class leading-edge + packaging capacity costs order-of-magnitude **$150–250B over ~a decade** [agent-inferred, scaled from TSMC's $167B/5yr at 70–80% advanced]. Intel's posture is the opposite: Magdeburg/Poland cancelled, Ohio slowed (July 2025), "no more blank checks" — 14A capacity only against firm commitments (Jan 2026 call). Anchor capital raised ($8.9B US-government 10% stake Aug 2025; $2B SoftBank; $5B NVIDIA Sept 2025) ≈ **$16B ≈ four months of TSMC's advanced-node capex run-rate** [computed]. | Intel 10-K FY2025; press 2025-07/08 |
| 2. Ramp time at leader yields | Intel has **never demonstrated an external-customer leading-edge ramp**. Its one internal proof, 18A, reached retail (Panther Lake) 2026-01-05 with yields only characterized as "improved significantly" (~7%/month defect-density gain [PRESS], no absolute disclosure). TSMC benchmark: N2 in HVM 4Q25 "with good yield" at two sites, revenue within one quarter (3% of 2Q26 wafer revenue), tape-outs ~4× N3 at the same stage (DigiTimes 2026-07-16). | 4Q25 call 2026-01-15; 2Q26 6-K |
| 3. Implied lag vs the moving target | External-foundry revenue ratio: TSMC **$122.4B** FY25 vs Intel Foundry **external $307M** → **~400:1**; even the charitable total-segment basis ($17.8B, mostly internal) is ~7:1. The module's lag rule (leader ramp × revenue ratio) puts replication in the **multi-decade** range on the charitable basis, off the chart on the honest one — against a target adding a node every ~2–3 years and raising capex 15% intra-year (2026-07-16). | Intel 10-K FY2025; TSMC 4Q25 6-K |
| 4. Economics of being #2 | Intel Foundry FY25 op loss **−$10.3B** on $17.8B segment revenue (op margin ≈ −58%) vs TSMC **GM 67.7% / op margin 60.3%** (2Q26 6-K). Even good trailing-edge foundries earn 27–29% GM (UMC 29.2%, GlobalFoundries 27.6% IFRS, Q1'26). A #2 must price below a leader *raising* prices 3–10%/yr through ~2029 [REPORT 2025-12-29] — early external wafers near margin-less [agent-inferred]. Intel's 10-K: may "pause or discontinue" 14A absent a significant external customer; two prospects, **zero committed** (Jan 2026). | Q1'26 UMC 6-K / GFS release |

**Verdict (Intel):** organic replication is **uninvestable** on the ladder — the same verdict the module produced for the analog-semis case at far smaller ratios. Intel's actual, rational strategy is the boutique one: fill 18A with internal tiles plus a handful of US-sovereign/hyperscaler second-source sockets (reported Maia 2 on 18A-P; AWS fabric chip; DoD RAMP-C). That erodes at the margin; it does not replicate. Critical nuance: the **state stake breaks the normal exit logic** — a challenger that cannot self-fund usually capitulates, but a 10%-government-owned Intel can sustain losses politically. That extends how long the threat *exists* without compressing the yield/ecosystem learning curve that determines when it *bites*. [skill: structural-change-threat-map / module: CAPABILITY_VS_WILLINGNESS_AND_FAILED_ENTRY_EVIDENCE.md — willingness is now state-underwritten; weight the failed-entry ladder accordingly, not capability.]

#### 1b. Samsung

| Ladder step | Number | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| 1. Capex for competitive throughput | Samsung Foundry revenue ~**$3.2B/quarter (6.5% share, 1Q26)** ≈ ~$13B/yr; TSMC's 2026 capex ($60–64B) alone is **~4.7–4.9× Samsung's entire foundry revenue** [computed]. Foundry+System LSI lost **~KRW 6.0T (~$4.3B) FY2025** (KRW 5.3T FY24) [press-compiled]; parent memory cash funds it, but capacity is rationed toward Taylor, TX and internal loads. | TrendForce 2026-06-12 / 2025-11-13 |
| 2. Ramp at leader yields | SF2 (Samsung 2nm) yields: ~20% late 2025 → ~60% peak Q1'26 → **mid-50s% April 2026**, vs TSMC N2 at a reported 60–70% [all REPORT — Samsung publishes no yield data]. Base rate: Samsung reached GAA *first* (SF3, 2022) and still fell from 8.1% share (4Q24) to 6.5% (1Q26). Yield-maturity lag ≈ 2+ years at the same nominal node [agent-inferred]. | supply-chain press 2026 |
| 3. Implied lag | Revenue ratio ~**11:1** (1Q26). Samsung's own targets concede the decade: foundry chief — annual profit unlikely before **2028**; aspiration ~20% share by 2027 [REPORT] would require roughly tripling external revenue while yields trail and TSMC compounds at $60B+/yr. | TechTimes 2026-06-14 |
| 4. Economics of being #2 | Marquee win: **Tesla AI5/AI6, ~$16.5B through 2033 ≈ ~$2B/yr average [computed]** — dual-sourced against TSMC, non-flagship (first AI5 units to Optimus robots/internal clusters; volume mid-to-2H 2027), won from a distressed-utilization base. First **monthly** profit in ~3 years only in June 2026, driven by internal anchors (Exynos 2600, HBM4 base dies) not external design-win economics. At mid-50s yields vs a rival at 67.7% GM, dual-source wafers are at best thin-margin [agent-inferred]. | Korea Times 2026-07-13; SamMobile 2026-06 |

**Verdict (Samsung):** a durable but structurally sub-scale #2 — self-anchored (Exynos + HBM4 + Tesla), recovering from distress, and useful to customers **as a leverage card**. No arithmetic path to share parity within a decade; a real path to shaving TSMC's pricing power at 2027–29 socket re-opens.

#### 1c. Duration of the process-leadership window

The module's capitulation test (incumbent capex ≫ challenger EBITDA, e.g. ~3×) is over-satisfied to the point of degeneracy: Intel Foundry EBITDA is negative and TSMC's capex is >4× Samsung foundry's *revenue*. Neither challenger can self-fund catch-up; both persist only on parent/state subsidy — so they **won't exit, and won't close**, absent a TSMC stumble. [skill: tech-hardware-and-semiconductors / module: ANCHOR_CUSTOMER_FLYWHEEL_AND_SELF_OUTSOURCING_TELL.md]

**Number-driven verdict:** organic replication of TSMC's leading-edge position is uninvestable for both would-be replicators on a 5-year view. The process-leadership window is defensible **through the A14 generation and its ramp (~2028 production + ~2 years), i.e., ~2030**, at high confidence; see §8b for the ≥80%-confidence statement and its shorteners.

---

### 2. The anchor-customer flywheel and the Nova Lake reversal

[skill: tech-hardware-and-semiconductors / module: ANCHOR_CUSTOMER_FLYWHEEL_AND_SELF_OUTSOURCING_TELL.md]

**The flywheel is running at maximum recorded strength.** A first node is ruinous to fill without an anchor; TSMC's N2 has an anchor *surplus*: Apple reportedly locked **>50% of initial N2 capacity** for A20/M6 [PRESS, 2025], **~15 customers** lined up (10 in HPC — high-performance computing) [TrendForce 2025-09-22], tape-outs **~4× N3 at the same stage** (DigiTimes, 2026-07-16). NVIDIA overtook Apple as largest customer in 2025 (19% vs 17% of revenue; FY2025 20-F filed 2026-04-16; identity per NVIDIA's CEO, 2026-01-22). Per the module's extension, when willing bleeding-edge anchors multiply from one to several, **leverage flips from anchor to supplier** — the pricing windfall. Observed: reported 3–10% advanced-node price increases for 2026 with annual hikes through ~2029 [REPORT 2025-12-29], a ~15% 3nm hike reported for 2H26 [REPORT], GM 67.7% (2Q26 6-K, 2026-07-16), and the through-cycle GM floor raised from "53%+" (2Q25 call, 2025-07-17) to "56%+" (4Q25 call, 2026-01-15). The energy-constraint amplifier is live: power-constrained data centers convert each node's efficiency step (N2: 25–30% lower power vs N3E [COMPANY via press]) into *incremental output*, making willingness-to-pay non-linear — consistent with customers absorbing the hikes [trade press, 2026].

**The tell, and its reversal — read it honestly.** Intel outsourcing its flagship compute tiles to TSMC (Arrow Lake/Lunar Lake on N3; 2025 reports of 60–70% of Nova Lake compute tiles on N2) was the textbook **capitulation tell**: a vertically-integrated challenger routing its *leading-edge flagship* to the incumbent while cutting capex and running foundry losses. On 2026-07-15, TrendForce reported Intel will insource **80–90% of Nova Lake compute tiles to 18A** as yields improve, scaling TSMC's role back to 10–20%. What this does and does not mean:

- **Revenue: immaterial near-term.** Intel-as-customer N3/N2 work is plausibly **~$2–4B/yr** [agent-inferred; Intel is not disclosed in the 20-F customer table], i.e., ~1–2% of FY26E revenue (~$172B at "slightly above 40%" growth on $122.4B [computed from 2026-07-16 guidance]). TSMC is supply-constrained — 3nm "above 100% utilization" [REPORT, 2026-07-16 call coverage], NVIDIA reportedly ~3× short of advanced-node supply [PRESS 2026-03-05] — so vacated N2 slots re-sell into the queue. In a sold-out regime a lost customer costs ~zero current revenue; the cost is future option value.
- **Credibility: one leg of the tell is neutralized.** The module's own falsifier — "the challenger reverses outsourcing and re-funds capex from returning competitiveness" — is *partially* triggered; we must stop citing Intel self-outsourcing as live capitulation evidence. But only one of three confirming legs reversed: Intel Foundry still lost $10.3B (FY25), still has zero committed external 14A customers (Jan 2026), and is still shrinking capex. Insourcing tiles is also what any IDM with empty fabs does for **fixed-cost absorption** — it proves 18A serves Intel's own P&L, not that it wins external sockets [agent-inferred].
- **Intel as customer vs competitor:** the dual role persists at reduced scope (10–20% of tiles is continuation, not wind-down — TrendForce, 2026-07-15). The correct posture: downgrade the flywheel thesis from "challenger capitulating" to "challenger stabilized at boutique scale," and move the burden of proof to the 14A external-anchor clock (commitments promised 2H26–1H27).

---

### 3. Socket book: design-win durability and customer concentration

[skill: tech-hardware-and-semiconductors / module: DESIGN_WIN_SOCKET_AND_CUSTOMER_CONCENTRATION.md]

Concentration per the FY2025 20-F (filed 2026-04-16): **largest customer 19%, second 17%, top-10 ≈ 78%** of net revenue. The module treats concentration as *the* underwriting problem — but for a sold-out near-monopoly foundry the power flow is partially inverted: customers currently cannot leave at acceptable PPA/yield, so the near-term risk is not a socket loss but the *slow reconstruction of customer leverage* via second sources. The socket card:

| Socket | State (mid-2026) | Effective re-open | Notes / forced-vs-voluntary read |
|---|---|---|---|
| Apple SoCs (A20/M6) | Sole-source; >50% of initial N2 [PRESS]; reported to skip A16 straight to A14 [REPORT] — a multi-generation design-in on TSMC's roadmap | Nominally annual; **effectively when a rival hits mobile-class yield+PPA parity — not before ~2028** [agent-inferred] | The N-2 rule (Taiwan keeps newest nodes home, §6b) is not a constraint for Apple, which queues at the Taiwan leading edge |
| NVIDIA GPUs (Blackwell/Rubin) | Sole-source wafers (N4/N3), ~60% of CoWoS allocation [REPORT]; reported **first A16 customer** [REPORT] | Rubin successors ~2027–28 | Constraint is TSMC supply (~3× short [PRESS 2026-03]), not NVIDIA's willingness; deepest packaging entanglement = highest switching cost |
| AMD CPUs/GPUs | Sole-source leading edge; Zen 6 on N2 (tape-out Apr 2025, MP 2026); pays the Arizona premium ("5–20% more... worth it" — AMD CEO) | Each Zen/Instinct generation (~18–24 mo) | Voluntary premium payment for geographic resilience = pricing-power evidence, not weakness |
| Qualcomm | Major N3/N4; **absent from first N2 wave** [PRESS] | Annual (each Snapdragon generation) | The natural swing customer — historically dual-sourced with Samsung; watch it as the first mover if SF2 matures |
| MediaTek | Dimensity 9600 taped out on N2, MP late 2026 [PRESS] | Annual | Sole-source at leading edge |
| Hyperscaler ASICs (TPU, Trainium, MTIA, OpenAI) | All on TSMC N3 (§4) — **except Maia 2 reportedly on Intel 18A-P** [REPORT 2026-01] | Every ASIC generation, ~12–18 mo — **the most contestable shelf** | Hyperscalers deliberately seed second sources to preserve leverage (the module's catch-up vector: pilot allocations to a follower) |
| Tesla AI5/AI6 | **Dual-sourced**: TSMC (Taiwan + Arizona) and Samsung (Taylor + Pyeongtaek) — Musk confirmed 2026-04-15; Samsung AI5 tape-out complete 2026-07-13 | Now open | The template of a re-opened socket: mid-volume, cost-sensitive, non-flagship — exactly where a yield-trailing #2 can compete |
| Intel products | N3 continuing; Nova Lake N2 tiles cut to 10–20% (§2) | At Intel's discretion | Competitor-customer; treat as swing volume |

**What forced (not voluntary) share looks like here.** Two symmetric forms. (1) *Forced-stay:* most of the book is sole-source not because customers chose dependence but because no alternative clears qualification — this is real share, but the sole-source premium is a **window, not a moat** (module §3); its unwind signature is Tesla-like dual-source announcements, pilot lots at SF2/18A, and prepayments to rivals. One strike exists (Maia 2), two near-strikes (Tesla dual-source; Qualcomm's N2 absence). (2) *Forced-leave:* export controls already removed China AI customers from TSMC's book by rule, not by competition (§6b) — that share was lost involuntarily and is unrecoverable under current policy. Neither form yet touches the top-two customers (36% of revenue), whose multi-generation design-ins (A20→A14; Rubin→A16) are the strongest durability class the module recognizes.

---

### 4. The custom-ASIC shift: share-neutral-to-positive, power-balance-negative

[skill: tech-hardware-and-semiconductors / module: CUSTOM_ASIC_DESIGN_WIN_RAMP_AND_COMP_PULL.md]

The module's cost-per-transistor inflection is the driver: past ~20nm-class economics, customers buy performance by specializing silicon. The named-win ledger ([COMPANY/PRESS] unless noted): Google TPU **Ironwood** (deployed Nov 2025, Broadcom co-design, TSMC-fabbed); AWS **Trainium3** (GA Dec 2025, TSMC N3, >1M Trainium chips deployed per AWS); Microsoft **Maia 200** (Jan 2026, TSMC 3nm, ~140B transistors) but **Maia 2 reportedly to Intel 18A-P** [REPORT]; Meta **MTIA** generations through 2027 (TSMC/Broadcom); OpenAI–Broadcom **"Jalapeño"** (TSMC N3, targeted end-2026; gen-2 planned on **TSMC A16**) [REPORT 2026-01-15]; Tesla AI5/AI6 dual TSMC/Samsung.

**Why share-neutral-to-positive for TSMC.** The ASIC wave redistributes the *design* layer (NVIDIA → Broadcom/Marvell/Alchip and in-house hyperscaler teams) while the *manufacturing* bottleneck — leading-edge wafer + CoWoS-class packaging — stays put. The scarce layer captures the rent regardless of which logo wins the accelerator socket; TSMC is the arms dealer to every side of the GPU-vs-ASIC war except Huawei (SMIC) and the one reported Maia 2 defection. [skill: structural-change-threat-map / module: VALUE_CHAIN_ROLE_MIGRATION_AND_BOTTLENECK_RELOCATION.md] It is share-*positive* at the margin because ASIC proliferation multiplies willing bleeding-edge anchors (§2's leverage flip) and pulls forward A16 commitments.

**Wafer economics, quantified [agent-inferred throughout this paragraph].** Reticle-class AI dies (~800mm²) yield ~60–65 candidate die per 300mm wafer, ~40 good die at mature defect densities; hyperscaler ASICs (Maia 200 at ~140B transistors) sit in the same die class on the same N3 wafers (reported $18–27K/wafer [REPORT, conflicting]) — so per-wafer revenue to TSMC is roughly logo-independent. Differences are second-order: reported price-hike tiers (HPC/AI up to +10%, CPU ~+7%, smartphone ~+5% [REPORT 2026]) apply equally to ASIC HPC wafers; flagship GPUs attach larger CoWoS-L interposers and more HBM stacks per package than most first-gen ASICs, so packaging revenue per unit modestly favors the GPU mix. Net: a mix shift toward ASICs is slightly ASP-dilutive per package, volume-additive, and customer-diversifying.

**The real cost is power balance.** ASIC buyers are five of the largest companies on earth running deliberate multi-vendor strategies, with a shared aggregator (Broadcom, ~$73B AI backlog [COMPANY, May 2026]) that can port designs; top-3 customers hold ~85%+ of CoWoS allocation [REPORT]. As ASIC volumes mature, the customer-power clock reasserts: pilot allocations to Intel/Samsung (Maia 2 is exactly this), dual-source clauses, pressure on the 2027–29 price-hike cadence. Share-neutral, margin-mix-manageable, leverage-eroding.

---

### 5. Roadmap cadence and the High-NA divergence

[skill: tech-hardware-and-semiconductors / modules: ROADMAP_CADENCE_AND_PROCESS_WINDOW.md, ARCHITECTURE_IS_ECONOMICS.md]

**Is the cadence stretching? Mildly, and manageably.** The dated record: N2 HVM 4Q25 ("good yield" — 4Q25 call, 2026-01-15); N2P and A16 originally "2H26" (same call), but the April 2026 Technology Symposium moved **A16 volume production to 2027** ("process ready 2026, ramp depends on customers" [COMPANY via press, 2026-04-23]); **A14 2028**; N2U 2028; **A13/A12 2029** (TSMC PR, 2026-04). Full-node spacing has stretched from ~2 years (N7 2018 → N5 2020 → N3 2022) to ~3 (N3 → N2 2025). But the stretch is industry-wide physics, and the module's test is *relative* cadence: Intel's 14A is a 2028-class node with no external commitments yet; Samsung's SF2P+ Taylor phase targets 2027 [PRESS 2026-05-31]. TSMC's window vs both is intact at every dated milestone, and the A16 "slip" is customer-ramp-gated with anchor demand already visible (NVIDIA reported lead A16 customer; OpenAI gen-2 planned on A16).

**The High-NA divergence is an architecture-is-economics judgment, and TSMC's side of it is the economically conservative one.** TSMC keeps low-NA EUV multipatterning **through A14/A13/A12 (2028–29)** [COMPANY via press, 2026-04-23]; Intel adopted High-NA early for 14A. High-NA tools cost ~US$350–400M each with a halved reticle field that forces die-stitching on big AI dies [agent-inferred from public tool specs]. TSMC is choosing known-yield, known-cost multipatterning over unproven tool economics — the same playbook as deferring EUV to N7+ and GAA to N2 while Samsung went GAA-first at 3nm and paid in yield (§1b). The bet is symmetric and datable: **if** Intel demonstrates a High-NA density/cost advantage in shipping 14A silicon (2028–29) that multipatterning cannot match at cost, TSMC's deferral becomes a one-generation cadence break; if High-NA underdelivers on throughput economics, Intel strapped capex to an option that didn't pay.

**What a genuine cadence break would look like** (vs today's benign stretch): (i) A14 slipping past 2028 at the 2027 symposium while Intel 14A holds schedule *with a marquee committed external customer*; (ii) an N2-generation PPA/yield gap to SF2/18A closing to near-zero in third-party product teardowns; (iii) anchor behavior — Apple or NVIDIA splitting a *flagship* across foundries (not Tesla-class work). None is present as of 2026-07-17. The A16 2027 + A14 2028 + no-High-NA-through-A12 roadmap is cadence *management*, not cadence loss.

### 6. Structural-change threat map — dated clocks

[skill: structural-change-threat-map — analog → local translation → clock → incumbent response → falsifier, per SKILL.md; technology claims staged per module: TECHNOLOGY_ADOPTION_CURVE_HUMILITY.md]

| # | Threat | Mechanism / analog | Dated clock | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| a | China mature-node flood + SMIC advanced DUV | State-subsidized capacity floods trailing nodes; DUV multipatterning claws toward 5nm | SMIC 7nm shipping; **5nm pilot now, MP target 2026, yields ~20%** [ANALYST]; advanced capacity ~45K→60K→80K wpm 2025→27 [ANALYST] | **Cannot threaten the leading edge by 2028–30.** 5nm-via-DUV adds ~33% process steps → structurally uneconomic yields serving a captive, policy-protected domestic market (Huawei/Alibaba). TSMC pre-empted the mature-node flood: China = 6% of 2Q26 revenue (6-K 2026-07-16); 6"/8" trimmed, Fab 2/5 reportedly shut by end-2027 [PRESS]. The flood lands on trailing-edge peers' economics, not TSMC's — and 2026 mature capacity actually *tightened* (SMIC util 95.8%, ~10% hikes) [PRESS 2026-03/05] |
| b | Export-control / design-out — cutting both ways | Per [skill: tech-hardware-and-semiconductors / module: EXPORT_CONTROL_REVENUE_CLIFF_AND_DESIGN_OUT.md]: rule-driven revenue is a dated cliff, and the designed-out customer builds a parallel ecosystem | TSMC side: China advanced-AI shipments halted **Nov 2024** (Commerce direction post-910B teardown); Sophgo suspended; unresolved BIS exposure **≥$1B** [REPORTED 2025-04; no settlement per 20-F filed 2026-04]; Nanjing VEU expired **2025-12-31** → annual licenses. Huawei side: TSMC-made die bank **exhausted early 2026** [ANALYST]; Ascend roadmap 950PR Q1'26 → 970 Q4'28 with in-house HBM (2025-09-18) | Both cliffs largely *in* the numbers: the China AI cliff cost low-single-digit revenue % and is absorbed; the reverse design-out creates, by ~2030+, a **policy-walled domestic Chinese ecosystem** that never re-opens to TSMC — a permanent TAM haircut (~mid-single-digit % [agent-inferred]), not a moat breach. Live tails: the BIS fine; the Jan 2026 25% Section 232 tariff (TSMC exempted via the 2.5×/1.5× US-Taiwan quotas, ART signed 2026-02-12) |
| c | Packaging-led disruption (CoWoS challengers) | The bottleneck could relocate from wafer to package; whoever owns the package owns allocation | TSMC >90% of CoWoS-class output [REPORT 2026-07-16]; ~90K wpm → ~120–140K by end-2026; OSATs add ~50–60K wpm *inside* TSMC's flow [REPORT]; challengers: Samsung turnkey HBM4+2.5D for its own wins (2027+), Intel EMIB/Foveros carried by Maia 2 (2027–28), OSAT CoWoS-variants [REPORT 2025-12]; **panel-level (CoPoS) is the discontinuity — TSMC pilot mid-2026, ramp 2028–30** | **No credible challenger to >75% share before ~2028.** Two defenses: the roadmap keeps moving (5.5-reticle now → 14-reticle 2028 → beyond 2029; SoIC A14-on-A14 2029 — TSMC PR 2026-04), and packaging is co-designed with the wafer (the socket module's take-rate/co-design lock). Watch the CoPoS format change 2028–30 — the one place a leapfrog is physically possible [agent-inferred]. Packaging ≠ existential: even a lost package can sit on a TSMC wafer |
| d | Demand-side architecture shifts (edge AI, on-device) | Compute migrates to devices / cheap inference silicon; does the leading edge stay the bottleneck? | On-device AI is ramping **now** into N2: A20/M6 are the anchor (§2); smartphone = 22% of 2Q26 revenue | **Reinforces, not threatens.** Edge AI raises silicon content and node intensity in exactly the sockets TSMC sole-sources; power-constrained inference rewards each node's efficiency step non-linearly (§2). The bear variant that matters is cyclical, not architectural: HPC = 66% of 2Q26 wafer revenue, hyperscaler 2026 capex ~$700–725B (+~77% YoY) [PRESS] — a capex digestion hits utilization, but that is a cycle event for valuation, not a moat event. Radical substrates (in-memory, neuromorphic, photonics) sit at concept/pilot with no financeable-scale evidence; per the humility module they do not yet modify the map |
| e | Japan Rapidus 2nm (2027 pilot) | Sovereign-subsidy greenfield entrant licensing IBM 2nm GAA technology | **[agent-inferred — not in evidence pack; from public reporting through early 2026]**: pilot line ~2025, MP target ~2027, ¥1T+ state funding, no disclosed anchor customer, no HVM record | **Noise for TSMC through 2030.** Run the §1 ladder: revenue base ~zero → implied lag unbounded; no anchor customer → first-node fill problem unsolved (§2's core theorem); no PDK/EDA/IP ecosystem maturity. Real as Japanese sovereign-supply option and specialty capacity; monitor only for an anchor-customer announcement, which would be the single fact that changes the classification |

### 7. The compounding record: what zero dilution across a $100B+ buildout proves

[skill: historical-compounding-quality]

From the multi-year table (TSMC 4Q management reports 2019–2025; SEC XBRL companyfacts, pulled 2026-07-17):

| | 2019 | 2025 | CAGR (6y) |
|---|---|---|---|
| Revenue (NT$B) | 1,069.99 | 3,809.05 | **+23.6%** |
| Revenue (US$B) | 34.63 | 122.42 | **+23.4%** |
| Diluted EPS (NT$) | 13.32 | 66.25 | **+30.7%** |
| Gross margin | 46.0% | 59.9% | +13.9pp |
| Equity (NT$B) | 1,614 | 6,474 (2Q26) | ~+23%/yr |
| Dividends paid (NT$B) | 259.3 | 466.78 | +10.3% |
| **Shares outstanding** | **~25.93B** | **25,932,524,521 (20-F, 2026-02-28)** | **0.0%** |

Share count is **flat at 25.9B across the entire period** — zero net dilution, zero buybacks — through US$167B of 2021–25 capex plus the 2019–20 spend, all internally funded, ending at **net cash ≈ NT$2,654B (~US$83B)** [computed: cash NT$3,518B − LT debt NT$864B; 2Q26 presentation, 2026-07-16]. Dividend-adjusted book value per share compounds ≈ **29%/yr** over 2019–1H26 [computed: equity/share NT$62.3 → NT$249.7, plus ~NT$74.5/share cumulative dividends FY20–FY25], with one down revenue year (2023, −4.5% NT$) and a recent/full slope ratio well above 1.0. ROE: 35.4% FY25 (4Q25 call, 2026-01-15); 45.9% annualized 2Q26 (presentation, 2026-07-16).

**Grade:** qualitatively a **candidate_historical_compounder** — high CAGR, high smoothness, no recent deterioration, and none of the module's flattering artifacts (no buyback share-shrink, no leverage, no serial M&A; the 2Q26 print does carry a one-off NT$63.2B VIS disposal gain inside net income — operating income +65.4% YoY is the cleaner line; 2Q26 presentation, 2026-07-16). Formal caveat: the deterministic quality card (hfdt `intrinsic-company-quality`) was not run in this session, so the card itself is **not_rateable** on process grounds; the qualitative classification stands on the primary-sourced table above.

**What the record does and does not prove.** It *proves* the reinvestment moat: every dollar of the buildout came from retained earnings reinvested at the moat edge, per-share — the signature §1's ladder predicts for the scale leader. It does *not* prove the next decade: current margins sit ~11–13pp above the 2020–24 through-cycle band [computed; note: the forward reversion anchor is the normalization section's 57–60% mid-cycle band — i.e., ~8–11pp of excess vs mid-cycle, not 11–13pp]; management itself guides overseas-fab dilution 2–3pp widening to 3–4pp (4Q25/1Q26/2Q26 calls), N2 dilution 2–3pp FY26, depreciation +high-teens% 2026, and capex intensity re-accelerating (1H26 = 35.2% of revenue [computed]). The record earns the *durability* prior; it must not be used to capitalize the *peak margin*. [skill: tech-hardware-and-semiconductors / module: SUBTYPE_ROUTING_AND_VALUATION.md]
