### 8. Synthesis

#### 8a. Key judgments

| # | Judgment | Confidence | Falsifier |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Organic replication of TSMC's leading edge by Intel or Samsung is uninvestable within 5 years (ladder ratios: ~400:1 / ~11:1 external revenue; challenger EBITDA negative-to-break-even vs $60–64B incumbent capex) | ~90% | Intel 14A lands a committed marquee external customer AND re-expands foundry capex; or Samsung sustains SF2 yields ≥70% with a flagship (non-Tesla-class) socket win |
| 2 | Process+packaging leadership window holds through the A14 generation ramp (~2030) | ~80–85% | A14 slips past 2028 while Intel 14A holds 2028 with committed external anchor; N2-class PPA/yield gap closes in shipping silicon |
| 3 | The Nova Lake insourcing reversal is margin erosion, not a flywheel break (~1–2% revenue at stake, backfilled in a sold-out regime) | ~85% | Intel Foundry external revenue exceeds ~$5B/yr by 2028 [agent-inferred threshold], i.e., real external-socket wins beyond internal fill |
| 4 | The hyperscaler ASIC shift is share-neutral-to-positive for TSMC through 2028 (all major ASICs TSMC-fabbed except reported Maia 2), while gradually eroding customer-power balance | ~85% | ≥2 of {TPU, Trainium, MTIA, OpenAI} place a *production* generation outside TSMC by end-2028 |
| 5 | Roadmap cadence is stretching with physics, not breaking; the no-High-NA-through-A12 call is economically conservative, not complacent | ~75% | Intel demonstrates a High-NA-enabled density/cost advantage in shipping 14A silicon that low-NA multipatterning cannot match at cost, 2028–29 |
| 6 | SMIC/China cannot threaten the leading edge externally by 2030 (DUV 5nm yield wall; captive market); China exposure is an absorbed cliff, not an open wound | ~90% | Domestic EUV-class lithography at production scale (currently concept-stage), or yields on DUV 5nm reported >50% at scale |
| 7 | CoWoS-class packaging leadership (>75% share) holds through 2028; CoPoS transition 2028–30 is the first genuine contest point | ~80% | A top-3 CoWoS customer moves a flagship package to Samsung/Intel/OSAT turnkey by 2027 |
| 8 | Rapidus is noise for TSMC through 2030 | ~85% | A named anchor customer with committed volume at Rapidus [agent-inferred; thin evidence base — not in pack] |
| 9 | The 2019–25 compounding record (EPS +30.7%/yr, shares flat, zero dilution, self-funded) proves the reinvestment moat but not the forward margin level | ~90% on the record; margin normalization is a valuation matter | Restated financials or evidence the record depended on non-recurring gains (checked: VIS gain flagged, one-off) |

#### 8b. Moat-duration verdict

**At ≥80% confidence, TSMC's combined process-plus-packaging leadership is defensible for ~4–5 years from today — through the A16 (2027) and A14 (2028) generations and their ramps, i.e., into 2030.** Basis: no challenger clears any rung of the §1 ladder; the anchor book has multiplied rather than thinned (~15 N2 customers, 4× tape-outs, two multi-generation flagship design-ins); and both dated challenger clocks (Intel 14A commitments 2H26–1H27; Samsung annual profit "realistic target 2028") sit *inside* the window. Beyond 2030 confidence decays toward coin-flip-plus as three uncertainties compound: the High-NA bet resolves (~2028–29 evidence), state capital keeps both challengers alive indefinitely, and hyperscaler second-sourcing incentives strengthen with ASIC maturity.

**Specific evidence that would shorten the window** (any one subtracts ~1–2 years): a committed marquee 14A external customer by 1H27; sustained SF2 yields ≥70% [REPORT-grade acceptable if triangulated] plus a flagship socket award by end-2027; A14 slipping past 2028; or a top-two TSMC customer publicly splitting a flagship product line across foundries.

#### 8c. Kill signals (dated thresholds)

1. **By 1H27:** Intel announces ≥1 committed (not test-chip) external 14A customer from TSMC's top-10 book → downgrade Judgment 1; two such customers → cut the moat window by ~2 years.
2. **By end-2027:** Samsung SF2 yields sustained ≥70% *and* a flagship mobile/datacenter socket (Apple, NVIDIA, Qualcomm flagship-tier) awarded → re-open every 2028+ socket assumption.
3. **By 2Q27:** 2nm falls short of ~10% of TSMC wafer revenue [agent-inferred threshold vs the 4×-tape-out signal; N3 reached 24% of FY25 revenue in year 3] → the tape-out-to-revenue conversion is failing; re-underwrite N2 demand quality.
4. **April 2027 symposium:** A14 slips beyond 2028, or A16 slips again past 2027 → cadence break per §5; simultaneously check Intel 14A schedule for the relative read.
5. **By end-2027:** TSMC CoWoS-class share reported below ~75% (from >90%), or a top-3 CoWoS customer shifts a flagship package to a rival turnkey → packaging leg of the moat eroding ahead of schedule.
6. **By end-2028:** ≥2 hyperscaler production ASIC generations fabbed outside TSMC (Maia 2 on 18A-P is strike one) → ASIC shift turning share-negative.
7. **Pricing tell, continuous:** failure to implement the reported 2027 advanced-node price increase, or GM guided below the "56% and higher" through-cycle floor (set 2026-01-15) absent an FX/dilution explanation → anchor leverage flipping back to customers.
8. **Policy tail, continuous:** BIS settlement materially above the reported ~$1B scale, denial of the Nanjing annual license, or Section 232 semiconductors expansion that voids the 2.5×/1.5× Taiwan quota exemption (ART, signed 2026-02-12) → re-price the export-control cliff.

#### 8d. Handoffs

- **To valuation (specialist-ledger):** carry (i) a **moat fade schedule** starting ~2030, not a perpetuity; (ii) through-cycle GM normalization anchored at management's 56%+ floor, *not* the 2Q26 67.7% print (sold-out utilization + favorable FX + pre-dilution mix; dilution stack ahead: overseas 2–3pp→3–4pp, N2 2–3pp, depreciation +high-teens% 2026, all [COMPANY]); (iii) capex intensity ~35%+ of revenue and rising (1H26 [computed]); (iv) the pricing-power option (3–10%/yr hikes through 2029 [REPORT]) as upside, not base case. Per SUBTYPE_ROUTING_AND_VALUATION.md, never capitalize peak-cycle margins at a peak multiple: TTM P/E ~33× already sits above the 10-year year-end band (11.2–28.8×, retrieved 2026-07-16).
- **To red-team (thesis-legs):** attack four legs specifically. (1) The **flywheel leg**: the strongest historical tell (Intel self-outsourcing) partially reversed in July 2026 — argue the Nova-Lake-plus-Maia-2-plus-Tesla triangle as the start of systematic second-sourcing rather than three idiosyncratic events. (2) The **demand leg**: HPC 66% of wafer revenue makes the moat P&L hostage to one spending cycle; a 2027 AI-capex digestion tests utilization exactly when the $60–64B capex cohort hits depreciation. (3) The **evidence-quality leg**: all 2026-07-16 call figures (capex raise, >40% growth, 3–4pp 2H dilution) rest on same-day secondary coverage — re-verify against the official transcript when posted (a flagged evidence-pack gap: 2H26 N2 dilution reported as 3–4pp vs 2–3pp full-year on prior calls). (4) The **jurisdiction leg**: Taiwan-strait risk is moat-irrelevant but value-dominant; it belongs to the geopolitical section, and no moat conclusion here hedges it.

*Compliance boundary: analytical research over public filings and cited public/press sources; press and market-research figures are labeled and are not certified data; no investment advice.*


## Section II.3 — Normalized Earning Power: Separating Franchise Margin from Allocation Rent

**TSMC (NYSE: TSM, 1 ADS = 5 common shares; TWSE: 2330). Section date: 2026-07-17. Reference price: $409.74 ADS close 2026-07-16 (Q2'26 print day).**

Citation convention: evidence-pack files are cited as [E01]…[E06] with the S-keys/dates carried from those files (E01 = results/financials, E02 = technology/capacity, E03 = competition/customers/AI, E04 = geopolitics/policy, E05 = market/consensus, E06 = hfdt/SEC primary; plus the primary transcripts/management reports in the pack, cited by document and date). Anything not traceable to the pack is marked **[agent-inferred]**. Acronyms: GM = gross margin, OM = operating margin, D&A = depreciation & amortization, FCF = free cash flow (TSMC definition: operating cash flow − capex), OCF = operating cash flow, NOPAT = net operating profit after tax, ROIC = return on invested capital, HPC = high-performance computing, CoWoS = Chip-on-Wafer-on-Substrate advanced packaging, SoIC = System-on-Integrated-Chips, wpm = wafers per month, kpcs = thousand 12"-equivalent wafers, N2/N3/N5/N7 = TSMC 2/3/5/7nm nodes, A16/A14 = TSMC 1.6/1.4nm-class nodes, GAA = gate-all-around, OSAT = outsourced assembly & test, LTA = long-term agreement, ASP = average selling price, ASIC = application-specific integrated circuit, TWD/NT$ = New Taiwan dollar, VIS = Vanguard International Semiconductor, QoE = quality of earnings, IC = invested capital.

---

### 1. Decomposing the 67.7%: what kind of margin is this?

Q2'26 GM printed **67.7%** (guide 65.5–67.5%; 1Q26 66.2%; 2Q25 58.6% → +9.1pp YoY) [E01 §1, S1/S2, 2026-07-16; E06]. Against the 2019–2024 annual band of **46.0% → 59.6%** (2019: 46.0%, 2020: 53.1%, 2021: 51.6%, 2022 peak: 59.6%, 2023 trough: 54.4%, 2024: 56.1%) [E01 §7; E06 XBRL series], the current print sits **8–22pp above every full-year outcome in the 2019–2024 window (and ~7.8pp above FY2025's 59.9%)**. Stage 1 of the normalization workflow demands the distortion be named before the bridge is built [skill: capacity-cycle-and-normalized-earnings / SKILL.md Stage 1]; here **multiple distortion families fire simultaneously** — allocation rent, output-price spike, utilization above nameplate, FX, and a depreciation-timing artifact — which triggers the stacked-windfall diagnostic [skill: capacity-cycle-and-normalized-earnings / module: OVER_EARNING_STACK_DIAGNOSTIC.md].

One immediate QoE flag before the margin work: 2Q26 **net income** (+77.4% YoY) contains **NT$63.20B of disposal/mark-to-market gains on VIS shares** (8.1% stake sold 2026-05-15) inside NT$95.83B of non-operating items (vs NT$28.83B in 1Q26) [E01 §1, S2; E06]. Operating income (+65.4% YoY) is the cleaner run-rate line; ex-VIS EPS is roughly NT$24.8 ≈ **US$3.93/ADS** vs the reported US$4.31 **[agent-inferred: 706.56 − 63.20 ≈ NT$643.4B ÷ 25,932M shares × 5 ÷ 31.60]**.

#### 1.1 The depreciation-holiday layer (the least discussed, largest single artifact)

The pack's management-report cash-flow data reveals the quiet driver of the 2025–26 margin explosion: **D&A nearly stopped growing while revenue grew >30%**.

| Period | D&A (NT$B) | Revenue (NT$B) | D&A/revenue |
|---|---|---|---|
| FY2024 | 662.80 | 2,894.31 | **22.9%** |
| FY2025 | 688.10 (+3.8% YoY) | 3,809.05 (+31.6%) | **18.1%** |
| 1H26 | 363.99 (1Q26 165.45 + 2Q26 198.54) | 2,404.48 | **15.1%** |
| 2Q26 alone | 198.54 (+20.0% QoQ, +5.6% YoY vs 188.07) | 1,270.38 | 15.6% |

Sources: 4Q25 Management Report (2026-01-15) D&A lines FY25 688.10 vs FY24 662.80; 2Q26 Management Report (2026-07-16) quarterly D&A 198.54/165.45/188.07; revenue [E01 §3, §7]. Ratios computed.

Why: the 2023–24 capex trough (US$30.4B in 2023, **US$29.8B** in 2024, down from US$36.3B in 2022 [E01 §7; E02 §4]) met TSMC's **5-year straight-line equipment depreciation** policy (2025 20-F, filed 2026-04-16 [E02 §5]), so 2020–21-vintage tools rolled off depreciation just as AI revenue inflected. Quarterly D&A actually *declined* through 2025 (4Q24 170.38 → 3Q25 162.79 → 4Q25 162.11, per 4Q25 Management Report) while revenue rose ~32%. With fab depreciation overwhelmingly in cost of goods sold **[agent-inferred, standard foundry cost structure]**, the fall in D&A intensity from 22.9% (FY24) to ~15.5% (2Q26) contributed roughly **+6.5pp of GM versus FY24 cost structure, and ~+4pp of the +9.1pp YoY move** (2Q25 D&A intensity was 188.07/933.79 = 20.1%) **[agent-inferred arithmetic]**. This is the foundry capex-cycle mechanic in its purest form: depreciation lags economics in both directions, flattering the fill phase and punishing the build phase [skill: tech-hardware-and-semiconductors / module: IP_STRATEGIC_FLOOR_AND_CAPEX_CYCLE.md §capex-cycle]. The 2Q26 sequential D&A jump of +20% is the wave turning: N2 fabs entered service (HVM 4Q25 at Hsinchu/Kaohsiung [E02 §1]) and management guides FY26 depreciation **+high-teens% YoY** (CFO, 4Q25 call, 2026-01-15 [E02 §5]).

#### 1.2 The four-layer decomposition of 67.7% [agent-inferred throughout; inputs cited]

**(a) Structural/franchise layer — ~57–60% mid-cycle.** The through-cycle franchise has genuinely stepped up versus the 2019–24 band, for reasons that survive a normal-supply regime:
- Node mix: advanced (≤7nm) wafer revenue 69% (FY24) → 74% (FY25) → **77%** (2Q26: 2nm 3%, 3nm 30%, 5nm 33%, 7nm 11%) [E02 §6; E01 §1]. Platform mix: HPC 58% of FY25 revenue → **66%** of 2Q26 [E01 §1, §3].
- Effective monopoly at the leading edge: **72.3% foundry share 1Q26** (TrendForce, 2026-06-12), up ~11pp in nine quarters, vs Samsung 6.5%, SMIC 5.1% [E03 §1]; N2 tape-outs running **~4x N3 at the same stage** (DigiTimes, 2026-07-16 [E02 §1]).
- Peer margin spread is the moat evidence: UMC GM 29.2%, GlobalFoundries 27.6% (both Q1'26), Intel Foundry −$10.3B operating loss FY25, Samsung foundry ~KRW 6T loss FY25 [E05 §5]. Applying the industry-wide-windfall test *in reverse*: peers did **not** get the margin lift, so this is not a shared cyclical windfall — the lift is idiosyncratic, which makes a large part of it durable [skill: mean-reversion-and-forensic-risk / module: INDUSTRY_WIDE_MARGIN_LIFT_IS_A_WINDFALL_NOT_A_MOAT.md, falsifier branch].
- The scarcity-vs-normal-regime test also passes: in the last normal/down regime (2023: shipments −21%, revenue −8.7% US$), GM held **54.4%** — top of industry by a mile — and pricing decayed slowly rather than reverting to marginal cost [E01 §7] [skill: capacity-cycle-and-normalized-earnings / module: SCARCITY_REGIME_VS_NORMAL_REGIME_MOAT_TEST.md, 5+ rows pass → earned moat].

**(b) Allocation-rent layer — ~4–6pp of the current print, of which ~2.5–4pp should revert.** The rent evidence: 2026 price hikes of **3–10% across all advanced nodes** (~74% of wafer revenue; smartphone ~+5%, CPU ~+7%, HPC/AI up to +10%), with annual hikes reportedly planned through ~2029, plus a reported **~15% 3nm hike in 2H26** [E03 §7, TrendForce 2025-12-29 and Tom's Hardware 2026, cryptobriefing 2026 — all supply-chain reports, not company-confirmed]; 3nm lines running **"above 100% utilization"** (Q2'26 call coverage, 2026-07-16 [E02 §1]); CoWoS **fully booked** (TrendForce 2025-12-08), TSMC >90% of global CoWoS output, NVIDIA holding ~60% of allocation [E02 §2; E03 §6]; CoWoS wafer ASP "nears 7nm levels" (TrendForce 2026-04-28 [E02 §2]); NVIDIA-attributed reporting that TSMC advanced-node supply is **~3x short of AI demand** (2026-03-05 [E03 §6]). Arithmetic: a ~5% weighted ASP uplift on ~75% of revenue drops essentially 100% to gross profit (costs unchanged) ≈ **+3.8pp GM**; >100% utilization over-absorbs fixed cost by ~+1–1.5pp; CoWoS/packaging scarcity premium ~+0.5–1pp **[agent-inferred]**. Under the rent-vs-quality split [skill: allocation-rent-and-shortage-normalization / SKILL.md Stage 4], part of the hike layer is a monopolist finally re-pricing to value ("Our pricing will remain strategic, not opportunistic, to earn our value" — CFO, 4Q25 call [E02 §1]) and survives normalization; the shortage-conditional partial (the HPC up-to-10% tier, the 2H26 3nm surcharge, the >100% utilization over-absorption) does not. Split retained ~40–50% / reverting ~50–60% **[agent-inferred]**.

**(c) FX layer — ~+0.7pp YoY, ~+2.3pp vs the 3Q25 trough.** Disclosed sensitivity: **1% TWD depreciation ≈ +40bps GM** (CFO, 2Q25 call, 2025-07-17 [E01 §6, S10]). Path: 2Q25 average 31.05 → 3Q25 29.91 → 2Q26 **31.60** [E01 §6]. YoY: (31.60−31.05)/31.05 = **+1.77%** TWD depreciation → **+0.71pp** GM. Versus the 3Q25 trough: +5.65% → **+2.26pp**. TSMC itself quantified the mirror image in 2025: ~4.4% appreciation cut 2Q25 GM ~180bps [E01 §6, S10]. FX is a pure windfall layer — management: "we have no control over the foreign exchange rate" (2Q26 call [E01 §1, S17]) — and at the 3Q26 guidance rate of 32.0 it adds a further ~+0.5pp before any reversal [skill: finite-windfall-and-capacity-response / module: BORROWED_ECONOMICS_ATTRIBUTION.md — exposure, not ownership].

**(d) Known headwinds deferred/suppressed in the print:**
- **Overseas-fab dilution**: guided **2–3pp in early stages, widening to 3–4pp in latter stages** of the ramp (reiterated 4Q25, 1Q26, 2Q26 calls [E01 §1, S17/S18/S7; E02 §5]). Early-stage drag is already partly inside the print (~1–2pp **[agent-inferred]**); the *incremental* 1.5–2.5pp arrives as Arizona P2 (N3, HVM 2H27), P3 (2nm/A16, pulled to ~2027), Kumamoto-2, and Dresden ramp [E02 §3]. The structural cost premium is real: Arizona wafers reportedly priced 25–30% above Taiwan; AMD's CEO put the cost premium at 5–20%; TechInsights estimates true cost only ~+10% [E03 §7] — priced pass-through offsets part but not all.
- **N2 ramp dilution**: **2–3pp on FY26 GM** (CFO, 4Q25/1Q26 calls), with same-day 2Q26 coverage citing **3–4pp in 2H26** [E01 §1 (discrepancy flagged); E02 §1]. N2 "capex per 1k wpm is substantially higher than N3; A14 will be even higher" (CFO, 4Q25 call [E02 §1]) — each node now starts deeper in the margin hole.
- **Depreciation wave** (see §3): FY26 +high-teens%, and the FY26 US$60–64B budget (raised twice from $52–56B: "high end" 2026-04-16, $60–64B 2026-07-16 [E02 §4]) lands in the 2027–28 P&L on 5-year straight-line.

#### 1.3 Bridge check and the honest mid-cycle number

YoY bridge, 2Q25 58.6% → 2Q26 67.7% (+9.1pp) **[agent-inferred allocation, inputs cited above]**: depreciation intensity ~+4.1pp; pricing/rent ~+3–4pp; FX +0.7pp; utilization/mix/cost improvement ~+1.5pp; less early N2 + incremental overseas dilution ~−1pp. It sums; nothing about the print is unexplained — but roughly **6–8pp of the 67.7% is timing (depreciation holiday + FX) and reverting rent** rather than franchise.

Normalized construction (mid-cycle, ~2028 structure) **[agent-inferred]**:

```
2Q26 print                                     67.7%
− FX above ~31.1 long-run average              −0.6
− depreciation normalization (capex/rev ~35%
  regime replacing the 2023–24 trough vintage) −3.0 to −4.0
− reverting allocation rent                    −2.5 to −4.0
− incremental overseas dilution (to latter-
  stage 3–4pp total)                           −1.0 to −1.5
− N2/A16 steady-state ramp drag (net of the
  N3-style crossover to corporate average)     −0.5 to −1.0
= mid-cycle normalized GM                      ~57–60%  (point: 58%)
```

**Is management's "56% and higher through the cycle" floor sandbagged?** The floor was raised from **"53% and higher"** (2Q25 call, 2025-07-17) to **"56% and higher through the cycle"** with ROE "high-twenties percent" (4Q25 call, 2026-01-15) [E01 §6] — a 3pp upgrade in six months, immediately followed by two capex raises and a quarter printing 11.7pp above the floor. As a *mid-cycle* claim, 56% is clearly sandbagged: FY24 — a year with recovering-but-not-tight conditions, unfavorable FX and overseas drag — already delivered 56.1%, before the 2026 price hikes and with worse mix [E01 §7]. As a *trough* claim it is **not** conservative: 2023's downturn produced 54.4% under a lighter depreciation base, and the next downturn will carry the 2025–27 capex vintage. Our verdict: **mid-cycle 57–60% (point 58%), management floor 56% credible for a mild digestion, and a hard-downturn trough of 52–55%** — i.e., the floor breaks in a 2023-magnitude utilization shock **[agent-inferred]** [skill: mean-reversion-and-forensic-risk / module: PEAK_FACT_REVERSION_BURDEN.md — reversion needs actors and mechanisms, which §2 dates].

---

### 2. Capacity-response clocks: when does the rent normalize?

Allocation rent dies by supply response, competitor response, customer behavior change, or rule change; each clock must be dated [skill: allocation-rent-and-shortage-normalization / asset: SHORTAGE_RESPONSE_CLOCK.md; skill: finite-windfall-and-capacity-response / asset: CAPACITY_RESPONSE_CLOCK.md].

**Clock 1 — TSMC's own supply response (the dominant clock).** Capex US$29.8B (2024) → **US$40.9B** (2025) → **US$60–64B** (2026, raised twice) [E02 §4]; "in the last three years our capex totaled US$101B, but is expected to be significantly higher in the next three years" (CFO, 4Q25 call [E02 §4]). CoWoS: ~35k wpm (2024) → ~70–75k (end-2025) → ~90k (mid-2026) → **120–140k wpm (end-2026)**, plus OSAT partners ~50–60k (industry ~200k); TrendForce sees the CoWoS supply-demand gap narrowing **from ~20% to ~10% by end-2026** [E02 §2, analyst estimates]. N3 capacity +40% YoY toward ~180k wpm in 2026 (TrendForce, 2026-04-27); N2 ramping across ~five fab phases in year one toward 90–140k wpm end-2026 (analyst bands) [E02 §1]. AP7 Chiayi P1 producing June 2026; three more Chiayi AP fabs broke ground 2026-07-12/13; AP8 Tainan toward 40k+ wpm end-2026 [E02 §2]. **Date: packaging scarcity premium substantially closed during 2027; leading-edge wafer tightness relieved 2027–28** unless demand keeps compounding at management's mid-to-high-50s% AI-accelerator CAGR [E02 §6]. Important discipline check: TSMC still under-builds relative to claimed demand (supply ~3x short per the NVIDIA-attributed report; CEO verifies demand from "customers' customers" to keep chips out of inventory [E01 §1, S17]) — a real hedge for the bull case, but only as long as the capex data keep showing under-building rather than racing; a $60–64B budget raised twice in six months is the *beginning* of racing [skill: tech-hardware-and-semiconductors / module: CONSERVATIVE_CAPACITY_DISCIPLINE_AS_A_HEDGE_FOR_YOUR_OWN_BULL_CASE.md; skill: capacity-cycle-and-normalized-earnings / module: CONSOLIDATED_COMMODITY_TECH_CAPACITY_DISCIPLINE.md — classify capex into migration vs true new capacity before crediting "discipline"].

**Clock 2 — Competitor response (slow; nibbles from 2027, no parity before ~2028).** Samsung: SF2 Taylor entered production phase for Tesla AI5 (tape-out completed ~2026-07-13); yields reported ~mid-50s–60% vs TSMC N2 60–70%; AI5 volume realistically mid-to-2H 2027; foundry returned to (quarterly) profitability 1Q26, first monthly profit June 2026, chief targets annual profit 2028 [E03 §3; E05 §5]. Intel: 18A shipping in Panther Lake (CES, 2026-01-05); reported Microsoft Maia 2 win on 18A/18A-P (Jan 2026, "claims report"); Nova Lake compute tiles being pulled back in-house **80–90% to 18A** (2026-07-15) — which cuts Intel's TSMC N2 order, a customer-response datum as much as a competitor one; 14A has **zero committed external customers**, commitments expected 2H26–1H27, and Intel's 10-K retains the may-pause-14A warning [E03 §2]. SMIC: 5nm DUV pilot targeting 2026 mass production at ~20% reported yields, ~45k→80k advanced wspm 2025→2027, export-constrained and non-competitive at the leading edge outside China [E03 §4]. Rapidus: **no evidence in pack** — flag for follow-up, treat as ≤0 impact inside the underwriting window **[agent-inferred]**. **Date: earliest structural pricing pressure at N2-class ~2028**, and only if Samsung yields hold >60–70% and/or Intel lands a 14A anchor.

**Clock 3 — Customer behavioral response (already visibly ticking).** Multi-sourcing: Tesla dual-sources AI5/AI6 across TSMC and Samsung (Musk, 2026-04-15); Maia 2 reportedly to Intel 18A-P; Nova Lake tiles insourced [E03 §2, §3, §5]. ASIC substitution: every major hyperscaler ASIC (TPU Ironwood, Trainium3, Maia 200, MTIA, OpenAI/Broadcom XPU on N3 → A16 2nd gen) is **still TSMC-fabbed** [E03 §5] — ASIC share shift is an NVIDIA problem, not a TSMC volume problem; it is, however, a *mix/pricing* problem in the 2030s if hyperscalers gain credible second sources. Over-ordering risk: allocation rewards inflated demand [skill: allocation-rent-and-shortage-normalization / SKILL.md Use-when]; mitigants in evidence — CEO's customers'-customers verification and data-center-progress monitoring [E01 §1, S17]; aggravators — CoWoS "fully booked," NVIDIA booking >half of 2026–27 CoWoS expansion, consumer pre-building against tariff risk cited by TrendForce as a 1Q26 demand driver [E02 §2; E03 §1]. **Date: behavioral unwind is event-driven — it arrives with the first hyperscaler capex plateau, not on its own schedule.**
**Clock 4 — Price-rule/policy response.** Implemented: Section 232 **25% tariff** on advanced computing chips (effective 2026-01-15) with data-center/consumer end-use exemptions; US–Taiwan agreement (signed 2026-02-12) gives Taiwanese producers building US capacity **duty-free import quotas of 2.5x planned US capacity during construction / 1.5x after completion** and caps the reciprocal tariff at 15% [E04 §1]. TSMC is the compliant expander (US$265B announced US total after the +$100B on 2026-07-16; no equity-stake demand on record, unlike Intel's converted grants) [E04 §3]. Net: policy currently *protects* TSMC's rent (tariff wall raises rivals' costs of not-building-in-US; TSMC exempt via investment) but taxes its structure via the overseas-dilution layer already counted in §1.2(d). The July 1, 2026 Commerce data-center report and the open threat of broader semiconductor/SME tariffs are the live re-pricing dates [E04 §1].

**Glide path and the windfall NPV wedge [agent-inferred].** Modeling the reverting rent (~2.5–4pp of GM) as: 100% present in 2026, ~75% in 2027 (CoWoS gap halves; hikes still stick), ~45% in 2028 (packaging parity, first competitor volume), ~20% in 2029, ~0 in 2030. On FY26E revenue of ~US$172B ("slightly above 40%" growth on US$122.42B [E01 §2/§3; E05 §3]), 3.25pp midpoint of GM ≈ **US$5.6B/yr pretax at peak → ~US$4.6B after tax** (18.1% 2Q26 effective rate [E01 §1]); with revenue growing along the guide, the decaying stream discounted at 10% ≈ **NPV ~US$13–20B, i.e. ~US$2.5–4.0 per ADS, well under 1% of the ADR-implied US$2.125T market cap** [E06 ADS math]. Using the wider 4–6pp gross-rent band as the reverting layer bounds it at ~US$25–35B (~1.2–1.6% of market cap). The windfall must be valued as this NPV wedge — **never as a terminal multiple on 67.7% economics** [skill: finite-windfall-and-capacity-response / module: VALUE_A_WINDFALL_AS_PAYBACK_NOT_A_MULTIPLE.md; module: EARNINGS_DURATION_AND_VALUATION_TREATMENT.md]. The investment-relevant conclusion is asymmetric: the rent is small as NPV but large as *optics* — it inflates the "E" that the market's 33x trailing multiple [E05 §4] is capitalizing. The valuation risk is not losing the wedge; it is the multiple being applied to the wedge.

---

### 3. Capex/depreciation forensics: is record margin + surging capex masking anything?

#### 3.1 The 5-year book life vs tool economics

Equipment depreciates straight-line over **5 years** (buildings 10–20) [E02 §5, 20-F]. Tool economics run far longer: N5 (ramped 2020) and N7 (ramped 2018–19) are largely or fully depreciated **[agent-inferred from the 5-yr policy + node ramp dates]**, yet still produced **44% of 2Q26 wafer revenue** (5nm 33% + 7nm 11% [E01 §1]) at 2026 prices that were just hiked 3–10% [E03 §7]. TSMC explicitly re-tasks depreciated fleets: "convert N5 capacity to support N3… cross-node capacity optimization among N7, N5, and N3" (CFO, 4Q25 call [E02 §1]). So book D&A *overstates* the economic consumption of mature-node assets (they out-earn their book lives) while *understating* the forward cost of staying at the leading edge (each node's capex/k rises: N2 ≫ N3, A14 > N2 [E02 §1]) — both halves of the D&A-vs-maintenance trap [skill: asset-intensity-and-maintenance-capex-trap / module: D_AND_A_OVERSTATES_MAINT_CAPEX_BUT_VERIFY_THE_REFRESH_RESERVE.md]. Consequence: current-node margins are structurally flattered ~2–3 years after each capex wave and penalized during it; 2025–1H26 sat at the maximum-flattery point of that cycle (§1.1).

#### 3.2 What *is* maintenance capex for a leading-edge foundry?

The naive answer (maintenance ≈ D&A, growth = the rest) fails the horizon-match test: TSMC's valued cash flow is *leading-edge* earning power, and the cost of staying in place over that horizon is **the full node-migration treadmill**, not tool repair [skill: asset-intensity-and-maintenance-capex-trap / module: MAINTENANCE_CAPEX_HORIZON_MATCH.md]. Forcing the FY26 US$62B midpoint through the capex taxonomy [skill: asset-intensity-and-maintenance-capex-trap / module: CAPEX_TAXONOMY.md]:

- **Keep-competitive (economic maintenance): ~US$28–32B [agent-inferred].** The revenue-flat counterfactual: hold leading-edge relevance (R&D fabs, N2/A16 migration of the *existing* revenue base, mask/test refresh) plus mature-fab upkeep. Anchor: 2023–24, when TSMC held/extended technology leadership while shipping 12–15M wafers/yr, ran US$29.8–30.4B [E01 §7]. Allocation guide: 70–80% of budget to "advanced process technologies" [E02 §4], much of which is migration of existing demand rather than incremental units [skill: capacity-cycle-and-normalized-earnings / module: CONSOLIDATED_COMMODITY_TECH_CAPACITY_DISCIPLINE.md — migration capex adds effective supply only if output/wafer-value rises].
- **True growth (new capacity against contracted AI demand): ~US$25–30B** — N2 five-phase ramp, CoWoS/SoIC doubling, gigafab cluster expansions. Demand-commitment quality is high for capacity businesses: engagement lead time "at least two to three years in advance," disciplined top-down/bottom-up capacity planning (CFO, 4Q25 call, 2026-01-15), CoWoS pre-booked by named customers [E02 §2].
- **Geopolitically mandated duplication: ~US$5–8B/yr and rising [agent-inferred]** — the Arizona/Kumamoto/Dresden overseas premium. This bucket is strategically *maintenance-like* (it preserves the customer relationship and the tariff exemption [E04 §1]) but earns structurally lower returns (2–3pp→3–4pp GM dilution guided [E02 §5]); it is the closest thing TSMC has to "capex that keeps you alive without keeping you competitive."

**Owner-FCF implication:** normalized maintenance ≈ **US$30B ± 3B**; everything above that is growth spend that must be underwritten on its own returns, not deducted from earning power [skill: asset-intensity-and-maintenance-capex-trap / asset: OWNER_FCF_BRIDGE.md].

#### 3.3 Owner FCF and the conversion trend

| Period | Net income (NT$B) | OCF (NT$B) | Capex (NT$B) | FCF (NT$B) | FCF/NI |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| FY2024 | 1,173.27 | 1,826.18 | 956.01 | 870.17 | **74%** |
| FY2025 | 1,717.88 | 2,274.98 | 1,272.41 | 1,002.57 | **58%** |
| 1H26 | 1,279.04 | ~1,482.3 | 846.76 | 635.57 | **50%** |

Sources: [E01 §3, S6; E01 §1/§2, S1/S2/S4 — 1Q26 FCF 348.21 + 2Q26 287.36; 1Q26 OCF ≈ 348.21+350.76 = 698.97 + 2Q26 783.36]. Ratios computed. 1H26 NI includes the VIS gain; ex-VIS conversion ≈ 52%.

FCF conversion has fallen ~24pp in 18 months *while margins set records* — the signature of a build phase, not of earnings-quality decay: OCF/NI remains >115% and net cash stands at **NT$2,486B (~US$78B)** (2Q26 Management Report). Dividends (NT$466.8B paid in FY25, +28.6% YoY; declared/share NT$4.50→7.00 from 4Q24 to 1Q26; "at least TWD23/share in 2026" [E01 §5]) consume only ~47% of FY25 FCF. The test the module demands — is depressed FCF a construction-to-harvest bridge or a treadmill? — resolves on whether the 2026–27 vintage earns its keep [skill: capacity-cycle-and-normalized-earnings / module: CONSTRUCTION_TO_HARVEST_FCF_INFLECTION.md]:

#### 3.4 Incremental ROIC, 2023 → 2026E [agent-inferred; inputs cited]

- ΔNOPAT: FY23 operating income NT$921.47B ×(1−0.15) ≈ NT$783B; FY26E revenue ~NT$5.42T (US$172B at ~31.5) × OM ~58% ×(1−0.18) ≈ NT$2.58T. **ΔNOPAT ≈ NT$1.79T.**
- ΔIC: net PP&E FY23 NT$3,064.5B [E06] → 2Q26 NT$4,302.9B (2Q26 presentation) → end-26E ≈ NT$4.95T (2H26 capex ~NT$1.05T, most entering service) + working-capital build ~NT$0.35T (A/R +205B, inventory +81B vs 2Q25 [2Q26 presentation], net of payables). **ΔIC ≈ NT$2.2–2.4T.**
- **Incremental ROIC ≈ 75–80%.** Even re-run at *normalized* economics (GM 58%, OM 48%), incremental ROIC ≈ 55–60%. Corroboration: ROE 30.3% (FY24) → 35.4% (FY25) → **45.9% annualized 2Q26** [E01 §3, S7; E06] — versus a "high-twenties through cycle" management anchor [E01 §6].

**Verdict: the capex surge is genuinely NPV-positive scaling, not a declining-ROIC mask** — but note what the forecast advertises: 55–80% incremental returns are the loudest possible invitation to competitors, customers, and states to respond [skill: capacity-cycle-and-normalized-earnings / module: FORECAST_IMPLIED_ROIC_ENTRY_INVITATION.md]. §2's clocks say the response is slow and partially blocked (process learning curve, N-2 Taiwan export rule keeping N2/A16 home [E04 §5], customer qualification cycles) — that blockage *is* the franchise. The forward vintage is also structurally lower-return than the trailing one: rising capex/k (N2, A14 [E02 §1]), overseas mix, and post-rent pricing imply the *next* increment earns perhaps 30–45% **[agent-inferred]** — extraordinary, but declining at the margin. Watch that trajectory, not the level.

---

### 4. Working capital and cash conversion: mix effect or demand-quality signal?

The prints: **A/R days 23 → 26 → 29** and **inventory days 76 → 80 → 87** across 2Q25 → 1Q26 → 2Q26 (2Q26 presentation/management report; [E06]). A/R NT$235.7B → 440.9B (+87% YoY vs revenue +36%); inventory NT$304.2B → 385.5B (+27% YoY, i.e., *below* revenue growth) [2Q26 presentation].

**Inventory: benign, and company-explained.** The management report states days of inventory rose 7 days QoQ "**primarily due to N2 ramp**" (2Q26 Management Report, 2026-07-16). Mechanically consistent: N2 wafers carry reported ~US$30k pricing vs N3 US$18–27k [E02 §1, unofficial], cycle times and WIP (work-in-process) are longer at 2nm and in multi-die CoWoS/SoIC flows, and 2H26 has a "steep ramp-up" to feed (CFO [E01 §1, S1]) — higher-cost WIP layered ahead of recognized revenue. The recognition-lag frame cuts *both* ways: build-ahead inventory converts into 3Q26's guided +12% QoQ revenue [E01 §1], but it also means any 2027 demand crack would surface in margin one to two quarters late [skill: working-capital-and-cash-conversion-quality / module: INVENTORY_TURN_RECOGNITION_LAG.md]. At 87 days against a made-to-order, pre-booked leading-edge book (CoWoS fully booked; 2–3-year customer engagement [E02 §2; 4Q25 transcript]), obsolescence risk is low [skill: working-capital-and-cash-conversion-quality / module: MADE_TO_ORDER_INVENTORY_NEUTRALIZES_OBSOLESCENCE_FEAR.md — applied directionally].
**A/R: watch, don't panic.** +6 days YoY with hyperscaler/fabless mega-caps as counterparties (top-10 customers = 78% of FY25 revenue; largest 19%, second 17% [E03 §5, 20-F]) is more plausibly billing-cycle mix (larger HPC invoices, quarter-end-weighted N2/CoWoS shipments, June revenue +67.9% YoY [E01 §4]) than collectability stress. The **structural cushion the pack cannot fully size**: TSMC's model embeds customer prepayments/LTAs — capacity is pre-booked years out and the presentation's "Accrued liabilities and others" (NT$951.4B, +NT$48.4B QoQ [2Q26 Management Report]) historically houses temporary receipts from customers — but the pack contains **no broken-out prepayment balance**; pull the 20-F temporary-receipts note before treating the float as sized **[gap flagged]**. Prepayment-funded capacity is a genuine balance-sheet substitute while scarcity holds, and it unwinds pro-cyclically when allocation ends [skill: working-capital-and-cash-conversion-quality / module: CUSTOMER_PREPAYMENT_AS_BALANCE_SHEET_SUBSTITUTE.md].

**Kill-signal calibration** (thresholds in §6c): the working-capital lines become a demand-quality signal only when they diverge *against* decelerating revenue — rising days into rising growth is a ramp; rising days into falling growth is channel stuffing's foundry cousin (customers hoarding allocation they will later walk from) [skill: capacity-cycle-and-normalized-earnings / module: SELL_THROUGH_SHIPMENT_AND_PRICE_CRACK_CLOCK.md — adapted].

---

### 5. Cycle position: year ~2.5 of the AI buildout, and the digestion scenario

#### 5.1 Position

By management's own dating, AI-accelerator revenue was mid-teens% of revenue in 2024, "high-teens%" in 2025, guided to mid-to-high-50s% CAGR 2024–2029 [E02 §6; E03 §6]; hyperscaler capex is $700–725B in 2026, **+77% YoY** [E03 §6]; NVIDIA data-center revenue +92% YoY in the April-2026 quarter [E03 §6]. Utilization >100% on N3, capacity pre-sold, pricing rising — every Stage-2 late-cycle indicator is lit (peak margins, tight capacity, rising prices) *except* input-cost inflation and inventory bloat downstream [skill: macro-cycle-sensitivity / SKILL.md Stage 2]. Transmission bridge for the dominant exposure: hyperscaler AI capex → accelerator orders (NVIDIA ~19% of revenue; top-10 78%) → leading-edge wafer + CoWoS demand → HPC 66% of revenue, allocation rent, >100% utilization → GM/EPS and the exit multiple. That single factor now controls revenue, margin, *and* multiple — a macro-beta-dominance flag: no micro edge decouples TSMC from a hyperscaler capex plateau [skill: macro-cycle-sensitivity / module: MACRO_BETA_DOMINANCE_KILL_SWITCH.md]. The market half-agrees: the stock fell −2.3% on a record beat-raise print [E05 §3].

#### 5.2 What TSMC downcycles actually look like

- **2019**: GM 46.0%, OM 34.8% — the modern margin trough; capex was cut to US$14.9B [E01 §7]. (Pack lacks 2018 comparatives; 2019 stands as the trough exemplar.)
- **2023** (the relevant analog — post-shortage digestion): revenue **−4.5% NT$ / −8.7% US$**; GM **−5.2pp** (59.6% → 54.4%); OM −6.9pp; net income **−17.5%**; wafer shipments **−21.3%** (15,253 → 12,002 kpcs); capex cut US$36.3B → 30.4B [E01 §7; E06]. Note the shape: shipments fell 21% but revenue only 9% — mix and pricing held; that is the franchise cushioning the cycle. Margins still gave back 5pp+.

#### 5.3 The 2027 digestion scenario [agent-inferred scenario math; anchors cited]

Trigger: Big-4 hyperscaler 2027 capex guided flat-to-down after 2026's +77% (the bear case already reported in the pack: "AI capex-to-revenue gap widening," 24/7's digestion-begins-late-2026 path [E03 §6; E05 §7]). Mechanics, using the decremental-margin bridge [skill: embedded-capacity-and-operating-leverage-option / module: DECREMENTAL_MARGIN_COMPRESSION_BRIDGE.md]:

| Driver | Base FY26E | Digestion FY27 | Mechanism |
|---|---|---|---|
| Revenue (US$) | ~$172B (+40%+ guide) | **~$155–160B (−8±3%)** | 2023 analog on a bigger HPC base; smartphone/IoT flat |
| GM | ~66.5% | **~55–57%** | rent layer −3–4pp (hikes stop, CoWoS parity); utilization to ~85–90% −2–3pp; depreciation +3.5–5pp intensity on flat/down revenue (FY27 D&A ≈ NT$0.99–1.05T vs FY26E ≈ NT$0.81T, +22–28%, as the $60–64B vintage annualizes); overseas dilution −0.5–1pp incremental; FX unmodeled |
| OM | ~58.5% | **~44–46%** | opex ratio rises on lower revenue |
| Net margin | ~48% (incl. VIS) | **~38–40%** | tax ~18%, non-op normalizes |
| EPS | ~NT$100 / **US$15.9/ADS** (consensus [E05 §3]) | **≈NT$74–81 / US$11.5–12.7/ADS (−20–28%)** | at FX 32 |

Severe case (2023-magnitude shipment shock on today's fixed-cost base): revenue −15%, GM ~53%, EPS ~**US$9.5–10/ADS** — this is where the "≥56% floor" breaks. Recovery-shaped FY28: revenue $185–215B, GM 58–62%, EPS **US$15–21/ADS**. For calibration, consensus FY27 EPS is NT$128.8 ≈ **US$19.99/ADS** (derived at 32.213 [E05 §3]) — consensus therefore embeds *continued upcycle economics through 2027*, ~60–75% above our digestion path.

#### 5.4 The "this time is structural" claims, tested honestly

The denial-of-the-cycle module warns that near peaks, cyclicals get re-rated as seculars on claims the business cannot deliver — and that masking (pre-building, allocation hoarding) delays the visible crack by 1–2 quarters [skill: tech-hardware-and-semiconductors / module: CYCLICAL_RERATED_AS_SECULAR_SHORT.md]. Scoring each claim:

| Claim | Verdict | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| Node monopoly at leading edge | **STRUCTURAL** | 72.3% share; N2 tape-outs 4x N3; competitors' 2nm captive (Tesla/Samsung), internal (Intel), or export-blocked (SMIC) [E03 §1–4; E02 §1] |
| HPC/advanced mix shift (77% ≤7nm) | **STRUCTURAL** | multi-year, customer-roadmap-driven; survives a capex pause (chips still get designed at N2/A16) [E02 §6] |
| Pricing regime ("earn our value", annual hikes) | **PARTLY structural** | the value-repricing survives; the up-to-10% HPC tier and 15% 3nm surcharge are shortage-conditional [E03 §7; E02 §1] |
| 67.7% GM level | **NOT structural** | ~6–8pp is depreciation holiday + FX + reverting rent (§1) |
| >100% utilization; CoWoS premium | **NOT structural** | TSMC's own 2026–27 capacity closes it by design [E02 §2] |
| "AI demand strong to 2029–30" | **UNVERIFIABLE / management book** | C.C. Wei's conviction statement [E01 §1, S17]; the honest posture is Clock-3 monitoring, not underwriting [skill: macro-cycle-sensitivity / module: MACRO_AS_KICKER_NOT_UNDERWRITE.md] |
| FY26 growth >40% | **CYCLICAL AMPLITUDE** | real, but growth *rate* is the most mean-reverting series in the pack (monthly YoY: +17.5% Apr → +67.9% Jun 2026 [E01 §4]) |

Net: **TSMC is a structurally improved franchise currently printing cyclically unrepeatable margins.** Both halves are true; the analytical sin is letting either erase the other.

#### (c) Kill signals (dated thresholds)

1. **2026-10-15 (3Q26 print) and 2027-01 (4Q26 print):** GM below 64% against the 65–67% guide, or 4Q26 GM guide <63% → dilution stack (N2 + overseas + depreciation) running hotter than modeled; move mid-cycle point to ≤57%.
2. **Monthly revenue (10th of each month):** two consecutive months with YoY growth <+15% (vs +35.6% 1H26 [E01 §4]) before 2Q27 → deceleration phase begun; start the 1–2-quarter masked-crack timer [module: CYCLICAL_RERATED_AS_SECULAR_SHORT.md].
3. **Jan–Feb 2027 hyperscaler guides:** aggregate Big-4 2027 capex guided ≤2026's ~$700–725B [E03 §6] → digestion scenario becomes base case; cut FY27 EPS to the $11.5–12.7 path.
4. **TrendForce CoWoS tracking, 2H26–1H27:** supply-demand gap ≤5% or OSAT outsourcing volumes cut → packaging rent ending ahead of schedule; remove CoWoS premium from 2027 estimates.
5. **Dec 2026 pricing reports (annual hike season):** 2027 advanced-node hikes cancelled/shrunk from the 3–10% pattern [E03 §7] → rent decay accelerating; also the *first confirmation* the hikes were shortage-priced, not value-priced.
6. **Any quarter:** inventory days >95 or A/R days >32 *with* signal #2 active → demand-quality downgrade (allocation hoarding unwind), not mix.
7. **2H26–1H27:** Intel announces a committed external 14A anchor customer [E03 §2], or Samsung SF2 yields confirmed >70% with a second external HPC logic win → start the 18–24-month clock on N2-era pricing power; trim structural layer by 1–2pp GM from 2029.
8. **Jan 2027 (4Q26 call):** FY27 depreciation guided >+25% YoY while FY27 revenue framing is <+15% → the §5.3 GM compression arithmetic locks in; floor test (≥56%) goes live.
9. **Policy tripwire, any date:** Section 232 scope broadened to non-exempt categories or the 2.5x/1.5x Taiwan quota mechanism [E04 §1] altered → overseas dilution re-rates from 3–4pp toward 5pp+ [E04 §1 flags scope "still unclear" per TSMC's own 20-F].

#### (d) Handoffs

- **→ valuation-and-return-profile:** consume block (b). Terminal multiple on 57–60% GM economics only; windfall as separate NPV (US$13–35B); FY27 consensus-vs-normalized gap (~$20 vs $12–17) is the quantified downside asymmetry at 33x trailing / ~26x FY26E [E05 §4].
- **→ finite-windfall-and-capacity-response:** rent decomposition (§1.2b) and response clocks (§2) as the filled WINDFALL_DECOMPOSITION / CAPACITY_RESPONSE_CLOCK inputs; prediction locks per kill signals #3–5.
- **→ asset-intensity-and-maintenance-capex-trap:** maintenance ≈ US$30B ± 3B and the 5-yr-book-life-vs-tool-economics distortion for the owner-FCF bridge; revisit after the FY26 20-F breaks out prepayments/temporary receipts (flagged gap, §4).
- **→ short-research-module (conditional):** this is *not* a standalone short — franchise real, balance sheet net cash NT$2.5T, incremental ROIC extreme; but kill signals #2+#3 firing together create a dated catalyst short on consensus FY27, sized for 1–2 masked quarters [module: CYCLICAL_RERATED_AS_SECULAR_SHORT.md].
- **→ thesis-drift-monitor:** register kill signals #1–9 with their dates.
- **→ position-sizing / decision layer:** carry the macro-beta-dominance flag (§5.1): one factor (hyperscaler AI capex) controls revenue, margin, and exit multiple; treat any long as partially a levered expression of that factor, whatever the franchise quality.
- **Evidence gaps to close:** official 2Q26 transcript (2nm-dilution figure 2–3pp vs 3–4pp discrepancy [E01 gaps]); FY25 20-F temporary-receipts/prepayment balance; FY25 depreciation-only (vs D&A) split; Rapidus status (absent from pack).

*Compliance: public-data research synthesis; no trade, sizing, or execution instruction; no MNPI. All price/consensus data as of 2026-07-16/17 per the evidence pack.*


## Section II.4 — Governance, Control, Capital Allocation, and Earnings Quality

**Company:** Taiwan Semiconductor Manufacturing Company Limited (TSMC; NYSE: TSM, 1 American Depositary Share (ADS) = 5 common shares; TWSE: 2330; SEC CIK 0001046179).
**As of:** 2026-07-17. TSM ADS close $409.74 (2026-07-16, print day) [hfdt prices, polygon live, evid-06].
**Citation convention:** [S#] keys refer to the source list in evidence file `01_results_and_financials.md`; "evid-0X" = evidence files 02–06 in the same pack; "20-F FY2025" = TSMC's annual report on Form 20-F, filed 2026-04-16 (EDGAR accession 0001628280-26-025362). All arithmetic shown is mine and labeled [computed]; inferences beyond sourced facts are labeled [agent-inferred]. NT$ = New Taiwan dollar.

---

### 1. Management and alignment — what alignment looks like when there is no founder and no option pile

*Method: [skill: management-alignment / modules: LAYERED_INCENTIVES_AND_INSTITUTIONAL_IDENTITY.md, OWNER_OPERATOR_STEWARDSHIP_DIAGNOSTIC.md, FORM4_FOOTNOTE_TAXONOMY.md]*

**The post-founder configuration.** Dr. C.C. Wei has been CEO since 2018 and was unanimously elected Chairman & CEO on 2024-06-04, consolidating both roles after Mark Liu's retirement (TSMC 6-K, 2024-06-04, evid-04 §6). Founder Morris Chang (b. 1931) retired in 2018 and holds no operational role (evid-04 §6). Below Wei sit two EVP co-COOs (Y.J. Mii, R&D; Y.P. Chyn, Operations, since 2024-03-01) with two deputy co-COOs (Kevin Zhang, Cliff Hou) — a deliberately deep bench structure (TSMC 6-K 2024-02-29, evid-04 §6). CFO Wendell Huang has held the finance chair across the entire guidance record examined below; the widely-referenced "CFO transition" is not supported by the evidence — the 2026 executive change was Lora Ho (former CFO, later SVP) *retiring* on 2026-04-04 (20-F FY2025, evid-04 §6). Flag for the master document: treat "CFO transition risk" as absent, not pending.

**Board.** Ten directors, seven independent: Sir Peter Bonfield, Michael Splinter, Moshe Gavrielov, L. Rafael Reif (ex-MIT president), Ursula Burns (ex-Xerox CEO), Lynn Elsenhans (ex-Sunoco CEO), and Chuan Lin (former Taiwan premier); the non-independents are Wei, F.C. Tseng, and Chun-Hsien Yeh — the board representative of the National Development Fund (NDF), Taiwan's Executive Yuan state fund (20-F FY2025, evid-04 §§5–6). A 70%-independent board with genuinely heavyweight members, plus one sovereign seat, is the structural signature: this is an **institution supervised by professionals and anchored by a state**, not an owner-operated company.

**Compensation design.** Directors and executive officers as a group (excluding the NDF) own **0.23%** of shares (20-F FY2025, evid-04 §7) — roughly $4.9B at market [computed: 0.23% × $2.12T], material human wealth but trivial control. TSMC's compensation structure is, by long-standing design, cash-and-profit-sharing-heavy with modest equity: base salary plus cash bonus plus employee profit-sharing tied to profitability, with restricted-share awards a recent and comparatively small addition **[to-verify against 20-F Item 6.B — the evidence pack does not include the compensation note; the 0.23% ownership figure and the flat share count below are the pack-supported anchors]**. Run the pay-pool test from LAYERED_INCENTIVES_AND_INSTITUTIONAL_IDENTITY: a formulaic cash profit-sharing pool is a claim on the residual ahead of shareholders, and it is pro-cyclical — it pays out most when margins are at records (67.7% gross margin in 2Q26 [S1]). Two guardrails keep this from being a red flag here: (i) the pool scales with *net income on a fixed share count* — TSMC's 25,932M shares have not moved for years [S1, evid-06], so employees are paid from the same per-share base shareholders own, with zero dilution offset games; and (ii) the revealed history — through the 2023 down-cycle (revenue −8.7% in US$ [computed from S-table §7]) the company still grew the dividend and did not reset any equity strike, because there are no meaningful strikes to reset. Award-design sub-score: high, by *absence* of the gameable machinery (no revenue-only PSUs, no adjusted-EBITDA vesting, no option repricing surface) rather than by presence of per-share hurdles [skill: management-alignment, Stage 3].

**Insider transaction flow.** The hfdt EDGAR pull for CIK 0001046179 shows "numerous Form 4s (July 2026)" in the filing stream alongside the Q2 6-K (evid-06). Two cautions before anyone reads that as signal: (a) as a foreign private issuer (FPI), TSMC's insiders are ordinarily exempt from Section 16 Form 4 reporting, so Form 4s appearing on this CIK are anomalous and most likely reflect reporting persons filing in other capacities — they must be individually opened and classified before use **[to-verify; typed gap per insider-and-13F-signals Stage 1]**; (b) even once parsed, the FORM4_FOOTNOTE_TAXONOMY discipline applies — only true discretionary open-market transactions count [skill: insider-and-13F-signals / module: FORM4_SUBSTANCE.md]. Verdict: **no usable insider-conviction signal either way**; alignment at TSMC does not run through personal equity anyway.

**What alignment actually is here.** Per the layered-incentive map, the dominant incentive layer at TSMC is **institutional identity**: a national-champion engineering institution whose culture, career ladders, and state supervision all reward *technology leadership and profitability* ("We earn our value, and we make sure that our profit, our gross margin, is enough for our long-term sustaining expansion" — C.C. Wei, 2Q26 call, 2026-07-16 [S17]; "Our pricing will remain strategic, not opportunistic" — CFO Huang, 4Q25 call, 2026-01-15 [S7]). The institution defends itself ferociously: the 2nm trade-secret leak was prosecuted under Taiwan's National Security Act "national core technology" provisions — the first such prosecution — ending in a 10-year sentence for the lead defendant and a ~US$5M fine for Tokyo Electron's Taiwan unit (2026-04-27, IP Court; evid-04 §6), and TSMC sued former SVP Wei-Jen Lo over his move to Intel (2025-11-25, evid-04 §6). Institution-preserving and shareholder-friendly behaviors have so far pointed the same direction — record margins, record dividends, zero dilution. The honest caveat from the diagnostic: this is an **`aligned_professional` institution with a sovereign anchor**, not a `true_steward` owner-operator — meaning alignment holds *until the state's definition of the institution's mission diverges from shareholder value* (see §2). Alignment verdict: **8/10**, docked for near-zero executive ownership and the sovereign layer's priority claim in tail states [skill: management-alignment, Stage 6].

---

### 2. Control-party map — the state stack, and who wins when interests collide

*Method: [skill: control-party-and-minority-holder-dynamics / modules: CONTROLLER_AS_CEILING_AND_FLOOR.md, EM_SOVEREIGN_MINORITY_CLAIM_TAIL_RISK.md, POLITICALLY_RISKY_FOREIGN_CONTROLLER_HIDDEN_ASSET_DISCOUNT.md]*

**The de facto control stack (no one owns control; several parties exercise it):**

| Layer | Instrument | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| Taiwan state — equity | NDF holds 1,653.7M shares = **6.38%**, largest single holder, one board seat (Chun-Hsien Yeh); has "from time to time" sold shares as ADSs | 20-F FY2025 (evid-04 §5) |
| Taiwan state — technology law | **N-2 rule**: Statute for Industrial Innovation Art. 22 amended Nov–Dec 2025 — overseas deployment must trail Taiwan's leading edge by ~two generations; outbound investment needs regulatory/Investment-Commission approval; consequence: 2nm (N2) and A16 stay Taiwan-only for now | evid-04 §5 [IMPLEMENTED, press formulations differ — statutory text to-verify] |
| Taiwan state — macro | Central bank/currency: TWD +7% in two sessions May 2025; TSMC sensitivity: 1% TWD appreciation ≈ −1% NT$ revenue, −40bp gross margin (CFO Huang, 2Q25 call, 2025-07-17 [S10]) | evid-04 §5 |
| US state — carrot/stick | Section 232 25% tariff on covered advanced chips (effective 2026-01-15) with exemption quotas for US builders (2.5× construction / 1.5× post-completion); CHIPS $6.6B grant + $5B loans (Nov 2024, $1.5B received Q4'24); the **$265B** cumulative US investment commitment (raised +$100B on 2026-07-16); Intel grants-to-equity precedent (Aug 2025) explicitly *not* applied to TSMC | evid-04 §§1,3; evid-02 §3 |
| Minority holders | 20.5% of shares held as ADSs (5,313.5M shares / 1,062.7M ADSs, Citibank depositary); foreign institutional ownership ~73–75% [soft sourcing]; D&O 0.23% | 20-F FY2025 (evid-04 §7) |

**Where minority ADS holders sit.** They own a residual claim on a company whose *distribution policy* is board-controlled and demonstrably minority-friendly, but whose *capital-deployment geography and technology location* are decided in Taipei and Washington. The claim itself (per the EM_SOVEREIGN two-ledger discipline) is operationally superb and legally clean in peacetime — NYSE-listed ADSs, 20-F disclosure, dividends remitted on schedule (NT$155.59B paid in 2Q26 alone [evid-06]) — but the tail state is the known one: in a Strait crisis, the state will protect the *island franchise*; nothing about that protects the *foreign ADS claim*. Write the sentence explicitly, per the module: *"The state is likely to protect the fabs, the engineers, and the domestic system; that does not protect the ADS holder, whose claim can be impaired even while the franchise survives."*

**Scoring the three-way pull with 2025–26 evidence:**

- **Distribution policy — shareholders win.** Dividend per share declared quarterly: NT$4.00 (1Q24) → 4.50 → 5.00 → 6.00 → **7.00 (1Q26, board-approved 2026-05-12)** — +75% in eight quarters; cash received: NT$14 (2024) → NT$18 (2025) → "at least NT$23" guided for 2026 (CFO Huang, 4Q25 call, 2026-01-15 [S7][S9]). No expropriation, no forced issuance, no CHIPS-style equity conversion (US officials explicitly declined equity in TSMC, Aug 2025, evid-04 §3). A controller extracting from minorities does not raise the dividend 28%/yr [computed: (23/14)^(1/2)−1 = 28.2%].
- **Technology location — Taiwan wins.** The N-2 statute hard-codes the crown-jewels-stay-home policy; 2nm/A16 are Taiwan-only today (evid-04 §5). Note the two-sided read: this *caps* geographic diversification (raises the geopolitical tail on the claim) while *protecting* the moat's concentration and the state's silicon-shield rent.
- **Balance-sheet deployment — US policy wins.** The $265B commitment (announced with Q2 results, 2026-07-16, "tied to the Jan 2026 US-Taiwan trade/investment deal" — US Commerce/NIST, Bloomberg, evid-02 §3) is the proof that **policy can commandeer the balance sheet**. But look at what was received in exchange: the Taiwan reciprocal tariff capped at 15% (vs the 32% announced Apr 2025), duty-free import quotas during construction, no equity demand, and $6.6B+$5B of CHIPS support (evid-04 §§1,3,5). The controller stack has taken its rent **in kind (capacity location) rather than in cash (fees, related-party leakage)**.

**Ceiling-and-floor verdict** [module: CONTROLLER_AS_CEILING_AND_FLOOR.md]: run the justified-vs-exploitable table and TSMC's governance discount is **justified-but-benign**: there is no related-party extraction, no blocked dividend, no captive-pricing leakage (rows all land "exploitable/clean"), yet the discount cannot be arbitraged away because its source is not governance at all — it is the sovereign tail on the claim plus the policy right to redirect capex. The NDF is simultaneously floor (a state whose income and prestige depend on TSMC's profitability wants wafer-level profit maximization — "earn our value" pricing is state-compatible) and ceiling (the state will always choose national survival over ADS-holder NPV). Controller-alignment direction: **+600/1000 in peacetime, sign-flips in the tail** [skill-conformant signed read; scoring per control-party skill].

**Whose interests win when they conflict — the one-line answer:** *shareholders keep the income statement; the states share the balance sheet; and in a true crisis the ADS holder is structurally last.* The 2025–26 record (dividend ramp + $265B commandeering, simultaneously) is exactly what that regime looks like when it is working.

---

### 3. Capital allocation — quantified uses of cash, per-share compounding on a frozen share count, and the $265B as a capital-allocation event

*Method: [skill: capital-allocation-and-per-share-compounding / modules: BOOM_CAPEX_NEGATIVE_NPV_TEST.md, BALANCE_SHEET_CAPACITY_OPTION_VALUE.md, NET_CASH_HAIRCUT_BY_GOVERNANCE.md]*

**Uses-of-cash ledger, FY2019–FY2025 (NT$B, from TSMC management reports [S6, S11–S15] and SEC XBRL companyfacts [evid-06]):**

| Use | Cumulative FY19–25 | Share of OCF |
|---|---|---|
| Operating cash flow (OCF) | 9,503.7 | 100% |
| Organic capex | 6,067.8 (≈US$196B) | **63.8%** |
| Cash dividends | 2,191.2 | 23.1% |
| Buybacks | **0** | 0% |
| Material M&A | ~0 (none of note; VIS was a *disposal*) | 0% |
| Residual → cash build + minor debt | balance | ~13% |

[all computed from the cited series]. Management's own frame: 2021–25 capex "totaled USD167 billion" with USD30B of R&D (4Q25 call [S7]). FY26 capital budget: US$52–56B (Jan) → "high end" (Apr) → **US$60–64B (Jul 16)** [S7][S18][S17]; 1H26 capex NT$846.8B = 35.2% of 1H26 revenue, re-accelerating (2Q run-rate ~39%) [computed, evid-06].

**Per-share compounding on a flat denominator.** Shares outstanding: 25,932M — flat for years; weighted diluted count identical [S1, evid-06]. Therefore every per-share series equals the company-level series — the cleanest possible compounding audit:

- **EPS:** NT$13.32 (2019) → NT$66.25 (2025) = **+30.7%/yr CAGR** [computed]; to FY26E consensus NT$100.40 (stockanalysis, 2026-07-16, evid-05 §3) = **+33.4%/yr over 7 years** [computed].
- **BVPS (book value per share):** NT$62.26 (YE2019: equity 1,614.4B [evid-06]) → NT$249.67 (6/30/26: equity 6,474.5B [evid-06]) = **+23.8%/yr over 6.5 years** [computed] — *while* paying out NT$2.2T of dividends.
- **DPS (received basis):** NT$10.00 (2019 [computed: 259.3B/25.93B]) → NT$18 (2025) → ≥NT$23 (2026 guided [S7]) = +12.6%/yr over 7 years, but **+28.2%/yr 2024→2026** [computed] — the ramp is recent and deliberate.
- **ROE (return on equity):** ~21.4% (2019 [computed: 345.26/1,614.4, year-end basis]) → 35.4% (2025 [S7]) → **45.9% annualized 2Q26** (Q2 presentation, evid-06). Through-cycle guide: "high-twenties percent" (4Q25 call [S7]).

Policy is explicit: "committed to a sustainable and steadily increasing cash dividend per share on both an annual and quarterly basis" (CFO Huang, 4Q25 call, 2026-01-15, transcript p.4 [S7, verified verbatim in pack transcript]). Cumulative payout FY19–25 = 35.3% of net income [computed]; forward declared payout ≈ NT$28/yr vs FY26E EPS NT$100.4 ≈ **28%** [computed]. Zero buybacks is the correct reading of the next-dollar fork, not a governance failure: with reinvestment ROIC in the 30s–40s (ROE 35–46% on a business still supply-constrained — "3nm above 100% utilization," call coverage 2026-07-16, evid-02 §1 [REPORT]) and the stock at ~26–33× earnings (evid-05 §4), organic capex dominates repurchase on every per-share test [skill: capital-allocation-and-per-share-compounding, Stage 2 next-dollar fork].

**The $265B US program as a capital-allocation event.** Use the company's own quantification of the economics — overseas-fab gross-margin dilution of "2%–3% in the early stages, widening to 3%–4% in the latter stages" (CFO Huang, stated identically on the 4Q25, 1Q26 and 2Q26 calls [S7][S18][S17]) — and price it [agent-inferred, math shown]:

- *Drag:* Revenue base ~US$300B by 2030 [agent-inferred from management's ~25% 2024–29 US$ CAGR frame, 4Q25 call, evid-02 §6]. Later-stage dilution 3.5pp × $300B ≈ **$10.5B/yr pre-tax**; after ~17.5% tax ≈ $8.7B/yr; capitalized at 10–12% ≈ **$72–87B gross NPV drag** [computed]. (If the drag grows with revenue the number is larger; if Arizona matures to Taiwan cost curves it shrinks — treat $70–90B as the static band.)
- *Offsets:* (i) direct subsidies — CHIPS $6.6B grant + $5B loans (evid-04 §3) plus Japan/Germany support for JASM/ESMC ≈ **$12–15B PV** [agent-inferred]; (ii) *price recapture* — Arizona wafers reportedly priced 25–30% above Taiwan (press) while AMD's CEO publicly accepts "5–20% more," and TechInsights estimates true Arizona production *cost* only ~10% above Taiwan (evid-03 §7) — if premium exceeds cost premium on even half the US output, a third to a half of the GM dilution guidance is recovered at the dollar-margin line [agent-inferred]; (iii) *tariff/quota insurance* — the 25% Section 232 tariff regime plus the 2.5×/1.5× exemption quotas mean the build-out is the admission ticket for TSMC's customers' US demand (78% of 2Q26 revenue is North America customers [S2]); illustrative expected value: if non-compliance risked punitive tariffs touching even 5% of revenue-equivalent economics at 10% probability, that is ~$1.5B/yr ≈ ~$15B PV [agent-inferred illustration]; (iv) *continuity option* — "around 30% of our 2nm-and-more-advanced capacity" eventually in Arizona (4Q25 call, evid-02 §3) is a deep-out-of-the-money put on the franchise in a Taiwan-disruption tail; at any material tail probability its value is tens of billions [agent-inferred].
- *Net:* **$25–60B net NPV drag ≈ 1–3% of the ~$2.12T ADR-implied market cap** [computed] — a real cost, honestly disclosed via the dilution guidance, and cheap as geopolitical insurance. The governance point is sharper than the valuation point: the program proves the balance sheet is **commandeerable**. Also note what the $265B is *not*: it is an announced cumulative intention (12 facilities, ~decade), milestone-paced, not a contracted take-or-pay; the binding annual number remains capex guidance ($60–64B for 2026 [S17]).
- *Boom-capex discipline check* [module: BOOM_CAPEX_NEGATIVE_NPV_TEST.md]: the re-underwrite question — approved at AI-boom demand assumptions, arriving 2027–2030 — is the single biggest capital-allocation risk. Mitigants: TSMC builds against "customers and customers' customers" demand verification (Wei, 2Q26 call [S17]), CoWoS supply still 10–20% short of demand (TrendForce, 2026-06-15, evid-02 §2), and management's own history of *cutting* capex into the 2023 downturn ($36.3B → $30.4B → $29.8B, 2022→2024 [S-table §7]) — they have demonstrated the cancellation option is real.

**Is NT$2,654B of net cash lazy?** Net cash = NT$3,518.0B cash & marketable securities − NT$864.3B long-term interest-bearing debt = **NT$2,653.7B ≈ US$83B at 32** [computed; evid-06]. That is only **3.9% of market cap** [computed] and **~1.35× one year of the FY26 capital budget** [computed: 2,654 / (62×31.6)]. Run the NET_CASH_HAIRCUT_BY_GOVERNANCE schema: required operating cash for a company committing ~NT$2T/yr of capex with quarterly tool prepayments is enormous; the demonstrated behavior (dividend +28%/yr, no idle hoarding, cash deployed counter-cyclically through 2023) places it governance Tier 2 (10–20% haircut, mostly for the sovereign-commandeering channel evidenced by the $265B event) → **usable net cash ≈ US$66–75B** for the valuation bridge [computed]. One elegant mechanical detail: Taiwan levies a surtax on undistributed retained earnings — 2Q26's effective tax rate rose to 18.1% "mainly reflect[ing] the tax on undistributed retained earnings" [S2] — the tax code itself pushes the dividend ramp. Verdict: **war chest, not laziness**; this is the balance sheet the capex cycle and the geopolitical overhang require [module: BALANCE_SHEET_CAPACITY_OPTION_VALUE.md — management-likely and rational-owner uses nearly coincide, so capacity is close to face value].

### 4. Quality of earnings — forensic pass on 2Q26 and FY26E

*Method: [skill: quality-of-earnings / modules: EARNED_VS_GIFTED_EARNINGS_CLASSIFIER.md, CONSERVATIVE_ACCOUNTING_HIDDEN_CHEAPNESS.md, ACCOUNTING_POLICY_CROSS_NORMALIZATION.md]*

**(a) Strip the one-offs from 2Q26.** Reported: revenue NT$1,270.4B (+36.0% YoY); operating income NT$766.6B (+65.4%); pre-tax income NT$862.4B; net income NT$706.6B (+77.4%); EPS NT$27.25 / US$4.31 per ADS [S1]. Non-operating items were a **gain of NT$95.83B** (vs NT$28.83B in 1Q26, NT$29.61B in 2Q25), decomposed in the management report as: long-term investments NT$1.44B + **net interest income NT$26.96B** + other gains and losses NT$67.43B, "mainly due to disposal and mark-to-market gains of NT$63.20 billion on Vanguard International Semiconductor Corp. (VIS) shares" after selling 8.1% of VIS on 2026-05-15 (2Q26 Management Report §II-3 [S2, verified verbatim]). Non-op was **11.1% of pre-tax income; the VIS gain alone 7.3%** [computed].

Clean restatement [computed; two tax treatments since Taiwan-domestic securities disposal gains are generally income-tax-exempt — treatment to-verify in the 20-F tax note]:

| | Reported | Clean (VIS fully untaxed) | Clean (VIS taxed at 18.1%) |
|---|---|---|---|
| Net income (NT$B) | 706.56 | 643.36 | 654.80 |
| EPS (NT$) | 27.25 | **24.81** | 25.25 |
| EPS (US$/ADS at 31.60) | 4.31 | **3.93** | 4.00 |
| YoY growth | +77.4% | **+61.5%** (+64.4% if 2Q25's NT$6.89B other-gains also stripped) | +64.4% |

So the VIS gain flattered Q2 EPS by **~7–9%** and YoY growth by **~13–16 points**. The cleaner run-rate line was in plain sight: operating income +65.4% YoY [S1]. Two smaller items: (i) other operating income of NT$5.27B rose "mainly due to insurance claims from the April 3, 2024 earthquake" [S2] — ≤0.7% of operating income, immaterial but one-off; (ii) the residual NT$4.23B of other gains ex-VIS [computed] contains FX and sundries — FX at TSMC flows overwhelmingly through revenue/GM translation (1% TWD move ≈ 40bp GM [S10]), not the non-op line, so there is no hidden FX-gain engine in the P&L. Net interest income (NT$26.96B/qtr, ~NT$108B/yr annualized [computed]) is recurring — it is the yield on the war chest — but classify it as *financing* earnings, not operating, per the classifier's component split.

**(b) MTM tail from the retained VIS stake.** "Disposal **and mark-to-market** gains" [S2] implies the residual VIS stake is now fair-valued through the P&L (loss of significant influence → revaluation) [agent-inferred; verify classification]. Expect recurring non-op volatility from VIS's share price henceforth — small but a permanent noise source in the non-op line.

**(c) Depreciation policy — the anti-flattering item.** TSMC depreciates fab equipment straight-line over **5 years** (20-F FY2025 PP&E policy, verified from filing text, evid-02 §5). Intel moved to 8 years (2023); Samsung uses similar longer lives [task premise; consistent with public filings — to-verify exact Samsung schedule]. FY26 depreciation is guided up "high-teens %" YoY on the 2nm ramp (CFO Huang, 4Q25 call [S7]); estimate FY26 D&A ≈ NT$880B [agent-inferred: FY24 actual NT$653.6B (evid-06 XBRL) × ~1.14 (FY25E) × ~1.18]. Counterfactual on 8-year lives [agent-inferred, math shown]: equipment ≈ 85% of depreciation; young fleet ⇒ savings ratio ~33–37.5% of equipment depreciation ⇒ NT$248–281B lower D&A ⇒ on FY26E revenue of NT$5.21T (consensus, evid-05 §3) with ~90% of D&A in cost of goods sold, **gross margin would print roughly +4.3–4.9pp higher — ~72% instead of 67.7%**. TSMC's margins are *understated* relative to peer accounting, the mirror image of the usual QoE finding [module: CONSERVATIVE_ACCOUNTING_HIDDEN_CHEAPNESS.md]. Any peer-margin comparison (Intel Foundry −$10.3B operating loss FY25; Samsung foundry ~KRW 6T loss FY25, evid-05 §5) already flatters the *peers*.

**(d) Government grants and subsidies.** Received: US$1.5B of the $6.6B CHIPS award in Q4 2024, milestone-based (evid-04 §3); JASM/ESMC carry Japanese/German support within JV structures (evid-02 §3). Where they sit: capital grants of this type reduce the carrying value of PP&E or sit as deferred income amortized against depreciation, rather than boosting revenue **[to-verify in 20-F government-grant note — the pack does not contain the accounting policy text]**. Materiality check: $1.5B received against FY25 capex of $40.9B [S6] — ~3.7% of one year's capex [computed]; the P&L effect is spread over asset lives and is immaterial to any single quarter's margin. The 20-F itself warns there is "no assurance" remaining CHIPS incentives arrive "at the levels we anticipate or at all" (evid-04 §3). Grants are not what is driving 67.7% GM.

**(e) Customer prepayments / contract liabilities.** TSMC has historically taken capacity prepayments from customers; the pack contains **no current contract-liability balance** — flag as a data request from the 20-F/quarterlies [typed gap]. Directionally, prepayments are both cash-flow support (OCF NT$783.4B in 2Q26 vs net income NT$706.6B [evid-06]) and a *demand-quality* signal — customers financing their supplier's capex is the strongest form of order-book commitment [agent-inferred]. What the pack does show on working capital: **A/R days 29 (up from 23 a year ago); inventory days 87 (up from 76)** (2Q26 presentation, evid-06). Both are mild builds consistent with the 2nm ramp and advanced-packaging build-out, not channel-stuffing signatures (there is no channel to stuff at a made-to-order foundry), but they are the two lines to watch [skill: quality-of-earnings, Stage 2].

**(f) Other 20-F items worth flagging.** (i) The unresolved BIS (Bureau of Industry and Security) probe over the Huawei/Sophgo diversion — Reuters reported potential penalties of "US$1 billion or more" (2025-04-08); no settlement as of the 20-F, which says results "have not been materially affected" to date but flags possible penalties (evid-04 §2). That is ~1.2% of FY26E net income if it lands at $1B [computed: $1B vs ~NT$2.6T/32 ≈ $81B] — headline risk, not earnings-power risk; an un-accrued contingency to note for the valuation section. (ii) Effective tax rate: 16% (2025) → guided 17–18% (2026) [4Q25 transcript, evid pack] with the undistributed-earnings surtax embedded — a structural, slightly rising tax drag, honestly guided.

**Verdict.** Apply the earned-vs-gifted split: the margin level (67.7% GM) is **earned** — pricing ("earn our value"), utilization, and node mix — with a *conservative-accounting understatement* of ~4–5pp against peer conventions; the *EPS print* is modestly **flattered**: ~7–9% of Q2 EPS and ~2.4% of FY26E EPS is the one-off VIS gain [computed: NT$2.44/sh on consensus NT$100.40]. Cyclicality is the honest asterisk: HPC is 66% of revenue [S1] and the AI capex cycle is one shared driver behind revenue, margin, and mix simultaneously (one-driver-many-outputs flag). **Earnings-quality grade: A− / "good business, high-quality earnings, with a one-off in the print"** — 2026 EPS quality is HIGH after a ~2.5% full-year haircut (Q2 alone ~8%), and margins are understated, not overstated, relative to peer accounting [skill: quality-of-earnings, Stage 5].

### 5. Communication quality — the sandbag machine, graded

*Method: [skill: management-communication-quality / modules: MGMT_TRACK_RECORD_AS_BAYESIAN_PRIOR_ON_GUIDANCE.md, STALE_LONG_TERM_GUIDANCE_AS_SOFT_BULLISH_TELL.md]*

**Guidance-to-actual ledger, 2024–2026 (all company statements, dated):**

| Date | Statement | Outcome |
|---|---|---|
| 2026-01-15 (4Q25 call) | FY26 revenue "+close to 30%" US$; capex $52–56B [S7][S5] | superseded upward twice |
| 2026-04-16 (1Q26 call) | FY26 "above 30%"; capex "high end" of range [S18] | superseded upward |
| 2026-07-16 (2Q26 call) | FY26 "slightly above 40%"; capex **$60–64B** [S17] | +10pp guide-raise in six months |
| 2026-04-16 (2Q26 guide) | Rev US$39.0–40.2B; GM 65.5–67.5%; OM 56.5–58.5% [S4] | Actual: $40.20B (top); **GM 67.7% (above range)**; **OM 60.3% (175bp above top)** [S1] — beat every line |
| 2026-01-15 (1Q26 guide) | Rev US$34.6–35.8B [S7 transcript] | Actual US$35.90B — above the top [S4] |
| 2025-07-17 (2Q25 call) | Long-term GM floor "53% and higher" [S10] | floor raised |
| 2026-01-15 (4Q25 call) | Floor raised to "**56% and higher through the cycle**," ROE "high-twenties %" [S7] | actual prints 66.2%, 67.7% — floor trails actuals by >10pp |
| Jan 2025 → Jan 2026 | AI-accelerator revenue CAGR (2024–29): mid-40s% → **mid-to-high-50s%** (evid-03 §6) | serial upward revision |

Pattern: **systematic, calibrated sandbagging** — initial guides set where the company is highly confident, then raised as the year de-risks; Q2'26 beat its own guide on *every* line. Per the Bayesian-prior module (which itself cites TSM as the type specimen): treat soft TSMC guides as **floors**, require the bear case to beat the sandbag rather than consensus, and — the flip side — treat any *rare cut* as a loud, high-signal concession, because it would break a deliberately conservative pattern. The "56%+" floor functions as the stale-conservative-target tell: a company already printing 67.7% that re-anchors its floor only to 56% is withholding a beatable bar, a soft-bullish signal per the module — with the honest caveat that the floor embeds real known headwinds (overseas dilution 2–4pp [S7][S17], N2 ramp dilution 2–3pp FY26 [S7][S18], FX at 40bp/1% [S10]), so the gap is not pure sandbag.

**Consistency and specificity.** CFO Huang's framework language is verbatim-stable across calls (FX sensitivity, dilution bands, "sustainable and steadily increasing" dividend policy [S7][S10]); guidance is always bridged with named drivers (FX assumption stated each quarter: 32.5 → 29 → 30.6 → 31.6 → 31.7 → 32.0 across 2025–26 guides [S1][S4][S5][S7][S10]). C.C. Wei volunteers the demand-verification method ("customers and customers' customers... make sure chips are not going into inventory," 2Q26 call [S17]) — an admission-aware answer to the AI-bubble question rather than evasion. One discipline note: the 2Q26 quotes rest on secondary same-day transcription [S17]; the official transcript should be re-verified when posted, including the ambiguous 2nm-dilution figure (2–3pp FY26 vs "3–4pp in 2H26" — flagged discrepancy, evid-01 gaps; evid-02 §1).

**Grade: A− credibility** (improving/stable verdict; docked only for the unverified secondary-source quarter and the structural note that guidance this conservative transfers information *slowly*, which the market now front-runs — the stock fell 2.32% on a beat-everything print because the *expectations* bar sits above the guidance bar (2026-07-16, evid-05 §§1,3) [skill: management-communication-quality, Stage 8].)

### 6. Ownership and flow — crowded via the index door, not the hedge-fund door

*Method: [skill: holder-flow-and-crowding / modules: HOLDER_FLOW_METHOD.md, INVESTOR_BASE_AS_SECOND_ORDER_PRICING_VARIABLE.md; skill: insider-and-13F-signals]*

**Structure.** 20.5% of shares sit in the ADS (20-F FY2025, evid-04 §7); foreign institutions own ~73–75% of 2330 [softer sourcing, evid-04 §7]; NDF 6.38%; D&O 0.23%. TSM is **15.18% of EEM (iShares MSCI Emerging Markets ETF) — the #1 holding — and >14% of the MSCI EM index (57% of Taiwan's country weight)**; TSMC+Samsung+SK Hynix ≈ 30% of the EM index (evid-05 §6, 2026-03/07 sources). EM ETFs took in **$38B in H1'26 — the best first half on record** (SSGA, 2026-06-30, evid-05 §6). Short interest: **0.57%** of shares (2026-07-16); options put/call OI ratio 1.31; pre-print 30-day implied vol ~57%, IV rank 79 (evid-05 §6). TSM is *not* an S&P 500 constituent (foreign domiciliary, structural, evid-05 §6).

**13F flow (Q4'25–Q1'26, aggregator-sourced, evid-04 §7 / evid-05).** Hedge funds long TSM: 158 → 186 (Q3'25→Q4'25, Insider Monkey count). Fisher Asset Management +1.8% to ~28.35M ADS (~$5.6B) — largest tracked active holder (2026-05-25). Sanders Capital **−12.2%** (−3.90M shares, ~$1.27B). UBS Group +51%; Susquehanna +1,341% (Q1'26, Quiver aggregation — likely partly derivative/market-making inventory [agent-inferred]; position-level verification against EDGAR required before weighting). Historical scar tissue worth remembering: Berkshire round-tripped a >$4.1B stake within ~two quarters in 2022–23, citing geopolitics (Reuters, 2023-04-11, evid-04 §7) — the highest-quality holder validation this name ever received *reversed on the tail risk, not the fundamentals*.

**Crowding state.** By hedge-fund standards TSM is consensus-long but not levered-crowded (0.57% short interest means no squeeze fuel either way; 19 analysts: 12 Strong Buy / 6 Buy / 1 Hold / 0 Sell, avg PT $516.76, evid-05 §3). The real crowding is **passive-structural**: TSM is the single largest brick in the EM index complex, so its marginal holder is an asset-allocation flow, not a stock-picker [module: INVESTOR_BASE_AS_SECOND_ORDER_PRICING_VARIABLE.md — the marginal investor's metric is EM allocation weight and AI-theme beta, not clean EPS].

**Flow asymmetries to underwrite:**

- **Risk-off / EM outflow:** the H1'26 record $38B inflow implies ~$5.4B of mechanical TSM buying at a ~14–15% index weight [computed] against ~$6.3B/day of ADS dollar volume [computed: 15.4M ADS avg × $410] — comfortably absorbed in normal times, symmetric and *simultaneous* on the way out. In a risk-off, US-listed holders sell the liquid ADS leg first: expect the **ADS premium (+6.9% on 7/16 closes; +9.4% on the 7/15 pre-print close — both DERIVED with a timing caveat, evid-05 §1) to compress toward zero or below**, as it is the pressure-relief valve between the two fungibility-constrained lines [agent-inferred; ADS-creation friction underpinning the premium is a known structural feature to-verify]. The premium is therefore a live crowding gauge: wider = US/AI-tourist demand; collapsing = risk-off distribution.
- **Index-reweight event:** any MSCI capping methodology change on mega-weights, or a Taiwan-weight action, converts a passive anchor into a forced seller of size; with 20.5% of the company in the ADS and the rest in a 13:30-Taipei-close market, the ADS absorbs the gap risk overnight [agent-inferred scenario; no live MSCI consultation in the pack].
- **Print-day evidence of expectations-crowding:** −2.32% close on a record beat-raise day (2026-07-16, evid-05 §1) — positioning, not disclosure, set the bar.

**Verdict:** crowding_intensity moderate-high (structural/passive), borrow/squeeze risk nil, flow signal (Fisher add vs Sanders trim) mixed and second-order versus the index plumbing. Ownership **confirms** the quality thesis and **adds** a flow-fragility overlay that belongs in position sizing, not in the fundamental view [skill: holder-flow-and-crowding, Stage 7].
