### (c) Kill signals (dated thresholds)

1. **~2026-10-15 (3Q26 print):** FY26 revenue-growth guide below "slightly above 40%," or Q3 actual GM <65% (guided low end) — first crack in the sandbag regime → downgrade communication prior immediately [S1][S17].
2. **By the Nov-2026 board meeting:** quarterly dividend declared below NT$7.00, or 2026 cash received falls short of the guided ≥NT$23 — breaks the stated "sustainable and steadily increasing" policy [S7][S9].
3. **Jan-2027 (4Q26 call):** FY27 capex guided >US$75B *with* GM guided <60% — boom-capex NPV test fails; the cancellation option is not being exercised into a softening tape [module: BOOM_CAPEX_NEGATIVE_NPV_TEST.md].
4. **Any date:** credible reporting that Washington seeks equity/warrants in TSMC (Intel-precedent contagion) or converts the $265B into contracted obligations; or Taipei blocks/unblocks N-2 in a way that forces uneconomic re-siting — reprice the control stack [evid-04 §§3,5].
5. **Any quarter:** non-operating items ex-interest >10% of pre-tax income for two consecutive quarters; A/R days >35 or inventory days >100 without a node-ramp explanation [evid-06 baselines: 29d / 87d].
6. **Flow:** NDF sell-down >0.5% of shares within 6 months (state monetization signal); ADS premium sustained >12% or negative; EEM weight >17% (evid-04 §5; evid-05 §6 baselines).
7. **BIS probe:** settlement announced materially above the ~$1B reported frame, or with license conditions binding advanced-node sales [evid-04 §2].

### (d) Handoffs

- **→ Valuation section:** use clean FY26E EPS (≈NT$98 / ≈$15.5 per ADS); net cash usable at US$66–75B after Tier-2 governance haircut; peer-margin comparisons on depreciation-normalized GM (+4–5pp); carry the $25–60B net NPV drag of the US program and the un-accrued ~$1B BIS contingency as line items.
- **→ Geopolitics/tail section (C-series):** claim-quality ledger (EM_SOVEREIGN two-ledger split), N-2 statute text verification, ADS-premium behavior as the tail-pricing gauge.
- **→ Thesis-legs-and-redteam:** alignment leg graded 8/10 with the sovereign-tail carve-out; sandbag-prior makes the bear case obligated to beat guidance floors, not consensus.
- **→ Catalyst calendar:** ~2026-10-15 3Q26 print; Nov-2026 dividend declaration; Jan-2027 FY27 capex guide; Apr-2027 20-F (comp note, grant accounting, contract liabilities, VIS accounting treatment — four open verification items flagged above).
- **→ QoE monitor (thesis-drift):** A/R days, inventory days, non-op ex-interest share of pre-tax, contract-liability balance once pulled.

## Section II.5 — The Wrapper, the Tail, and the Policy Stack: What a TSM ADS Actually Owns

**Date: 2026-07-17. Reference price: TSM ADS $409.74 (NYSE close 2026-07-16, print day). 2330.TW NT$2,470 (TWSE close 2026-07-16, pre-print).**
Method: [skill: foreign-and-cross-border-claim-enforceability], composed with [skill: policy-cliff-and-regulatory-duration] and [skill: regulatory-franchise-and-local-market-power]. Every factual claim carries a source and date; probabilities and unpublished mechanics are flagged **[agent-inferred]**.

Acronyms used: ADS/ADR = American Depositary Share/Receipt; NDF = National Development Fund (Taiwan Executive Yuan); TWSE = Taiwan Stock Exchange; CBC = Central Bank of the Republic of China (Taiwan); BIS = U.S. Bureau of Industry and Security; EAR = Export Administration Regulations; OFAC = Office of Foreign Assets Control; SDN = Specially Designated National; FINI = Foreign Institutional Investor registration (Taiwan); ART = U.S.–Taiwan Agreement on Reciprocal Trade; SME = semiconductor manufacturing equipment; VEU = Validated End-User; MOEA = Taiwan Ministry of Economic Affairs; LY = Legislative Yuan; GM/OM = gross/operating margin; COE = cost of equity; LGE = loss-given-event; EM = emerging markets; FPI = foreign private issuer.

---

### 1. Instrument wrapper card — what the security actually is

[skill: foreign-and-cross-border-claim-enforceability / module: INSTRUMENT_WRAPPER_TAXONOMY.md]

**Exact instrument.** One NYSE-listed TSM ADS = a receipt for **5 common shares** of Taiwan Semiconductor Manufacturing Company Limited, a Republic of China (Taiwan)-incorporated issuer (TWSE 2330; SEC CIK 0001046179, foreign private issuer). The ADS program is **sponsored**; the depositary is **Citibank, N.A.** As of 2026-02-28, **1,062,690,167 ADSs** representing 5,313,450,863 common shares — **20.5% of the 25,932,524,521 shares outstanding** — were registered to the depositary's nominee, held of record by Cede & Co. plus 173 registered holders (TSMC FY2025 Form 20-F, filed 2026-04-16).

**Chain of intermediaries:** TSMC (Hsinchu) → Taiwan local custodian/TDCC settlement → Citibank N.A. as depositary → DTC → prime broker → holder. Six links; each can fail independently [module: INSTRUMENT_WRAPPER_TAXONOMY.md — "each link can fail... sanctions can hit any single link"].

**Conversion mechanics.** ADSs are cancellable into 2330 ordinary shares (depositary fee, and the holder needs Taiwan market access — practically, FINI registration and a local custodial account **[agent-inferred; standard Taiwan plumbing, verify against the deposit agreement/F-6]**). Creation of *new* ADSs from ordinary shares is the constrained direction: Taiwan regulation has historically limited new ADS issuance to roughly the quantum of prior cancellations absent fresh regulatory approval, so the ADS float grows mainly via approved offerings and NDF-type sales — the 20-F notes the NDF "has from time to time in the past" sold TSMC shares *as ADSs* (20-F FY2025). **[Creation-cap mechanism agent-inferred; direction of asymmetry corroborated by the persistent premium.]**

**The ~+7% ADS premium.** On 2026-07-16 closes: ordinary-share value per ADS = 5 × NT$2,470 ÷ 32.213 = **$383.38**; ADS closed **$409.74** → premium ≈ **+6.9%** (derived; timing caveat: the TWSE close preceded the U.S. print-day −2.3% session; the same-day-information figure using the 7/15 ADS close is ≈ +9.4%) (evidence file 05, 2026-07-16). The premium is the market-cap gap: ADR-implied cap ≈ $2.12T vs local-listing cap ≈ $1.98–1.99T. Why it exists and persists: (i) **one-way arbitrage** — cancelling ADSs into ords kills a *discount*, but closing a *premium* requires creating ADSs, the capped direction **[agent-inferred]**; (ii) **foreign-ownership plumbing** — U.S. accounts avoid FINI registration, TWD settlement, and the 10% TWSE daily price limit by paying up for the wrapper; (iii) **index/mandate demand** — the ADS is the only U.S.-listed access, with a deep options market, while TSM is excluded from the S&P 500 (foreign domicile; structural fact, evidence file 05 §6) and is simultaneously the #1 EM-index weight (15.18% of the leading EM ETF; >14% of MSCI EM; 57% of Taiwan's index weight — stockanalysis.com 2026-07-16; Lazard/Callan, Mar 2026).

**Diagnostic reading of the premium.** Per the sign table in [module: COUNTRY_DISCOUNT_DISAGGREGATION.md], a foreign wrapper trading *wider* (cheaper) than the local share signals wrapper-specific actuarial impairment. TSM's wrapper trades **rich**, not wide. The claim path is currently unimpaired; the "Taiwan discount" therefore lives in the **business-value term** (the fabs are in the potential war zone), not in the instrument term. This is the structural inverse of the Russia-2022 pattern, and it drives everything in §2–§3.

**Wrapper failure modes (sponsored ADR class)** [module: INSTRUMENT_WRAPPER_TAXONOMY.md]: program termination with a cancellation window; sanctions on a chain link; local-exchange halt leaving the ADS trading with no reference price; cancellation friction (fees, withholding, FX, delay). Taiwan-specific instantiation in §2.

### 2. Claim-enforceability cascade — current and stressed

[skill: foreign-and-cross-border-claim-enforceability / modules: CLAIM_ENFORCEABILITY_CASCADE.md, REALIZABLE_RETURN_MULTIPLIER_FORMULA.md, SANCTIONS_FROZEN_CLAIM_AND_ADR_ORD_CONVERSION_CLOCK.md]

Realizable Return = Business Value × Claim Enforceability × Convertibility × Time Access.

#### Current regime (mid-2026)

| Factor | Estimate | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| Claim enforceability | **0.97** | Taiwan is a strong rule-of-law jurisdiction for corporate claims; the IP & Commercial Court actively enforces (2nm trade-secret convictions 2026-04-27, Al Jazeera; TSMC's civil suit vs a departed executive accepted 2025-11-25, CNBC). Board 7/10 independent (20-F FY2025). No minority cram-down precedent surfaced. |
| Convertibility | **0.95** | Dividends flow reliably in cash: holders received **NT$18/share in 2025** (NT$90/ADS), NT$14 in 2024; quarterly rate raised NT$4→NT$7 (1Q26 dividend payable 2026-10-08); management guided "at least TWD23 per share in 2026" (TSMC 4Q25 call 2026-01-15; IR dividends page 2026-07-16). No capital controls on this channel. Haircuts are ordinary: non-resident dividend withholding (~21% **[agent-inferred; verify treaty status]**), FX conversion at a managed TWD. |
| Time access | **0.98** | ADS is among the most liquid securities in the world (~15.4M ADS/day 20-day average ≈ $6B+/day, stockanalysis 2026-07-16). Program active; no suspension precedent for this depositary/issuer. |
| **Product (wrapper multiplier)** | **≈ 0.90–0.92** | The ~8–10% standing haircut is mostly withholding + FX regime + the residual plumbing risk, and is largely offset in practice by the ADS premium a seller receives. |

#### Stressed regimes — walking the conversion clock honestly

The Russia-2022 playbook (broker internal no-trade directives within hours; depositary conversion windows of 30–90 days; forced liquidation at 5–15% of fair value; OTC third-country exits that themselves risk sanctions violations) is the template in [module: SANCTIONS_FROZEN_CLAIM_AND_ADR_ORD_CONVERSION_CLOCK.md] — but Taiwan inverts its key mechanism. In Russia, the *investor's own government* sanctioned the issuer, making the wrapper illegal to hold: legal ownability, broker tradability, and dividend access all collapsed from the U.S. side. In a Taiwan conflict, **the U.S. would sanction China, not Taiwan**; the ADS remains legal to own and trade. What breaks is the *underlying* — Taiwan-side settlement, FX, and the fabs themselves. Consequences by scenario:

**(i) Blockade / quarantine (the PLA-exercised scenario: "Justice Mission 2025" blockade simulation 2025-12-29/30, sixth large-scale exercise since Aug 2022, The Diplomat 2026-01; Taiwan's own 2026-06-25 tabletop simulated a maritime quarantine, AEI 2026-07-02).**
Sequence of freezes **[agent-inferred sequencing, anchored to precedent]**: (1) TWSE hits repeated 10% daily limit-downs (precedent: 2330 limit-down −10% on 2025-04-07 on a mere tariff shock, Bloomberg) and may halt; (2) CBC intervenes in FX, then plausibly queues or restricts foreign repatriation to defend TWD (Taiwan ran a restrictive FX regime pre-liberalization; the May 2025 7%-in-two-sessions TWD move, CNBC 2025-05-06, shows the regime is managed, not free) — convertibility falls first toward 0.3–0.5; (3) with local settlement impaired, the depositary suspends *issuance and cancellation* — mechanical, not legal — and the ADS becomes a **closed-end claim trading in New York**: time access for the ADS holder stays near 1.0 in the narrow sense (you can sell), but at a price discovering the tail; (4) NT$ dividends fail at the remittance step. Note the inversion of Russia: **the ADS is the best-functioning venue in this scenario, not the first casualty.** Converting ADS→ORD mid-blockade is value-destructive: you would pay fees to swap a tradeable NY claim for a locally trapped one — the exact opposite of the Russia calculus where conversion defended against depositary forced liquidation [module: SANCTIONS_FROZEN_CLAIM_AND_ADR_ORD_CONVERSION_CLOCK.md, Operator 2]. Fabs idle (materials resupply — specialty gases, chemicals, spares — interrupted), but capital stock intact; net cash ≈ NT$2,654B ≈ **US$83B** (2Q26 balance sheet, 6-K 2026-07-16) funds a long siege. Time-value math from the module: at a 15% hurdle, a 3-year interruption destroys ~34% of PV before any fundamental damage. Stressed cascade: Business 0.75 × Enforceability 0.9 × Convertibility 0.4 × Time-access 0.8 ≈ **0.22 of pre-event value** as the trading range floor during the event, recovering on resolution.

**(ii) Invasion / occupation.**
Business value collapses with the asset: leading-edge fabs are non-operable without continuous vendor support (EUV lithography service, materials, and TSMC's own engineers), and are widely assessed as likely to be disabled rather than captured intact **[agent-inferred; consistent with the "seizure yields nothing" logic — the aggressor cannot run the machine without the operator and the global supply chain]**. The ADS holder's residual claim: (a) offshore subsidiaries — Arizona (target ~30% of 2nm-and-below capacity once scaled; $265B committed, US Commerce/NIST 2026-07-16), Japan (JASM), Dresden — held *through the Taiwan parent*, whose control seat is in the war zone; (b) ~US$83B net cash, largely in marketable securities of unknown situs mix **[agent-inferred]**. If a PRC-controlled successor administration asserted control of the parent, U.S. sanctions on that successor would then apply Russia-style mechanics to any surviving claim, while U.S. courts/OFAC would more plausibly freeze or vest the U.S. assets protectively for legitimate owners than hand them over **[agent-inferred; WWII vesting precedent]**. Honest stressed cascade: Business 0.10–0.20 × Enforceability 0.5 × Convertibility 0.5–0.8 × Time-access 0.6–0.75 ≈ **0.02–0.06**. This is an absorbing state, not a drawdown.

**(iii) TWD convertibility stress without conflict.**
The lightest scenario: a disorderly TWD move (May 2025 precedent) plus informal CBC moral suasion on outflows. TSMC's own guidance quantifies the earnings channel: **1% TWD appreciation ≈ −40bps GM** and ≈ −1% NT$ revenue (CFO, 2Q25 call 2025-07-17); the 2Q25→3Q25 appreciation cost ~180–260bps of GM. This is margin translation, not claim impairment — cascade multiplier ~0.95, absorbed in the FX/COE bucket of §3.

**Five-layer split** [module: BUSINESS_QUALITY_VS_CLAIM_QUALITY_FIVE_LAYER_SPLIT.md]: L1 business 0.95 (72.3% foundry share, TrendForce 2026-06-12; GM 67.7%, 6-K 2026-07-16); L2 balance sheet 0.99 (net cash $83B); **L3 jurisdiction 0.75** (the war tail); L4 governance 0.92 (dividend record; NDF benign to date); L5 instrument 0.95 (premium wrapper). Product ≈ **0.60**. The weakest layer is jurisdiction, and — the defining feature of this name — **L1 and L3 are correlated ≈ 1 in the tail** because the business *is* the jurisdiction's crown jewel, physically. Refuse to net: the superb L1 does not repair L3.

### 3. Disaggregating the "Taiwan discount" — and the implied probability math

[skill: foreign-and-cross-border-claim-enforceability / modules: COUNTRY_DISCOUNT_DISAGGREGATION.md, NOMINAL_VS_REAL_COE_NORMALIZATION.md; market-implied-probability method from CCP_INSTITUTIONAL_THREAT_MAP_AND_PRICED_DISCOUNT.md, Operator 2, method only]

**The observed gap.** On a single consistent source (stockanalysis.com statistics pages, 2026-07-16/17): TSM forward P/E (NTM) **20.0×** vs NVDA **20.8×**, AVGO **23.8×**, ASML **33.4×** — despite TSMC's arguably superior competitive position (72.3% foundry share and >90% of CoWoS advanced-packaging output, TrendForce/BigGo 2026; every major merchant and hyperscaler AI accelerator except Huawei's (and the reported Maia 2, on Intel 18A-P) is fabbed at TSMC, evidence file 03 §5). Against a "claim-clean counterfactual multiple" of ~24–25× **[agent-inferred: between NVDA's quality premium and AVGO, below ASML]**, the gap is **~17–20%**. Against sell-side consensus fair value ($516.76 average / $513 median price target, 19 analysts, S&P Global via stockanalysis, 2026-07-16), the gap at $409.74 is **20.7%**.

**Step 1 — reverse-engineer the composite implied probability** (method: `P = (V_base − Price)/(V_base − V_event)`, applied over the 12-month price-target horizon):

- V_base = $517; Price = $409.74; gap = $107.26 (20.7% of V_base).
- If the *entire* gap were war-tail and LGE = 90% (V_event = $51.70): P = 107.26 / 465.30 = **23.1%/yr**.
- LGE = 80% (V_event = $103.40): P = 107.26 / 413.60 = **25.9%/yr**.
- LGE = 60% (blockade-weighted; V_event = $206.80): P = 107.26 / 310.20 = **34.6%/yr**.

Published expert and prediction-market base rates for cross-strait conflict cluster far lower: roughly **1–2%/yr for full invasion and ~2–5%/yr for blockade/quarantine-class coercion** **[agent-inferred, order-of-magnitude synthesis of public forecasting-platform and expert-survey figures; no single citable consensus number surfaced in the evidence pack — the pack itself notes no major-bank named %-discount estimate was found (evidence file 04 §4)]**. A 23–35%/yr implied event probability is 5–15× those base rates — which proves the gap **cannot be predominantly war-tail**; the residual must sit in the other buckets.

**Step 2 — the perpetuity check (the more honest frame).** A persistent annualized expected loss *e* = Σ pᵢ×LGEᵢ warrants a multiple discount d = e/(r−g+e). Inverting: the observed d ≈ 17–20% at (r−g) ≈ 5% implies the market is pricing **e ≈ (r−g)·d/(1−d) ≈ 1.0–1.25%/yr** of expected geopolitical loss. Compare the actuarial requirement from base rates: e = (1.5% invasion × 85% LGE) + (3% blockade × 35% LGE) ≈ **2.3%/yr**, warranting d ≈ 2.3/7.3 ≈ **31%**; even a dovish blend (1% × 85% + 2% × 30%) gives e ≈ 1.45%/yr, d ≈ 22%. **Conclusion: at $409.74 the market prices the *low* end of expert base rates and near-zero weight on blockade partial-loss scenarios — the "Taiwan discount" is thin relative to the tail, not fat.** All math shown; all probabilities [agent-inferred].

**Bucket allocation of the ~17–21% gap** (ranges; buckets overlap and cannot be identified exactly):

| Bucket | Share of gap | Stigma or actuarial? | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| (a) Genuine actuarial war tail | **~10–15pp** | Actuarial | §2 scenarios; PLA exercise cadence (6 large-scale since Aug 2022); the ±2–4 P/E-turn scenario framing in public commentary (Investing.com analysis, 2026) |
| (b) Stigma / mandate exclusion | **~2–4pp** | Stigma (residual alpha) | A prominent U.S. conglomerate investor round-tripped a ~$4.1B ADS stake in 2022–23 explicitly citing geopolitics (Reuters 2023-04-11) — mandate/sleep-at-night selling exists; but with the stock ~14% off an all-time high ($477.57 close 2026-06-30, macrotrends) and 186 hedge funds long (Q4-25 13F count, Insider Monkey), the stigma bucket is currently small |
| (c) FX / COE normalization | **~1–3pp** | Actuarial | TWD −9.5% y/y (2026-07-16); −40bps GM per 1% TWD appreciation (2Q25 call); revenue is USD-denominated (78% North America, 2Q26 6-K) so this is translation, not claim risk [module: NOMINAL_VS_REAL_COE_NORMALIZATION.md] |
| (d) EM-index plumbing | **~1–3pp** | Structural, currently a *tailwind* | Not in S&P 500; #1 in EM benchmarks (15.18% of the leading EM ETF); EM ETF flows +$38B in H1-2026, best first half on record (SSGA 2026-06-30) — the plumbing discount is real (no S&P passive bid; EM-fund single-name caps **[agent-inferred]**) but partially offset now |
| (e) Cyclical margin skepticism | **~3–5pp** | Actuarial | GM 67.7% vs management's own through-cycle floor "56% and higher" (4Q25 call 2026-01-15); overseas-fab dilution guided 2–3pp early → 3–4pp late (4Q25/2Q26 calls); N2 ramp dilution 2–3pp FY26; depreciation +high-teens% 2026; the stock fell −2.3% on a record beat-and-raise (2026-07-16) — the market is rationally refusing to capitalize peak margins |

Cross-check that governance is *not* the issue: local minorities (2330) trade at the same underlying multiple — indeed the foreign wrapper trades at a premium — which per the module's sign table exonerates the governance and instrument buckets and localizes the discount in jurisdiction + cycle.

### 4. Sovereign and deterrence analysis — the silicon shield in both directions, and the N-2 statute as a two-sided moat

[skill: foreign-and-cross-border-claim-enforceability / modules: SOVEREIGN_SELF_INTEREST_AND_DETERRENCE_PROTECTS_MINORITY.md, STATE_AS_TWO_SIDED_MOAT.md; skill: regulatory-franchise-and-local-market-power]

**Deterrence stack, scored:**

- **Crown-jewel alignment — strong.** TSMC is 72.3% of global foundry revenue (TrendForce 2026-06-12), ~57% of Taiwan's equity index weight, and the anchor of Taiwan's alliance value. Impairing foreign minorities would destroy Taiwan's single most effective claim on U.S. protection. Taiwan's incentives run *through* foreign shareholders, not against them.
- **MAD / deterrence via foreign exposure — strong but two-edged.** For **China**: seizure yields nothing (fabs die without ASML service, global materials, and TSMC staff **[agent-inferred]**), and a TSMC outage maims China's own electronics supply chain. For the **U.S.**: dependence on Taiwan-sited leading edge is precisely what Washington is spending $265B of TSMC's capital to reduce.
- **Network lock-in — strong today, explicitly decaying.** This is the "de-risking makes Taiwan more expendable" vector: Arizona Fab 1 in HVM (4Q24, yields "almost equal to Taiwan," CEO 2026-01-15), Fab 2 (N3) pulled to 2H27, Fab 3 (2nm/A16-class) reported pulled to 2027 (TrendForce 2025-09-30), first U.S. advanced-packaging fab permitting (4Q25 call), target ~30% of 2nm-and-below capacity in Arizona once scaled, 12 U.S. facilities on the $265B trajectory (NIST 2026-07-16). Public debate already frames the buildout as eroding Taiwan's leverage ("silicon statecraft," The Diplomat 2025-07). The module's warning applies verbatim: network lock-in has "a time horizon, not permanent validity" once decoupling is underway. **[Agent-inferred: the shield decays on a ~5–8 year half-life at the announced buildout pace; the tail probability is therefore not stationary — it likely *rises* into the 2030s even as tariff-policy risk falls.]**
- **Bilateral hostage — strong.** Foreign institutions own ~73–75% of 2330 (TWSE data via commentary, early 2025 — softer sourcing, flagged); the NDF (6.38%, largest holder, one board seat via director Chun-Hsien Yeh, 20-F FY2025) has historically *sold into* the ADS market — the state monetizes the foreign bid; Taiwan actively courts foreign capital. Pre-event, this deters mistreatment of minorities; the module is explicit that it evaporates *post*-event.

**The N-2 statute as a two-sided state moat.** The rulebook, precisely [skill: regulatory-franchise-and-local-market-power — "name the rulemaker and rule-change mechanism"]: Article 22 of the Statute for Industrial Innovation, amended by the Legislative Yuan Nov–Dec 2025, in force from end-2025: technology deployed abroad must be **at least two generations (N-2) behind Taiwan's leading edge**, with outbound investments requiring MOEA/Investment-Commission approval (TechSpot 2025; Tom's Hardware 2025-12; Taiwan News). Reported consequence: N2 and A16 stay Taiwan-only for now; overseas fabs limited to 3nm-and-older until Taiwan advances (press formulations differ — the evidence file flags that statutory text should be checked before modeling Arizona 2nm timing).

- **Moat side:** the statute *guarantees* that the scarcest asset on earth — leading-edge logic capacity — remains concentrated where TSMC's cost base, R&D flywheel, and pricing power live. It also hard-blocks the nightmare competitive scenario (leading-edge process technology escaping Taiwan's jurisdiction to a competitor's benefit). For minority holders, it protects the franchise economics: Taiwan-fab wafers carry no ~25–30% Arizona price/cost premium (financialcontent 2026-04-15; TechInsights 2025: true Arizona cost ~+10%).
- **Trap side:** the same statute **caps TSMC's ability to de-risk its own tail**. It legally pins ≥2 generations of the company's value in the war zone, keeps LGE-given-invasion near maximum, and creates a live policy cliff in *both* directions (§5f): tightening slows Arizona 2nm and strains the ART quota bargain; loosening accelerates the shield's erosion. Note the arithmetic tension already visible: Fab 3 Arizona targets 2nm/A16 from ~2027, but under N-2 that requires Taiwan to be ramping A14 (scheduled 2028, TSMC PR 2025-04) — either the timeline slips, the generation-counting is looser than press suggests, or the statute moves. That unresolved seam is a dated, monitorable cliff.
- **State-role decomposition** [module: STATE_AS_TWO_SIDED_MOAT.md]: state as *regulator* (N-2, outbound-approval) — moat now, cliff later; as *controller-adjacent holder* (NDF 6.38% + board seat) — benign, dividend-aligned, and a seller into strength; as *currency manager* (CBC) — the convertibility factor in §2; as *geopolitical actor* — the entire tail. **Hierarchy question answered honestly:** in stress, Taiwan protects (1) national survival and the war effort, (2) the fabs and their engineers, (3) domestic depositors/employment, (4) domestic holders, and only then (5) foreign minorities — expect output requisition, FX restrictions, and dividend suspension long before anyone worries about the ADS holder [module: STATE_AS_TWO_SIDED_MOAT.md protection hierarchy]. But uniquely, pre-war the state's *deterrence strategy itself* requires happy foreign shareholders, which is about as good as the sovereign-incentive map ever gets for a minority.

### 5. Policy-cliff tree, dated

[skill: policy-cliff-and-regulatory-duration — expire/extend/replace/bridge cases; all probabilities **[agent-inferred]**]

**(a) Section 232 tariff regime.** In force: proclamation of 2026-01-14, effective 2026-01-15 — **25% ad valorem on advanced computing chips/derivatives** meeting performance thresholds (bracketing H200/MI325X-class), with broad end-use exemptions (U.S. data centers, R&D, consumer, public sector) (White House 2026-01-14; White & Case Jan 2026). TSMC's shield: the U.S.–Taiwan framework (announced 2026-01-15, ART signed 2026-02-12) grants duty-free import quotas of **2.5× planned U.S. capacity during construction and 1.5× after completion** for Taiwanese producers building in the U.S. (USTR fact sheet 2026-02; 20-F FY2025, which still calls ultimate Section 232 scope "unclear"). Dated triggers: 90-day negotiation update ~2026-04-14 (passed; broader semis/SME tariffs remain expressly reserved); **Commerce data-center market report due 2026-07-01** — a named modification trigger whose outcome is not yet public as of this writing → *immediate monitoring item*. Cases: extend as-is **55%**; broaden coverage with TSMC quotas intact **25%**; quota renegotiated adversely (e.g., a revived "1:1" matching rule — reported 2025-09-26, never issued) **15%**; repeal **5%**. Exposure: direct tariff cost ≈ nil while quotas hold (the 2Q26 print showed zero tariff drag: revenue $40.20B, top of guide, 6-K 2026-07-16); the real exposure is second-order demand/pricing on the ~66%-of-revenue HPC platform and the precedent that quota generosity is contingent on ever-larger U.S. investment (§5d).
**(b) U.S.–Taiwan ART durability.** Signed 2026-02-12: U.S. tariff on Taiwan goods = higher of MFN or 15%; Taiwan commits ≥$250B U.S. investment + $250B credit guarantees; certain elements implemented by Federal Register notice 2026-05-28; **entry into force pending Taiwan legislative review** (USTR 2026-02; Focus Taiwan 2026-02-14; CFR). Cliffs: LY ratification (unratified = executable-goodwill, not law); U.S. political cycle (the agreement is executive-branch; IEEPA-adjacent instruments have faced litigation **[agent-inferred]**). Cases: ratified and stable **60%**; ratified with renegotiated side-terms **20%**; stalls but administered informally **15%**; collapse **5%**.
**(c) Export controls.** Binding stack: Oct 2022/2023 rules; Jan 2025 foundry due-diligence rule (compliance 2025-01-31, Morgan Lewis 2026-01); AI Diffusion Rule issued 2025-01-15 then **rescinded 2025-05-13** and replaced with guidance incl. GP10 (use of Huawei Ascend-class chips anywhere violates the EAR) (BIS 2025-05-13); BIS final rule 2026-01-15 moving H200/MI325X-class China exports to **case-by-case** with U.S. third-party testing — which pulls the chips through the 25% tariff, a de facto revenue share (Morgan Lewis 2026-01) — after which **China blocked H200 imports at customs** (semiconductorsinsight 2026). TSMC-specific: since **2024-11-11** TSMC stopped supplying 7nm-and-below AI/GPU dies to mainland AI-chip designers at Commerce's direction (Tom's Hardware 2024-11); no lift found as of mid-2026; VEU authorizations for the Nanjing fab expired **2025-12-31** — annual licensing now, an every-December cliff (introl 2026). Cases: status quo **60%**; further transactional loosening **20%**; re-tightening **20%**. Exposure: China = **6% of 2Q26 revenue** (6-K 2026-07-16), mostly mature nodes; the advanced-AI China revenue is already ~zero — the cliff here is *upside* optionality, not downside.
**(d) CHIPS grant/equity politics.** Contracted: up to **$6.6B direct + $5B loans** (Nov 2024, NIST), **$1.5B received** Q4 2024, milestone-based; parent guarantee is "irrevocable, unconditional" (20-F FY2025). Precedent shock: Intel's remaining grants converted into a **~10% government equity stake** (~$8.9B) Aug 2025 (CNBC 2025-08-19); Washington explored the same for TSMC and then exempted it because TSMC had already raised its U.S. commitment ($165B then) (CNBC 2025-08-20; TrendForce 2025-08-22). Read through the recurrent-extra-legal-levy lens [method analog: CCP module Operator 3]: the **+$100B announced 2026-07-16** (total $265B) is the tribute that keeps the equity demand off the table — politically calibrated capital deployment, a recurring implicit tax not in any model line. Cases: no equity demand while investment escalates **70%**; equity/royalty demand revived **15%** (trigger: perceived under-delivery or political shift); grant clawback friction **10%**; improved terms **5%**. 20-F caveat stands: no assurance remaining incentives arrive "at the levels we anticipate or at all."
**(e) The unresolved BIS matter.** Reuters 2025-04-08: potential penalty of **US$1B or more** re ~3M dies that reached a restricted entity via a third-party designer (TSMC self-reported Oct 2024; it has not supplied the restricted end-user since Sept 2020; the intermediary was Entity-Listed 2025-01-16). No settlement, charge, or fine as of the 20-F (filed 2026-04-16). Scale: $1B ≈ 0.8% of FY25 revenue (US$122.42B) ≈ ~1.8% of FY25 net income — financially immaterial; the tail is **conduct terms** (compliance monitorship, licensing conditions on the ~19%-of-revenue largest customer relationship **[agent-inferred linkage]**). Cases: cash settlement ≤$1.5B **55%**; settlement with conduct conditions **25%**; no action **15%**; escalation touching export privileges **5%**.
**(f) Taiwan N-2 amendments.** In force end-2025 (§4). Cases: status quo **60%** (Arizona 2nm gated on Taiwan's A14 ramp ~2028); loosened toward N-1 under U.S. trade pressure **25%** (watch ART implementation politics and any MOEA "times have changed" signaling — precedent: the 2025 loosening of the old N-1 practice, Data Center Dynamics 2025); tightened/strictly enforced **15%** (slips Arizona Fab 3's 2nm/A16 target from ~2027, embarrasses the $265B narrative, invites §5a quota retaliation). Exposure: timing of ~30% of future leading-edge capacity; the margin effect is second-order (overseas dilution already guided at 2–4pp).

**Joint read:** the policy stack is currently in an unusually benign equilibrium — tariff-exempt via quotas, equity demand bought off, export-control losses already absorbed, trade deal signed — but every leg is **executive-discretionary and renewal-shaped** rather than statutory-permanent. Policy duration, not policy level, is the risk. The stack's common failure mode is a U.S. political demand spiral in which exemption terms ratchet with each cycle (the 2025 exemption → +$100B 2026 pattern is one full turn of that ratchet, observed).

### 6. Verdict

**Verdict: INVESTABLE-WITH-HAIRCUT** (per the skill's four-state contract). Not "investable-clean": the tail is absorbing and non-diversifiable within the position. Not "tail-dominated": the current-regime wrapper multiplier is ~0.90+, the dividend proves the cash path, the deterrence stack is intact, and the tail probability × LGE (≈1.5–2.5%/yr expected loss) is a haircut-sized number, not an option-sized one. The crown-jewel-collapse *entry* operator does not apply — the stock is ~14% off its all-time high, not in a fear collapse; you are being paid a thin, roughly actuarial premium, not a stigma premium.

**jurisdiction_validity_gate: PASS** (evaluated explicitly per the scoring surface): ADR program active (no F-6 termination activity); no sanctions on issuer, depositary, or any custody link; no capital controls on dividends/proceeds (NT$18/share remitted 2025); TWSE functioning normally. A failed gate — any §8-class wrapper event — caps any bullish call regardless of business quality.

**Realizable-return multiplier** [module: REALIZABLE_RETURN_MULTIPLIER_FORMULA.md]: **current ≈ 0.90–0.92**; stressed: blockade ≈ **0.22** (event-period), invasion ≈ **0.02–0.06** (absorbing). The valuation section should apply the current multiplier to base/bull cases and must NOT average the stressed states into the base case — they belong in a separate scenario line (module anti-pattern).

**Pre-committed kill triggers** (≥1 wrapper-event, ≥1 controller-incentive, per Stage 8):
- **Wrapper-event kill:** CBC imposes any formal restriction or queuing on foreign-investor repatriation, or TWSE suspends trading >3 consecutive sessions, or the depositary suspends ADS issuance/cancellation — exit/resize immediately, do not wait for clarification.
- **Wrapper-event kill (kinetic):** China Coast Guard/PLA moves from exercising to *enforcing* — any boarding, inspection, or turn-back of commercial shipping bound for Taiwan sustained >72 hours (baseline: continuous CCG presence in Taiwan's eastern EEZ since 2026-06-01 and Pratas/Kinmen intrusions are presence, not enforcement — AEI 2026-07-02).
- **Controller-incentive kill:** Taiwan tightens N-2 further or the Investment Commission denies/slow-walks an Arizona leading-edge approval **while** the NDF accelerates ADS-format stake sales — the state restricting shareholders' de-risking while monetizing its own is an incentive inversion.
- **Controller-incentive kill (U.S. side):** Washington revives an equity/royalty demand on TSMC's CHIPS package or conditions Section 232 quotas on it — converts the implicit levy into explicit expropriation-by-installments.

#### (a) Key judgments table

| # | Judgment | Confidence | Falsifier |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | The ADS wrapper is currently a *premium* claim (+~7%), not an impaired one; the Taiwan discount sits in business value, not the instrument | High | ADS premium inverts to a persistent discount vs 2330 (>5% for >1 month) — wrapper-specific impairment emerging |
| 2 | At $409.74 the market prices ≈1.0–1.25%/yr of expected geopolitical loss — the low end of expert base rates; the discount is thin-to-fair, not fat — no exploitable edge in either direction; there is no stigma-alpha to harvest | Medium | Demonstration that consensus $517 already embeds a full war haircut (then the market's implied e is higher than computed); or credible base-rate revisions below 1%/yr |
| 3 | In blockade, the ADS keeps trading and NY becomes the price-discovery venue; conversion to ORDs is value-destructive (inverse of Russia 2022) | Medium-High | U.S. policy response that restricts *Taiwan*-linked securities (contradicts the sanctions-asymmetry premise) |
| 4 | Invasion is an absorbing state for the ADS (recovery 2–6%), regardless of TSMC's offshore assets, because the claim runs through the Taiwan parent | High | Legal restructuring that ring-fences U.S./Japan/EU subsidiaries under a non-Taiwan holding entity (none disclosed; would be a major bullish wrapper event) |
| 5 | N-2 is a two-sided moat: it protects franchise economics now and caps tail de-risking permanently; Arizona-2nm timing vs the statute is an unresolved, dated seam (~2027 fab vs ~2028 A14) | High | Statutory text/implementing rules permitting 2nm abroad earlier; or A14 pull-in |
| 6 | The policy stack is benign but renewal-shaped; the binding constraint is the ratchet (exemptions repriced each cycle in required U.S. investment) | Medium-High | A statutory (not executive) safe harbor for Taiwanese producers; or two consecutive cycles with no new investment demanded |
| 7 | Deterrence is intact but decaying on a ~5–8yr half-life as U.S. capacity scales; tail probability is non-stationary and likely rises into the 2030s | Low-Medium | Evidence that U.S. buildout *increases* U.S. commitment to Taiwan's defense (deeper formal security guarantees alongside fab investment) |

#### (b) Asset cards (compact)

**INSTRUMENT_WRAPPER_CARD** — Sponsored ADR; 1 ADS = 5 ords; depositary Citibank N.A.; ADSs = 20.5% of shares o/s (20-F, 2026-02-28); governing law of deposit agreement NY **[verify F-6]**; conversion: cancel→ORD open (fee + FINI friction), create→ADS capped **[agent-inferred]**; premium +6.9% (2026-07-16, derived); voting pass-through per deposit agreement; English SEC-grade disclosure (20-F/6-K); no suspension precedent.
**CLAIM_CASCADE_CARD** — Current: 0.97 × 0.95 × 0.98 ≈ **0.90**. Blockade: 0.9 × 0.4 × 0.8 on 0.75 business ≈ **0.22**. Invasion: ≈ **0.02–0.06**. Moving events: CBC controls, TWSE halt, depositary suspension, kinetic enforcement.
**COUNTRY_DISCOUNT_DECOMPOSITION_CARD** — Gap ~17–21% vs claim-clean value: war-tail 10–15pp (actuarial, thin vs base rates), stigma 2–4pp, FX/COE 1–3pp, index plumbing 1–3pp (flows currently a tailwind), margin skepticism 3–5pp (actuarial). Governance/instrument buckets ≈ 0 (wrapper trades at premium).
**CONTROLLER_STATE_INCENTIVE_MAP** — NDF 6.38%, board seat, historical seller via ADSs; Taiwan state = regulator (N-2), currency manager (CBC), geopolitical actor; pre-war incentives strongly minority-aligned (deterrence requires foreign shareholders); in-war hierarchy: survival > fabs > domestic > foreign minorities. U.S. = second controller-adjacent actor via quota/grant leverage; incentive: maximize onshore investment, ratchet observed (2025 exemption → +$100B 2026-07-16).
**ABSORBING_TAIL_SIZING_CARD** — Worst case is not −30%; it is a frozen/absorbing claim (blockade: trapped-but-trading at ~0.2×; invasion: 0.02–0.06×). Size so a 100%-loss-of-position is survivable at portfolio level; cap total Taiwan-jurisdiction exposure (TSM + supply-chain + EM-index look-through — TSMC alone is >14% of MSCI EM) as a *country-level* cap, not per-position ("RSXification" discipline). Time-value: 3-yr freeze at 15% hurdle destroys ~34% PV even with full recovery.
**REALIZABLE_RETURN_SCENARIO_CARD** — Base (consensus-anchored, NOT the house base case — the report's own valuation puts base at ≈+2.1%/yr over 3 years; wrapper holds, AI cycle holds): consensus $517 × 0.91 multiplier ≈ **$470 realizable**, +15% (12mo, ex-dividend ~0.7%). Friendly (margin persistence + N2 ramp beat): $590–600 × 0.91 ≈ **$540**, +32%. Cyclical bear (AI digestion, GM→56–58%): ~$330 × 0.91 ≈ **$300**, −27%. Governance bear (ratchet hardens: equity demand + N-2 tighten): ~$430 × 0.85 ≈ **$365**, −11%. Blockade: ≈ **$90–120** event-period mark, partial recovery on resolution. Invasion: ≈ **$10–25** absorbing. Probabilities [agent-inferred]: 45/20/25/5/3.5/1.5%.

#### (d) Handoffs

- **To the valuation section:** apply a **0.90 realizable-return multiplier** to base/bull equity values (wrapper + withholding + FX regime), and separately a **jurisdiction COE add-on of ~1.5–2.5pp** (the actuarial war-tail expectation) — do *not* also take a peer-multiple haircut on top, or the tail is double-counted. Do not "close the gap to NVDA/AVGO": on our math the 20.0× vs ~24× gap is approximately the actuarially fair price of the tail plus rational margin-cycle skepticism, not stigma to be harvested. Value margins off the company's own "56%+ through-cycle" floor, not the 67.7% print.
- **To the sizing discussion:** carry the **blockade scenario (≈0.22× event-period mark, indeterminate duration, ~2–5%/yr [agent-inferred])** as the sizing-binding tail — it is more probable than invasion and still absorbing-shaped (trapped time-value even with eventual recovery). Enforce a **country-level cap** on total Taiwan-jurisdiction exposure including index look-through; pre-commit the four kill triggers in §6 verbatim; treat mark-to-market during any strait event as uninvestable information (Schrodinger's-shares discipline).
- **To the catalyst calendar:** 2026-07-01 report fallout window; ART LY ratification; Dec-2026 Nanjing license cycle; BIS settlement; 4Q26/1Q27 statutory or symposium clarity on Arizona-2nm vs N-2.

## Section II.6 — Expectations Map, Driver Model, and the Return Profile

**TSMC (NYSE: TSM ADS; TWSE: 2330) — as of 2026-07-17, entry reference price US$409.74/ADS (close 2026-07-16)**

### 1. Expectations map — what $409.74 already believes

[skill: market-expectations-map / Stages 1–4; module: IMPLIED_EXPECTATIONS.md]

#### 1.1 Stage 1 — Extract consensus

| Item | Consensus | Source / as-of |
|---|---|---|
| FY2026 revenue | NT$5.21T (+36.75%) ≈ US$164–166B at FX 31.6–31.8 [agent-inferred conversion] | [E5 §3], 2026-07-16 |
| FY2026 EPS | US$15.91/ADS (one aggregator; +49.3%) / US$15.35/ADS (a second); NT$100.40/common (+51.6%) | [E5 §3] |
| FY2027 EPS | NT$128.80/common (+28.3%) ≈ US$19.99/ADS derived at 32.213 | [E5 §3, DERIVED] |
| FY2026 GM | 65.90% | [E5 §3] |
| Ratings / PT | 19 analysts: 12 Strong Buy / 6 Buy / 1 Hold; avg PT $516.76, median $513, range $354–700 (US$/ADS) | [E5 §3] |
| Post-print action | One post-print ADS PT raised to $600; pre-print local-share PTs raised to NT$3,400–3,800 by two large banks (names withheld per section rules) | [E5 §3] |
| Positioning | Short interest 0.57% of shares; put/call OI ratio 1.31 ("defensive tilt"); 30d implied vol ~57%, IV rank 79; spot below the $443 gamma-flip | [E5 §6] |

**Revision path Jan→Jul 2026 (the guidance-led cascade).** Company FY26 US$-revenue-growth guidance: "close to 30%" (2026-01-15) → "above 30%" (2026-04-16) → "slightly above 40%" (2026-07-16); capex US$52–56B → "high end" → US$60–64B [E1 §2]. AI-accelerator revenue CAGR guidance (2024–29): ~mid-40s% (Jan 2025) → mid-to-high-50s% (Jan 2026) [E3 §6]. Execution vs guidance: 2Q26 revenue US$40.20B hit the top of the $39.0–40.2B guide; GM 67.7% printed 20bp **above** the top of its 65.5–67.5% guide; OM 60.3% printed 180bp above the top of its guide [E1 §1–2]. Pre-print consensus expected NT$632.6B net profit; actual was NT$706.6B — an **+11.7% beat** — and the ADS still closed **−2.32%** [E5 §3, computed]. Per [skill: consensus-and-revisions / Stage 4], that is the classic serial-beater signature: the *whisper* bar sits far above published consensus, so published consensus is not the market's real expectation. Treat published numbers as a floor, not the bar.

#### 1.2 Stage 2 — Reverse-engineer the price (transparent reverse-DCF, US$/ADS)

[skill: market-expectations-map / module: IMPLIED_EXPECTATIONS.md — "Reverse-DCF Framework"]

Setup (all [agent-inferred]): value the ADS as PV(dividends 2026–2030) + PV(terminal value at YE2030), discount rate r = 10% (module prescribes 8–12% equity cost; beta 1.25 [E5 §1] argues high end, $15.9/ADS net cash and monopoly economics argue lower; 10% is the midpoint), EPS base = consensus FY26 $15.91/ADS (US$), dividends at ~25% payout (actual payout 24% [E5 §4]), PV(dividends) ≈ $21/ADS.

Algebra: `409.74 = 21 + [P/E_T × EPS_2030] / 1.10^5` → `EPS_2030 = (409.74 − 21) × 1.6105 / P/E_T`.

| Terminal P/E (2030) | Required 2030 EPS (US$/ADS) | Required EPS CAGR 2026→30 | Required EPS CAGR 2025→30 (from actual $10.65/ADS [E1 §3]) |
|---|---|---|---|
| 14× (ex-growth semi) | $44.72 | **29.5%** | 33.2% |
| 20× (today's NTM multiple [E5 §4]) | $31.30 | **18.4%** | 24.1% |
| 25× (upper half of own 10-yr band) | $25.04 | **12.0%** | 18.6% |

Discount-rate sensitivity at the 20× terminal: r = 8% → 15.7% required CAGR; r = 12% → 21.2% [agent-inferred].

**Implied revenue and margin.** $31.30 × 5,186.4M ADS = US$162.3B of 2030 net income. Working back at alternative steady-state NPMs (history: 2019–24 average NPM 38.8%; 2024 40.5%; 2025 45.1%; 2026E ~50% [E1 §3, E6, computed]):

| Assumed 2030 NPM | Implied 2030 revenue | Implied revenue CAGR 2026→30 (from $172B) | Implied top-10 foundry industry size at 75% share (2025: $169.5B [E3 §1]) |
|---|---|---|---|
| 50% (2026 record level) | $325B | **17.2%** | $433B (20.6%/yr industry CAGR) |
| 44% (half-normalized) | $369B | **21.0%** | $492B (23.8%/yr) |
| 40.5% (2024 level) | $401B | **23.6%** | $534B (25.8%/yr) |

**Implied duration.** The 20× terminal itself encodes continued above-GDP growth beyond 2030 (at r=10%, 20× ≈ 5% earnings yield + ~5% perpetual growth). If instead elevated growth *ends* at 2030 (14× terminal), the price needs 29.5%/yr for four years — supercycle-squared. Conclusion: **$409.74 requires roughly a decade of above-market growth**, which module IMPLIED_EXPECTATIONS flags as fragile ("most companies cannot sustain >15% growth for more than 5–7 years").

**Flags vs historical best / peer ceiling (Stage 2 gate):**

- Implied revenue CAGR 17–24% vs TSMC's own best-ever 5-yr window: 21.9% (2020→25, containing *both* the COVID and AI supercycles) and 21.1% (2019→24) [E1 §7, computed]. The price sits **at the all-time-best ceiling**.
- Implied NPM 44–50% vs 38.8% 2019–24 average; implied GM ≥60% vs 53.5% 2019–24 average and vs management's *own* "56% and higher through the cycle" floor language [E1 §6]. **Exceeds own history; exceeds management's floor by 4–10pp.**
- Implied industry growth ~21–26%/yr for five years vs management's foundry-2.0 2026 forecast of +14% [E1 §2] and one hot TrendForce year of +24.8% (2026E) [E3 §1]. **Exceeds the vendor's own industry frame.**
- Terminal 20× vs own 10-yr year-end TTM band 11.2–28.8, median ≈21× [E5 §4]. **Within band — the only non-heroic implied input.**
- Counter-flag in the market's favor: management's own 2024–29 revenue guide is ~25% CAGR (US$) with AI accelerators at mid-to-high-50s% CAGR [E2 §6, E3 §6] — i.e., **the company's own deck exceeds the price-implied revenue path**. The stretch is not the revenue claim; it is the conjunction of revenue *and* margin *and* terminal multiple *and* duration.

[skill: market-expectations-map / module: ITEMIZE_IMPLIED_ASSUMPTIONS_AS_INDIVIDUALLY_LUDICROUS.md] — the minimum conjunction the price requires: (i) supercycle-rate revenue persists ~5 years; (ii) NPM stays ≥44% (GM ≥~60%) despite management's guided dilution stack; (iii) the 2030 buyer still pays ~20× amid unchanged geopolitics; (iv) no AI-capex digestion year lands inside the window. Clauses (i), (ii), (iv) are correlated (all = "AI supercycle persistence"), so the honest joint structure is roughly: persistence (~50–60%) × margin durability given persistence (~60–70%) × terminal-multiple/geopolitical status quo (~70–80%) ≈ **25–33% joint probability that the full priced-in scenario obtains** [agent-inferred judgment]. That is not "ludicrous" — it is a fair-value claim resting on an above-coin-flip conjunction, which is precisely what "priced for excellence" means.

#### 1.3 Stage 3 — The variant gap, in both directions

**Direction A — the market (and consensus) underprices 2026–27 earnings momentum:**

1. Consensus FY26 revenue NT$5.21T ≈ US$164–166B implies only ~34% US$ growth vs the fresh "slightly above 40%" guide (≥US$171.4B). Gap: **+4–5% on FY26 revenue**, mechanically correctable [E5 §3 vs E1 §1].
2. Mechanical FY26 EPS build (below, §3): NT$107.5/common reported (~NT$105.4 core ex-VIS) ≈ US$16.8 (reported) / **US$16.5 (core) per ADS** vs consensus $15.91/$15.35 → **+4–7% FY26 EPS variance** [agent-inferred].
3. The whisper dynamic: an 11.7% net-profit beat plus a double guidance raise produced a −2.3% print-day close [E5 §3] — near-term expectations are saturated *in price* even while *published* consensus lags. Both facts can be true; the tradeable revision gap is real but small and will close by the October print.

**Direction B — the market/consensus overprices terminal margin durability:**

1. Consensus 2027 EPS NT$128.80/common on a ~+20% revenue year implies **NPM ≈ 51%** (NT$128.8 × 25,932M = NT$3,340B ≈ US$104B net income on ~US$203B revenue) [agent-inferred from E5 §3] — i.e., consensus extrapolates the 2026 record margin structure into 2027 essentially un-normalized.
2. Management's own stack against that: overseas-fab GM dilution "2–3% early stages, widening to 3–4% in the latter stages" [E1 §1, E2 §5]; N2 ramp dilution 2–3pp on FY26 (with 2Q26-call coverage citing 3–4pp in 2H26 — unverified against the official transcript [E1 gaps]); depreciation +high-teens% in 2026 and rising with a $60–64B capex year and 5-year equipment lives [E2 §4–5]; FX sensitivity −40bp GM per 1% TWD appreciation [E1 §6]; and the company's own through-cycle floor of "56% and higher" [E1 §6]. GM at 67.7% is ~11–13pp above the 2020–24 band [E6] — though the operative reversion anchor is the normalization section's 57–60% mid-cycle band (~8–11pp of excess), not the stale 2020–24 band.
3. Quantified: 2028 at GM 61% vs a 66% extrapolation is roughly **−13% EPS** (≈$21 vs $24 US$/ADS) [agent-inferred].

**Net expectations verdict:** the near-term numbers are slightly too low and the medium-term margin/duration assumptions are too high. The price pays for the good half of both. Per [skill: market-expectations-map / module: MISPRICING_SOURCE_BURDEN.md], the candidate *durable* long-side mispricing claim — that the geopolitical discount exceeds the actuarial risk — does not survive the wrapper math (the market prices ~1.0–1.25%/yr of expected geopolitical loss vs ~2.3%/yr warranted, i.e. the discount is thin-to-fair; see §7); the consensus-lag gap is real but fast-decaying, and "AI is big" is fully filed. On the short side, the candidate mispricing source is mechanical: passive/thematic flow — TSM is the #1 holding of the leading EM ETF at 15.18%, >14% of the MSCI EM index, with record H1-2026 EM inflows of $38B [E5 §6] — a marginal buyer indifferent to margin normalization math. Neither source is strong enough to clear the burden for a directional edge by itself.

#### 1.4 Stage 4 — Required-return hurdle

If consensus-track outcomes obtain and the multiple holds at 20×, the price offers roughly the dividend yield (0.67% [E5 §4]) plus EPS growth to 2028 ≈ low-single-digit to ~10% annualized depending entirely on the margin path (§4–5). The mediocrity case is deeply negative. The margin of safety to the variant being wrong is thin: base-case IRR ≈ +2% (below any reasonable hurdle), so the position only clears a 15%+ hurdle in the bull cell. Price sets the burden; §7 renders the verdict.

### 2. Theme-proxy, salience, and the compounder label

[skill: market-expectations-map / module: THEME_PROXY_AND_ISSUER_EXPOSURE_AUDIT.md]

**The chain, link by link (TSMC as "the safe AI proxy"):**

| Link | Finding | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| Theme | AI datacenter buildout is real and accelerating: big-4 hyperscaler 2026 capex ≈ $700–725B, +77% YoY; NVDA FY26 revenue $215.9B (+65%), Q1 FY27 DC revenue $75B (+92%) | [E3 §6] |
| Subsegment | Leading-edge logic (N4/N3/N2) + advanced packaging (CoWoS). Note the theme also feeds HBM memory, networking, power — layers TSMC does not own | [E2 §1–2, E3 §6] |
| Issuer exposure | HPC = 66% of 2Q26 revenue (+20% QoQ); advanced nodes (≤7nm) = 77% of wafer revenue; advanced packaging ~low-teens% of 2026 revenue (guide). AI accelerators narrowly defined were "high-teens%" of 2025 revenue (~$22B); at the guided mid-50s% CAGR that is ~$34B ≈ **~20% of 2026E revenue** [agent-inferred] | [E1 §1, E2 §2, E3 §6] |
| Margin layer | TSMC captures the *manufacturing* margin layer at 67.7% GM — vs merchant foundry peers at 27–29% GM (UMC 29.2%, GlobalFoundries 27.6% Q1'26) and two rivals losing money at scale (Intel Foundry −$10.3B op FY25 on $307M external revenue; Samsung foundry+LSI ≈ −KRW 6.0T FY25) | [E5 §5] |
| Security claim | The **ADS** trades at ≈ **+6.9% premium** to the underlying 2330 line (non-synchronous closes caveat; +9.4% on the 7/15 ADS close) [E5 §1]. Foreign ownership ~73–75% of 2330; TSM is 15.18% of the leading EM ETF; TSMC+two Korean chip names ≈ 30% of the EM index | [E4 §7, E5 §6] |
| Recognition path | Estimate revisions (mechanical, post-raise) + index/thematic flows | [E5 §3, §6] |

**Where the proxy holds:** issuer exposure and margin capture are the strongest links in any AI-supply-chain name — 66% of revenue touching the theme at the deepest margin layer in the chain, with >90% CoWoS share and every major merchant and hyperscaler accelerator except Huawei's (SMIC) and the reported next-gen Maia (Intel 18A-P) fabbed at TSMC [E2 §2, E3 §5]. **Concentration IS the theme exposure now** — HPC went 58% (2025) → 61% (1Q26) → 66% (2Q26) [E1 §1, E2 §6].

**Where it leaks:** (i) *customer concentration:* top-10 customers = 78% of 2025 revenue; largest (NVIDIA per press math) 19%, second (Apple) 17% [E3 §5]; NVIDIA reportedly holds ~60% of CoWoS allocation [E2 §2] — the "safe, diversified picks-and-shovels" framing understates single-customer and single-workload dependence; (ii) *second-source erosion at the margin:* Intel reportedly insourcing 80–90% of Nova Lake compute tiles (from a 60–70% TSMC plan), reported Maia 2 on Intel 18A-P, Tesla AI5/AI6 dual-sourced with Samsung SF2 [E3 §2–3] — none is material yet, all are direction-of-travel; (iii) *the vehicle:* the ADS buyer pays a ~7% wrapper premium and inherits EM-index redemption mechanics — the flow that lifted it can reverse identically; (iv) *timing:* the theme is priced on 2029–30 visibility claims ("demand very strong through probably 2029, 2030" [E1 §1]) while the security must survive the intervening capex-digestion print.

[skill: market-expectations-map / module: SALIENCE_VS_ECONOMIC_MAGNITUDE.md] — **Is the salient thing the biggest FCF driver?** Mostly yes, with two under-salient offsets. The salient noun ("AI accelerators") is only ~20% of 2026E revenue — *smaller than smartphone* (~24%E; 29% in 2025 [E1 §3]) — but the *incremental* revenue and gross profit are overwhelmingly HPC: FY26E adds ~$50B of revenue [agent-inferred], and HPC grew +20% QoQ in 2Q26 while smartphone fell −4% [E1 §1]. The genuinely under-salient drivers: (a) **pricing** — reported hikes of 3–10% across all advanced nodes (74% of wafer revenue) plus a reported ~15% 3nm hike in 2H26, with annual hikes planned through ~2029 [E3 §7] — at a 67.7% GM, price falls through at ~100% incremental margin, worth several points of net-income growth per year on its own; and (b) **smartphone leading-edge content** — Apple reportedly took over half of initial N2 capacity for A20/M6 [E3 §5]; the boring 22–24% revenue block is also the first N2 payer. So salience and magnitude are aligned on direction, but the market's mental model ("AI accelerator proxy") underweights pricing and mix, which are the higher-persistence margin drivers.

[skill: market-expectations-map / module: COMPOUNDER_LABEL_PERSISTENCE.md] — **Is 20× forward paying for compounding still in the deck?** Running the 12-test label-decay diagnostic: organic growth is *accelerating* (score 0); incremental returns are *rising* (ROE 35.4% FY25 → 45.9% annualized 2Q26 [E1 §3, E6]) (0); reinvestment runway *widening* (capex $40.9B→$60–64B; $265B US program) (0); no unit-growth→operating-leverage narrative shift (0); payout ~24–31% with rising dividends but no defensive buyback (0–1); no acquisition treadmill (0); guidance raised, not compressed (0); no "transformation" language (0); insider ownership trivial but stable (0–1); leadership stable (0). **Score ≈ 1–2 / 24 → the label is backed by live economics; this is not a label-decay short.** The relevant failure mode is the *other* one the module warns about: paying a quality multiple on **cyclically-peak economics** as if they were the structural base. The ex-growth downside mark (the module's floor scenario) is a mature-semis 14–16× on *normalized* — not peak — EPS, which is exactly what the mediocrity case in §4 prices.

### 3. Driver model FY26E→FY30E

[skill: driver-model-grammar / Steps 1–3, 5]

**Business equation:** `Revenue = Σ_nodes (wafer shipments × revenue per wafer)  +  advanced packaging & other backend`, where mix (node ladder), like-for-like price hikes, and overseas premium set revenue/wafer; margins follow utilization, node maturity, depreciation, overseas dilution, and FX.

**Base-case table (US$B unless noted; each line tagged fact / estimate / assumption):**

| Driver line | FY25A | FY26E | FY27E | FY28E | FY29E | FY30E | Tag / basis |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Wafer shipments (M 12"-equiv) | 15.0 | 17.6 | 19.1 | 20.5 | 21.7 | 22.8 | FY25 fact [E1 §3]; FY26E = 1H actual 8.51M [E1 §1–2] + ~9% 2H HoH [assumption]; then +8.5/7.5/6/5% — capacity-led, capex $60–64B [E2 §4], >17M-wafer 2025 capacity base [E2 §6] |
| Blended wafer revenue/wafer (US$K) | 7.3 | 8.6 | 9.4 | 10.1 | 10.8 | 11.4 | FY25 ≈ (0.9×122.4)/15.0 [agent-inferred]; growth from node mix (N2 share of wafer rev 2Q26: 3% [E1 §1] → ~8% FY26 → ~15% FY27 → ~22% FY28, mirroring the N3 arc [E2 §1]); N2 ~$30K/wafer vs N3 ~$18–27K [E2 §1, REPORT-grade]; hikes 3–10%/yr through 2029 [E3 §7, REPORT]; Arizona premium 5–30% on a growing overseas share [E3 §7] — all [estimate] |
| = Wafer revenue | ~110 | 151 | 180 | 207 | 234 | 260 | [agent-inferred] |
| Advanced packaging + other backend | ~12 | 21 | 27 | 31 | 36 | 40 | FY25 "slightly over 10%" of revenue, FY26 guided "low-teens %" [E2 §2, fact-guide]; CoWoS ~90K→120–140K wpm end-2026, AP capex CAGR ~24% 2025–27, CoWoS ASP nearing 7nm-wafer levels [E2 §2, REPORT] |
| **Total revenue** | **122.4** | **172** | **206** | **238** | **270** | **300** | FY25 fact [E1 §3]; FY26E reconciled below; FY27+ [assumption]: +20/+15/+13/+11% |
| Gross margin | 59.9% | 66.5% | 63.5% | 61.0% | 60.0% | 59.5% | FY25 fact [E1 §3]; FY26E = 1H actual 67.0% [E1 §1–2] + guided 3Q 65–67% + ~65% 4Q [estimate]; fade = overseas dilution 2–3→3–4pp + N2 dilution + high-teens depreciation growth, partly offset by price hikes and N3 crossing corporate average [E1 §6, E2 §1, §5] |
| Operating margin (≈ GM − 9.5pp) | 50.8% | 57.5% | 54.0% | 51.5% | 50.5% | 50.0% | FY25 fact; opex ratio ≈ 2019–25 pattern [estimate] |
| NPM (≈ 0.89 × OM) | 45.1% | ~50% | 48.1% | 45.8% | 44.9% | 44.5% | FY25 fact [E1 §3]; ratio from 2025 actual OM→NPM conversion [estimate] |
| Net income | 55.2 (NT$1,717.9B [E1 §3]) | ~86 | 99 | 109 | 121 | 134 | [agent-inferred] |
| **EPS, US$/ADS** (NT$/common ×5 ÷ FX 32) | 10.65A | **16.8 rep / 16.5 core** | **19.1** | **21.0** | 23.4 | 25.8 | FY25 fact US$10.65/ADS [E1 §3]; FY26E build below |

**FY26E reconciliation to the guide and actuals (the Step-3 traceability gate):** 1H26 actual revenue US$76.10B (Q1 $35.90B + Q2 $40.20B, facts [E1 §1–2]) + 3Q guide midpoint $45.2B (guide, FX 32.0 [E1 §1]) = $121.3B through 9M. "Slightly above 40%" growth on FY25's $122.42B ⇒ ≥$171.4B; our $172B mid requires Q4 ≈ **$50.7B, +12.2% QoQ** — the same sequential rate as the Q3 guide midpoint (+12.4%), consistent with the "steep ramp-up of our 2-nanometer technology" [E1 §1] plus reported 2H price increases [E3 §7]. A strict reading of "slightly above 40%" spans ~$171.5–173.5B; the task brief's $168–172B envelope [agent-inferred elsewhere] is the conservative end — below $170B is *not* consistent with the Q3 guide unless Q4 goes QoQ-flat, which no evidence supports. EPS build [agent-inferred]: 1H actual NT$49.33/common (fact [E6]) + Q3E ≈ NT$27.7 (rev NT$1,446B at FX 32, GM 66%, OM 57%, ~16% tax) + Q4E ≈ NT$30.5 (rev NT$1,622B, GM 65%, OM 56.5%) = **NT$107.5/common ≈ US$16.8/ADS reported; ~NT$105.4 ≈ US$16.5/ADS core** after stripping the ~NT$2.1/common after-tax VIS disposal gain (NT$63.2B pre-tax one-off inside 2Q non-op [E1 §1, E6 QoE flag]).

**Load-bearing sensitivity (Step 5):** ±1pp of GM ≈ ±US$0.37/ADS of 2028 EPS (≈±1.8%); ±5pp of 2027–28 revenue growth ≈ ±US$2.0/ADS of 2028 EPS; both are dominated by the exit multiple (±2 turns ≈ ±US$42/ADS at base EPS). The single decision-level variable is **whether hyperscaler capex keeps growing through 2027–28** — it sets revenue *and* utilization-driven margin *and* the multiple simultaneously (correlated, per §1.2).

### 4. Return profile to YE2028 — four cases plus a separated tail

[skill: valuation-and-return-profile / Stages 1–5; assets/RETURN_PROFILE.md template; module: PRICE_SETS_UNDERWRITING_BURDEN.md consumed in §7]

Horizon: 2026-07-17 → 2028-12-31 = 2.45 years. Entry $409.74/ADS (US$). Dividends: board-approved NT$7.00/common for 1Q26 (payable 2026-10-08); management commitment "at least NT$23/common received in 2026"; declared path NT$4→7 over 2024–26 [E1 §5]. Modeled receipts over the hold ≈ NT$70/common (Oct-26 NT$7; 2027 ≈ NT$29; 2028 ≈ NT$34) ≈ **US$11/ADS cumulative** ($12 bull, $10 bear) [agent-inferred]. Exit multiples are NTM-style P/E on 2028 EPS. Terminal-comp anchors, per Stage 4 of the skill: NVDA 20.8× fwd, AVGO 23.8× fwd, ASML 33.4× fwd, TSM itself 20.0× fwd (all 2026-07-16/17, single source [E5 §4]); TSMC's own 10-yr year-end TTM P/E band 11.2–28.8, median ≈21× [E5 §4].

**Where should a geopolitically-discounted monopoly trade?** The two other "irreplaceable node" businesses trade at 23.8× (AVGO) and 33.4× (ASML) forward; TSMC's 20.0× embeds a persistent Taiwan discount that published commentary frames as ±2–4 P/E turns [E4 §4]. We do not underwrite discount *removal* in any case below bull; we underwrite discount *persistence* (base) and discount *widening plus cycle* (mediocrity/bear).

| Case | Revenue path 26→28 (US$B) | GM / OM (2028) | 2028 EPS (US$/ADS) | Exit P/E & justification | Exit price + cum. divs | **MOIC** | **IRR (2.45y)** |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Bull** — supercycle persists on management's own 2024–29 ~25% CAGR track [E3 §6]; pricing hikes land; GM holds near-record (as 2025–26 out-execution repeated) | 172→215→265 | 64% / 55% | **$25.0** | **24×** — quality-peer parity (AVGO 23.8×); partial discount expiry as Arizona diversifies; still 5 turns below the 2021/2024 peaks | 600 + 12 = 612 | **1.49×** | **+17.8%** |
| **Base** — AI grows but decelerates; margin normalization begins per management's own dilution guidance | 172→206→238 | 61% / 51.5% | **$21.0** | **20×** — status-quo forward multiple; 10-yr median; geopolitical discount persists | 420 + 11 = 431 | **1.05×** | **+2.1%** |
| **Mediocrity (mandatory)** — one digestion year (2028 +4–5% growth) + margin normalization toward the floor + multiple to historical mid | 172→193→200 | 58% / 48% | **$16.5** (≈ flat vs FY26 core — two years of zero EPS growth) | **16×** — mid of the 10-yr band (11–29×); the 2018–19-style "mature foundry, growth pause" mark | 264 + 11 = 275 | **0.67×** | **−15.0%** |
| **Bear (operating)** — genuine AI-capex downcycle: 2023 rerun on the now-66% HPC base (2023: revenue −8.7% US$, GM −5.2pp [E1 §7]) | 172→181→170 | 55% / 44% | **$12.8** | **14×** — recession-trough multiple on trough EPS (2022 traded 11.2× on peak-ish EPS [E5 §4]) | 179 + 10 = 189 | **0.46×** | **−27.0%** |
| **Tail-bear (conflict) — separate line, NOT blended** | Taiwan production halted/blockaded | n/m | n/m | n/m — residual value only | **$40–80/ADS residual** [agent-inferred]: overseas fabs ≈ ~10% of capacity near-term (AZ P1 N4 running; Kumamoto; Dresden [E2 §3]) + net cash $15.9/ADS (accessibility uncertain) + non-transferable IP; wrapper-section-style ~85–90% haircut | **0.10–0.22×** | n/m |

**Probability framing** [agent-inferred judgments]: bull 25% / base 40% / mediocrity 25% / bear 10% → probability-weighted terminal value = 0.25×612 + 0.40×431 + 0.25×275 + 0.10×189 = **$413 ≈ entry price (+0.8% total)**. Layering the conflict tail at a 7% carve-out with $70 residual: 0.93×413 + 0.07×70 = **$389 ≈ −5.0% total (−2.1%/yr)**. Read plainly: at these weights the operating distribution alone prices the stock almost exactly fairly, and the tail makes expected value negative. To get paid at $409.74 you must either (a) assign >35–40% to the bull cell, (b) extend the horizon into 2029–30 where management says demand remains strong [E1 §1] and let compounding absorb the tail premium (the "phantom fear for now" posture per [skill: market-expectations-map / module: MARKET_IMPLIED_TAIL_PROBABILITY_REVERSE_UNDERWRITE.md]), or (c) believe the geopolitical discount narrows. Note also the case-level skew: +18% bull vs −15/−27% mediocrity/bear — **negative skew at entry**, the mirror image of the 2025-04 tariff-selloff entry ($223.70 52-wk low [E5 §1]) when the same distribution was positively skewed.

**Market-implied conflict odds cross-check** [module: MARKET_IMPLIED_ODDS_AND_BAD_EVENT_RESIDUAL_VALUE.md]: if an undiscounted TSMC would trade at quality-peer ~23× NTM (V+ ≈ $471/ADS) and conflict residual V− ≈ $70, then p = (471 − 409.74)/(471 − 70) ≈ **15%** cumulative probability-equivalent of the near-total-loss event — equivalently the published "2–4 P/E turns" [E4 §4]. Against the 2026 de-escalatory temperature read (ADIZ incursions down to ~200/month, Trump–Xi assurance, US$14B arms package pending [E4 §4]) a bull calls 15% too high; against six large-scale PLA exercises since 2022 and a blockade-simulation in Dec-2025 [E4 §4] it is not obviously wrong. We treat it as fairly priced and refuse to make the tail the source of edge in either direction.

### 5. Sensitivity grid and break-even

[skill: valuation-and-return-profile / Stage 6]

**Annualized IRR from $409.74 (2.45-yr hold, +$11/ADS cumulative dividends; $10 at bear EPS):**

| 2028 EPS (US$/ADS) ↓ \ Exit P/E → | 14× | 16× | 18× | 20× | 22× | 24× | 26× | 28× |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **$12.8 (bear)** | −27.0% | −23.2% | −19.6% | −16.2% | −13.0% | −9.9% | −7.0% | −4.2% |
| **$16.5 (mediocrity)** | −19.3% | −15.0% | −11.0% | −7.2% | −3.7% | −0.3% | +3.0% | +6.0% |
| **$21.0 (base)** | −11.4% | −6.6% | −2.1% | **+2.1%** | +6.0% | +9.8% | +13.4% | +16.8% |
| **$25.0 (bull)** | −5.0% | +0.1% | +4.9% | +9.4% | +13.7% | +17.7% | +21.6% | +25.2% |

**Break-even (0% total return from $409.74):** exit price must recover $409.74 − $11 dividends = **$398.7/ADS**, i.e. `2028 EPS × exit P/E ≈ 399`. Combinations: **$25.0 × 16.0× · $21.0 × 19.0× · $16.5 × 24.2× · $12.8 × 31.2×**. Read: the base case breaks even only if the multiple holds at ~19× or better; the mediocrity case requires the market to pay a *near-peak-band* 24× on flat earnings (it will not); the bull case tolerates compression to 16× and still returns capital. only 14 of the 32 cells beat zero, all in the bottom-right triangle — the payoff is a call on the joint (EPS momentum × multiple persistence), which is one correlated bet, not two independent ones [module: MULTI_LEVERED_UPSIDE_CORRELATION_MAP.md discipline applied].

### 6. Replacement-cost triangulation

[skill: external-mark-and-replacement-cost-triangulation / Stages 1–4]

**The marks (Stage 1 inventory), normalized (Stage 2):**

| Mark | Value | Comparability verdict |
|---|---|---|
| Net PP&E (property, plant & equipment), 6/30/26 | NT$4,302.9B ≈ **US$133.6B** at 32.213 [E6; agent-inferred conversion] | Book, 5-yr equipment depreciation [E2 §5] — badly understates economic replacement (floor only) |
| Cumulative capex 2019–2025 | **US$199.6B** [E1 §7, computed: 14.9+17.2+30.0+36.3+30.5+29.8+40.9]; 2021–25 alone US$167B + US$30B R&D (management) [E1 §7] | Historical cost of the *current* footprint; a rebuild costs more (N2 capex/1k-wpm "substantially higher" than N3; A14 higher still [E2 §1]) |
| Marginal build benchmark | US$265B ÷ 12 leading-edge fab/packaging facilities ≈ **~US$22B per facility**; earlier tranche $165B for 3 fabs + 2 AP plants + R&D center [E2 §3, E4 §3] | The live, government-negotiated marginal cost of leading-edge capacity — the cleanest current mark |
| Full replacement estimate | **≈ US$400–550B** [agent-inferred: 2019–25 cost base ~$200B × 1.5–2.0× current-cost inflation + packaging complex + the fact that the $265B US program targets only ~30% of future 2nm-and-beyond capacity [E2 §3]] | Order-of-magnitude; treated as a band, not a point |
| "Proof of irreproducibility" marks | Intel Foundry: −$13.3B (FY24) and −$10.3B (FY25) operating losses on **$307M** external revenue; zero committed 14A customers; grants converted to a ~10% US government equity stake. Samsung foundry+LSI: ≈ −KRW 13.8T (~US$10B) cumulative 2023–25; 2nm yields reported mid-50s% vs TSMC's 60–70%; annual profit targeted only 2028 | [E3 §2–3, E5 §5] — capital alone demonstrably does not reproduce the asset |

**The multiple the market pays for the moat layer (Stage 3):** EV ≈ $2,043B → **EV / net PP&E ≈ 15.3×**; **EV / replacement band ≈ 3.7–5.1×**. Decomposed: physical assets at replacement ≈ $400–550B (~20–27% of EV) and the residual **≈ $1.5–1.6T (~75–80% of EV) is the moat layer** — yield learning, process cadence, customer lock-in, ecosystem, and 72.3% share [E3 §1] capitalized. Sanity checks: (i) a >4× premium to replacement cost is only rational when rivals *cannot* convert capex into competing supply — the Intel/Samsung loss ledger above is the strongest such evidence in modern industrial history, and the skill's supply-response anti-pattern (high returns attract capacity) is answered by the observed failure of ~$35B+ of cumulative rival losses to dent share (TSMC share went ~60%→72.3% while those losses accrued [E3 §1–3]); (ii) vs ASML — the other "irreplaceable node," ~100% of EUV (extreme ultraviolet lithography) supply — the market pays 33.4× forward earnings with zero conflict-site risk vs TSMC's 20.0× [E5 §4]: TSMC's asset gets the *larger* moat capitalization in absolute EV but the *thinner* earnings multiple — the entire "irreplaceability premium vs Taiwan-site discount" tug-of-war is visible in those 13 turns; (iii) mark classification per Stage 4: replacement cost = **floor logic only** (it tells you a competitor needs ~half a trillion dollars *and* a decade *and* yields — it does not tell you TSMC is cheap); the Intel/Samsung marks = **moat-durability anchors**; the $22B/facility benchmark = **supply-response signal to monitor** (if US-subsidized rival capacity ever *does* yield, the moat multiple compresses first).

### 7. Cheapness verdict — what operating state is priced at ~20× forward

[skill: valuation-and-return-profile / module: PRICE_SETS_UNDERWRITING_BURDEN.md — output card]

- **Current price / TEV:** $409.74/ADS; market cap $2,125B; EV $2,043B; 20.0× NTM P/E, 33.25× TTM (above the 10-yr 11.2–28.8 year-end band); EV/EBITDA 21.4×; FCF yield 1.65% TTM (capex-suppressed) [E5 §4, E6].
- **Actionable price/liquidity:** 2026-07-16 close; 20-day ADV 15.4M ADS ≈ $6.3B/day [E5 §1] — no size constraint.
- **Market-implied premise (plain language):** high-teens-to-low-20s% revenue CAGR through 2030, net margins held at-or-above any pre-2025 year forever, a ~20× multiple still paid in 2030, no digestion year, and the Taiwan tail staying a tail (§1.2).
- **Required proof burden at this price:** **excellence.** Survival, mediocrity, even "very good" proof is insufficient — a premium price demands compounder-grade evidence on *duration*, per the module's ladder.
- **Available proof:** the pack proves excellence **through 2026 and probably 2027**: beat-raise-raise cadence, GM/OM above their own guides, 2nm ramping with ~4× N3's tape-outs [E2 §1], pricing power through 2029 (reported), 72.3% share, rivals structurally loss-making, $60–64B capex as a demand signal, and management's 2029–30 visibility claim [E1, E2, E3]. It **cannot prove the 2028–2030 leg**: hyperscaler capex's second derivative (+77% in 2026 [E3 §6]) is unknowable and unguided; management's own margin-dilution arithmetic contradicts consensus's 51% 2027 NPM; and the one thing the market did on a +11.7% profit beat was sell the stock −2.3% [E5 §3] — the marginal holder is already underwriting the excellence case.
- **What must be false for $409.74 to be fair:** any one of — supercycle persistence to 2030, ≥60% structural GM, the 2030 buyer paying 20×, or tail quiescence (joint ≈ 25–33%, §1.2).
- **Minimum acceptable outcome at this price:** the base case — and it returns ≈ +2%/yr, below any hurdle.
- **Verdict: the price underwrites *excellence*, not ordinary operation and certainly not damage.** TSMC at $409.74 is not expensive the way a story stock is expensive — the reverse-DCF clears at management's own guidance with a median-band terminal multiple — but the *underwriting burden* is excellence-grade while the evidence pack is excellence-grade only to a ~2027 horizon. On [module: PRICED_IN_DAMAGE_BURDEN_OF_PROOF.md] logic, nothing adverse is pre-paid at this price except the geopolitical tail (~15% probability-equivalent, §4): margin normalization is not priced, digestion is not priced, multiple mean-reversion is not priced. The mediocrity case — merely a *pause* — costs a third of the position. **Classification: fairly-to-fully priced for a monopoly at cyclical peak; the burden of proof now rests on duration, and duration is precisely what cannot be proven from here.**

| # | Judgment | Confidence | Falsifier |
