| 2 | Consensus 2027 NPM ~51% embeds no margin normalization; 2027–28 GM lands 61–64%, not 66%+ | Medium-high | Two consecutive quarters of GM ≥66% *after* 2nm reaches ~10% of wafer revenue and overseas share rises |
| 3 | Price-implied scenario (≈18.4% EPS CAGR to 2030 at 20× terminal, r=10%) requires an at-historical-ceiling conjunction with joint odds ~25–33% | Medium | Management's 2024–29 ~25% CAGR guide *and* ≥60% GM both intact at the Jan-2028 guide |
| 4 | The compounder label is economically backed (score ~1–2/24); the risk is peak-cycle capitalization, not label decay | High | ROE and incremental margins rolling over while growth persists |
| 5 | Base-case return from $409.74 is ~0–3%/yr; the entry price, not the business, is the problem | Medium-high | Multiple expands through 24× without an EPS acceleration (flow-driven rerating we refuse to underwrite) |
| 6 | Conflict tail is priced thin-to-fair at ~15% probability-equivalent (2–4 P/E turns; ~1.0–1.25%/yr priced vs ~2.3%/yr warranted); a haircut, not a source of long-side edge | Low-medium | Sustained ADIZ/quarantine escalation (7th large-scale exercise with customs enforcement) or, opposite, a formalized US–China Taiwan modus vivendi |
| 7 | Replacement-cost floor is ~$400–550B (~20–27% of EV); irrelevant to downside math except in the conflict case, where it is also destroyed | High | A rival (Intel 14A / Samsung SF2) landing a flagship external accelerator at competitive yield |

### (c) Kill signals (dated thresholds)

1. **2026-10 (Q3'26 print):** revenue below the $44.6B guide floor, or Q4'26 guide < $48B (< +7% QoQ), or GM guide < 63% → momentum crack; base→mediocrity re-weight.
2. **2027-01 (Q4'26 call):** FY27 US$-revenue growth guided < +15%, or capex guided *down* YoY (capex is the demand tell — it was raised twice in 2026 [E1 §2]) → digestion arriving; exit base case.
3. **2027-01→02 (hyperscaler guides):** big-4 aggregate 2027 capex guided < +15% YoY (vs +77% in 2026 [E3 §6]) → the decision-level variable has flipped; mediocrity becomes the modal case.
4. **2026-12-31 (channel):** CoWoS 2027 bookings reported cut, or the largest customer's CoWoS allocation share falling >10pp (TrendForce-class reporting cadence [E2 §2]) → order-book leading indicator.
5. **Any quarter before 2027-06-30:** reported GM < 60% → normalization running ahead of base; judgment #2 accelerates.
6. **2026-H2→2027-H1 (competitive):** a committed external 14A anchor customer at Intel (management said commitments expected 2H26–1H27 [E3 §2]), or Samsung SF2 yields confirmed ≥ TSMC-class on a flagship part → first crack in the monopoly-duration assumption; trim the moat multiple.
7. **2027-04 (policy):** Section 232 semiconductor tariffs broadened without renewal of the Taiwan quota exemption (2.5×/1.5× construction-linked duty-free mechanism [E4 §1]) → US-cost drag on the margin bridge.
8. **Standing (geopolitical):** blockade-simulation exercises escalating to actual maritime enforcement around Taiwan, or N-2 export rule (2nm/A16 Taiwan-only [E4 §5]) tightened further → tail re-rate; the position must already be sized for $40–80 residual.

### (d) Handoffs — numbers the decision section should consume

- **Entry reference:** $409.74/ADS (2026-07-16 close); ADS = 5 common; ADS premium to 2330 ≈ +6.9% (non-synchronous) [E5 §1]; net cash US$15.9/ADS; EV $393.85/ADS.
- **Earnings path (US$/ADS, core):** FY26E **$16.5** (reported ~$16.8; consensus $15.91/$15.35); FY27E base **$19.1**; FY28E by case **$12.8 / $16.5 / $21.0 / $25.0** (bear/mediocrity/base/bull).
- **Price-implied at entry (r=10%):** EPS CAGR 2026→30 of **18.4% at a 20× terminal** (29.5% at 14×; 12.0% at 25×); implied 2030 revenue **$325–401B** depending on NPM 50%→40.5%; implied growth duration ≈ a decade.
- **Return profile (2.45y):** IRR/MOIC — bull **+17.8% / 1.49×**; base **+2.1% / 1.05×**; mediocrity **−15.0% / 0.67×**; bear **−27.0% / 0.46×**; conflict residual **$40–80/ADS**, market-implied tail ≈ **15%** probability-equivalent; probability-weighted expected ≈ **−2% total** at 25/40/25/10 + 7% tail.
- **Break-even:** 2028 `EPS × P/E ≈ $399/ADS` → $21.0 needs ≥19.0×; $16.5 needs 24.2×; $25.0 tolerates 15.9×.
- **Dividend stream:** ≈ US$11/ADS cumulative over the hold (NT$7.00/quarter/common current, rising; ≥NT$23/common received in 2026 per management [E1 §5]).
- **Structural marks:** EV/replacement ≈ **3.7–5.1×** ($400–550B band); moat layer ≈ $1.5–1.6T of EV; peer anchors NVDA 20.8× / AVGO 23.8× / ASML 33.4× fwd vs TSM 20.0×; own 10-yr band 11.2–28.8×, median ~21×.
- **Verdict handed to the decision section:** price underwrites excellence; evidence proves it only to ~2027; base-case IRR ~+2% fails the hurdle; the trade at this price is a probability bet on bull-cell weight >35–40% or a horizon extension into 2029–30 — with kill signals 1–3 as the binding trip-wires.

*Compliance: analytical work over public data; facts, estimates, and judgments separated and tagged; not investment advice, a price target, or a trade instruction.*


## Section II.7 — Thesis Legs, Red Team, Refutation Ledger, Catalyst Calendar, and the Decision

**TSMC — Taiwan Semiconductor Manufacturing Company Limited (NYSE: TSM, 1 ADS = 5 common shares; TWSE: 2330). Section date: 2026-07-17. Reference price: US$409.74/ADS (2026-07-16 close, Q2'26 print day).**

*Role: red-team and decision lead. This section consumes the six upstream sections — the business-architecture section, the normalization section, the moat section, the wrapper/geopolitics section, the governance section, and the valuation/expectations section — plus evidence files 01–06 (compiled 2026-07-16/17). Facts carry source + date; probabilities and judgments are marked **[agent-inferred]**; methods are attributed [skill: slug]. Acronyms: ADS = American Depositary Share; GM/OM/NPM = gross/operating/net profit margin; EPS = earnings per share; D&A = depreciation & amortization; P/E = price-to-earnings; IRR = internal rate of return; CAGR = compound annual growth rate; YoY/QoQ = year-over-year/quarter-over-quarter; HPC = high-performance computing; ASIC = application-specific integrated circuit; CoWoS = Chip-on-Wafer-on-Substrate advanced packaging; N2/N3 = TSMC 2nm/3nm nodes; A16/A14 = 1.6nm/1.4nm-class nodes; wpm = wafers per month; FX = foreign exchange; BIS = U.S. Bureau of Industry and Security; §232 = Section 232 of the Trade Expansion Act; ART = U.S.–Taiwan Agreement on Reciprocal Trade; N-2 = Taiwan's two-generations-behind overseas-deployment statute; NDF = National Development Fund (Taiwan); EM = emerging markets; MOIC = multiple on invested capital. No position-sizing content appears in this section by design.*

---

### 1. Question of record and hypothesis slate

[skill: investment-hypothesis-arena — question of record, minimum slate, prediction locks]

**Question of record:** *At $409.74 on 2026-07-17, does fresh money in TSM ADS earn ≥10%/yr to YE2028 after honestly weighting (i) margin normalization against the 67.7% Q2'26 print, (ii) AI-capital-expenditure cycle risk on a 66%-HPC revenue base, and (iii) the Taiwan jurisdiction tail — and if not, what dated observation would change that answer?*

**Slate (each with claim, mechanism, falsifier):**

| ID | Hypothesis | Mechanism | Falsifier | Status at close of this section |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| H-L (long) | The monopoly compounds through the window: revenue near management's ~25% 2024–29 frame (4Q25 call, 2026-01-15), GM holds ≥63%, take-rate expands via the ASIC shift, the 2030 buyer pays ≥22× → ≥15%/yr | Sold-out leading edge + annual price hikes + packaging attach; second sources fail to mature | Two consecutive monthly revenue prints <+15% YoY, or GM <64% at the 3Q26/4Q26 prints, or a committed marquee Intel 14A external customer by 1H27 | **active, not underwritable at this price** — the price already pays for most of it (§3, §7 of the valuation/expectations section) |
| H-S (short) | Peak-cycle economics crack in 2027: depreciation wave + rent reversion + hyperscaler digestion take FY27 EPS to ~$11.5–12.7 vs consensus ~$20; multiple compresses toward 16× → −30%+ | 5-year depreciation lives meet a $60–64B capex vintage exactly as demand decelerates | Big-4 hyperscaler 2027 capex guided ≥+20% (Jan–Feb 2027); FY27 revenue guided ≥+20% with GM ≥63% | **active but undated** — no break mechanism is observable before 2026-10 at the earliest (§3) |
| H-C (consensus/no-edge) | The market is roughly right: base-case IRR ≈ +2%, tail priced ≈ actuarially, whisper bar already above published consensus | Serial-beater expectations saturation (stock −2.3% on an +11.7% net-profit beat, 2026-07-16, evidence file 05 §3) | Either flip trigger in §6 firing | **provisionally the survivor** — carried into the decision |
| H-3 (forced third alternative) | Neither "demand forever" nor "cycle reverts": the decisive mechanism is the **pricing/mix ratchet** — annual hikes through ~2029 (reported), the N2 ~$30k wafer tier, and packaging attach immunize EPS against a *unit* digestion (2023 analog: shipments −21%, revenue only −9%, revenue/wafer +16%) so a 2027 pause produces flat EPS, not −25–30% | Wafer pricing is input-priced and contract-laddered; scarcity converts to list-price ratchet before it expires | 2027 advanced-node hike round suspended/rolled back (Dec-2026 reporting season), or blended revenue-per-wafer down YoY for two consecutive quarters ex-FX | **live and under-priced by both bull and bear** [agent-inferred]; it softens H-S's EPS path and is the best reason the mediocrity case lands at $16.5 EPS rather than $12 |

**Prediction locks** (pre-registered so outcomes can grade process, not narrative) [skill: investment-hypothesis-arena]: (1) 3Q26 print (2026-10-15): revenue ≥$44.6B and GM ≥65% — the sandbag prior says guides are floors (governance section); (2) monthly prints ≥+20% YoY through November 2026 given the 2H25 comparison base [agent-inferred]; (3) zero committed external Intel 14A customers by 2026-12-31 (commitments window per Intel's January 2026 call runs 2H26–1H27, evidence file 03 §2); (4) at least one more 2027 advanced-node price-hike report by 2027-01-31 (pattern per TrendForce, 2025-12-29). Misses on (1) or (4) are regime information, not noise.

---

### 2. The four falsifiable legs of the LONG thesis

[skill: thesis-legs-and-redteam — leg quality: claim + falsifiable metric + line in the sand + evidence refs; variant perception per the schema in that skill's variant-perception module]

#### L1 — Moat: process + packaging leadership defensible through ~2030

- **Claim:** No competitor takes flagship-tier leading-edge share before the A14 generation ramps (~2028 production + ramp → ~2030).
- **Evidence chain:** foundry share 60%→72.3% over nine quarters (TrendForce, 2026-06-12); N2 tape-outs ~4× N3 at the same stage (DigiTimes, 2026-07-16); ~15 committed N2 customers (TrendForce, 2025-09-22); Intel 14A has **zero committed external customers** and a 10-K that contemplates pausing the node (Intel FY2025 10-K, filed Jan 2026); Samsung SF2 yields reported mid-50s% vs TSMC N2 60–70% (press, 2026); rival economics: Intel Foundry −$10.3B FY25 operating loss on $307M external revenue, Samsung foundry ~KRW 6T FY25 loss (evidence file 05 §5). The moat section's replication ladder puts organic replication at multi-decade on honest arithmetic.
- **Line in the sand:** by **2027-06-30**, no Intel 14A committed external customer from TSMC's top-10 book AND no Samsung flagship-tier (Apple/NVIDIA/AMD/Qualcomm flagship) socket award; and **2nm ≥ ~10% of wafer revenue by 2Q27** (tape-out-to-revenue conversion test; N3 hit 24% of annual wafer revenue in year 3).
- **Cheapest hypothesis-killing observation:** a single press-verified headline — a top-5 TSMC customer committing a *flagship* product to 14A or SF2P. One story, zero modeling.
- **Variant perception:** *Market believes* the moat is effectively permanent (12 of 19 analysts at Strong Buy; average price target $516.76, 2026-07-16). *We believe* the moat is intact but **dated** — defensible to ~2030 at ≥80% confidence, decaying toward coin-flip-plus beyond as state-funded challengers persist and hyperscalers deliberately seed second sources (Maia 2 reportedly to Intel 18A-P; Tesla dual-sourcing; Nova Lake tiles insourced). *What's knowable:* the 2028 window is observable years in advance through commitments, tape-outs, and yield reports — this leg is monitorable, not faith-based.

#### L2 — Normalized gross margin ≥57% (franchise floor above management's 56%)

- **Claim:** After the depreciation wave, rent reversion, overseas dilution, and the N2 ramp, mid-cycle GM lands in the 57–60% band (point 58%) — far below the 67.7% print but above every pre-2025 year.
- **Evidence chain:** the normalization section's four-layer decomposition (depreciation-holiday ~3–4pp, reverting allocation rent ~2.5–4pp, FX ~0.6pp; structural franchise ~57–60%); management's own floor raised "53% and higher" (2Q25 call, 2025-07-17) → "56% and higher through the cycle" (4Q25 call, 2026-01-15); FY24 delivered 56.1% under worse mix and adverse FX; the 2023 trough held 54.4% at −21% shipments (evidence file 01 §7).
- **Line in the sand:** 3Q26 GM print **<64%** or 4Q26 GM guide **<63%** (2026-10-15 / 2027-01); any quarter **<60% before 2027-06-30** = normalization running below band; FY27 depreciation guided **>+25%** with FY27 revenue framed <+15% (Jan-2027 call) = the compression arithmetic locks.
- **Cheapest hypothesis-killing observation:** the **December 2026 pricing-round reports**. If the 2027 advanced-node hike round is suspended, the "value-priced, not shortage-priced" half of the rent split fails, and the 57–60% band loses ~1–2pp — one supply-chain story, available free, months before any GM print shows it.
- **Variant perception:** *Market believes* (via consensus FY27 EPS of NT$128.8 ≈ US$20/ADS derived) that 2026's record margin structure persists un-normalized into 2027 — implied NPM ~51%. *We believe* normalization to 57–60% GM is coming and is survivable — the franchise is real, the print is not the run-rate. *What's knowable:* fully — quarterly GM, the D&A guide, and the pricing reports resolve this leg on a published cadence.

#### L3 — AI demand: no air pocket through 2028

- **Claim:** Hyperscaler AI capital spending keeps growing (2027 aggregate ≥+15%), TSMC monthly revenue YoY stays ≥+15%, utilization holds; the digestion scenario stays a scenario.
- **Evidence chain:** big-4 hyperscaler 2026 capex ~$700–725B, +77% YoY (evidence file 03 §6); NVIDIA data-center revenue +92% YoY (April-2026 quarter, SEC press release); CoWoS fully booked with the supply-demand gap ~20% (TrendForce, 2026-06-15); advanced-node supply reportedly ~3× short of AI demand (press, 2026-03-05); TSMC's own demand verification "customers and customers' customers… make sure chips are not going into inventory" (CEO, 2Q26 call via secondary transcript, 2026-07-16); FY26 guide raised twice to "slightly above 40%" US$ growth.
- **Line in the sand:** **two consecutive monthly revenue prints <+15% YoY** (prints ~10th of each month; 1H26 ran +35.6%, but note April 2026 printed +17.5% — single sub-15 months are noise, two consecutive are signal); **big-4 2027 capex guided <+15%** in Jan–Feb 2027; confirmation threshold: two consecutive months **<+10%** = air-pocket onset, 2023 playbook applies.
- **Cheapest hypothesis-killing observation:** the monthly revenue print — free, published on a fixed cadence, and it front-runs every quarterly disclosure. Second cheapest: TrendForce-class CoWoS 2027 booking cuts (Dec-2026 window).
- **Variant perception:** *Market believes* management's "demand very strong through probably 2029, 2030" and prices roughly a decade of above-market growth (valuation/expectations section reverse-DCF). *We believe* demand is proven through 2027 and **unknowable beyond** — the honest posture is monitoring, not underwriting; this leg is the one the price forces you to assume and the evidence cannot prove. *What's knowable:* nothing directly about 2028 demand today; only the leading indicators above. This asymmetry between what the price assumes and what is knowable is the crux of the decision (§6).

#### L4 — The tail stays a tail and the wrapper holds

- **Claim:** No blockade/quarantine enforcement or invasion inside the horizon; the ADS wrapper (sponsored program, one ADS = 5 common shares, ~20.5% of shares in ADS form per the FY2025 20-F) keeps functioning; the policy stack (§232 with the 2.5×/1.5× Taiwan quota exemptions, ART, CHIPS terms, N-2) stays benign.
- **Evidence chain:** wrapper currently a **premium** claim (+6.9% vs the local line on 2026-07-16 closes, non-synchronous caveat) — impairment would show as a persistent discount; dividends remitting on schedule (NT$18/share received 2025; ≥NT$23 guided 2026); policy equilibrium: 25% §232 tariff with TSMC exempt via quotas (proclamation effective 2026-01-15; ART signed 2026-02-12), no U.S. equity demand on record, the +$100B/$265B announcement (2026-07-16) as the ratchet payment; temperature mixed-to-de-escalatory mid-2026 (ADIZ incursions ~134–200/month vs 300+ in 2024; sixth large-scale PLA exercise Dec 2025) (evidence file 04).
- **Line in the sand:** any of the wrapper/geopolitics section's four pre-committed kill triggers — CBC (Taiwan's central bank) restricting foreign repatriation; TWSE halt >3 sessions or depositary suspension; **kinetic enforcement of commercial shipping sustained >72 hours**; §232 quota mechanism altered or a U.S. equity/royalty demand revived. Wrapper gauge: **ADS premium flipping to a >5% discount for >1 month**.
- **Cheapest hypothesis-killing observation:** the daily ADS-premium calculation (two closing prices and an FX rate), plus a single headline of a Coast Guard boarding. Both free, both same-day.
- **Variant perception:** *Market believes* — per the valuation section's cross-check — roughly a 15% cumulative probability-equivalent of the near-total-loss event (2–4 P/E turns). *We believe* the discount is roughly actuarial-to-thin: it is **not** a stigma premium to harvest, and not by itself a reason to avoid (reconciled in §4, entry 7). *What's knowable:* only the monitorables — the event itself is not forecastable, which is why this leg carries kill triggers rather than a probability edge.

**Load-bearing ranking** [skill: thesis-legs-and-redteam, Stage 2]: **L3 is the load-bearing leg** — it simultaneously controls revenue, utilization-driven margin (hence L2's realized path), and the exit multiple (the normalization section's single-factor-dominance flag). L2 is second. L1 breaks nothing before 2028 even if it starts breaking. L4 is low-probability/absorbing: it caps the whole stool rather than supporting it.

---

### 3. Red team — kill the idea, both ways

[skill: thesis-legs-and-redteam — anti-thesis argued to win, mediocrity case, even-if structure; skill: short-research-module — bull-case inversion discipline only]

#### 3.1 The best short case at $409.74, built from the report's own admissions

Steelman, argued to win:

1. **You are paying a record multiple on provably unrepeatable margins.** The trailing P/E of 33.25× sits **above** the entire 10-year year-end band (11.2–28.8×, evidence file 05 §4) while ~7–11pp of the 67.7% GM is timing and reverting rent, not franchise (normalization section). The single largest artifact is the **depreciation holiday**: D&A intensity fell from 22.9% of revenue (FY24) to ~15.5% (2Q26) because the 2023–24 capex trough rolled off 5-year lives exactly as AI revenue inflected. That artifact now runs in reverse: 2Q26 D&A jumped +20% QoQ, FY26 is guided +high-teens, and the twice-raised $60–64B 2026 capex vintage annualizes into FY27 D&A of roughly +22–28% [agent-inferred, normalization section]. The margin tailwind of 2025–1H26 is mechanically becoming a 2027 headwind.
2. **Consensus has not modeled a cycle that management itself keeps describing.** Consensus FY27 ≈ US$20/ADS implies ~51% net margin — *above* the 2026 record — while management's own stack (overseas dilution 2–3pp widening to 3–4pp; N2 dilution; depreciation growth; a "56% and higher" through-cycle floor) points the other way. In the digestion scenario (big-4 hyperscaler 2027 capex flat after +77%), FY27 EPS is **~$11.5–12.7/ADS — 40% below consensus** — and the severe case is $9.5–10. The distance between $20 and $12 is the quantified mean-reversion exposure, and none of it is priced: the valuation section's base case returns **+2.1%/yr** and the probability-weighted expected value including the tail is ≈ −2%.
3. **The demand is partially circular and the funding line is showing seams.** TSMC's revenue sits inside customers' capex budgets funded by *expected* AI revenue; the capex-to-revenue gap is widening and being publicly noticed (press, 2026-06-02); one flagship custom-silicon program hit a reported ~$18B financing snag (press, 2026-05-08); 1Q26 foundry demand included consumer **pre-building against tariff risk** (TrendForce, 2026-06-12) — pull-forward, not end demand. A/R days 23→29 and inventory days 76→87 YoY are each company-explained (N2 ramp) but are exactly what allocation hoarding would look like one quarter before it unwinds.
4. **The second-source window opens inside the holding period.** Intel 14A external commitments are due 2H26–1H27; Samsung's Taylor fab enters AI5 volume mid-to-2H27 on SF2; CoWoS supply-demand gap narrows ~20%→~10% by end-2026 with packaging rent fading first (business-architecture and moat sections). The 2028 design cycle is when anchor customers first negotiate with a real alternative — and the market is paying a 2030-duration multiple through that window.
5. **The tail is not paying you.** The wrapper/geopolitics section's own math says the market prices the *low end* of expert base rates for conflict (~1.0–1.25%/yr expected loss vs an actuarial ~2.3%). Even on the friendlier "fairly priced" reading, you collect **zero** excess compensation for an absorbing, non-diversifiable jurisdiction tail — at a still-record trailing multiple, ~14% off the all-time high.
6. **The market has already told you the bar.** On 2026-07-16 the company beat consensus net profit by +11.7%, raised the year twice over, added $100B to the U.S. program — and the ADS closed **−2.32%**. Expectations saturation is the short's entry evidence: when a perfect print sells off, the marginal holder is fully underwritten to excellence.

**Even-if form** [skill: thesis-legs-and-redteam, even-if structure]: even granting the bulls the moat, the pricing power, and no recession — merely a *pause* (mediocrity case: 2028 EPS ~$16.5, flat vs 2026 core, at 16×) marks the stock at ~$264–275, **−15%/yr**. The short does not need TSMC to be a bad business; it needs 2027 to be an ordinary year.

#### 3.2 The attack on the short — argued equally hard

1. **No dated break mechanism.** The short-research discipline is explicit: do not short dominant essential infrastructure without a credible dated break [skill: short-research-module; skill: equity-research-decision-framework anti-patterns]. Every observable is still accelerating: June 2026 revenue **+67.9% YoY**; Q3 guided +12.4% QoQ with a "steep" N2 ramp behind it; reported 2H26 price increases; CoWoS booked out. The earliest date a crack can *show* is 2026-10-15, and the realistic one is the January–February 2027 guide season. A short entered today pays 3–7 months of carry against a company that beats every line of its own guidance.
2. **The sandbag machine is the short-killer.** The governance section's guidance ledger shows systematic calibrated sandbagging — FY26 guided "close to 30%" → "above 30%" → "slightly above 40%" in six months; Q2'26 beat its guide on every line. The bear must beat the *guidance floor*, not consensus: near-term estimate revisions are still **up** (+4–7% on FY26 EPS mechanically), which is the worst tape to short into.
3. **Carry and compounding.** EPS compounded +30.7%/yr 2019–25 on a flat share count with zero dilution; FY26E is +50%+; a 20× forward multiple is *inside* the stock's own 10-year band and below quality peers (23.8×/33.4× for the two other irreplaceable-node businesses). Time works for the long: every quarter the short waits, the "E" grows ~7–10% and the dividend ticks up (0.67% yield, NT$23+/share guided 2026). Structural bid: record EM-index inflows ($38B H1'26) buy the stock mechanically at a ~14–15% index weight.
4. **The third alternative eats the short's EPS math.** In 2023 — the best available digestion analog — shipments fell 21% but revenue fell only 9% because revenue-per-wafer rose 16%. Today the ratchet is stronger: hikes reported annual through 2029, the N2 tier at ~$30k/wafer, packaging attach growing to low-teens % of revenue. If pricing/mix does in 2027 what it did in 2023, the digestion EPS lands nearer $14–16 than $11.5–12.7 [agent-inferred], and the short's −40%-vs-consensus punchline shrinks to −20–25% — real, but no longer a fat pitch at a 20× forward entry.
5. **Take-rate expansion is live.** The ASIC wave routes through TSMC (every major hyperscaler accelerator except one reported program is TSMC-fabbed), and per dollar of AI spend that shifts from merchant GPUs to ASICs, TSMC's content share of the end-dollar *rises* (business-architecture section; direction confirmed, magnitude inference — §4 entry 8). The short's "AI slows" scenario partially self-hedges the long's unit exposure.
6. **Squeeze mechanics are absent but so is crowding** — short interest 0.57% — meaning the short earns no crowded-unwind kicker and fights a put/call ratio already defensive (1.31). The market is *not* naively long.

#### 3.3 Red-team verdict

Both cases steelman to genuine strength, and they are **not symmetric in time**: the short's evidence is structural-but-undated; the long's evidence is dated-but-expiring (proven excellence through ~2027 only). The mediocrity case — the single best over-fitting guard [skill: thesis-legs-and-redteam] — fails the long at this price (−15%/yr on a mere pause), and the absence of a dated break fails the short today. That shape — *long fails mediocrity, short fails dating* — is the signature of a name where the price, not the analysis, is the problem. Carried to §6.

### 4. Adversarial-refutation disposition ledger

[skill: adversarial-refutation-engine — False-Positive Rule: a claim is not accepted until the concrete benign/alternative path producing the same evidence has been constructed and beaten in writing; every REAL carries a fourth-instance-style exhaust note; no UNKNOWN may ride into the call. Convention: **REAL** = claim survives refutation; **BENIGN** = the alternative path holds, claim killed/softened; **UNKNOWN** = unresolved, with the resolving evidence named.]

| # | Claim (owner) | Refutation attempt — the benign/alternative path, constructed | Disposition |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | "67.7% GM is ~8–11pp above sustainable" (normalization section) | Alternative path A: *the margin step is fully structural* (node monopoly + mix + repriced value). Test: peers got no lift (UMC 29.2%, GlobalFoundries 27.6%), so the lift is idiosyncratic — but the decomposition still finds ~3–4pp depreciation timing (D&A intensity 22.9%→15.5% on a 5-yr-life artifact that mechanically reverses), ~2.5–4pp shortage-conditional rent (hikes into >100% utilization, CoWoS booked out), ~0.6pp FX. Path A fails: it cannot explain why D&A intensity fell 7pp while capacity was being *added*. Alternative path B (the governance section's counterpoint): *TSMC's 5-year depreciation is conservative vs peers' 8-year lives, so margins are understated ~4–5pp — the "above sustainable" claim double-counts.* Path B fails as a refutation because it is **cross-sectional, not intertemporal**: on TSMC's own consistent policy, the holiday and its reversal are real cash-cost dynamics regardless of what peers book. Reconciliation: both sections stand — use 57–60% (point 58%) as TSMC's own mid-cycle band, and use the +4–5pp peer-accounting adjustment **only** for peer comparisons, never added to TSMC's forward path. Exhaust note: the same timing artifact checked across 2019–2025 — flattery peaks ~2–3 years after each capex trough (2020–21, 2025–1H26), penalizes during builds (2019, 2023) — a recurring mechanism, not a one-off. | **REAL** (gap band 7.7–10.7pp vs the 57–60% band; the evidence-file phrasing "~11–13pp above the 2020–24 band" is vs a stale band and must NOT be used as the reversion target — flag for synthesis) |
| 2 | "The price implies ~18.4% EPS CAGR 2026→30 at a 20× terminal" (valuation/expectations section) | Arithmetic re-verified: $409.74 = ~$21 dividends + (20× × EPS₂₀₃₀)/1.10⁵ → EPS₂₀₃₀ = $31.30 → 18.4% off consensus $15.91. Benign paths: (a) *base-EPS choice* — off the guide-anchored core $16.5 the required CAGR is ~17.3%; (b) *discount rate* — at r = 8% it is 15.7%. Neither path breaks the conclusion: every variant sits at or above TSMC's best-ever 5-year revenue CAGR window (21.9%, containing two supercycles) once margins are held near records simultaneously. The framing "priced for excellence" survives all parameterizations. | **REAL** (state the band 15.7–21.2%, headline ~17–19%) |
| 3 | "Consensus 2027 embeds no normalization" (valuation/expectations section) | Benign path: *the implied ~51% NPM is an artifact of an assumed revenue denominator (~$203B); a higher consensus 2027 revenue would imply a normalizing margin.* Test: 2027 revenue consensus was premium-gated (evidence file 05 gap), so try the extremes: even at +28% revenue growth (matching the EPS growth rate), implied NPM is ~50% — still record-level; at +15% it is ~54%. There is **no plausible denominator** under which NT$128.8 embeds management's own dilution stack. Benign path fails. | **REAL** (the softening: it is unknowable whether the *margin* or the *revenue* line carries the error — either way consensus prices upcycle economics as run-rate) |
| 4 | "The depreciation wave turns 2H26–2027 (FY27 D&A +22–28%)" (normalization section) | Benign paths: (a) *capex under-delivery/service-date slippage* — fails: the budget was raised twice to $60–64B, N2 fabs entered service 4Q25, and management itself guides FY26 depreciation +high-teens; (b) *revenue outruns it* — partially holds: at FY27 revenue +20%, D&A intensity is ~flat and GM holds ≥62–63%. | **REAL on dollars, CONDITIONAL on margins** — the wave is certain; its GM effect is a dependent function of leg L3. Flag for synthesis: never cite the depreciation wave as a standalone bear point without the revenue conditional |
| 5 | "The moat is defensible to ~2030" (moat section) | Adversarial path: *Nova Lake insourcing + reported Maia 2 on 18A-P + Tesla dual-source are the start of systematic second-sourcing, not idiosyncratic events.* Test against the boundary pattern: all three defectors either own a fab, own the architecture, or don't need the frontier; zero flagship-tier defections; 14A has zero committed external customers; SF2 yields still reported below TSMC's; N2 anchors multiplied (~15 customers, 4× tape-outs). The adversarial path cannot yet produce a single flagship counter-example — it fails today, with three dated shorteners (14A commitment by 1H27; SF2 ≥70% yields + flagship award by end-2027; A14 slip at the April-2027 symposium). Exhaust note: searched the socket book (business-architecture and moat sections) for a fourth defection instance beyond the three known — none found; Qualcomm's N2-wave absence is the nearest watch item. | **REAL** (moat intact and dated; confidence ~80–85% to 2030, decaying after) |
| 6 | "Clean FY26 EPS ≈ $15.5–16.5/ADS" (governance and valuation sections) | The two numbers conflict on the surface. Benign path: *they measure different bases* — holds. $15.53 is stale published consensus minus the one-off VIS (Vanguard International Semiconductor) disposal gain; $16.5 is the guide-anchored mechanical build (1H26 actual NT$49.33/common + guided 3Q + implied 4Q ≈ NT$105.4 core) net of the same NT$63.2B gain. Since the guide ("slightly above 40%") is primary-sourced and consensus demonstrably lags it by 4–5% on revenue, the guide-anchored number is the better estimator. | **BENIGN conflict — resolved: use $16.5 core as the working FY26 number, $15.5 as the consensus floor.** Both sections survive; the synthesis should quote "$16.5 core (consensus floor $15.5)" |

### 6. The decision

[skill: equity-research-decision-framework — decisive call, one-line rationale, exactly two flip triggers, monitoring plan, JSON audit trace; DO-NOTHING gate respected]

#### DO-NOTHING-WATCHLIST

**One-line rationale:** At $409.74 the price underwrites excellence through 2030 while the evidence proves it only through ~2027; the base case returns ~+2%/yr with negative skew (+17.8% bull vs −15%/−27% mediocrity/bear plus an absorbing tail), the load-bearing demand leg is an honest UNKNOWN until the January–February 2027 capex guides, and the refutation-ledger discipline converts a standing UNKNOWN on the load-bearing leg into exactly this call.

**Why not the other five lanes** (the honesty the DO-NOTHING gate demands): **BUY** fails the mediocrity case — a mere pause costs a third of the position; conviction may not live in the bull cell [skill: thesis-legs-and-redteam]. **ACCUMULATE-ON-WEAKNESS** fails as a standing order because "weakness" here is more likely to be information than noise: the plausible path to a materially lower price runs *through* the kill signals (monthly deceleration, GM miss, capex guide-down), at which point the fair value has moved down with the price; accumulation is only right if the price falls while the legs hold — which is precisely what flip trigger 1 plus the monitoring plan tests, so it is handled as a trigger, not a stance. **TRIM** is a holder's answer, not a fresh-money answer (and for holders the same analysis supports harvesting toward neutral — noted for the synthesis, not this card). **AVOID** overstates the negative: the business quality, balance sheet (~US$78–83B net cash), compounding record (EPS +30.7%/yr 2019–25 on a flat share count), and the datable resolution calendar make this a name to watch actively, not to blacklist. **SELL/SHORT** fails the short-research gates: no dated break mechanism before 2026-10 at the earliest, a sandbagging serial-beater into a 2H26 N2 ramp, ~30%+ EPS compounding as carry against you, and a structural EM-index bid — a correct thesis with an unpayable path is not a position [skill: short-research-module].

This is not "insufficient evidence" in the thin-file sense — the file is deep; it is **insufficient edge at this price**: our view equals the marginal holder's view on the only variable that matters and neither of us can observe it yet. Saying so is the capital-allocation answer the framework requires.

#### Exactly two flip triggers

1. **FLIP → BUY:** *Aggregate calendar-2027 capital-expenditure guidance from the four largest hyperscalers, as published on their Q4'26 earnings calls, is ≥ +20% year-over-year — deadline **2027-02-28**.* (Atomic: one aggregate number vs one threshold. Observable: public earnings calls. Tests leg **L3**, the load-bearing UNKNOWN.) Firing resolves ledger entry 10 to REAL-for-the-long, pushes the bull-cell weight above the 35–40% bar the valuation section set, and extends the proven-excellence horizon into 2028. Execution note (not part of the condition): the BUY assumes entry near current levels; if the tape has already re-rated above ~$470 (the bull-path exit territory) when it fires, re-run the reverse-DCF before acting.
2. **FLIP → SELL/AVOID:** *TSMC monthly revenue growth prints below +15% year-over-year for two consecutive months — any window before **2027-12-31**.* (Atomic: one metric with a consecutive-period criterion. Observable: company IR monthly release, ~10th of each month. Tests leg **L3** from the bear side.) Firing makes the digestion scenario modal (FY27 EPS path $11.5–12.7, softened toward $14–16 only if the December-2026 pricing round confirmed hypothesis H-3), converts the undated short into a dated one, and — per ledger entry 9 — should be trusted *ahead of* guidance, which will move last.

# PART III — Deep-Cut Modules (second-wave, single-skill depth)


> Provenance: fourteen further passes, each exhausting one or a few specific library skills against the same evidence base, written accretive-only against Parts I–II.


## Section III.1 — Product-Cycle Clocks and the Non-AI Engine

*Scope: the ~34% of TSMC's book that is not HPC (high-performance computing) — smartphone (22% of 2Q26 revenue), IoT (Internet of Things, 5%), automotive (4%), DCE (digital consumer electronics, 1%), others (2%) — plus the product-cycle clocks governing the whole book. Node economics, the AI profit pool, moat replication arithmetic, and the digestion scenario are owned by Section II.1–Section II.2; this extends, not repeats. Platform shares per TSMC's 2Q26 Management Report [P1]; segment US$ revenues are computed as platform % × reported US$ revenue [agent-inferred: computed].*

---

### 1. The smartphone engine: a declining-unit TAM that pays TSMC more every year

[skill: product-cycle-and-replacement-clock / Stage 1–2, module: REPLACEMENT_CYCLE_DEFERRAL_AS_ELASTIC_DEMAND.md; skill: tech-hardware-and-semiconductors / modules: PRODUCT_CYCLE_TAM_UNITS_AND_COMMODITIZATION.md, ADJACENT_SEGMENT_SILICON_CONTENT_TAILWIND.md]

**The unit clock is saturated-to-declining.** Global smartphone shipments: ~1.17B (2023, the decade trough), ~1.23B (2024) [agent-inferred: back-computed from IDC's cited 2025 figures], 1.25B in 2025 (+1.5%, a record 247.4M of them Apple) [W1], and a forecast **1.09B in 2026, −13.9% — the steepest annual contraction in smartphone history** — as the AI-driven memory shortage (LPDDR diverted to HBM/server DRAM) inflates bills of materials 8–15% and prices out the low end [W2][W3]. IDC then models −1.1% in 2027 and +5.5% in 2028 as memory normalizes [W2]. The replacement clock has stretched for a decade: ~43.4 months at the 2023 peak (Counterpoint), holding periods now ~3.5–4.2 years [W4][W5]. Per the deferral-elasticity module, "must eventually replace" is true but elastic — so the 2026 memory shock is a textbook deferral event, not demand destruction: phones not bought in 2026–27 age into a datable 2028 echo cohort [skill: product-cycle-and-replacement-clock / module: STIMULUS_ECHO_REPLACEMENT_CYCLE.md, run on a price-shock deferral rather than a subsidy spike].

**Yet TSMC's smartphone revenue doubled across this stagnation.** Computed from TSMC platform disclosures [P1][P2][P3][P4]:

| Year | Smartphone % of revenue | Smartphone US$ revenue [computed] | Global units | TSMC $/global unit [computed] |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2019 | 49% | ~$17.0B | ~1.37B | ~$12 |
| 2021 | 44% | ~$25.0B | ~1.35B | ~$18 |
| 2022 | 39% | ~$29.6B | ~1.21B | ~$24 |
| 2023 | 38% | ~$26.3B | ~1.17B | ~$22 |
| 2024 | ~34.5% | ~$31.1B | ~1.23B | ~$25 |
| 2025 | 29% | ~$35.5B | 1.25B | ~$28 |
| 2026E | ~23% | **~$40B [agent-inferred]** | 1.09B | **~$37** |

2026E build: 1H26 actual smartphone revenue = 26%×$35.90B + 22%×$40.20B = **$18.2B** [P1][P5]; 2H26 at ~23% of the ~$96B implied by the ~$172B full-year frame ("slightly above 40%" guide [P6]) ≈ $22B, helped by the iPhone-18 ramp — the "steep ramp-up of our 2-nanometer technology" is the stated driver of the Q3 guide [P7]. Net: **~$40B, +13% YoY, in the worst unit year ever recorded.** The share percentage falls (29%→23%) only because the HPC denominator explodes; the dollar line keeps compounding — the module's warning against reading platform mix % as segment health [skill: tech-hardware-and-semiconductors / module: PRODUCT_CYCLE_TAM_UNITS_AND_COMMODITIZATION.md — unit TAM, content, share, and ASP are four separate clocks].

**Decomposing the content engine.** Three stacked drivers, none unit-dependent:

1. **Die size / NPU (neural processing unit) area.** Primary anchor, C.C. Wei, 2Q25 call: on edge devices "the number of the units is actually mild, but the die size increased... by about 5% to 10%. And that kind of trend continued" [P8]. Product-level corroboration: the A20 Pro (iPhone 18, fall 2026) shows a visibly enlarged NPU at unchanged package size, on N2 with WMCM (wafer-level multi-chip module) packaging [W6][W7] — more TSMC silicon *and* a packaging attach per flagship.
2. **Node-migration price ladder.** Flagship APs (application processors) move N3→N2 through 2026–27: Apple A20/M6 on N2, Apple reportedly holding >50% of initial capacity [W8]; MediaTek Dimensity 9600 on N2 late 2026 [W9]; N2 wafers reportedly ~$30k vs $18–27k for N3 (unofficial) [W10], plus reported ~+5% smartphone-tier hikes in 2026, annual through ~2029 [W11].
3. **GenAI mix.** GenAI-capable smartphones: 36% of 2025 shipments → 45% (2026E) → >50% (2027E) (Counterpoint) [W12][W13] — each conversion moves a socket up the content curve.

**Sizing the tailwind — content × units × share, 2026→2029 [agent-inferred, math shown].** Take 2026E TSMC content per global unit ≈ $37 (above; the 2026 jump is partly mix — the units dying are low-end phones with minimal TSMC content, so TSMC-weighted units fall far less than global units). Assume forward content/unit growth of **+8–10%/yr** (die +5–10%/gen per management, +~5%/yr price hikes, N2→A16 migration; deliberately below the ~+15%/yr realized 2019–2025). Unit path per IDC: 1.09B → 1.08B (2027) → 1.14B (2028) → ~1.16B (2029E, +2% assumed). Then:

- 2029 smartphone revenue ≈ 1.16B × $46–49 ≈ **$53–57B** → **+$13–17B over 2026E (~10–12% CAGR)**.
- Zero-unit-growth stress (units stuck at 1.09B): ≈ **$50–53B → still +$10–13B.**
- Module falsifier check [ADJACENT_SEGMENT_SILICON_CONTENT_TAILWIND.md]: is content growth multi-generation (yes — A18→A19→A20 NPU enlargement, management's "trend continued") and does host-segment volume decline cancel it (2026 answers this empirically: −13.9% units, +13% TSMC smartphone revenue — the content clock out-ran the worst unit print on record)?

**The salience-inversion test.** The module predicts the market anchors on the glamorous new TAM and rounds the boring content engine to zero. Measured on this quarter's coverage: all four reported bull arguments and all four bear arguments in the consensus evidence file are AI/geopolitics/valuation; smartphone appears only as the *weakness being offset* ("AI-server/HPC demand... offsetting smartphone/PC weakness" — Bloomberg Intelligence, 2026-07-16 [W14]). Meanwhile the "weak" segment is on track to add 2026→2029 revenue (+$13–17B) equal to **~two-thirds of TSMC's entire 2025 AI-accelerator base (~$21–22B, "high-teens %" of revenue [P3])**. Inversion confirmed: the market stares at HPC while smartphone content compounds silently at ~$4–5B/yr.

### 2. The edge-AI replacement supercycle: bull claim vs. actuals, with dated kill criteria

[skill: product-cycle-and-replacement-clock / Stage 2–3, module: ASP_UPLIFT_CATALYZED_BY_THE_REFRESH_EVENT.md]

**The bull claim:** on-device AI forces an accelerated replacement wave in phones and PCs 2026–2028 — "over 315 million iPhones haven't been upgraded in four years" [W15]; Gartner had AI PCs at 31% of 2025 PC shipments [W16], IDC at 143M AI PCs = ~55% of the 2026 market [W17]; Windows-10 end-of-support was supposed to compound the PC leg.

**The actuals refuse to confirm it.** Units: 2024 ~+6% (post-slump recovery), 2025 +1.5%, 2026E −13.9% [W1][W2]. Replacement cycles *lengthened* through the entire AI-feature era [W4][W5]. The forecast-revision history is itself evidence: IDC saw 2026 at −0.9% in December 2025, −13% by February, −13.9% by mid-2026 [W1][W18][W2] — the AI wave provided no floor. PCs: the Windows-10 + AI-PC year is now forecast **down 5–9%** (IDC scenarios; broader personal-computing −10.4%) because memory inflation swamped the refresh [W19][W20]. And the strongest phone cycle in years — iPhone 17, Restate to what the source supports — 'best-selling phone globally in 2025 [W21]' — or keep the 1Q26 ranking only with a 1Q26-dated source added to the reference list., record 247.4M Apple units in 2025 [W1] — is precisely the module's *refresh-event ASP uplift*: an aged cohort hit its natural replacement moment and stepped up to the current AI-capable, higher-content generation. The AI silicon rode the existing clock; it did not accelerate it. GenAI arrives as **attach-at-refresh** (36%→45%→52% of shipments [W12][W13]) — exactly what §1's content math monetizes, and worth more to TSMC per unit than the supercycle narrative would be.

**Confirm signals (dated):** IDC 4Q26 tracker (~late Jan 2027) landing better than −13.9% with premium mix intact; Apple FQ4'26 (Oct 2026) / FQ1'27 (Jan 2027) showing iPhone 18/A20 units *and* ASP up despite memory-inflated prices; Qualcomm's ~Nov 2026 guide raising handset *units*, not just mix. **The clean experiment is the 2028 rebound:** deferral-echo alone ≈ IDC's +5.5% [W2]; a genuine supercycle shows as 2028 overshooting toward +8–10% with replacement-cycle length finally *shortening* in Counterpoint's series [W4]. **Kill signals:** 2027 units below IDC's −1.1%; NA premium (the AI showcase tier) down >6–7% again in 2027 [W2]; GenAI penetration stalling below ~45% in 2026 [W12]. If 2028 prints ~+5% and cycles keep lengthening, the supercycle is dead — the content engine (§1) is unaffected either way.

### 3. Automotive and IoT: one slow-compounding clock, one genuinely stagnant shelf

[skill: product-cycle-and-replacement-clock / Stage 1 map; skill: tech-hardware-and-semiconductors / module: ADJACENT_SEGMENT_SILICON_CONTENT_TAILWIND.md]

**Automotive: the same content-over-units structure as smartphone, one decade earlier in its curve.** Global light-vehicle units are ~flat (~90M/yr), but semiconductor content per vehicle runs ~$500–600 (ICE) to >$1,500 (EV), average ~$1,000 today → ~$1,400 by 2030 (+40%) [W22]; ADAS (advanced driver-assistance systems) silicon specifically: $165 (2022) → $285 (2025) → $420E (2030), the fastest-growing slice at ~15.6% CAGR [W23][W24]. TSMC's automotive platform revenue [computed from P1–P4]: ~$1.4B (2019, 4%) → $3.8B (2022, +74% YoY) → $4.2B (2023, +15% — it grew straight through the 2023 semis downcycle) → ~$4.5B (2024) → **$6.1B (2025, +34% YoY [P3])** → ~$7.3B 2026E run-rate (4% of revenue; +15% QoQ in 2Q26 [P1]). A 4.4x in six years that looks like "4–5% forever" only because the denominator quadrupled.

The design-in pipeline dates the next leg: automotive compute is migrating from lagging-edge MCUs to leading-edge SoCs on TSMC — Renesas R-Car X5H, the industry's first 3nm automotive SoC (announced 2024-11-13, ~2028 vehicles) [W25]; NVIDIA Thor-class and the major ADAS platforms on N4/N3-class nodes [agent-inferred from public platform disclosures]; and TSMC's own roadmap now carries **N2A (automotive 2nm), qualification 2028** and N16HV in 2026 [P9]. Automotive qualification cycles are 4–6 years, so 2026's design-ins are 2029–2031 revenue — a clock, not a catalyst. Watch: Tesla AI5/AI6 dual-sourced TSMC + Samsung [W26] marks automotive as the *contestable* tier (see §5). 2029E automotive revenue at a 15–20% CAGR: **~$11–13B [agent-inferred]**.

**IoT: the stagnation is real, and it is structural, not cyclical.** IoT platform revenue [computed]: $6.8B (2022, 9%) → $5.5B (2023, −17% [P4]) → ~$5.3B (2024) → $6.1B (2025, +15% [P3]) — 2025 merely regained 2022's level, and the 2Q26 share is 5% (~$2.0B/qtr). Diagnosis per the durability-paradox module [skill: product-cycle-and-replacement-clock / module: DURABILITY_PARADOX_LONG_LIFE_WEAK_REPEAT.md]: consumer IoT endpoints (wearables, earbuds, smart home, meters) are long-lived, near-saturated per household, and carry no consumable pull — no replacement clock, so growth needs new categories, which have not arrived at scale. Structurally, IoT silicon sits on N7-and-older specialty nodes where there is no ladder to climb — content per device is static, ASPs erode by default [PRODUCT_CYCLE_TAM_UNITS_AND_COMMODITIZATION.md §4], and TSMC is actively *shrinking* the commodity tail (6-inch Fab 2 and 8-inch Fab 5 reportedly closing by end-2027 [W27]; management: "we reduce our eight inch wafers capacity and six inch" [P3]). Edge-AI could reset this shelf (an inference-capable MCU class would restart the content clock); until attach evidence exists, model IoT at +5–8%/yr: **~$10–11B by 2029E [agent-inferred]**. DCE (1%) is a managed run-off.

### 4. PC/client and the Intel-tile reversal: swing capacity, read from the customer's side

[skill: swing-capacity-and-outsourcing-reversal / Stages 1–4, module: CONSTRAINED_CUSTOMER_OVEREARNING.md]

Section II.2 §2 read Nova Lake as a moat data-point (the capitulation-tell reversal). Here we run the *supplier-revenue* framework the skill actually prescribes: was Intel's outsourcing base demand, swing capacity, or a bridge?

**Stage-1 role map.** Customer-owned capacity: yes — Intel operates leading-edge fabs and was ramping 18A through 2025. Product/IP/roadmap ownership: entirely Intel's. Outsourcing reason: 18A was not ready/yielding at flagship-launch risk tolerance — Arrow Lake/Lunar Lake went to N3, and the original Nova Lake plan put **60–70% of compute tiles on TSMC N2** [W28]. Contract structure: product-generation purchase orders, no disclosed take-or-pay. Insourcing ability: proven — 18A shipped Panther Lake at CES 2026-01-05 [W29], yields "improved significantly" (reported ~7%/month defect-density gains) [W30]. **Verdict: a *technology-maturation bridge*, not base load** — the module's learning-bridge pattern: the customer buys the frontier component externally until its own process matures, then brings volume home for margin and fixed-cost absorption [CONSTRAINED_CUSTOMER_OVEREARNING.md, Learning-Bridge Reversion]. The reversal arrived on schedule: TrendForce, 2026-07-15 — Intel to insource **80–90% of Nova Lake compute tiles to 18A**, cutting TSMC to 10–20% [W31].

**Revenue at risk, modeled [agent-inferred, math shown].** Intel-as-customer is not disclosed in TSMC's 20-F customer table; Section II.1/Section II.2 carry it at ~$2–4B/yr on N3 (Arrow/Lunar Lake era). Had Nova Lake ramped at 60–70% of tiles on N2 through 2027–28, Intel's TSMC bill plausibly ran **$3–5B/yr**; at 10–20% it is **$0.7–1.5B** → **~$2.5–3.5B/yr of 2027–28 revenue at risk, ≈1.5–2% of the ~$172B 2026E base**. The sensitivity is regime-dependent, and that is the actual finding:

| Regime | Backfill of vacated N2 slots | Net P&L effect |
|---|---|---|
| Sold-out (today: N2 tape-outs ~4x N3 at same stage [W32]; NVIDIA reportedly ~3x short of advanced-node supply) | ~100% — the queue absorbs the slots | ≈ zero; option value lost, not revenue |
| 2027 digestion (Section II.3 §5.3 scenario) | Partial to none | $2.5–3.5B lands at ~60%+ decremental gross margin ≈ −$1.5–2B gross profit, stacking ~0.5–1pp on the digestion GM bridge |

The Intel reversal is thus a **conditionally material** risk: free today, real exactly when the AI cushion deflates. It should be carried inside the digestion scenario, not as a standalone headline.

**Generalizing: who else can actually insource?** Score the book per the module's share-causation taxonomy (earned / forced / rented / inherited / subsidized / transitional):

| Customer | Internal leading-edge capacity? | Share class | Reversal clock |
|---|---|---|---|
| Apple (~17% of 2025 rev [P10]) | None | **Earned** (multi-gen design-in, A20→A14) | Foundry-switch only; no credible alternative at mobile PPA before ~2028 (Section II.2 §3) |
| NVIDIA (~19% [P10]) | None | **Earned + capacity-forced stay** | Constraint is TSMC supply, not intent |
| Hyperscaler ASICs (Google/AWS/Microsoft/Meta/OpenAI) | **None — hyperscalers cannot insource fabs**; they can only second-source | Earned, **deliberately transitional at the margin** | Every ASIC generation (~12–18mo); Maia 2 → Intel 18A-P is the observed leverage-seeding move [W33] |
| Qualcomm / MediaTek | None (fabless) | Earned, historically **transitional** (Qualcomm dual-sourced Samsung before) | Each Snapdragon/Dimensity generation; watch SF2 maturity |
| Tesla | None, but dual-sources by policy | **Transitional** | Already split: AI5/AI6 on TSMC + Samsung [W26] |
| Intel | **Yes — 18A/14A** | **Bridge (now reversing)** | Executed 2026-07; residual 10–20% is tactical |
| Samsung LSI | **Yes — SF2** (self-supply) | Self-supply; hits TSMC *indirectly* by displacing Qualcomm sockets (Exynos 2600 in ~25% of Galaxy S26 [W34]) | Each Galaxy generation |

**Swing-capacity verdict:** only Intel and Samsung LSI possess credible internal capacity, and both are now exercising it. Total insourcing-exposed revenue ≈ Intel $2.5–3.5B + Qualcomm-socket displacement via Exynos ~$1–2B [agent-inferred] ≈ **2–3% of 2026E revenue**. The remaining ~97% is base-load demand from customers who structurally *cannot* insource — the strongest possible answer a supplier can score on this skill's gate. The hyperscaler row is the one to monitor: they cannot insource, but they are the most motivated second-source seeders, and their ASIC re-bid cadence (12–18 months) is the shortest freedom-of-action calendar in the book.

### 5. Turning the copycat lens on TSMC itself: which edges decay, and where the price umbrella leaks

[skill: temporary-product-edge-and-copycat-commoditization / Stages 1–4, modules: FALSE_MOAT_AND_INCUMBENT_NORMALIZATION.md, PRIOR_SUBSTITUTION_AS_BASE_RATE.md, INCUMBENCY_AS_LAUNCH_PARTNER_IN_NASCENT_CATEGORY.md]

Section II.2 established the replication arithmetic (~2030 window). Here we run the decay tests edge-by-edge, as the skill requires — including against the bull's own position.

**Edge 1 — N2 process lead: real, qualification-protected, decaying on a ~2028–30 clock.** Copycat timeline (Stage 2): Samsung SF2 is *shipping* (Exynos 2600 since ~Sept 2025; Tesla AI5 tape-out complete 2026-07-13, Taylor fab entering production [W35]) at reported mid-50s% yields vs TSMC N2's 60–70% [W36]; Intel 18A ships Panther Lake internally [W29] with 14A holding **zero committed external customers** as of Jan 2026 [W37]; Rapidus has a 2nm pilot line, first PDK (process design kit) shipped Feb 2026, >60 prospects, **zero volume agreements**, 2027 mass production at a boutique 25–30k wafers/month [W38][W39]; SMIC is export-walled at DUV 5nm, ~20% reported yields [W40]. Stage-1 classification: the edge is protected by qualification, yield learning, and switching cost — not merely timing. But "2nm-class capability exists elsewhere" is now true for the first time since ~2018; what does not exist elsewhere is 2nm-class *economics*. Catch-up dates: Samsung parity-capable on non-flagship silicon **now**, flagship-credible ~2028 if SF2P yields mature; Intel externally credible only upon a marquee 14A commitment (window 2H26–1H27, Intel's own schedule [W37]); Rapidus ~2029–30 at scale.

**Edge 2 — CoWoS/advanced packaging: the *fastest-decaying* edge, partly by TSMC's own choice.** TSMC holds >90% of CoWoS-class output [W41], but the copy clock is 2–3 years, not 7: packaging is precision assembly, not transistor physics, and TSMC itself outsources ~240–270k CoWoS wafers/yr to OSATs (outsourced assembly-and-test: Amkor ~180–190k, SPIL ~60–80k) [W42] — qualified second sources already exist *inside* the supply chain, OSATs are adding ~50–60k wpm, and the supply-demand gap is projected to narrow from ~20% to ~10% by end-2026 [W43]. The scarcity premium dies by capacity response, on schedule; what survives is the SoIC/CoWoS-L system-integration roadmap (14-reticle packages, 2028–29 [P9]) with real per-generation design lock-in. Underwrite packaging *leadership* as durable, packaging *rents* as a 2025–27 window.

**Edge 3 — Yield/execution culture: hardest to copy, the residual claim.** Arizona Fab 21 reached defect density "almost equal to Taiwan" in its first HVM year [P3]; N2 entered HVM "with good yield" at two sites simultaneously [P3]; the counterexamples proving the difficulty are Samsung's GAA-first 3nm yield struggle and Intel's 18A maturation arc. No dated copy clock exists for organizational capability — the one edge FALSE_MOAT analysis affirms rather than punctures: it survived the incumbent-normalization test three consecutive times (28nm, EUV at N7+, GAA at N2).

**Edge 4 — PDK/EDA ecosystem lock-in: the compounding edge.** Fabless design flows, IP libraries, and sign-off methodologies (Cadence/Synopsys reference flows) default to TSMC; N2 tape-outs at ~4x N3 same-stage [W32] is the flywheel, measured. Per the incumbency-as-launch-partner module — the deliberate *inversion* of this skill's default suspicion — the integrated incumbent becomes the default launch partner for every new silicon program because adopting it changes nothing (OpenAI's first chip: TSMC N3; second: TSMC A16 [W44]), while a challenger must re-qualify dozens of flows. Consequence: **TSMC can cede share in a growing leading-edge TAM and still compound** — the correct model for 2027–2030, and why "Samsung/Intel win some sockets" and "TSMC compounds" are not contradictions.

**The price-umbrella problem (Stage 3 erosion bridge).** TSMC's reported 2026 pricing — +3–10% across advanced nodes (smartphone ~+5%, CPU ~+7%, HPC up to +10%), a reported further ~15% on 3nm in 2H26, annual hikes planned through ~2029, N2 at ~$30k [W11][W45][W10] — is the umbrella under which every competitor now bids. Where is its edge — who defects first, at what discount [agent-inferred throughout]:

| Tier | Defection economics | Evidence state |
|---|---|---|
| Flagship mobile/GPU (Apple, NVIDIA, AMD) | Defect only at *yield parity*, not price — silicon cost is small vs. device economics; AMD pays the Arizona premium voluntarily ("5–20% more... worth it") [W46] | No defection; A20→A14 and Rubin→A16 design-ins deepen |
| Hyperscaler ASIC gen-N+1 | Defect at *strategic* discounts (leverage-seeding), ~free option on 18A-P/SF2 | **One observed: Maia 2 → Intel 18A-P** [W33] |
| Mid-volume auto/robotics (Tesla-class) | Defect at ~15–25% effective discount + supply security | **Observed: AI5/AI6 dual-source** [W26][W35] |
| Mid-tier mobile APs | Defect at ~20–30% discount if SF2 yields hold mid-50s+ | Not yet; Qualcomm's N2-wave absence is the watch item [W9] |
| Trailing/mature nodes | Umbrella already leaking — SMIC/Hua Hong/Nexchip raised prices ~10% into tight mature capacity [W47]; TSMC is exiting the bottom (6/8-inch closures [W27]) | Structural, export-politics-shaped |

The prior-substitution base rate disciplines the complacency case: the last time a customer felt over-dependent at the flagship tier it split the socket — Apple's A9 was dual-sourced TSMC/Samsung in 2015 and reverted to sole-source only because the Samsung parts underperformed [agent-inferred: widely documented history]. The umbrella's edge is not hypothetical; it activates wherever a copycat reaches *good-enough* yield at a discount that matters to the buyer's BoM. Today that describes Tesla-class silicon and second-gen hyperscaler ASICs — ~2–4% of revenue — and does not describe flagships. Reprice annually against Samsung yield prints and Intel's 14A anchor-customer clock (2H26–1H27).

### 6. Net verdict: stack the clocks, and name the mispricing

[skill: product-cycle-and-replacement-clock / Stage 4 decision card]

**Segment bridge, 2026E → 2029E [agent-inferred; all math above]:**

| Segment | 2026E | 2029E | Δ | Clock verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Smartphone | ~$40B | ~$53–57B | **+$13–17B** | *Reset via content* — units irrelevant within ±10%; falsifier: die-size trend breaks or premium tier cracks |
| Automotive | ~$7.3B | ~$11–13B | +$4–6B | *Normal cycle + content*, design-in-dated (N2A 2028) |
| IoT | ~$9B | ~$10–11B | +$1–2B | *Decay-to-flat*; durability paradox until an edge-AI attach appears |
| DCE + others | ~$6B | ~$6–7B | ~0 | Managed run-off |
| **Non-AI stack** | **~$62B (36%)** | **~$80–88B** | **+$18–26B** | Compounds ~9–12%/yr with **zero credit in the narrative** |
| HPC (owned by Section II.1–Section II.2) | ~$110B | path-dependent: ~$120–140B digestion / $190B+ momentum | — | The only clock the market watches |

Three conclusions. **First**, the non-AI engine alone adds ~$20B of revenue by 2029 — comparable to TSMC's entire 2025 AI-accelerator revenue — on a demand base (replacement cohorts, content physics, automotive qualification pipelines) nearly orthogonal to hyperscaler capex. In Section II.3's digestion scenario it is the shock absorber: a +9–12% non-AI stack offsets ~3.2–4.3pp/yr of HPC decline at the consolidated line [agent-inferred: 36% weight × 9–12%]. **Second**, the most mispriced clock is the **smartphone content clock**: consensus reads 22%-and-falling as secular decay ("offsetting smartphone weakness"), when the dollar line just grew +13% through the worst unit market ever printed, on drivers — die size, node price ladder, GenAI attach — that require no unit growth. The cheap refuter is quarterly and public: TSMC's platform table against IDC's tracker. **Third**, the most mispriced *date* is 2028: the 2026–27 memory-crisis deferral plants an echo cohort that retires into N2/A14-generation, GenAI-default devices — units *and* forced mix-shift arriving together [STIMULUS_ECHO_REPLACEMENT_CYCLE.md] — on the same calendar as A16/A14 ramps and first N2A automotive volume. If an AI-capex digestion trough also lands in 2027 (Section II.3 §5.3), the boring engine's echo is what the recovery is built on; the long-side symmetry of this pull-forward belongs on the 2027 shopping list, not the 2026 one [skill: product-cycle-and-replacement-clock / module: LONG_SIDE_SYMMETRY_OF_THE_PULL_FORWARD.md].

**Dated triggers for this section's ledger:** (i) IDC 4Q26 print, ~late Jan 2027 — 2026 units vs −13.9% and NA premium mix; (ii) Apple FQ4'26 (Oct 2026) / FQ1'27 (Jan 2027) — A20-cycle ASP and units; (iii) TSMC 4Q26 call (Jan 2027) — smartphone platform dollars and any die-size commentary update; (iv) Intel 14A external-commitment window closing 1H27; (v) Samsung S27 Exynos share and any Qualcomm N2/SF2 split disclosure; (vi) Nova Lake teardowns (2027) confirming the 10–20% residual; (vii) April 2027 TSMC symposium — N2A qualification on schedule for 2028.

## Section III.2 — Metric Comparability, Denominator Rebuild, and Fab-Level Unit Economics

**TSMC (Taiwan Semiconductor Manufacturing Company; NYSE: TSM, 1 ADS = 5 common shares; TWSE: 2330). As of 2026-07-17; TSM $409.74 (2026-07-16 close).**

Scope: this module audits whether the numbers the rest of the report leans on mean what they appear to mean, then rebuilds the economics the blended figures abstract away — one leading-edge fab. It extends (does not repeat) the blended revenue/GP-per-wafer trajectory (business-architecture section), the company-level EV/rebuild triangulation of 3.7–5.1× (valuation section), and the depreciation-policy normalization (governance section). Methods: [skill: metric-definition-and-comparability-audit], [skill: unit-economics], [skill: external-mark-and-replacement-cost-triangulation]. Anything not tied to a cited source is marked **[agent-inferred]** with math and a confidence grade.

---

### 1. Comparability audit of the standard foundry comparisons

#### 1a. "TSMC 67.7% GM vs Samsung Foundry vs Intel Foundry" — not a like-for-like comparison

Per the reporting-convention gate [skill: metric-definition-and-comparability-audit / module: REPORTING_CONVENTION_COMPARABILITY_GATE.md] and add-back normalization [module: PEER_MULTIPLE_NORMALIZE_ADDBACK_CONVENTIONS.md], the three "foundry margins" are built on different revenue perimeters, different transfer-pricing regimes, and different depreciation lives:

| Dimension | TSMC | Intel Foundry | Samsung Foundry |
|---|---|---|---|
| Revenue basis | ~100% external merchant sales, NT$1,270.4B / US$40.2B in 2Q26 (TSMC 2Q26 release, https://pr.tsmc.com/english/news/3326, 2026-07-16) | FY2025 segment revenue $17.826B, of which **external = $307M (1.7%)**; the rest is internal transfer pricing (Intel 10-K FY2025, https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/50863/000005086326000011/intc-20251227.htm, filed Jan 2026) | Not separately disclosed; sits inside the DS division (Samsung 4Q/FY25 release, https://news.samsung.com/global/samsung-electronics-announces-fourth-quarter-and-fy-2025-results); TrendForce shows $3.2B of Q1'26 foundry revenue (https://www.trendforce.com/presscenter/news/20260612-13095.html, 2026-06-12) |
| Margin disclosed | GM 67.7%, OM 60.3% (2Q26) | No GM; **operating loss −$10.318B FY25 → OM ≈ −57.9%** (computed: −10.318/17.826) | No GM; press-compiled Foundry+System LSI operating loss ≈ KRW 6.0T FY25 (TrendForce, 2025-11-13) |
| Equipment depreciation life | 5 years straight-line (FY2025 20-F PP&E policy, https://investor.tsmc.com/sites/ir/sec-filings/2025_20F%20Report.pdf, filed 2026-04-16) | 8 years **[agent-inferred: Intel extended machinery lives 5→8 years effective FY2023 per its 10-K disclosures; not re-pulled this session — verify]** | ~5 years for fab equipment **[agent-inferred from Samsung's disclosed useful-life ranges; low confidence — verify]** |
| Utilization definition | Output vs planned capacity, per node (see 1d) | Not disclosed at fab level | Press figures ("~60% overall", "~80% advanced lines") with unknown denominators (Korea Times 2026-02-24; SeDaily 2026-02-22) |

Adjusted comparison, honestly built:

1. **Perimeter.** Intel Foundry's "revenue" is 98% a bookkeeping transfer from Intel Products; its −58% operating margin measures how far internal pricing sits below full cost, not a market outcome. Samsung's foundry P&L nets internal Exynos volume (a related-party sale, not merchant demand). Only TSMC's 67.7% is a *market-cleared* margin. Verdict: **NOT COMPARABLE as labeled** — the honest cross-firm statistic is external merchant revenue: TSMC ~$40.2B/quarter vs Samsung ~$3.2B vs Intel ~$0.08B/quarter ($307M/yr ÷ 4).
2. **Depreciation-life normalization (direction matters).** TSMC's 5-year life *understates* its GM relative to Intel's 8-year convention. 2Q26 D&A was NT$198.5B (2Q26 management report cash-flow section, investor.tsmc.com, 2026-07-16) vs cost of revenue NT$410.1B. **[agent-inferred, medium confidence]**: if ~90% of D&A sits in COGS and ~85% of that is 5-year equipment (NT$152B ≈ 12.0% of revenue), stretching to an 8-year life cuts the steady-state charge by ~3/8, adding ~4.5pp of GM → TSMC's 2Q26 GM on Intel's convention ≈ **~72%**. Symmetrically, Intel's loss would be *larger* on TSMC's 5-year life. Every convention adjustment **widens** TSMC's lead; the raw comparison is conservative for TSMC and flattering for Intel.
3. **So what survives?** A ~120pp operating-margin spread that no convention bridge can flip. The comparability failure changes magnitude and trend-reading (e.g., Intel's FY25 loss "improvement" from −$13.3B is mainly lower impairments/accelerated depreciation, not operations — Intel 10-K), not the ordering. Use the spread directionally; never quote "TSMC GM premium = 67.7% − X%" as a precise number.

#### 1b. "Advanced node" — a company-defined bucket wearing a physics costume

TSMC's disclosed "advanced technologies" bucket = **7nm and below**, 77% of 2Q26 wafer revenue (2nm 3%, 3nm 30%, 5nm 33%, 7nm 11%) (2Q26 release). Three traps [module: SEMANTIC_DUE_DILIGENCE_AND_WORD_IMPORTATION.md — "advanced" and "2nm" are trusted words investors import]:

- **The bucket is TSMC-defined and share-of-revenue, not share-of-wafers.** 77% of wafer *revenue* maps to roughly ~40% of wafer *shipments* **[agent-inferred: 77% × ~$35B quarterly wafer revenue ≈ $27B at a ~$15k blended advanced ASP ⇒ ~1.8M of 4.34M shipped wafers; medium confidence]**. Statements like "77% of TSMC is advanced" are true of dollars, not fab floor.
- **nm labels are marketing, not physical dimensions.** No feature on an "N2" wafer measures 2nm; cross-firm labels (Intel "18A", Samsung "SF2") are not calibrated to each other. Even TSMC only quantifies nodes *relative to its own prior nodes* (N2: 10–15% speed or 25–30% power vs N3E — 4Q25 transcript, 2026-01-15). "Samsung is also at 2nm" is a naming fact, not a parity fact; the comparable evidence is behavioral — reported SF2 yields mid-50s% vs TSMC N2 60–70% (phonearena/wccftech, 2026, unconfirmed press), and N2 tape-outs ~4x N3-equivalent-stage (DigiTimes, 2026-07-16).
- **Bucket drift risk.** As 2nm ramps, "advanced ≤7nm" gets less informative (it now spans five price tiers ~$9k–$30k). Watch for any future redefinition (e.g., "advanced = ≤3nm") — a definition change without a bridge is a monitoring contradiction [skill: metric-definition-and-comparability-audit, Stage 2].

#### 1c. Foundry "market share" — three different denominators, three different numbers

- **Top-10 merchant revenue share (the "72.3%"):** TrendForce Q1'26: TSMC $35.86B = 72.3% of top-10 foundry revenue (https://www.trendforce.com/presscenter/news/20260612-13095.html, 2026-06-12). Denominator excludes IDM (integrated device manufacturer) internal volume (Intel's own CPUs, Samsung's internal LSI) and includes TSMC's advanced-packaging revenue (~10%+ of TSMC revenue — 4Q25 call) that mature-node peers mostly lack. It is a *revenue* share, so it overweights price.
- **Capacity share:** TSMC 2025 capacity >17M 12"-equivalent wafers (FY2025 20-F). TSMC's blended revenue per shipped wafer ≈ **$9.3k** (computed: NT$1,270.4B ÷ 4,336k wafers ÷ 31.60) vs mature-node merchant peers around ~$2.5–3.5k **[agent-inferred, medium confidence]** — so a 72% revenue share corresponds to a far smaller share of global foundry *wafer capacity* (plausibly ~25–35% — **[agent-inferred, low confidence; do not quote without a capacity census]**).
- **Leading-edge-only share:** computed honestly: TSMC 2Q26 advanced (≤7nm) wafer revenue ≈ 0.77 × ~$35B ≈ **$27B/quarter**. Generously assuming *all* Samsung foundry revenue ($3.2B) were leading-edge merchant, plus Intel external (~$0.08B) and SMIC ≤7nm (~$0.8–1.0B **[agent-inferred]**), TSMC's floor share = 27/(27+3.2+0.08+1.0) ≈ **86%**; on realistic Samsung/SMIC advanced-merchant mixes, **~90%+**.
- **Leading-edge MERCHANT capacity (≤5nm class): ~100% today, but as a stock, not a flow.** Samsung's SF2 volume today is substantially internal Exynos 2600 (Korea Times, 2025-10-20); its flagship merchant win (Tesla AI5, $16.5B agreement) reached tape-out July 2026 with volume realistically mid-to-2H 2027 (Korea Times/TechTimes, 2026-07-13/14); Intel 18A external commitments (Microsoft Maia 2 [REPORT], AWS fabric chip) are pre-revenue-scale, and Intel has **zero committed external 14A customers** (Intel Jan 2026 call, Tom's Hardware, Jan 2026). So "~100% of leading-edge merchant capacity" is accurate *as of mid-2026* and should be written with a decay date: contracted competing merchant supply arrives 2027–28. Never blend the three shares; each answers a different question (pricing power → merchant revenue share; replacement cost → capacity share; customer alternatives → leading-edge merchant share).

#### 1d. ">100% utilization" — a denominator artifact, and a modeling constraint

3nm lines were reported "above 100% utilization" at the Q2'26 call (BigGo call coverage, 2026-07-16 [REPORT]). Utilization = output ÷ *rated* capacity, where rated capacity is a planning number (standard mix, standard cycle time). Output exceeds it via tool-productivity gains, mix, and fewer engineering wafers — not physics. Two consequences: (i) **cross-firm utilization comparisons are meaningless** (Samsung's "~60%", SMIC's "95.8%" use different denominators); (ii) **there is no utilization upside left in the model at the leading edge** — blended 2025 utilization was ≤~88% (computed: 15,022k shipped ÷ >17,000k capacity, both 20-F), so incremental leading-edge revenue must come from capacity adds and price, which is exactly what the raised capex ($60–64B) and reported 2026 price hikes (+3–10% advanced nodes, TrendForce 2025-12-29 [REPORT]) are doing.

### 2. Denominator rebuild: shares, ADS, market cap, EV, and FX bases

Per [skill: metric-definition-and-comparability-audit / module: MARKET_CAP_AND_SHARE_COUNT_DENOMINATOR_REBUILD.md] — do not let the quote screen choose the denominator.

**Share stack (primary sources):**
- Common shares outstanding: **25,932,524,521** (FY2025 20-F, as of 2025-12-31 and re-stated as of 2026-02-28; SEC XBRL dei:EntityCommonStockSharesOutstanding, verified this session from companyfacts CIK0001046179). Authorized 28.05B. Share count is essentially frozen: 25,930.4M (2021) → 25,932.5M (2025) = +2.1M shares in four years.
- Diluted weighted shares 2Q26: 25,932M (2Q26 management report). FY2024 basic 25,927.6M vs adjusted-diluted 25,929.7M — a **0.008%** gap (XBRL, verified): dilution is de minimis (restricted stock awards). Last XBRL row for option-dilution is CY2015 (0.1M shares); **no outstanding option overhang, no convertibles in the interest-bearing debt stack (NT$1,031.7B, straight bonds/loans — 2Q26 report; verify against 20-F debt note)**. Treasury activity is trivial: NT$0.87B (2022) and NT$3.09B (2024) of treasury-share transactions and a 3.2M-share (0.012%) repurchase authorization dated 2024-06-05 (XBRL) — employee-award mechanics, not capital return **[agent-inferred purpose, high confidence]**. **Verdict: dilution instruments ≈ none — verified.**
- ADS layer: 1,062,690,167 ADSs = 5,313.45M common = **20.5%** of shares (20-F, 2026-02-28). Largest holder: Taiwan's National Development Fund (NDF) 6.38%.

**Two market caps — state which one you are using:**
- Local basis: NT$64.05T ÷ 32.213 = **US$1.99T** (tradingeconomics 2330 + FX, 2026-07-16).
- ADS basis: 25,932.5M × ($409.74 ÷ 5) = **US$2.125T** (computed).
- The ~+6.9% gap is the **ADS premium** (with a timing caveat: Taipei's 7/16 close pre-dates the US print-day −2.3%; same-information comparison ≈ +9.4% — evidence file 05, 2026-07-16). All shares are fungible, so this is a venue/flow phenomenon (EM-index anchoring: TSM = 15.2% of the leading EM ETF; H1'26 EM ETF inflows a record $38B — SSGA, 2026-06-30), not two economic values.
- **Honest EV build:** net cash = NT$3,518.0B cash & marketable securities − NT$1,031.7B interest-bearing debt = NT$2,486.3B = **US$77.2B** (2Q26 report; ÷32.213). EV(ADS basis) ≈ 2.125 − 0.077 ≈ **US$2.05T**; EV(local) ≈ **US$1.91T**. Note the vendor trap we actually caught: the same statistics page quotes P/E 33.25 off the *ADS* price while its EV ($1.91T) is *local-basis* (stockanalysis, 2026-07-16) — mixed venues within one screen. **Recommendation:** valuation of the ADS should run on the ADS-basis EV (US$2.05T) — that is the price a TSM buyer pays — with the local-basis EV disclosed as the underlying mark; the ~7% wedge is a real drag on expected ADS returns if the premium mean-reverts, and it flatters every "EV/x" multiple quoted off local caps by ~7%. Minor open item: non-controlling interests within NT$6,474.5B equity are not split in the quarterly report — immaterial but unverified **[agent-inferred]**.

**FX and reporting-basis traps — the same quarter has three growth rates, and FY2024 has two net incomes:**
1. **2Q26 revenue growth: +36.0% YoY (NT$) vs +33.7% YoY (US$)** (both in the 2Q26 release) vs guidance framed in US$ ($39.0–40.2B). FY2026 guidance "slightly above 40%" is **US$ terms** (2Q26 call [SECONDARY]), while NT$ consensus shows +36.75% (stockanalysis, 2026-07-16). Rule: volume/capacity work in wafers; pricing and guidance in US$ (TSMC bills predominantly in US$); statutory margins and EPS in NT$; sensitivity 1% TWD appreciation ≈ −1% NT$ revenue, ≈ −0.4pp GM (CFO, 2Q25 transcript, 2025-07-17).
2. **Convenience-translation wedge:** FY2024 revenue is **US$90.08B** in the release convention (quarterly-average rates; 4Q25 management report) but **US$88.27B** in the 20-F convenience translation (single ~year-end rate; XBRL, verified) — a 2.0% gap between two "official" US$ revenues for the same year. Any US$ CAGR must use one convention throughout [module: CONSISTENT_DENOMINATOR_THROUGH_THE_WINDOW.md].
3. **Release-vs-20-F earnings wedge (new catch, verified this session):** FY2024 net income attributable to parent is **NT$1,173.27B / EPS NT$45.25** in TSMC's quarterly-release convention (4Q25 management report) but **NT$1,158.38B / basic EPS NT$44.68** in the 20-F XBRL (SEC companyfacts, verified) — a **−1.3%** wedge; FY2023 flips sign (release NT$838.50B vs 20-F NT$851.74B, +1.6%). Both series are internally consistent (EPS × weighted shares reconciles in each). **[agent-inferred, cause]**: TIFRS (Taiwan-release basis) vs IFRS-as-issued-by-IASB (20-F basis) presentation differences; verify against the 20-F basis-of-preparation note before relying. Practical rule: a P/E built on SEC-sourced EPS and one built on TSMC-release EPS differ by ±1–2.5% before FX even enters; pick one basis for the whole report (this report uses the release/6-K series, which is what consensus tracks).
4. **Quarter-quality overlay for the headline +77.4%:** 2Q26 net income includes NT$63.20B of disposal and mark-to-market gains on Vanguard International Semiconductor (VIS) shares and NT$5.27B of earthquake insurance recoveries (2Q26 report) — ~8% of pre-tax income. The comparable growth line is **operating income +65.4%**. Also, US$ ADS EPS ($4.31) converts at the quarter-*average* rate (31.60) while the ADS price is spot — a small, permanent FX-timing mismatch inside every "P/E on US$ EPS."
5. **Consensus-basis spread:** "2026E EPS" of $15.91 (24/7 Wall St) vs $15.35 (Zacks) vs NT$100.40 × 5 ÷ 32.213 = $15.58 (computed from stockanalysis NT$ consensus) — most of the spread is the FX assumption, not earnings disagreement. Forward-P/E screens mixing NTM (next-twelve-months) and FY2026E denominators (20.0x vs 26.4x on the same site) are different metrics, not disagreement.
