### 3. Fab-level unit economics: one N2 GigaFab, one CoWoS fab

**[agent-inferred throughout — TSMC discloses none of this at fab level; built from public anchors per [skill: unit-economics / module: UNIT_ECONOMICS_FRAMEWORK.md]: gross investment fully loaded, ramp force-included, denominator audited. Confidence: medium on structure, low-to-medium on point values.]**

#### 3a. Model P&L — 100k-wpm (wafers-per-month) N2 complex, Taiwan

**Capex (gross investment).** Bottom-up: N2 needs ~20–25 EUV (extreme-ultraviolet lithography) layers; at ~90k wafer-exposures/month per low-NA tool, 100k wpm ⇒ **~24 EUV tools × ~$235M ≈ $5.6B**; EUV is typically ~20–25% of equipment ⇒ equipment ~$22–28B; shell/cleanroom/facilities ~$6–8B ⇒ **total ~$28–36B, base case $32B (~$320M per 1k wpm)**. Anchors: CFO — N2 capex per 1k wpm "substantially higher" than N3, A14 higher still (4Q25 transcript, 2026-01-15); TSMC stays on low-NA EUV through A14/A13/A12 (2026 symposium, https://pr.tsmc.com/english/news/3302, Apr 2026), capping the tool-cost tail (no $380M High-NA units in this plan). High-NA adoption post-A14 is upside risk to capex per 1k, not to this model.

**Steady state (year 3+):** 1.2M wafers/yr; N2 price ~$30k (unofficial — §4):

| Line | $/wafer | $B/yr | Basis |
|---|---|---|---|
| Revenue | 30,000 | 36.0 | 100k wpm × 12 × $30k; foundry sells wafers, so customer die-yield sits in the *customer's* cost, not TSMC revenue |
| Depreciation | ~4,600 | 5.5 | equipment $26B/5yr + building $6B/20yr (20-F lives) |
| Cash COGS | ~3,200 | 3.8 | materials/consumables ~$2,000; labor ~$250 (Taiwan); utilities ~$150 (~130MW × ~$0.10/kWh); maintenance/spares/other ~$800 |
| **Fab gross profit** | **~22,200** | **26.7** | **fab GM ~74%** (vs corporate 67.7% — sane: corporate blends mature-node prices *and* fully-depreciated fabs) |
| Allocated opex (~8% rev) | 2,400 | 2.9 | corporate opex ratio 7.8% (2Q26 report) |
| EBIT / NOPAT (18% tax) | — | 23.8 / 19.5 | tax rate 18.1% (2Q26) |

**Ramp (force-included J-curve):** year-1 average utilization ~45% with engineering wafers ⇒ depreciation/wafer roughly doubles (~$9–10k), cash cost/wafer +~30% ⇒ fab GM ~50% — consistent with management's 2–3pp corporate GM dilution from N2 in 2026 on a 3%-of-revenue node (4Q25/1Q26 calls; the 2Q26-call "3–4pp in 2H26" variant is unverified secondary — evidence file 01).

**Payback and ROIC:** run-rate cash flow ≈ NOPAT + depreciation ≈ **$25B/yr** ⇒ payback ≈ **1.3 run-rate years**, or **~4–4.5 years from first capex** including the 3-year build and ramp. Fab ROIC ≈ 19.5/32 ≈ **~60% at current pricing**. At *normalized* pricing (wafer −20%, 85% utilization): revenue $24.5B, EBIT ~$13.7B, ROIC ~**35%**, cumulative payback ~5 years from start. Cross-check against reality: TSMC's corporate revenue ÷ net PP&E ≈ 1.18x (NT$5,082B annualized ÷ NT$4,302.9B, 2Q26 report) vs this model's $36B/$32B = 1.13x — the fab model reproduces the company's capex-to-revenue conversion, grounding the valuation section's "capex → future revenue capacity" at **≈ $1 of leading-edge capex → ~$1.0–1.1/yr of revenue capacity at current prices** (→ ~$0.8 at normalized prices).

**What moves fab ROIC most (ranked elasticities, base $32B/$36B):** (1) **wafer price** — ±10% ⇒ ∓~$3.4B EBIT ⇒ ±8–9pp ROIC; (2) **utilization** — ±10pp ⇒ ±~$3.0B; (3) **capex per 1k wpm** — ±10% ⇒ ∓~5pp ROIC; (4) cash costs — ±10% ⇒ ±~$0.4B (~1pp). Depreciation *life* moves GM optics and timing, not cash ROIC — which is why the 5-vs-8-year debate (governance section) matters for peer optics, not for this fab's NPV (net present value). Price and utilization are demand variables: the fab model is an AI-demand bet wearing a hard-asset costume.

**Taiwan vs Arizona:** quoted Arizona premiums range +5–20% (AMD CEO, Tom's Hardware) to ~+25–30% ([REPORT], financialcontent 2026-04-15), while TechInsights estimates true Arizona *cost* only ~+10% (techinsights.com blog, 2025). If the premium sticks, an Arizona fab's ROIC approaches Taiwan's; management's corporate guidance of 2–3pp → 3–4pp overseas GM dilution (4Q25 call) implies premiums do not fully cover early-ramp costs. The N-2 technology-export rule (Taiwan statute amended Nov–Dec 2025) keeps N2/A16 domestic near-term, so the modeled fab is a Taiwan fab through at least 2027–28.

#### 3b. The advanced-packaging (AP) fab — CoWoS economics are a different animal

CoWoS (Chip-on-Wafer-on-Substrate) fab, AP8-class, ~40k wpm: **capex ~$4–8B** (**[agent-inferred]**: 2026 AP/testing capex bucket = 10–20% of $60–64B ≈ $6–13B vs TSMC CoWoS adds of ~+50–60k wpm ⇒ ~$100–200M per 1k wpm); revenue 480k wafers × ~$8–10k (CoWoS ASP "nears 7nm levels" — TrendForce, 2026-04-28 [REPORT]) ≈ **$3.8–4.8B/yr**; GM below corporate average **[agent-inferred, medium confidence]** at ~40–50% ⇒ EBIT ~$1.3–2.0B ⇒ payback ~3–4 years. Two structural points: (i) the "AP is capex-light" narrative is true per *fab*, false per *revenue dollar* — $1 of AP capex buys only ~$0.6–0.9/yr of revenue vs ~$1.1 for front-end, because the wafer ASP is one-third at broadly similar per-wafer line cost; (ii) the AP fab's real return is *portfolio* return — >90% CoWoS share (BigGo call coverage, 2026-07-16 [REPORT]) is the bottleneck that locks AI customers to the front-end monopoly. Value it as attach/lock-in plus modest standalone ROIC, not as a second front-end.

---

### 4. Wafer-price reliability audit — the report's unofficial numbers, banded

TSMC discloses no pricing; every per-node price is press-sourced. Triangulation against the one hard anchor — reported revenue and shipments — per [module: FINDING_THE_ECONOMIC_TRUTH_METRIC.md]:

| Node price claim | Sources (conflicting) | Independent anchor (computed) | Band & confidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| N3 "$18–20k" vs "$25–27k" | wccftech 2026 vs TechNode 2025-10-09 — mutually exclusive | 3nm = 30% of 2Q26 wafer revenue ≈ $10.5B/qtr; at 130–180k wpm capacity (TrendForce 2026-04-27) ⇒ 390–540k wafers/qtr ⇒ **implied ASP $19–27k** | **$20–26k blended** (base/P variants, post-hike, HPC-weighted). Medium confidence. "$18–20k" reads as stale/base-variant; "$25–27k" as 2026 HPC-weighted |
| N2 "~$30k" | TechNode 2025-10-09; Tom's Hardware (China Times) | 2nm = 3% of 2Q26 wafer revenue ≈ $1.05B on an early ramp of ~35–40k wafers/qtr **[agent-inferred output]** ⇒ implied ~$26–30k. Die math: $30k ⇒ ~$71/good-die on a ~100mm² 70%-yield die — consistent with flagship SoC cost estimates | **$27–32k**. Medium-low confidence (output estimate is the weak leg) |
| A16/A14 "up to $45k" | China Times via Tom's Hardware [RUMOR] | None — pre-commercial | **Unusable for valuation; scenario input only.** Low confidence |
| Blended sanity check | — | Total: ~$35B wafer revenue ÷ 4,336k wafers ≈ **$8.1k blended**; solves consistently with N3 ~$21–24k, N5 ~$13–15k, N7 ~$9–10k, mature ~$3k | Internally consistent — the *structure* of the price ladder is reliable even where points are not |

**Where the report's arithmetic is most price-sensitive:** (i) the fab model and every "capex→revenue" conversion scale linearly with the N2 price — at $24k instead of $30k, fab ROIC drops ~60%→~45% and $1 capex converts to ~$0.85/yr revenue; (ii) 2027+ revenue builds, where N2 becomes the largest node ("bigger than N3 from the start" — CFO, 4Q25 call) — today's 3% revenue share means point errors barely touch reported results but dominate forward models; (iii) any GP-per-wafer trajectory extension (business-architecture section) should carry the band, not the point. The mature-node tail (23% of wafer revenue) is the *least* price-uncertain and the least valuable.

### 5. External marks: what real money actually paid for foundry assets

Per [skill: external-mark-and-replacement-cost-triangulation / modules: OWN_TRANSACTION_PRINT_AS_SENIOR_VALUATION_EVIDENCE.md, CONTEMPORANEOUS_TRANSACTIONS_AS_LIVE_COMPS.md, PRIVATE_MARKET_EVIDENCE_WEIGHT_LADDER.md] — weight prints by bindingness and economic-twin fit:

| Print (date) | What cleared | What it marks | Comparability verdict |
|---|---|---|---|
| **TSMC sells 8.1% of VIS** (2026-05-15; 2Q26 report) | Own-asset, arm's-length equity sale; NT$63.20B combined disposal + mark-to-market gain | Mature-node/specialty foundry equity, at the cycle top of a mature-node tightening (SMIC/Hua Hong price hikes, 2026) | **Senior-tier print, wrong perimeter for the leading edge.** Marks the ~15–20% mature tail of TSMC-like economics, not N2. The realized multiple is not computable from the pulled evidence (gain is not decomposed; VIS price/cap not captured) — **data request: VIS TWSE filings & 2026-05-15 price** |
| **US government → Intel, ~10% for ~$8.9B** (2025-08-22; manufacturingdive) | Grants-to-equity conversion ⇒ implied ~$89B Intel equity | A policy print, not a market-clearing price (converted grants, non-voting) | **Discard as a value anchor; keep as a floor-of-strategic-support mark.** Its 6x paper appreciation to ~$56.5B by May 2026 (Yahoo) marks sentiment re-rating, not foundry earnings (Intel Foundry still −$10.3B FY25) |
| **NVIDIA $5B + SoftBank $2B into Intel** (Sept/Aug 2025) | Strategic minority stakes at ~$90–150B-class valuations | What sophisticated buyers paid for *optionality on a second US leading-edge source* | **Contemporaneous live comp, moderate weight**: sized at ~1–3% of the buyers' cash — an option premium, not an underwriting of Intel Foundry economics |
| **Tesla → Samsung, $16.5B wafer agreement through 2033** (Jul 2025; confirmed by Tesla) | Multi-year purchase commitment (~$2B/yr) for SF2-class capacity | The utility value of *non-TSMC* leading-edge capacity: real, but priced as a diversification hedge with 2027+ volume | **Supply-response signal** — the mark that eventually erodes the "~100% merchant leading-edge" stock in §1c |
| **CHIPS award: $6.6B grants + $5B loans vs TSMC's $65B Phoenix complex** (Nov 2024, NIST) | The state co-pays ~10% of build cost | Replacement-cost subsidy floor: governments pay to *create* capacity | Confirms rebuild economics are policy-subsidized — mild downward pressure on true replacement cost |
| **UMC/GF merger exploration** ([REPORTED ~Mar 2025 — **agent-inferred from recall, not in pulled evidence; verify before use**]) | No transaction | Would have marked mature-node consolidation value | **Discard until sourced** |

**Fab-level convergence with the valuation section:** market pays EV(ADS) ≈ $2.05T for ~1.42M wpm of 12"-equivalent capacity (17M/yr ÷ 12) ≈ **$1.4M per wpm**, vs new-build N2 cost ~$300–360k per wpm (§3a) and far less for mature capacity ⇒ ~4–5x new-build at the leading edge — independently consistent with the valuation section's 3.7–5.1× EV/rebuild band. The premium over rebuild is the price of the moat (yield learning, customer lock-in, CoWoS bottleneck), which is exactly what the §5 prints show competitors paying option money to replicate rather than underwriting outright.

---

### 6. What this measurement layer changes downstream

1. Quote foundry-margin comparisons only with the perimeter/depreciation caveats of §1a; TSMC's lead *widens* on normalization — a rare case where the naive number is conservative.
2. State every market share with its denominator; "72% / ~90%+ / ~100%-merchant" are three different facts with three different decay rates.
3. Run the ADS valuation on the US$2.05T ADS-basis EV; treat the +6.9% ADS premium as a named return drag, not noise; never mix venue bases within one multiple.
4. Pick one earnings basis (release/6-K) and one US$ convention (quarterly-average) report-wide; the FY2024 release-vs-20-F wedge (±1.3–1.6%) and the $90.08B-vs-$88.27B translation gap are now known, bounded artifacts.
5. Underwrite fabs on the §3 model with §4's price bands: at current prices a leading-edge fab pays back in ~4–4.5 years from first capex against a 5-year depreciation life — the depreciation schedule is *not* conservative accounting theater at today's prices, it approximately matches the cash cycle; the bet is that prices, not fabs, are the wasting asset.
6. Highest-value data requests: official 2Q26 transcript (2nm-dilution wording), VIS sale terms, and a wafer-capacity census to pin the capacity-share leg.

*Research process only; public sources; not investment advice. Word count ≈ 2,850.*


## Section III.3 — Guidance-Credibility Archaeology and the Cut-Risk Signal

It does not re-grade communication quality (graded A− there) and does not re-state the serial-beater signature or the 53%→56% gross-margin floor ratchet (covered there). Wei, Jan 18, 2024 [primary-transcript via https://www.fool.com/earnings/call-transcripts/2024/01/18/taiwan-semiconductor-manufacturing-tsm-q4-2023-ear/] | none downward; executed above | **+30.0%** (US$90.08B, evid-01 §7, computed) | **+7.5 vs 22.5 mid** |
| 2025 | **"increase by close to mid-20s percent in US dollar terms"** (Jan 16, 2025 call) [secondary: search-retrieved 4Q24-call coverage incl. Insider Monkey transcript, retrieved 2026-07-17] | Apr 17, 2025: reiterated despite the tariff shock [secondary, widely reported; not re-verified this session]; **Jul 17, 2025: raised — "we now expect our full-year 2025 revenue to increase by around 30% in US dollar term"** [PRIMARY: 2Q25 official transcript, evidence pack]; **Oct 16, 2025: raised — "close to mid-30s percent"** and capex narrowed to $40–42B [secondary: https://www.investing.com/news/transcripts/earnings-call-transcript-tsmc-q3-2025-shows-strong-revenue-growth-93CH-4291402] | **+35.9%** (US$122.42B) [primary: 4Q25 release, evid-01 §3] | **+11.9 vs 24** |
| 2026 | **"close to 30%"** (Jan 15, 2026) [PRIMARY: 4Q25 transcript, evidence pack] | **Apr 16, 2026: "above 30%"** [secondary: evid-01 §2]; **Jul 16, 2026: "slightly above 40%"** [secondary transcript coverage + 2Q26 presentation, evid-01/evid-06] | in progress (1H26 +35.6% NT$, evid-01 §4) | **already +10pp of intra-year raise** |

**The sandbag distribution (final actual − initial guide, 2018–2025, n=8):** mean **+3.9pp**, median **+6.8pp**; positive in **5 of 8** years (met-or-beat in 6 of 8, counting 2019's marginal met); the two negative years are the cycle turns — **2018 (−6pp)** and **2023 (−10.7pp)**. Expansion years only (n=5, excluding 2019's marginal met): mean **+9.7pp**, minimum +6.0pp, maximum ~+13pp; including 2019, the n=6 set gives mean +7.9pp, minimum −0.7pp. [computed from the table; 2020's value uses an assumed ~18% initial midpoint — flagged]

Three findings the headline "serial sandbagger" label hides:

1. **The sandbag is state-dependent, not unconditional.** In every expansion year the initial guide understated the actual by 6–13pp — a machine-like calibration consistent with the never-missed-initial-guide read the governance section applies [skill: management-communication-quality / MGMT_TRACK_RECORD_AS_BAYESIAN_PRIOR_ON_GUIDANCE.md]. But at the two demand turns in the panel the initial guide was not a floor — it missed by 6 and 11pp. The correct prior is: *TSMC's January number is a floor conditional on no cycle turn; it carries zero protective information about the turn itself.* The Bayesian-prior module's falsifier ("the record rests on… the current guide-down being the first crack") is exactly what fired in April 2018 and April 2023.
2. **The tell is revision velocity, not the initial level** [skill: management-communication-quality / FORECAST_REVISION_VELOCITY.md]. Both misses arrived as *sequences*: 2018 (Jan 10–15% → Apr ~10% → actual 6.5%) and 2022→23 (Oct-22 "growth year" → Jan-23 "slight growth" → Apr-23 decline → Jul-23 −10%). TSMC has never in this panel cut once and been done; the first trim was always followed within 1–2 quarters by another. Per SANDBAG_PRIOR_BREAKS, the *second* same-metric cut is the credibility event — and in TSMC's case it is also the empirical confirmation that the down-leg has 2+ quarters to run.
3. **2026 is tracking the 2025 shape, one gear higher.** The Jan→Jul intra-year raise (+10pp) already matches the largest full-year upward walks in the panel (2025: +12pp; 2022: +7pp by July). Per the deliberately-conservative-engine read [skill: consensus-and-revisions / CONSERVATIVE_GUIDANCE_UPWARD_REVISION_ENGINE.md], the raise cadence itself is the catalyst path — and it has historically continued at the October call in up-years (2025: second raise in October).

### 2. The capex ledger and the learned cut corridor

TSMC's capital budget is guided each January and revised with unusual candor; unlike the revenue guide, capex is where TSMC *acts* on private demand information first. Initial vs final [same skill/method attribution as §1]:

| FY | Initial budget (Jan) | Intra-year action | Final/actual (US$B) | Initial→final |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2018 | $11.5–12B [secondary: https://www.fool.com/investing/2018/07/23/tsmc-slashes-capital-expenditure-forecast.aspx] | **Jul 2018 CUT to $10–10.5B** [same] | ≈10.5 [secondary; final actual not separately re-verified] | **cut, −11% vs mid** |
| 2019 | $10–11B (Jan 17, 2019) [secondary: search-retrieved 4Q18 coverage] | drifting up by Sep (~$12.5B) [secondary: DigiTimes, https://www.digitimes.com/news/a20190909VL202.html, 2019-09-09]; **Oct 17, 2019 RAISED to $14–15B** — C.C. Wei: "5G smartphone growth momentum is stronger than we expected… We have good reasons to increase our capex this year and next year" [secondary: Reuters via https://finance.yahoo.com/news/tsmc-third-quarter-profit-rises-054042391.html, 2019-10-17] | 14.9 (evid-01 §7) | **raise, +42%** |
| 2020 | $15–16B (Jan 16, 2020) [secondary: 4Q19 6-K/coverage] | raised intra-year (not re-verified) | 17.2 (evid-01 §7) | +11% |
| 2021 | $25–28B (Jan 14, 2021) — "our 2021 capital budget is expected to be between $25 billion and $28 billion" [primary-transcript via Fool, above] | Apr 2021: ~$30B within the 3-yr US$100B plan [secondary: search retrieval] | 30.0 (evid-01 §7) | **raise, +13%** |
| 2022 | **$40–44B** (Jan 13, 2022) [primary-transcript via Fool, above] | **Jul 14, 2022: "closer to the lower end of the guidance of $40 billion to $44 billion"** (Huang), capex "pushout" framed on "tool delivery schedule changes" [PRIMARY: 2Q22 transcript PDF]; **Oct 13, 2022 CUT: "Now, we are further tightening up this year's capital spending and expect our 2022 capex to be around $36 billion"** (Huang) [primary-transcript via Fool 3Q22; corroborated by DigiTimes, https://www.digitimes.com/news/a20221013VL208/capex-ic-manufacturing-tsmc.html, 2022-10-13] | 36.3 (evid-01 §7) | **cut, −14% vs mid** |
| 2023 | $32–36B (Jan 12, 2023) — with "we continue to manage our business prudently and tighten up our capital spending where appropriate" (Huang) [primary-transcript via Yahoo 4Q22] | spent toward/below the low end through the year | 30.4 (evid-01 §7) | **−11% vs mid; below the initial low end** |
| 2024 | $28–32B (Jan 18, 2024) [primary-transcript via Fool 4Q23] | none material | 29.8 (evid-01 §7) | ≈mid |
| 2025 | $38–42B (Jan 16, 2025) [secondary: search retrieval, e.g. TrendForce preview] | 2Q25: held at $38–42B despite the revenue raise — Huang: "with all these macro uncertainties, we are mindful… it's unlikely that you see capex dollar amount suddenly drop a lot in any given year" [PRIMARY: 2Q25 transcript, evidence pack]; **Oct 16, 2025: narrowed to $40–42B** [secondary: Investing.com 3Q25 transcript] | 40.9 (evid-01 §3) | +2% vs mid |
| 2026 | **$52–56B** (Jan 15, 2026) [PRIMARY: 4Q25 transcript] | **Apr 16, 2026: "high end"** [secondary: evid-01 §2]; **Jul 16, 2026: RAISED to $60–64B** + US$100B additional Arizona commitment [secondary: evid-01 §1–2; Taipei Times, https://www.taipeitimes.com/News/front/archives/2026/07/17/2003860881, 2026-07-17] | in progress (1H26 $26.8B, evid-01 §1) | **raise, +15% vs mid, YTD** |

**Pre-2016 precedent (for corridor depth):** Jan 31, 2008 — TSMC trims the 2008 capex plan ~30% citing "conservative" customers [secondary: DigiTimes, https://www.digitimes.com/news/a20080131PD216.html, 2008-01-31] — three quarters before 4Q08 revenue fell 31% YoY and utilization collapsed below 40% [secondary: 4Q08 6-K retrievals; DigiTimes retrospective, https://www.digitimes.com/news/a20260307VL202.html]. The January 2009 budget was then set near US$1.5B — and famously U-turned upward (~$2.3B guided, ~$2.7B spent) after Morris Chang retook the CEO seat in June 2009, buying the 40/28nm leadership that defined the next decade [secondary: EE Times, https://www.eetimes.com/tsmc-surpasses-09-capex-estimate/; SEC 6-Ks retrieved 2026-07-17].

**The corridor, stated with its denominator** [skill: guidance-credibility-and-cut-risk / CUT_CORRIDOR_BASE_RATES.md — learned, never hard-coded; suppress-when-thin]: the modern panel contains **three intra-year capex-cut episodes (2008, 2018, 2022–23)**. In **3 of 3**, a US$-revenue down-or-flat year began **2–4 quarters after the first public capex-restraint language** (Jan-08 → 2H08 collapse; Jul-18 cut → FY19 +1.3% with the GM trough; Jul-22 "lower end" / Oct-22 cut → FY23 −8.7%). And in 3 of 3, the *revenue* guide was still positive when the capex action fired — capex led the revenue-guide cut by one to two quarters every time. Symmetrically, **intra-year capex raises (2019, 2020, 2021, 2025-narrow-up, 2026)** preceded or accompanied boom years in 5 of 5, with the Oct-2019 raise the cleanest ex-ante signal in the whole panel. With n=3 cut-years this corridor is a strong prior, not a law — per the skill's honesty rules it would print `corridor: directionally resolved, n=3, dispersion: low` rather than a percentage. But its *ordering* claim (capex language → capex cut → revenue-guide cut → trough 2–4 quarters later) has no counterexample in 18 years. This is the specific archaeology behind the capacity section's digestion clocks: **watch the capex sentence, not the revenue sentence.**

### 3. The language-marker playbook

Method note: these are dated phrase-events from calls at inflections, mapped against what followed — the transcript-language half that the L5 skill explicitly consumes from the communication-quality owner rather than re-deriving [skill: guidance-credibility-and-cut-risk / SKILL.md "composes"; skill: management-communication-quality / Stage 6].

**Episode inventories (dated):**

- **2018–19 (crypto/smartphone correction):** Apr 19, 2018 — "continuous weakness in smartphone demand and uncertainties in cryptocurrency mining segment" (Wei) accompanies the first revenue-guide cut [Nikkei, above]. Jan 17, 2019 — Q1'19 framed as "dampened by the overall weakening of the macroeconomic outlook, mobile product seasonality, and high levels of inventory in the semiconductor supply chain" [4Q18 release retrieval]; FY19 guided only to "slight" growth with "flattish" industry. The correction resolved in ~2 quarters; the October 2019 capex raise called the recovery.
- **2022–23 (post-shortage digestion):** Jul 14, 2022 — the full tell-cluster fires on one call [PRIMARY 2Q22 transcript]: "a few quarters of inventory adjustment, likely through first half 2023"; "we expect that a few quarters, at least into the first half of 2023, they will continue to do the inventory correction" (Wei); capex "closer to the lower end" on "tool delivery schedule changes" (Huang) — a spend cut dressed as logistics; and the reassurance later broken: "we see 2023, even just inventory correction, is still a growth year for TSMC" (Wei). Oct 13, 2022 — "further tightening up this year's capital spending… around $36 billion"; "adjust and tighten up our capital spending where appropriate" (Huang); "softness in consumer end market segment" (Wei) [PRIMARY-transcript via Fool]. Jan 12, 2023 — "semiconductor cycle to bottom sometimes in first half of 2023"; "customers' inventory adjustment" (Huang); "slight growth" (Wei) [primary-transcript via Yahoo]. Apr 20, 2023 — "customers' further inventory adjustment" (Huang) as the FY guide flips to a decline [Shacknews, 2023-04-20]. Post-mortem framing, Jan 18, 2024: macro "exasperate[d] and prolonged the global semiconductor inventory adjustment cycle" (Wei) [Fool 4Q23].
- **2008–09:** Jan 2008 "customers conservative" + 30% capex trim [DigiTimes]; 4Q08 utilization below 40%, 2009 capex initially ~$1.5B [retrievals above]. **2H2000–01:** dated verbatim quotes NOT RETRIEVED this session (see Gaps); the structural facts (record 2000 → ~24% revenue decline and deep capex cut in 2001) are consistent with the same sequence but are left out of the hit-rate math.

**The watchlist — phrases whose appearance in a future TSMC call is the cut-risk tell, each with hit-rate context:**

| Marker (verbatim family) | Historical appearances | What followed | Hit rate |
|---|---|---|---|
| Customer/supply-chain **"inventory adjustment" / "inventory correction"** applied to *current* demand (not history) | Jul 2022; Oct 2022; Jan 2023; Apr 2023; Jan/Apr 2018–19 era equivalents ("high levels of inventory in the supply chain," Jan 2019) | revenue-guide cut within 0–2 quarters; down-year within 2–4 | 2/2 episodes |
| Capex **"closer to the lower end"** / **"tighten(ing) up our capital spending"** / **"where appropriate"** | Jul 2022, Oct 2022, Jan 2023 | formal capex cut within 1 quarter of first use; FY-revenue decline the following year | 1/1 modern episode (plus 2008 analog: "customers conservative" + trim) |
| Capex pushout blamed on **"tool delivery schedule changes"** while demand language stays bullish | Jul 2022 | the "logistics" pushout became a $4–8B demand cut within 90 days | 1/1 |
| **"We adjust [the guidance] because…"** (any first same-metric trim) | Apr 2018; Apr 2023 | a *second* cut within 1–2 quarters, both times | 2/2 — first cut is never the last [skill: management-communication-quality / SANDBAG_PRIOR_BREAKS] |
| Reassurance conjunction: **"still a growth year for TSMC"** *while* capex is being restrained | Oct 2022 (and Jul 2022 variant) | reassurance abandoned within 2 quarters; actions led words | 1/1 — weight the capex action, not the sentence |
| **"prudent in our business planning"** | 2Q25, 3Q25, 4Q25, 4Q22 — appears in *both* regimes | none reliably | **not a tell alone** (fires in up-years too; treat as boilerplate unless paired with a Tier-1 marker) [skill: management-communication-quality / MANAGEMENT_DISCLOSED_LOW_VISIBILITY_AS_GUIDANCE_DISCOUNT.md — falsifier branch: boilerplate historically hit precisely] |

**Today's baseline, for contrast (all dated 2026):** "steep ramp-up of our 2-nanometer technology" (Huang, 2026-07-16 release [PRIMARY, evid-06]); "AI related demand continues to be extremely robust" (2Q26 call [secondary, evid-01 §1]); FY guide raised twice; capex raised $8B mid-year. The single most informative current-language artifact is Wei's January admission-against-interest: "you essentially try to ask whether the AI demand is real or not. **I'm also very nervous about it**… If we did not do it carefully, that will be a big disaster to TSMC for sure. So… I spent a lot of time in the last three-four months talking to my customers and then customers' customers" (4Q25 call, 2026-01-15 [PRIMARY transcript, evidence pack]). Note what is *absent* as of 2026-07-16: no "inventory," no "digesting," no "lower end," no "reassess." The 2022 archaeology says those words, when they arrive, will show up in the *capex* paragraph first — and that TSMC's own demand-verification narrative ("make sure chips are not going into inventory") gives the analyst a named, falsifiable claim to audit each quarter.

### 4. Consensus-revision mechanics in 2026 — the ladder, the echo, the whisper

*Method: [skill: consensus-and-revisions / REVISION_SIGNALS.md (breadth, whisper table), GUIDANCE_ECHO_AND_REVISION_CASCADE.md (echo score), CONSERVATIVE_GUIDANCE_UPWARD_REVISION_ENGINE.md (add-back)]. The valuation section owns the reverse-DCF and the two-direction variant math; this subsection documents only the revision mechanics feeding it.*

**How the sell side chased the ladder.** Documented FY26 EPS consensus path (US$/ADS): **$15.10** (Zacks universe, ≈Apr/May 2026 — cited as the 60-day-ago level in a mid-June note) → **$15.30** (mid-June 2026, same note: "two estimates higher, none lower… from $15.10 to $15.30" [secondary: Zacks via Yahoo, https://finance.yahoo.com/markets/stocks/articles/taiwan-semiconductor-manufacturing-company-ltd-130006888.html, retrieved 2026-07-17]) → **$15.35 Zacks / $15.91** on a second aggregator, pre-print July [evid-05 §3, 2026-07-10/16]. The Jan–Feb 2026 level was NOT RETRIEVED (see Gaps), but the shape is unambiguous: consensus lagged each guidance rung and closed the gap between prints — 6 upward Zacks-universe revisions, 0 down, in the 90 days post the April print [secondary: search retrieval of aggregator pages, 2026-07-17]. Revision breadth is *thin relative to coverage* (19 covering analysts on one aggregator [evid-05 §3]): this is a low-breadth, high-consistency drip — per REVISION_SIGNALS, "early-cycle" mechanics — except the *price* is not early (see whisper below).

**The guidance-echo trap, measured — and it is inverted.** The echo module's dangerous case is consensus importing an unproven management target. TSMC's 2026 configuration is the mirror image: pre-print consensus FY26 revenue of NT$5.21T (+36.75%) [evid-05 §3] sits **~4–5% below** the fresh company guide ("slightly above 40%" US$ ⇒ ≥US$171.4B ≈ NT$5.42–5.49T at FX 31.6–32.0) [computed; evid-01 §1–2]. Consensus EPS (+44–49%) similarly trails the mechanical guide-through EPS build the valuation section derives (+4–7% low). Scored on the echo rubric, TSMC lands ~3–4 of 16 — *consensus is not echoing hope; it is anchored below a serially-raised guide*, the classic lowball-anchor configuration in which "analysts won't model above company guide" [CONSERVATIVE_GUIDANCE_UPWARD_REVISION_ENGINE.md]. Two consequences: (i) published-consensus beats are near-mechanical while the sandbag regime holds — they carry little information; (ii) the entire informational burden shifts to the *whisper*.

**Whisper vs published.** The July 16 print is a controlled experiment: published pre-print consensus net income NT$632.6B (18 analysts) [evid-05 §3]; actual NT$706.6B (+11.7% beat); guide raised 10pp; capex raised $8B — and the ADS closed **−2.32%** at $409.74, with options having priced a ±5.6–6.7% move, put/call OI at 1.31, and 30-day IV around 57% (rank 79) pre-print [evid-05 §§3,6]. Per the whisper table in REVISION_SIGNALS (consistent-beat history >80% ⇒ whisper 2–5%+ above consensus; sandbagging management ⇒ whisper = guide + historical spread), the market's operative bar was approximately *guide + the historical +6–12pp sandbag*, i.e., already at or above the new "slightly above 40%" guide before it was announced. The quarterly EPS surprise series confirms the cushion is not shrinking — Q1'26 +4.8% ($3.49 vs ~$3.33 [secondary: Zacks via Yahoo, https://finance.yahoo.com/markets/stocks/articles/taiwan-semiconductor-q1-earnings-beat-144400227.html]), Q2'26 +14.3% ($4.31 vs ~$3.77 [secondary: Alphastreet, evid-05 §3]) — so `beat_cushion_trend` is positive (a pre-miss leading indicator it is *not*), while the *price* response says the whisper premium is fully saturated. Both can be true: cushions describe management vs published numbers; the −2.3% describes positioning vs the whisper.

**Into the October print.** Setup implication, strictly as a signal [skill: guidance-credibility-and-cut-risk / SIGNAL_NOT_THESIS_DISCIPLINE.md]: published consensus will mechanically mark to the new guide over the next weeks (the fast-mover/follower cascade, REVISION_SIGNALS steps 1–3), removing the small documented lag; after that, the *only* upside transmission channel left is a third intra-year raise (the 2025 October precedent) — while the downside channel is any Tier-1 language marker from §3, against a whisper bar that a +11.7% beat could not clear, dispersion that is modest at the mean but wide at the tails (PT range $354–700 [evid-05 §3]), 0.57% short interest, and record H1-26 EM-fund inflows into the #1 index weight [evid-05 §6]. The asymmetry into Oct 15, 2026 is therefore *reaction-function* asymmetry, not estimate asymmetry — the same structure the print-day tape just demonstrated.

**Confidence derivation** [skill: guidance-credibility-and-cut-risk / HISTORY_APPLICABILITY_AND_REGIME_BREAKS.md]: coverage ≈ 8 clean panel years / 10 → 0.8; management_continuity haircut applied *softly* (0.85 rather than 0.6: C.C. Wei has owned or co-owned the guide across the whole panel — co-CEO to 2018, CEO from 2018, Chairman+CEO from 2024; CFO Huang constant since 2019 [public record; secondary]); metric and guide-policy continuity intact (same US$ FY-growth + capex-budget format throughout). 0.8 × 0.85 ≈ 0.68, rounded down to 0.60 for the two SECONDARY-sourced rungs in the panel (2020 initial guide paraphrase; 2025 initial guide). The score is a prior that ranks attention — the decision surface lives in the decision section, not here.

The signal is in *which* sentences moved, not in bulk.

---

### 1. Risk factors (Item 3.D)

#### 1.1 The cross-strait core is deliberately frozen — and that is the finding

The load-bearing Taiwan/China sentences are **verbatim identical across all four filings**: "Our global manufacturing, design and sales activities subject us to risks associated with political, economic, financial, military or other conditions or developments in various jurisdictions, including in particular the R.O.C. …" and "…deterioration in general political, economic, financial or social conditions, military conflicts, the risk of outbreak of war or hostilities, terrorism events, security risks, social unrest… could have an adverse impact on our business" (FY2022–FY2025 20-Fs, Item 3.D; R.O.C. = Republic of China/Taiwan; P.R.C. = People's Republic of China). Even the example "the conflict in Ukraine since early 2022" carries unchanged into FY2025. Across six large-scale People's Liberation Army exercise rounds (evidence pack 04, §4), TSMC escalated not one word of filed war-risk language. [agent-inferred] This is lawyered stasis: any added specificity would itself be market-moving. Consequence: **the Taiwan tail must be monitored outside the 20-F**; the filing moves only in *adjacent* factors, below.

#### 1.2 The quiet de-Taiwanization of the "ecosystem" sentence (FY2025)

FY2022–FY2024: "An important aspect of our business operation is an ecosystem of interconnected semiconductor fabs, employees and suppliers **in the R.O.C.** that provides us with significant operational synergies, flexibility and efficiencies." FY2025 deletes "in the R.O.C." — in two separate places (the geopolitical factor and the global-expansion factor's first bullet). Class: `modified/compressed` [module: CHANGE_TAXONOMY.md]. [agent-inferred] TSMC has stopped describing its cost-and-efficiency moat as a *Taiwan* moat — tracking the Arizona/Kumamoto/Dresden buildout and the politics of the US$165B→265B US commitment (evidence 04 §3). It also weakens the filed basis for the "geography is destiny" margin argument; watch whether overseas-cost dilution language softens next.

#### 1.3 The P.R.C. chapter is being written out (FY2025)

Two sentences present through FY2024 were deleted outright (verified not moved): (a) "the R.O.C. government has not lifted some trade and investment restrictions… on the amount and types of certain investments that can be made in the P.R.C."; (b) "Our plans, investment applications and/or any relevant regulatory approvals to establish or possibly expand operations in the P.R.C. may be delayed, interrupted, suspended or cancelled…" — replaced by only: "Also, we may face potential operational risks arising from applicable export controls which impose license requirements on our P.R.C. fab's acquisition of certain manufacturing tools" (FY2025, Item 3.D). [agent-inferred] TSMC no longer contemplates expanding in China; Nanjing is a legacy site maintained under US license, and the risk narrative now runs through Washington, not Taipei investment approvals. Consistent with China falling to 9% of revenue (§3).

#### 1.4 Export controls: from boilerplate to a live investigation with pre-built defenses

- **FY2022/FY2023:** generic compliance-factor mention ("…export control, environmental and climate related laws…").
- **FY2024 (filed 2025-04-17), added:** "In October 2024, we notified relevant U.S. and Taiwan authorities that one type of our customer's chip manufactured by us might have been diverted to a restricted entity or incorporated into a restricted entity's product, and since then have been cooperating with the authorities' requests for additional information and documents" (Item 3.D). This is the Huawei/Sophgo matter before BIS (the US Commerce Department's Bureau of Industry and Security); the filing never names Huawei, Sophgo, or the Entity List — "Entity List" appears zero times in all four filings.
- **FY2025, three escalating edits around the identical core sentence:** (i) consequences extended from "government investigations and penalties" to "government investigations, **penalties or other financial exposures**" — new financial-exposure wording that reads directly onto the reported ≥US$1B potential BIS settlement (evidence 04 §2), otherwise unquantified anywhere in the filing; (ii) a new defensive sentence: "**export controls over certain semiconductors often depend on the products' specific features or performance characteristics that foundries may not be able to verify independently**…" — [agent-inferred] TSMC pre-litigating its inability-to-verify defense inside a risk factor; (iii) diversion attribution shifted from "including potentially by our **business partners**, or by third parties **with an intent of circumvention**" to "including potentially by our **customers**, or by third parties" — blame moved a step toward the customer. Under Stage 5B this stays a `watch_item` (FY2025 also says results "have not been materially affected" to date), but financial-exposure vocabulary appearing *before* any announced penalty is classic pre-provisioning [module: CHANGE_TAXONOMY.md, formal_notice]. [agent-inferred]
- **Tariffs:** "tariff" mentions run 6 → 10 → 21 → 23 (FY2022→FY2025). FY2024 added the April 2, 2025 US reciprocal-tariff announcement; FY2025 carries the full chain — the Section 232 investigation "concluded in December 2025," the resulting "25% ad valorem tariff on the import of certain advanced computing chips and derivative products," the January 2026 US–Taiwan agreement granting "preferential Section 232 treatment for Taiwanese semiconductor producers investing in U.S. manufacturing capacity," and "the U.S. Supreme Court decision striking down the IEEPA reciprocal tariffs" (IEEPA = International Emergency Economic Powers Act), ending: "…including any offsets, are still unclear" (FY2025, Item 3.D).

#### 1.5 Subsidy symmetry becomes a named competitive risk (FY2024)

FY2023 heading: "In light of the rise of new foundry service providers worldwide, if we are unable to compete effectively…". FY2024 inserted "…including through **equal access to governmental financial incentives, especially those available to our competitors**…"; FY2025 kept the subsidy clause but **deleted the "rise of new foundry service providers" preamble**. In parallel, the grant bullet escalated: "adverse changes relating to government grants or other government incentives" (FY2022/23) → adding "**non-receipt, delay and potential claw backs of government subsidies**" (FY2024; "clawbacks of government grants" FY2025). [agent-inferred] The competitive fear moved from *entrants* to *asymmetric state subsidy*, and clawback risk was flagged in the same cycle in which a US competitor's grants were converted to a government equity stake (Aug 2025; evidence 04 §3). TSMC's own award — "up to US$6.6 billion in total direct funding and up to US$5 billion of proposed loans," plus ESMC Germany's "up to EUR5 billion state aid" — is disclosed identically in FY2024 and FY2025.

#### 1.6 Taiwan power: quantified escalation, then de-quantification

FY2023 escalated the heading ("…power or other utility **outages or** shortages, **and our financial results may be adversely affected by increased prices**…") and quantified: electricity tariff "estimated to increase by 25%" from April 1, 2024. FY2024 marked it realized ("increased by 25%") and added "In October 2024, the electricity tariff rate further increased by 14%." **FY2025 deleted both numbers**, reverting to "we have experienced, and may continue to encounter utility price increases," while adding a new vector: outages "could further be exacerbated by changes in government energy policy or **shortages in the supply of power generation fuels**" (Item 3.D). Partly hygiene — the FY2024 MD&A named "higher electricity cost" as a margin offset, the FY2025 bridge does not — but replacing hard percentages with generalities while adding fuel-supply language is a net de-quantification plus a nod to Taiwan's imported-fuel grid fragility. Removal of a previously volunteered number warrants standalone attention [module: DISCLOSURE_WITHDRAWAL_AS_STANDALONE_SIGNAL.md]; motive test here says mostly benign (an external tariff, not a performance metric): watch item, no haircut. [agent-inferred]

#### 1.7 Other dated changes

- "**overseas expansion**" → "**global expansion**" (FY2023) — the buildout stopped being an away-game. [agent-inferred]
- COVID-19 standalone factor deleted (FY2023); "earthquakes and droughts" dropped from the disruptive-events heading (FY2023; specifics moved into the body — moved, not deleted).
- Standalone antitrust factor folded into the compliance factor in FY2025, with new substance: "With our success in the foundry business and the **increasing criticism on the concentration of the semiconductor industry and sometimes directly on us**, we are subject to heightened risks of antitrust investigations." Demotion in form, escalation in substance. [agent-inferred]
- IP bullet flipped from defensive ("limited or insufficient intellectual property protection…") to target-of-attack ("**increased intellectual property infringement assertions and enforcement actions against us**") in FY2025; the body adds that US Patent and Trademark Office limits on inter partes review "may result in an increase of such assertions and lawsuits."
- Demand-driver ordering: "smartphones, high performance computing…" (FY2022) → "HPC, smartphones…" (FY2023) → "**HPC (including AI applications)**, smartphones…" (FY2025). "AI/artificial intelligence" mentions: 7 → 17 → 19 → 30.

### 2. MD&A, pricing, and accounting-policy language (Item 5 / Notes)

#### 2.1 Pricing: the filing never says "value-based pricing"

"Value-based pricing," "strategic pricing," and "sell(ing) our value" appear **zero times in all four 20-Fs** — call-vocabulary only. The filed paragraph is nearly frozen: "We believe that customers find value in our flexible manufacturing capabilities… and this value is reflected in our pricing. Our pricing enables us to continue to invest significantly in research and development…" (Item 4, Pricing). The one change: **FY2024 appended** "…and **sustain healthy financial conditions to expand capacity and support customers' growth**" — the pricing-funds-capex doctrine entering the filing a year after it entered call rhetoric; carried in FY2025. [agent-inferred] Pricing power shows up in the margin line — gross margin 54.4% (FY2023) → 56.1% (FY2024) → **59.9% (FY2025)**, "mainly attributable to higher capacity utilization and cost improvement, partially offset by unfavorable foreign exchange rate" (FY2025, Item 5) — not in bolder pricing language.

#### 2.2 Prepayments: the shortage-era customer-funding stock is unwinding

"Temporary receipts from customers" — "payments made by customers to the Company to retain the Company's capacity" (Note 22; Note 23 in FY2022):

| Year-end (NT$M) | 2021 | 2022 | 2023 | 2024 | 2025 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Current | 30,613 | 107,724 | 114,640 | 198,603 | 146,559 |
| Noncurrent | 155,382 | 168,399 | 163,655 | 92,499 | 43,299 |
| **Total** | **185,994** | **276,123** | **278,295** | **291,102** | **189,858** |

Contract liabilities (advances under revenue accounting) at year-end: NT$70.8B (2022) → 52.7B (2023) → 89.4B (2024) → **50.0B (2025)** (same notes). Combined prepayment stock peaked end-2024 (~NT$380B) and fell ~37% in 2025; the noncurrent temporary-receipts tail is down 74% from its 2022 peak. The narrative sentence is verbatim-stable all four years — the change is entirely numeric. [agent-inferred] Customers are no longer topping up multi-year capacity reservations even as AI demand runs hot: either TSMC no longer demands them, or reservations are consumed faster than renewed. Read against 78% top-10 concentration (§3): more of the capex bet now sits on TSMC's balance sheet with shorter customer-commitment duration.

#### 2.3 Depreciation: no life-extension games

Machinery-and-equipment useful life is "**5 years**" in all four filings (Note 4); the only edit is land improvements "20 years" → "10 to 20 years" (FY2024). Unlike several large tech peers, TSMC has not stretched equipment lives to flatter margins — the 59.9% gross margin rests on an unchanged, conservative policy. Genuine quality signal. [agent-inferred]

#### 2.4 Government grants: stable words, violent numbers

Policy language is stable: asset-linked grants "are recognized as a **deduction from the carrying amount of the related assets** and recognized as a **reduced depreciation** … charge" (Note 4). Cash "Proceeds from government grants — property, plant and equipment": NT$0.8B (2021) → **7.0B (2022) → 47.5B (2023) → 75.2B (2024) → 76.3B (2025)** (cash-flow statements). [agent-inferred] Because grants net against PP&E rather than income, ~NT$200B of cumulative subsidy since 2022 becomes permanently *lower future depreciation* — a structural, invisible offset to overseas-fab margin dilution. The FY2024 clawback language (§1.5) is this stock's contingent-liability shadow.

### 3. Customer concentration and geography

| Share of net revenue | 2022 | 2023 | 2024 | 2025 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Ten largest customers | 68% | 70% | 76% | **78%** |
| Largest customer | 23% | 25% | 22% | **19%** |
| Second largest | <10% | 11% | 12% | **17%** |
| Top-10 share of accounts receivable | 82% | 91% | 93% | 84% |

(Each FY's 20-F, Item 3.D concentration factor + major-customers note.) The FY2025 note records a **crossover in plain numbers**: the customer at NT$352,271.2M / 12% in 2024 grew to **NT$726,974.3M / 19%** in 2025 and is now "Customer A," while the long-time #1 (NT$624,345.5M / 22% in 2024) reached only NT$645,178.7M / **17%** and dropped to "Customer B" (labels are re-ranked per filing; identities undisclosed — by market convention the incumbent is understood to be Apple and the riser Nvidia [agent-inferred]). Wording drift alongside: the structural shift is now "to **HPC and smartphone** applications" (HPC first), and "will subject our revenue to seasonal demand fluctuations" softened to "**may** subject" (FY2025). Geography (net revenue by customer headquarters, Item 5): North America 68% (2022) → 68% → 70% → **75%** (2025: NT$2,875,270M of NT$3,809,054M); China 11% → 12% → 11% → **9%** (absolute China revenue *fell*, NT$331.7B → 327.5B); EMEA (Europe, Middle East and Africa) 5% → 6% → 4% → 3%. [agent-inferred] Concentration is the compounding filed counterweight to the pricing-power narrative: top-10 +10pp in three years, one geography at 75%, and the marginal revenue dollar priced against two counterparties with their own foundry options.

### 4. Dividend, capital management, share count

Policy text (cash-preferred distributions, board-approved quarterly dividends, stock dividend ≤50% of total, legal-reserve mechanics) is verbatim-stable across all four filings (Item 8 + retained-earnings note). The numbers move monotonically: quarterly dividend per share NT$2.75 (2022) → 3.00/3.50 (2023) → 4.00/4.50 (2024) → **5.00/5.00/6.00 (2025)**, with 1Q26 approved at NT$7.00 (Q2-26 6-K; evidence 06). Implied share count from dividend totals: 25,930.4M (2022) vs 25,932.6M (2025) — **functionally frozen** (+0.01%; no buybacks, trivial restricted-share issuance). No policy sentence changed to accommodate the US$52–56B 2026 capex guide in the FY2025 20-F (since raised to US$60–64B at Q2-26). Rule-driven governance additions — the TSMC Clawback Policy (adopted August 2023 under NYSE §303A.14; FY2023 20-F Item 16G, Exhibit 97) and Item 16K cybersecurity disclosure — are hygiene, not signal [module: CHANGE_TAXONOMY.md].

### 5. Legal proceedings — and what is *not* there (Item 8)

All four Legal Proceedings sections contain **only patent matters**: Daedalus Prime (filed Sept 2022; terminated Nov 2023 per FY2023 20-F) and Longitude/Marlin (filed Feb 2025; ITC investigation instituted 2025-03-21; district-court case "statutorily stayed on April 23, 2025," FY2025). Each year closes: "Other than the matter described above, we were not a party to any material litigation as of December 31, [year]…" Absent from every Item 8 despite being live at the FY2025 filing date (2026-04-16): (a) the **BIS/Huawei-diversion investigation** (risk-factor-only treatment, §1.4; reported ≥US$1B exposure unquantified); (b) the **2-nanometer trade-secret criminal prosecutions** (engineers indicted Aug 2025 under Taiwan's National Security Act; sentences and a ~US$5M fine against a major equipment vendor's Taiwan unit landed 2026-04-27 — eleven days *after* filing; evidence 04 §6) — defensible, since TSMC is complainant, not defendant; and (c) **TSMC's own November 2025 trade-secret suit against a retired senior vice president who joined a US competitor** — that individual appears in the FY2025 20-F solely as a compensation-table footnote: "Dr. Wei-Jen Lo retired, effective July 27, 2025" (Item 6.B). A plaintiff is still "a party," so the no-material-litigation sentence embeds a judgment that a suit over alleged leading-edge (A16/A14) process leakage to a direct competitor is *immaterial*. [agent-inferred] Also absent: Taiwan's late-2025 amendment to the Statute for Industrial Innovation reportedly restricting overseas deployment to two generations behind the domestic leading edge ("N-2") — the FY2025 20-F cites that statute only for R&D tax credits (Item 5, Taxation), leaving the most binding legal constraint on Arizona 2nm/A16 timing undisclosed. Do not read the clean Item 8 as "no legal risk"; it is a scoping artifact [module: REDLINE_WORKFLOW.md, Operator 5; SKILL.md Stage 5C].

### 7. The five most informative single changes, ranked

1. **Deleting "in the R.O.C." from the ecosystem sentence (FY2025, twice).** Eight characters reversing a decade of filed doctrine: the synergy moat is no longer officially Taiwanese. Highest signal-per-word in the corpus. [agent-inferred]
2. **"Penalties or other financial exposures" + "foundries may not be able to verify independently" (FY2025).** Financial-exposure vocabulary and a litigation defense inserted around the open "restricted entity" matter — the filing signaling a possible check before any authority says so. [agent-inferred]
3. **The customer #1/#2 flip at 78%/75% concentration (FY2025).** The disclosed numbers record the platform shift (HPC before smartphones) and the largest customer-power change in TSMC's modern history.
4. **Temporary receipts NT$291B→190B, noncurrent tail −74% from peak (FY2025, Note 22).** Same boilerplate, different numbers: shortage-era customer co-funding expiring exactly as capex re-accelerates.
5. **"Equal access to governmental financial incentives" + clawback language (FY2024)** — the redline anticipated the 2025–26 subsidy-renegotiation politics (grants-to-equity precedent) almost verbatim. [agent-inferred]

### 8. FY2026 20-F (due ~April 2027): check first

1. **The "restricted entity" paragraph:** movement from "cooperating" to settlement/penalty language, any dollar quantification — or deletion. A number here is instantly high-materiality [module: DOLLAR_QUANTIFIED_RISK_FACTOR_AS_THESIS_BREAKER_GATE.md].
2. **Section 232 mechanics:** whether the US–Taiwan quota framework (2.5x/1.5x duty-free tiers; evidence 04 §1) enters with numbers; whether "still unclear" resolves; first MD&A tariff-cost or offset amounts.
3. **Temporary receipts + contract liabilities:** a third consecutive decline through an AI supercycle would confirm the risk-sharing regime change; a rebound (new AI-customer prepay deals) would refute it.
4. **Concentration:** does "Customer A" print >20% with top-10 >80%? Watch whether the risk-factor prose acquires any new specificity beyond "may render us vulnerable."
5. **Taiwan anchoring:** further stripping of R.O.C. references; first appearance of the N-2 statute in risk factors; whether Item 8 finally discloses the trade-secret suit against the former executive or the 2nm-leak verdicts (post-filing by 11 days — FY2026 silence would be a choice, not timing).
6. **Grant-clawback wording** after the US$265B pledge: any shift toward named conditions or equity-conversion language; and the grant-proceeds line in the cash-flow statement (a step-down means the subsidy-depreciation tailwind is peaking).

## Section III.5 — Disputes, Overhangs, and Episodic-Earnings Ledger

**TSMC (NYSE: TSM; TWSE 2330; SEC CIK 0001046179). As of 2026-07-17.**

Scope: this module is the consolidated dispute/overhang LEDGER. It extends — does not repeat — the wrapper section's policy-cliff probability treatment of the BIS matter (Section II.5 §e) and the governance section's VIS-stripped clean EPS (Section II.4 §4a). Here each live item gets full blast-radius sizing [skill: bounded-scandal-and-blast-radius], counterparty-BATNA decomposition [skill: counterparty-batna-and-dispute-overhang], and the episodic-item bridge that feeds "core operating earnings" [skill: episodic-earnings-quality-and-transitory-profit-normalizer].

Acronyms: BIS = Bureau of Industry and Security (U.S. Commerce); EAR = Export Administration Regulations; FDPR = Foreign Direct Product Rule; VSD = voluntary self-disclosure; SDO = suspended denial order (a stayed revocation of export privileges); KYC = know-your-customer; BATNA = best alternative to a negotiated agreement; ITC = U.S. International Trade Commission; ALJ = administrative law judge; NPE = non-practicing entity; TEL = Tokyo Electron; MOEA = Taiwan Ministry of Economic Affairs; VIS = Vanguard International Semiconductor; NDF = National Development Fund (Taiwan); ETR = effective tax rate; GM/OM = gross/operating margin; MTM = mark-to-market; NT$ = New Taiwan dollar; ADS = American Depositary Share (1 ADS = 5 common shares); DTSA = U.S. Defend Trade Secrets Act.

---

### 1. The BIS export-control matter — full bounded-scandal treatment

#### 1.1 What exactly happened (reported facts, separated from rumor)

**Facts.** In October 2024 a TechInsights teardown found a TSMC-made compute die inside Huawei's Ascend 910B AI processor; the die matched a part ordered by China-based design house Sophgo. TSMC notified U.S. and Taiwan authorities the same month — the 20-F's own words: "we notified relevant U.S. and Taiwan authorities that one type of our customer's chip manufactured by us might have been diverted to a restricted entity" (FY2025 20-F, filed 2026-04-16, https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1046179/000162828026025362/tsm-20251231.htm). TSMC has not lawfully supplied Huawei since mid-September 2020; reporting indicates roughly **3 million dies** ordered via Sophgo may have reached Huawei (TechCrunch, 2025-04-09, https://techcrunch.com/2025/04/09/us-may-fine-tsmc-1b-over-chip-allegedly-used-in-huawei-ai-processor/). TSMC was the whistleblower, not the target of a tip (The Register, 2025-04-08, https://www.theregister.com/2025/04/08/tsmc_blew_whistle_on_chinese/). Consequences already implemented: from 2024-11-11 TSMC halted all 7nm-and-below AI/GPU shipments to mainland-China designers at Commerce's direction (CNBC, 2024-11-10, https://www.cnbc.com/2024/11/10/us-ordered-tsmc-to-halt-shipments-to-china-of-chips-used-in-ai-applications-source-says.html); BIS Entity-Listed Sophgo and two dozen related parties on 2025-01-16 (The Register, 2025-01-16).

**Reported, unresolved.** Reuters (2025-04-08): TSMC "could face a penalty of $1 billion or more" to settle, the figure derived from the EAR's statutory maximum of ~2x transaction value (Reuters via investing.com, 2025-04-08, https://www.investing.com/news/stock-market-news/exclusivetsmc-could-face-1-billion-or-more-fine-from-us-probe-sources-say-3974550). **Status as of 2026-07-17: no settlement, charge, or fine announced.** The FY2025 20-F (April 2026) is silent on any amount and says results "have not been materially affected" to date. Our sweep of BIS enforcement announcements through July 2026 finds TSMC absent from the 2026 settlement docket (which instead features Applied Materials, Exyte, and Bosch — see §1.3), and BIS granted TSMC an *annual* license for its Nanjing fab on 2026-01-01 (CNBC, 2026-01-01, https://www.cnbc.com/2026/01/01/us-grants-tsmc-licence-to-import-us-chipmaking-tools-into-china.html) — a regulator does not usually extend operating licenses to a party it is about to declare a willful violator [agent-inferred].

Per the rhetoric classifier [skill: counterparty-batna-and-dispute-overhang / module: PUBLIC_RHETORIC_AS_NEGOTIATING_WEAPON.md], the "$1B+" leak is best classified as a **negotiation anchor plus statutory-maximum framing**, not a truth disclosure: it was sourced to unnamed officials mid-tariff-negotiation, cites the theoretical 2x cap, and has produced no process milestone in fifteen months.

#### 1.2 The at-risk pool — denominator discipline

[skill: bounded-scandal-and-blast-radius / module: BLAST_RADIUS_DENOMINATOR_DISCIPLINE.md]: convert the scary noun ("export-control probe") into a denominator.

| Denominator | Value | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Diverted transaction value (implied by "2x = $1B+") | ~US$0.5B, one-time, 2023–24 | [computed from Reuters framing, 2025-04-08] |
| China share of total revenue | 9% (2Q25) → 7% (1Q26) → **6% (2Q26)**, overwhelmingly mature-node | 2Q26 Management Report, 2026-07-16 |
| China advanced-AI (7nm-and-below AI/GPU) revenue today | **~0% since 2024-11-11** — the at-risk flow was already amputated 20 months ago | CNBC 2024-11-10; no lift reported through mid-2026 |
| Revenue growth through the amputation | +36.0% YoY (2Q26, NT$1,270.4B); FY26 guided "slightly above 40%" USD growth | 6-K 2026-07-16, https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1046179/000104617926000451/a2q26e_withguidancexfinal.htm |
| Huawei's residual stock of TSMC dies | "die bank" effectively **exhausted early 2026**; future Ascend output is SMIC-wafer-dependent | SemiAnalysis, https://newsletter.semianalysis.com/p/huawei-ascend-production-ramp |

The economic blast radius of the *conduct* is therefore already realized and absorbed: the China-AI pool went to zero in November 2024 and the company grew 36% over it. What remains open is only the *penalty and its terms*.

#### 1.3 Precedent penalty anchoring

BIS/DOJ semiconductor-adjacent enforcement, ordered by culpability [skill: bounded-scandal-and-blast-radius / module: HEADLINE_VS_ECONOMIC_SEVERITY_RATIO.md — precedent-peer sanity check on the denominator]:

| Precedent | Cash penalty | Penalty ÷ transaction value | Conduct terms | Culpability posture |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **ZTE** (2017 + 2018) | $1.19B combined (2017), then **+$1.0B + $400M escrow** (2018) | >1x | Entire board & senior management replaced; BIS-answerable special-compliance team; 10-yr SDO | Willful scheme + lied to BIS during probation (Akin, 2018, https://www.akingump.com/en/insights/alerts/commerce-department-signs-new-agreement-with-zte-lifting-denial; cmtradelaw, 2018-07, https://www.cmtradelaw.com/2018/07/zte-back-in-business-as-commerce-department-lifts-denial-order/) |
| **Cadence** (Jul 2025) | $140.6M (DOJ criminal plea + BIS) | ~3x ($45.3M sales to a PRC military university) | Guilty plea; compliance undertakings | Criminal; military end-user (DOJ, 2025-07-28, https://www.justice.gov/opa/pr/cadence-design-systems-agrees-plead-guilty-and-pay-over-140-million-unlawfully-exporting) |
| **Seagate** (Apr 2023) | **$300M** ($15M/quarter over 5 yrs) | ~0.27x (~$1.1B of HDDs to Huawei post-FDPR) | Multi-year audits; **5-yr SDO** | No VSD; continued shipping after peers stopped (BIS, 2023-04-19, https://www.bis.gov/node/20250; Paul Weiss, 2023-04-28) |
| **Applied Materials** (Feb 2026) | $252.5M | n/d | Standard undertakings | SMIC-related shipments (BIS press release, 2026-02, https://www.bis.gov/press-release/applied-materials-pay-252-million-penalty-bis-illegally-exporting-semiconductor-manufacturing-equipment) |
| **Bosch** (Jun 2026) | $36.2M | **~0.5x** ($72.4M to Huawei affiliates 2020–24) | Standard | Non-willful, cooperative (BIS, 2026-06, https://www.bis.gov/press-release/robert-bosch-gmbh-bosch-pay-36-million-penalty-bis-unlicensed-shipments-huawei) |
| **Exyte** (Jan 2026) | $1.5M | small | Standard | SMIC transfers (cmtradelaw, 2026-01, https://www.cmtradelaw.com/2026/01/bis-fines-exyte-1-5-million-for-unlicensed-transfers-to-chinas-smic/) |
| **GlobalFoundries** (Nov 2024) | **$0.5M** | **~0.03x** ($17.1M, 74 shipments to an Entity-Listed SMIC affiliate) | Standard | **VSD + cooperation + screening error, not intent** (Manufacturing Dive, 2024-11, https://www.manufacturingdive.com/news/commerce-department-globalfoundries-500k-fine-sj-semiconductor-entity-list/733768/) |

TSMC's fact pattern (self-reported, deceived by an intermediary that itself denies Huawei dealings, full cooperation, immediate unilateral-plus-directed remediation) sits between GlobalFoundries (0.03x) and Bosch (0.5x) on pure enforcement math — i.e., **$15M–$250M** on ~$0.5B of transactions. Three aggravators push the number up: the Huawei/Ascend national-security nexus (the single most politically charged end-use in the regime), the sheer die count (~3M units ≈ multiple Ascend generations of compute), and the government's incentive to price the settlement as a *demonstration* during a live tariff/subsidy negotiation. **Realistic cash band: $200M–$1.25B; modal ~$500M–$1.0B** [agent-inferred], consistent with, and refining, the wrapper's probability cases (Section II.5: cash ≤$1.5B 55% / conduct-conditions 25% / no action 15% / escalation 5%).

#### 1.4 The conduct-terms tail — the part that matters more than the check

Cash is a rounding error here ($1B ≈ 0.8% of FY25 revenue of US$122.4B, ~1.9% of FY25 net income of NT$1,717.9B [4Q25 6-K, 2026-01-15; computed], ~0.05% of the US$2.125T market cap [computed, close US$409.74, 2026-07-16]). Per the precedent set, the settlement's *conduct architecture* is where duration risk lives:

- **Near-certain in any settlement:** multi-year independent audits with BIS reporting (Seagate template); a **5-year suspended denial order**; formalized enhanced-KYC obligations on China-adjacent orders — extending the Jan-2025 foundry due-diligence rule (compliance date 2025-01-31; Morgan Lewis, 2026-01, https://www.morganlewis.com/pubs/2026/01/bis-revises-export-review-policy-for-advanced-ai-chips-destined-for-china-and-macau) from regulation into a *consent obligation with probation attached*.
- **Possible:** a BIS-approved compliance coordinator embedded in export operations; pre-shipment license conditions on classes of China-facing mature-node business (today ~6% of revenue).
- **Tail (requires a willfulness finding):** ZTE-style monitorship/management terms — no evidence supports this today.

Operational drag: incremental compliance opex (tens of US$M/yr [agent-inferred]) and onboarding friction for small design houses — but the *big* cost, refusing gray-zone China AI business, was already taken in November 2024 and is in the run-rate. The true price of an SDO is **convexity**: it converts any *repeat* incident during probation from a fine into a threatened loss of access to U.S.-origin technology — for a fab operator whose toolchain is EAR-jurisdictional, an existential term. That is the mechanism by which this bounded item could unbound.

#### 1.5 Verdict: bounded — with named unbounding triggers

[module: HEADLINE_VS_ECONOMIC_SEVERITY_RATIO.md — direct loss vs diagnostic signal]: the direct loss is sized ($0.2–1.25B + compliance drag); the residual question is whether the event is a *diagnostic signal* of a counterparty-diligence failure. The evidence contains the signal rather than confirming it: TSMC's own screening caught nothing, but the diversion was executed through a facially legitimate customer, the company self-reported within days of the teardown, and the industry-wide fix (the foundry due-diligence rule) treats this as a systemic KYC gap, not a TSMC-specific one. **Verdict: BOUNDED** — critical claim (the dividend/buildout-funding capacity of a net-cash NT$2,486B balance sheet, 2Q26 Management Report) survives any scenario in the band by two orders of magnitude [module: CRITICAL_CLAIM_FRAMEWORK.md].

**What would UNBOUND it:** (1) documentary evidence TSMC *knew* Sophgo was fronting for Huawei (flips VSD mitigation into Cadence/ZTE-class willfulness; penalty re-anchors at 2–3x and criminal referral opens); (2) a **second diversion incident** surfacing while under an SDO (probation convexity); (3) escalation from BIS civil to DOJ criminal; (4) explicit linkage of settlement terms to CHIPS/tariff concessions, which would signal the U.S. has repriced the whole relationship (see §2). Monitoring: BIS press-release feed; any 6-K reserving language; the every-December Nanjing license renewal.

---

### 2. The U.S. government as counterparty — the BATNA grid

The United States is simultaneously TSMC's **regulator** (BIS/EAR), **penal creditor** (the unresolved matter), **tariff-setter** (Section 232, 25% on advanced chips, effective 2026-01-15; White House, 2026-01-14), **subsidizer** (CHIPS: $6.6B grant + $5B loans contracted Nov 2024, $1.5B disbursed), and **commercial hostage-holder/hostage** (the $265B U.S. buildout announced through 2026-07-16; Fortune, https://fortune.com/2026/07/16/tsmc-100-billion-us-chip-investment/). [skill: counterparty-batna-and-dispute-overhang / module: GOVERNMENT_CUSTOMER_URGENCY_VS_PRICE.md — a government's urgency does not automatically translate into the supplier's economics; map the paths.]

**What each side actually holds** [assets: BATNA_GRID.md]:

| Lever | U.S. holds over TSMC | TSMC holds over U.S. |
|---|---|---|
| Technology | EAR jurisdiction over the U.S.-origin toolchain; FDPR reach over everything TSMC makes | — |
| Trade | 25% §232 tariff; quota exemption (2.5x/1.5x construction/completion multiples) is revocable policy, not statute (USTR, 2026-02) | Withholding/slowing the U.S. buildout the administration claims as its signature win |
| Money | Remaining CHIPS disbursements milestone-gated; the Intel grants-to-equity precedent (~10%, Aug 2025; CNBC, 2025-08-19) | — |
| Legal | The unresolved BIS penalty — a card that is **more valuable held than played** [agent-inferred] | — |
| Supply | — | **72.3% foundry share (TrendForce, 2026-06-12); effectively every U.S. merchant and hyperscaler AI accelerator is TSMC-fabbed** (evidence file 03 §5); advanced supply reportedly ~3x short of AI demand (chinastrategy.org, 2026-03-05) |
| Alternatives | Intel 18A ramping but **zero committed external 14A customers** (Tom's Hardware, 2026-01); Samsung SF2 yields reported mid-50s% (phonearena, 2026) | TSMC cannot walk: North America customers = 78% of 2Q26 revenue; Taiwan's N-2 statute (in force end-2025) caps what it may concede abroad anyway |

Neither side has a credible rupture BATNA before ~2030: the U.S. cannot replace the capacity; TSMC cannot replace the customer base or the tools. This is a **mutual-hostage equilibrium**, and the observed settlement currency is not cash but *capex*: each pressure cycle has been discharged by an investment escalation that lets both sides claim victory — the March-2025 +$100B pledge (weeks before the $1B fine reporting), the exemption-for-investment tariff architecture (Jan–Feb 2026), and the July-2026 +$100B to $265B [skill: counterparty-batna-and-dispute-overhang / modules: FACE_SAVING_SETTLEMENT_AND_COMMITMENT_TRAP.md, JOINT_SURPLUS_SETTLEMENT_INSTRUMENTS.md — the non-cash instrument that reframes capitulation as strategy]. Note the commitment-trap asymmetry: Washington's public posture ("Intel should dominate" — the President, quoted re the ITC matter, sedaily, 2026-06-12) is anchoring rhetoric, while TSMC has *never* publicly committed to a number it must walk back — it concedes in pre-emptive increments, which is why the equilibrium keeps clearing without a public loss of face on either side.

**Extension of Section II.5's ratchet observation:** because the exemption terms reprice at each cycle, the correct model is a **recurring toll, not a one-time settlement** — the overhang never "closes," it amortizes into capex intensity and overseas-fab margin dilution (the P&L channel where this ledger item actually lands; sized in Section II.3/Section II.6).

**Deterioration tells (dated, monitorable):** (1) revival of the equity-stake demand walked back on 2025-08-22 (TrendForce, 2025-08-22); (2) new conditions attached to remaining CHIPS disbursements; (3) a BIS settlement announced *with* unusually punitive conduct terms or explicit trade linkage; (4) revival of the reported-but-never-issued 1:1 domestic-production matching rule (Reuters, 2025-09-26); (5) quota-multiple tightening after the July-2026 Commerce data-center report window (proclamation, 2026-01-14); (6) non-renewal of the Nanjing license in December; (7) ITC exclusion order against TSMC-made chips left unmodified by the Commission (§3.3) — which would reveal that "no special treatment" politics outweigh supply-chain politics.

---

### 3. IP and people litigation — process-knowledge leakage as the structural question

#### 3.1 TSMC v. Wei-Jen Lo (and Intel, by implication)

Facts: Lo, a 21-year TSMC veteran (VP 2004, SVP 2014; corporate strategy from March 2024 — a role *without* R&D line management, per Taipei Times, 2025-11-26, https://www.taipeitimes.com/News/biz/archives/2025/11/26/2003847825), retired 2025-07-27 and surfaced at Intel in late October 2025 as an R&D executive. TSMC sued 2025-11-25 in Taiwan's IP & Commercial Court (employment contract, non-compete, Trade Secrets Act) and petitioned for an **interim injunction** the same week (Taiwan News, 2025-11-26, https://www.taiwannews.com.tw/news/6252871; CNBC, 2025-11-25, https://www.cnbc.com/2025/11/25/-tsmc-stock-lawsuit-former-executive-trade-secrets-intel.html). Reports allege A16/A14-related documents; Taiwan's High Prosecutors' Office opened a criminal probe and MOEA is examining national-security angles; Intel's CEO called the allegations meritless (DCD, 2025-11, https://www.datacenterdynamics.com/en/news/intel-claims-lawsuit-filed-against-former-tsmc-exec-accused-of-trade-secret-theft-has-no-merit/). **No public ruling on the injunction or the merits found through 2026-07-17** [agent-inferred from absence in searched dockets/press].

Realistic outcomes: the Taiwan court can bind *Lo* (injunction narrowing his Intel role, non-compete damages — personally ruinous, corporately trivial) but has **no jurisdiction over Intel's U.S. operations**; the suit's real function is deterrence signaling to TSMC's own bench (consistent with Section II.4's "institution defends itself" read). The escalation path that would matter: evidence that documents actually transited to Intel, which would support a U.S. DTSA action against Intel itself — a genuine roadmap-relevant fight between the #1 foundry and its state-backed challenger. Nothing public supports that today. Probability litigation touches *TSMC's* roadmap: ~nil; the exposure runs the other direction (whatever Lo carries shortens Intel's learning curve at the margin).

#### 3.2 The 2nm-leak criminal case — closed, and diagnostic

Outcome: on 2026-04-27 Taiwan's IP Court sentenced ex-TSMC engineer Chen Li-ming to 10 years and co-defendants to 2–6 years for photographing >1,000 screens of 2nm process data for the benefit of TEL's Taiwan unit, which was fined ~US$5M and dropped its appeal in May 2026 (Al Jazeera, 2026-04-27, https://www.aljazeera.com/economy/2026/4/27/taiwan-court-hands-down-jail-terms-in-tsmc-trade-secrets-case; DigiTimes, 2026-05-21). This was the **first prosecution under the National Security Act's "national core technology" provisions** (Focus Taiwan, 2026-01-05) — 7–14-year exposure now attaches to leaking TSMC's crown jewels.

#### 3.3 The NPE flank: Longitude/Marlin at the ITC

Irish NPEs Longitude Licensing and Marlin Semiconductor (patents acquired from UMC in 2021) are prosecuting ITC investigation **337-TA-1443** against TSMC *and its customers* (Apple, Broadcom, Qualcomm, Lenovo, Motorola, OnePlus); scope has narrowed to 2 patents/4 claims; trial was held February 2026; ALJ initial determination was expected June 2026 (outcome not verified in public sources at writing) and the **Commission's final decision ~October 2026**; a parallel district-court trial is set 2026-11-16 with five TSMC inter partes review petitions pending (Mintz ITC practice summary, https://www.mintz.com/industries-practices/international-trade-commission-section-337/experience; Seoul Economic Daily, 2026-06-12, https://en.sedaily.com/international/2026/06/12/tsmc-faces-us-patent-lawsuit-despite-pledging-265-billion). This is the **only litigation on the ledger with a theoretical shipment-stopping remedy** (an ITC exclusion order on infringing chips). Base rates and politics both argue for containment: foundry patent wars end in cross-licenses (GlobalFoundries v. TSMC, 2019 — global suits resolved into a broad cross-license within ~2 months; ciplawyer, 2019, https://www.ciplawyer.com/articles/143198.html); NPEs want royalties, not exclusion; and an exclusion order hitting Apple/Nvidia-class supply would collide with the same administration that just took $265B of investment — though Republican letters urging "no special treatment" (Yahoo Finance, 2026, https://finance.yahoo.com/sectors/technology/articles/scoop-republican-lawmakers-challenge-tsmcs-233656757.html) mean the tail is not zero. Expected outcome: license/settlement in the tens-to-low-hundreds of US$M [agent-inferred]; kill marker: Commission affirms a violation *and* declines to tailor the remedy (Oct 2026), or a Presidential-review non-veto (60 days after).

#### 3.4 Reconciliation with the moat section's tacit-knowledge argument

These three cases are the empirical test of Section II.2's claim that TSMC's moat is tacit process knowledge. What they show: **documents leak; capability doesn't.** The 2nm leak was >1,000 *photographs* — intercepted, prosecuted, and in any case orders of magnitude short of a transferable process recipe. The counterfactual evidence is live: SMIC runs 5nm pilot at ~20% reported yields on DUV multipatterning despite two decades of hiring ex-TSMC talent (marklapedus.substack.com, 2026); Samsung's SF2 sits at reported mid-50s% yields vs TSMC N2's 60–70% (phonearena, 2026) — and Samsung has been *legally* fast-following for thirty years. The genuine structural risk is not a defector with files but an **organized, state-funded team lift-out at scale** — precisely the scenario the National Security Act regime (7–14-year sentences, corporate liability reaching a tool vendor) is engineered to deter, and the Lo suit is engineered to make socially costly. Residual risk grade: real, chronic, slow — an erosion vector priced in the moat half-life (Section II.2), not an event overhang.

### 4. Episodic-earnings normalization sweep — beyond the VIS gain

[skill: episodic-earnings-quality-and-transitory-profit-normalizer / assets: EPISODIC_PROFIT_MAP.md → CORE_EARNINGS_REBUILD.md → EVENT_FADE_DECISION_CARD.md]. **The 2Q26 non-operating NT$95.83B, fully decomposed** (2Q26 Management Report §II-3): long-term investment income **1.44** + net interest income **26.96** + other gains and losses **67.43**, of which **63.20 = disposal + MTM gains on VIS shares** (8.1% stake sold 2026-05-15), leaving ~4.23 of residual FX/sundry gains [computed]. Sequence for context: non-op was 23.09 (4Q24) → 29.61 (2Q25) → 24.68 (3Q25) → 27.46 (4Q25) → 28.83 (1Q26) → **95.83 (2Q26)**; interest income is the stable riser within it (21.75 → 26.96 over six quarters; FY25 93.37 vs FY24 76.72).

|---|---|---|---|---|

| 6 | Government grants (CHIPS $1.5B received 4Q24; Japan/Germany grants; future §232 tariff-offset program) | Not visible as a quarterly income line | Predominantly netted against asset cost / deferred rather than run through income [agent-inferred — **open verification item** in the FY26 20-F grant note (Section II.4 flagged the same)] | n/a — watch for a future switch to income-statement recognition |

The headline conclusion survives the sweep: outside VIS, 1H26 contains remarkably little episodic profit — the insurance item is ≤0.3% of operating income, FX is a cyclical lean rather than a one-off, and the biggest permanent adjustment is *presentational* (classifying NT$107B/yr annualized interest income as financing). Operating income (+65.4% YoY in 2Q26) remains the clean line, as Section II.4 concluded; the incremental finding here is the **direction of the FX asterisk** — 2025's margins were FX-punished and 1H26's are mildly FX-flattered, so through-cycle comparisons should use the ~40bp/1% rule rather than reported GM deltas — and the **Q2 tax seasonality**, which mechanically understates Q2 EPS every year.

**Net verdict.** Sum the cash exposures at their modal values and the entire ledger is **≈$1.0–1.5B against a $2.1T market cap and ~NT$2.49 trillion of net cash — immaterial, category (b)**, and at a ~20x forward P/E that already sits below the fabless customer complex (Section II.6), it is more than priced. But the ledger's real content is not cash: rows 1–2 are **conduct-term and ratchet architecture** — a probation regime that makes a *second* incident existential, and a political toll that amortizes through capex and margin rather than through a reserveable line. Those two tails are (c) **modestly underappreciated**, precisely because they never appear as a number in anyone's model; the correct hedge is calendar discipline (Oct-2026 ITC final, Dec-2026 Nanjing renewal, each 20-F's investigation paragraph, every quota-review window), not a valuation haircut. The overhang most likely to *surprise* on the tape — an ITC exclusion headline — is also the one whose economic severity is most bounded; per the headline-time discipline, that gap would be an entry point, not an exit [module: HEADLINE_TIME_VS_LEGAL_TIME.md].
