## Section III.6 — The AI-Capex Theme Map, Evidence Loop, and Source-Edge Ladder

*TSMC (Taiwan Semiconductor Manufacturing Company; NYSE: TSM, TWSE: 2330). Section date 2026-07-17. Evidence-file citations: [E1]=01_results_and_financials.md, [E2]=02_technology_and_capacity.md, [E3]=03_competition_customers_ai.md, [E5]=05_market_consensus_valuation.md, [E6]=06_hfdt_and_sec_primary.md. Web citations carry URL + date. All probability and threshold judgments are [agent-inferred] unless cited. Acronyms: capex = capital expenditure; HBM = high-bandwidth memory; CoWoS = Chip-on-Wafer-on-Substrate (TSMC's 2.5D advanced packaging); ABF = Ajinomoto Build-up Film (the substrate laminate under CoWoS parts); OSAT = outsourced semiconductor assembly and test; ODM = original design manufacturer (server assemblers); MOEA = Taiwan's Ministry of Economic Affairs; EUV = extreme-ultraviolet lithography; ARR = annualized revenue run-rate; ASIC = application-specific integrated circuit; LTA = long-term agreement; VOI = value of information; FCF = free cash flow; DC = data center; wpm = wafers per month; GM = gross margin; RPO = remaining performance obligations; OCF = operating cash flow.*

Prior sections established what the AI-capex line is worth (Section II.6 priced the scenario tree), what it does to the model (Section II.3's 2027 digestion case: revenue −8±3%, EPS ~US$11.5–12.7/ADS vs consensus ~$20), and who captures it (Section II.1's per-accelerator profit pools). This section answers the remaining question: **how do we know, before the market does, which branch is unfolding?** The theme is stated falsifiably per [skill: thematic-read-across-and-evidence-loop / SKILL.md Stage 1]: *"Will Big-4 hyperscaler capex — guided ~$700–725B for 2026, +77% YoY ([Tom's Hardware, 2026](https://www.tomshardware.com/tech-industry/big-tech/big-techs-ai-spending-plans-reach-725-billion); [E3 §6]) — grow (H1), plateau (H2), or contract (H3) in 2027, and on what dated public evidence will each branch show first?"*

### 1. The Theme Map: every node where the theme prints public, dated evidence

Built per [skill: thematic-read-across-and-evidence-loop / module: THEME_MAP_AND_READ_ACROSS.md] (value-chain roles → direction → signal type → watch item) with reliability weights per [skill: evidence-triangulation-rules / SKILL.md Stage 2] (filings ~0.9; earnings-call guidance 0.5–0.7 with promotional haircut; third-party market research ~0.7 less methodology haircut; supply-chain press/rumor ≤0.4). Lead/lag is stated against **TSMC's own P&L recognition** (wafer revenue at shipment; the stylized chain: hyperscaler budget → accelerator order → TSMC wafer start ≈ 1–2 quarters before TSMC revenue → HBM/CoWoS/substrate coincident → NVIDIA system revenue ~1–2 quarters *after* TSMC's wafer revenue → ODM server exports after that → datacenter energization last) [agent-inferred].

**Sufficiency gate** [skill: thematic-read-across-and-evidence-loop / module: QUERY_LADDER_AND_EVIDENCE_ESCALATOR.md]: passed — 9 nodes, 4+ source types (filings, government statistics, market research, press/supply-chain), and live contradiction: every physical node reads *tailwind/accelerating* while the funding nodes (FCF, circular finance, inference margins) read *deteriorating*, and the stock fell 2.3% on a beat-and-raise [E5 §3]. That contradiction is the section's whole subject; per the skill we route it into discriminating evidence rather than averaging it away.

### 2. The circularity / vendor-financing audit

The normalization and valuation sections treated 2027 capex as an exogenous coin-flip. It is not fully exogenous: part of 2026 demand is **self-referential** — financed by suppliers' equity, suppliers' purchase backstops, or debt collateralized by the GPUs themselves. Mapping the reported flows ([Bloomberg circular-deals graphic, 2026](https://www.bloomberg.com/graphics/2026-ai-circular-deals/); [CNBC $1T deal guide, 2025-10-15](https://www.cnbc.com/2025/10/15/a-guide-to-1-trillion-worth-of-ai-deals-between-openai-nvidia.html)):

- **NVIDIA → OpenAI:** up to **$100B in staged $10B tranches** (announced Sep 2025) against OpenAI stocking NVIDIA systems — **reported stalled in early 2026** amid internal doubts about OpenAI's spending discipline ([tech-ish, 2026-02-03](https://tech-ish.com/2026/02/03/nvidia-openai-oracle-circular-financing-loop/)).
- **Oracle ↔ OpenAI:** ~**$300B over ~5 years beginning 2027 (~$60B/yr)** for Stargate compute ([IntuitionLabs](https://intuitionlabs.ai/articles/oracle-openai-300b-deal-analysis)). This sits in Oracle RPO today and turns into physical GPU/wafer demand from 2027 — meaning **the marginal 2027 demand dollar is more circular than the average 2026 dollar** [agent-inferred].
- **Microsoft ↔ OpenAI:** post-restructuring ~27% equity stake plus a ~$250B incremental Azure purchase commitment ([CNBC, 2025-10-15](https://www.cnbc.com/2025/10/15/a-guide-to-1-trillion-worth-of-ai-deals-between-openai-nvidia.html)).
- **NVIDIA → neoclouds:** ~$2B equity each in CoreWeave and Nebius; a **$6.3B contractual obligation to buy CoreWeave's unsold capacity through April 2032**; and a formalized backstop-financing program taking a cut of neocloud revenue ([MLQ](https://mlq.ai/news/nvidia-launches-gpu-backstop-financing-model-takes-cut-of-cloud-revenue-from-neocloud-partners/); [SemiAnalysis](https://newsletter.semianalysis.com/p/nvidia-gpu-debt-backstop-unleashes)). CoreWeave's debt has grown from <$8B (2024) to **>$21B**, with **Q1-2026 FCF of −$4.71B against $2.08B of revenue** ([TechTimes, 2026-07-12](https://www.techtimes.com/articles/320239/20260712/nvidia-circular-financing-249b-coreweave-debt-puts-pension-funds-risk.htm); [Quartz](https://qz.com/gpu-collateralized-debt-ai-neocloud-coreweave-financing-risks-050526)).
- **SoftBank:** Stargate co-sponsor alongside Oracle/OpenAI; separately a $2B Intel anchor investment [E3 §2].
- **Depreciation schedules:** Meta *extended* server lives to 5.5 years (booking ~$2.9B less depreciation) while Amazon *shortened* a server subset from 6 to 5 years with a $920M accelerated charge, explicitly citing AI hardware turnover; a widely publicized short-side critique estimates 2026–28 sector earnings overstatement of ~$176B if lives were marked to a 2–3-year economic reality ([National Law Review](https://natlawreview.com/article/deep-quarry-useful-lives-gpus-key-considerations); [Footnote Brief](https://footnotebrief.com/hyperscaler-depreciation-ai-capex-circularity/)).

**How much of 2026 demand is self-referential?** The honest decomposition: the Big-4's ~$700–725B is still overwhelmingly funded by their own operating cash flows earned in *non-AI* end markets (cloud, advertising, retail) — that is end-demand-funded in the sense that matters for solvency. The genuinely circular slice concentrates in (i) the OpenAI complex (supplier equity + committed cloud spend it cannot yet fund from revenue: ~$25B ARR vs hundreds of billions of contracted compute), (ii) debt-funded neoclouds whose collateral is the vendor's own product, and (iii) supplier-backstopped capacity. We estimate **~15–25% of 2026 accelerator-system demand is supplier-financed, backstopped, or GPU-collateralized-debt-funded — rising toward ~30% in 2027 as Oracle–OpenAI turns on** [agent-inferred]. TSMC does not see this directly — it sees NVIDIA (19% of revenue) and Broadcom-class ASIC orders [E3 §5] — which is precisely why the foundry is structurally the *last* to know.

**Does inference revenue cover compute cost?** The public debate numbers: Bain's September-2025 estimate that AI must generate **~$2T of annual revenue by 2030** to fund the buildout, with an **~$800B shortfall** on current monetization ([Bain press release, 2025](https://www.bain.com/about/media-center/press-releases/20252/$2-trillion-in-new-revenue-needed-to-fund-ais-scaling-trend---bain--companys-6th-annual-global-technology-report/); [Tom's Hardware](https://www.tomshardware.com/tech-industry/bain-says-compute-demand-is-outpacing-capital)). Against that: total disclosed frontier-lab ARR (~$47B + ~$25B + smaller labs, gross-basis, disputed) is **under ~$100B annualized against ~$450B of AI-related 2026 capex** ([valueaddvc](https://valueaddvc.com/blog/ai-hyperscaler-capex-compared-why-microsoft-google-meta-and-amazon-are-all-spending-at-once); [E3 §6]) — call it 15–20% current-year coverage before any return on capital [agent-inferred]. The bull rebuttal is that monetization migrates from IT budgets to the labor market ("orchestration revenue," [Decoding Discontinuity, 2026](https://www.decodingdiscontinuity.com/p/inference-economy-missing-800m)); Anthropic's 5× ARR growth in five months is the single best datapoint for that view. Both can be true: unit token economics improving 280× while aggregate spend outruns aggregate revenue is exactly what 1999 telecom bandwidth looked like [agent-inferred].

**Where cracks show first, in order** [skill: variant-source-map-and-research-edge / module: COUNTERPARTY_PERIPHERAL_BEHAVIOR_MOSAIC.md — watch the non-target actors whose behavior must change first]: (1) **neocloud credit tape** — GPU-collateralized bond/loan spreads reprice daily, and lenders exit before equity narratives change; (2) **NVIDIA's own 10-Q footnotes** — purchase commitments, inventory, receivables days, and backstop obligations (the $6.3B class) are quarterly and audited; (3) **hyperscaler depreciation and supplier-finance footnotes** — any second company following Amazon's shortening is an admission against interest; (4) **Oracle RPO composition and its capex-vs-OCF gap**; (5) **lab pricing behavior** — API price cuts are benign deflation, enterprise seat discounting is demand softness; (6) only then TSMC/ASML order books, because take-or-pay LTAs and prepayments make the foundry chain the stickiest layer. Big-4 FCF is already reported near **zero this summer** at record net income ([TechTimes, 2026-06-29](https://www.techtimes.com/articles/319306/20260629/big-tech-ai-spending-tops-725-billion-free-cash-flow-hits-zero-this-summer.htm)) — the cushion that made capex guides costless is gone; from here every raise is a *costly action* and therefore, perversely, better H1 evidence when it happens [skill: evidence-triangulation-rules / SKILL.md, irreversibility weighting].

### 3. The evidence loop: discriminating prints, Jul-2026 → Jul-2027

Per [skill: thematic-read-across-and-evidence-loop / module: QUERY_LADDER_AND_EVIDENCE_ESCALATOR.md], the loop runs recall→precision: monitor all nine nodes broadly; on first contradiction, escalate to full-section/lexical validation (pull the exact footnote, the exact MOEA table). Below, the precision layer: which print discriminates the hypotheses hardest, with thresholds and probability shifts (all shifts [agent-inferred]; priors in §5). H1 = sustained boom through 2028; H2 = 2027 plateau/digestion (Section II.3's −8±3% path); H3 = sharp 2027 correction.

| Date | Print | H1 keeps/gains if… | H2 gains if… | H3 gains if… | Shift |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Jul 22–30, 2026** | Big-4 Q2 calls (Alphabet 7/22; Microsoft+Meta 7/29; Amazon 7/30) | 2026 guides reaffirmed ≥$700B *and* any "2027 higher" framing; raises driven by units not component-price inflation | Reaffirm-but-hedge; "optimization/efficiency" vocabulary; raises attributed mainly to memory/component costs (a unit-deflator masquerading as demand) | Any Big-4 trims 2026 capex | H1 ±5; H2 +10–15 on hedge language |
| **Aug ~10, 2026** | TSMC July monthly revenue | 3-mo trailing YoY >+30% | Single month <+15% YoY absent FX/seasonal explanation | Outright MoM collapse ex-seasonality | ±3–5 (noisy alone; gate on 3-mo trend) |
| **Late Aug 2026** | NVIDIA Q2 FY27 + 10-Q | Guide ≥~$100B; $1T visibility ledger intact | DC QoQ growth to single digits; inventory/receivables build; backstop obligations growing | Guide-down or visibility claim quietly retired | H2 +10 on footnote deterioration even with a headline beat |
| **Sep–Oct 2026** | Micron FQ4 (late Sep), SK hynix Q3 (late Oct) | 2027 HBM contracts **signed** at firm pricing | 2027 volumes "in negotiation" slipping; any LTA reopened | HBM LTA renegotiation/cancellation (the 2022 memory tell) | H2/H3 +10 — memory contracts crack before foundry contracts |
| **Oct 14–15, 2026** | ASML Q3; TSMC Q3 + guide | ASML holds FY26/27 framing incl. +30% EUV capacity; TSMC Q4 guide implies FY26 exit >+35%; third 2026 capex raise possible | Any ASML walk-back of 2027 capacity adds; TSMC guides Q4 conservatively or holds capex flat | TSMC cuts capex intra-year (it cut 2022→23 by ~$6B [E1 §7]) | H1 +5 / H2 +10 / H3 +15 |
| **Late Oct 2026** | Big-4 Q3 calls — first formal 2027 capex color (Meta historically frames next-year at Q3/Q4) | Early 2027 framing "significantly higher" | "Roughly in line," "moderating growth rate" | Explicit digestion language | The **single highest-information print of 2H26**: H2 +15 on moderation language |
| **Nov–Dec 2026** | CoWoS/substrate trackers; OpenAI–Broadcom N3 chip deployment target (end-2026 [E3 §5]) | Gap stays ≥10%; OpenAI ASIC ships on time | Gap →~0; "CoWoS available on request"; OpenAI ASIC slips (its ~$18B financing snag already reported [E3 §5]) | Substrate order cuts at Ibiden/Unimicron monthly tape | H2 +10–15 — a de-bottlenecked queue reveals cancellations that a sold-out queue hides |
| **~Jan 14, 2027** | TSMC 4Q26 call: FY27 capex budget + AI-accelerator CAGR update | FY27 capex ≥$70B; mid-to-high-50s% AI CAGR (2024–29) reiterated [E3 §6] | Capex $60–65B flat; CAGR language softened to "mid-40s" | Capex guided down; CAGR guide withdrawn | H1 +10 / H2 +15 / H3 +20 — management sees 12 months of firm orders |
| **~Jan 27, 2027** | ASML Q4 + **annual backlog** (now the only backlog print) | Backlog up YoY | Backlog flat/down with "timing" explanations | Backlog down >20% | H2/H3 +10–15 |
| **Late Jan–early Feb 2027** | Big-4 Q4 calls: formal 2027 capex guides | Aggregate ≥ +20% (≥ ~$850B) → digestion consensus dies (the normalization section's kill-signal #3) | ~$700–780B, 0–10% growth → the normalization section's digestion math becomes base case | Two of four guide absolute declines | **The decision print.** H1 +15 / H2 +20 / H3 +20; position-review discipline per the decision section should key off this cluster |
| **Late Feb 2027** | NVIDIA Q4 FY27 | $1T cumulative claim reconciled and extended to Rubin-next | Claim met via pull-forward, no extension | Claim quietly dropped | ±10 |
| **Mar–Apr 2027** | MOEA Feb/Mar orders (post-lunar-new-year clean read); TSMC Q1'27 print | ICT orders still +20%+ YoY; Q1 revenue ≥ guide | ICT orders negative YoY; Q1 at low end + soft Q2 guide | Q1 miss + capex cut | H2 confirmation layer |
| **Jul 2027** | TSMC Q2'27 + 1H27 monthly tape | 1H27 revenue growing YoY → H1 confirmed | 1H27 tracking Section II.3's −8±3% digestion path | Tracking Section II.3's severe path (−15%, GM ~53%) | Terminal resolution of this section's question |

Two structural read-across rules from [module: THEME_MAP_AND_READ_ACROSS.md]: apply the **asymmetric peer-warning transfer** — a warning from a *strong* actor (Microsoft, Alphabet trimming) transfers to the whole chain with full weight; a warning from the *weak* layer (a neocloud restructuring) transfers to TSMC only through NVIDIA's willingness to absorb it via backstops. And down-weight **narrative-only** H1 evidence: "demand is insatiable" without a dated capacity/contract number is vocabulary, not evidence — TSMC's own "2029–2030 strong demand" line [E1 §1] is management assertion class 0.5 until a customer's signed capex underwrites it.

### 4. The research-edge ladder: highest-VOI cultivatable sources

Ranked per [skill: variant-source-map-and-research-edge / SKILL.md Stage 3] by `mind_change × falsifiability × option_value / (cost × ambiguity × source_risk)`, with edge-decay windows per [module: SOURCE_CADENCE_AND_LATENCY_MAP.md]. Compliance boundary: public or properly licensed sources; expert work through compliant channels only; no MNPI (material non-public information).

| Rank | Source | Moves which hypothesis | Edge-decay window | Cost/access |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **The Taiwan monthly mid-chain matrix**: TSMC + Unimicron/Nan Ya PCB (substrate) + ASE/SPIL (OSAT) + Quanta/Wistron/Foxconn (server ODMs) monthly revenue, cross-tabulated — every Taiwan-listed name publishes monthly by the ~10th | H2 hardest: deceleration *sequencing* shows where in the chain the air pocket starts (substrate first = order cuts; ODM first = deployment digestion with wafers still flowing) | 1–2 quarters — the data is free but the *joint* read is rarely assembled; decays when sell-side publishes the same matrix | Free; ~2 hrs/month [module: PUBLIC_FACTS_BEFORE_MODEL_FACTS.md — boring official surfaces before famous narrative surfaces] |
| 2 | **Semicap channel checks** (subsystem book-to-bill, used-tool brokers, fab-tool lead times) | H2/H3 via TSMC's *next* capex decision, 2–3 quarters before it is guided | ~1 quarter | Medium (expert calls); questions must name the mechanism per [module: MECHANISM_TESTING_EXPERT_QUESTIONS.md] — "have N2-phase tool slots moved?" not "how's demand?" |
| 3 | **Hyperscaler DC-ops interviews** (rack-deployment vs delivery lag; installed-Blackwell utilization) | Directly tests C.C. Wei's "we monitor that chips are not going into inventory" claim [E1 §1] — the one company claim we cannot otherwise verify; discriminates stockpiling (H2 precursor) from consumption (H1) | 1–2 quarters | High cost; compliance-sensitive; small-N — triangulate ≥3 independent operators per [skill: evidence-triangulation-rules / SKILL.md] |
| 4 | **Substrate/OSAT order-book terms** (who prepays, cancellation clauses in NVIDIA's ABF LTAs) | H3: prepayment *with* cancellation optionality is much weaker demand evidence than take-or-pay; the contract form is the signal | 2–3 quarters | Medium-high (supply-chain expert work in Taiwan/Japan) |
| 5 | **TSMC symposium & technical-paper flow** (April NA Technology Symposium, ECTC/VLSI papers: A16 customer count, 14-reticle CoWoS 2028, CoPoS timing [E2 §2]) | H1's *duration* leg: capacity engineered for 2028–29 products = multi-year demand conviction expressed in R&D, a costly action | Long (6–12 months) — the market systematically underprocesses technical disclosures [module: ATTENTION_ARBITRAGE_TEST.md] | Free-to-cheap; requires technical literacy |
| 6 | **Taiwan export granularity** (MOEA orders by product; customs exports by destination; ODM Mexico/Texas server-assembly ramp) | H1/H2: system-level demand ex-tariff-noise; destination split separates US DC buildout from pull-in inventory | 1–2 months | Free |
| 7 | **Power-procurement filings** (Dominion/NextEra-class interconnect queues, study→contracted conversion rates, PJM (the mid-Atlantic grid operator) capacity auctions) | The 2028+ leg of H1: conversion *rate* of the 45GW study-phase queue is the real number; the 70GW headline is option volume [module: CONSTRAINT_OWNER_SOURCE_LADDER.md — the constraint owner is the utility/regulator, not the chip company] | Years — generalist semis investors do not read utility dockets | Free but laborious |
| 8 | **Neocloud credit tape** (GPU-ABS spreads, CoreWeave-class bond pricing, private-credit gates) | H3 hardest and earliest | Days-to-weeks — fastest decay; crowded among credit specialists, *uncrowded at the semis-equity seam* | Cheap (market data) |

**The variant-source thesis — where the market's processing is genuinely weakest.** The market metabolizes the famous prints (NVIDIA, Big-4 capex) in minutes; there is no edge there. It is demonstrably weak at three seams: (a) **the joint monthly Taiwan tape** — rank 1 above — because it sits across ~20 tickers, three sub-industries, and a language barrier, and no single analyst owns it; (b) **footnote-level accounting state changes** — depreciation lives, purchase obligations, supplier finance — which arrive quarterly, unheralded, and were the earliest honest signals in both the 2000 vendor-financing and 2022 memory cycles [agent-inferred]; and (c) **the equity-credit seam** — semis analysts read guides, credit analysts read covenants, and the GPU-collateralized debt stack ([Quartz](https://qz.com/gpu-collateralized-debt-ai-neocloud-coreweave-financing-risks-050526)) is priced by people who do not model wafer starts. An analyst who cultivates sources 1, 2, and 8 owns the H2/H3 detection problem roughly one to two quarters ahead of consensus estimate revisions, at trivial cost [agent-inferred].

---

### 5. Verdict, the most underpriced public fact, and the monitoring cadence

**Theme-level verdict** [agent-inferred]: current evidence favors **H1 into mid-2027 at roughly 60/30/10 (H1/H2/H3)** — directionally consistent with Section II.6's case weights (bull+base 65 / mediocrity 25 / bear 10) but with H2 shaded up ~5 points for four facts the physical tape does not capture: the 2026 capex raises are increasingly cost-inflation-composed (Microsoft's ~$25B component line; Meta citing memory prices); Big-4 FCF has reached ~zero, converting future raises from costless to costly; the marginal 2027 demand dollar is more circular (Oracle–OpenAI turn-on); and the chain's best early-warning series (ASML quarterly bookings) has gone dark, which raises the variance of *everyone's* estimate, including ours. Every dated physical node — HBM sold out into 2027, CoWoS gap still ~10–20%, ICT orders +50–90% YoY, ASML "close to all orders needed" for 2027 with +30% EUV capacity — reads H1. Every funding node reads "H2 risk rising." Per the skill's own rule, contradiction is information: the boom is real and increasingly fragile-financed, which is exactly the configuration where the discriminating table in §3, not conviction, should move the book.

**The single most underpriced piece of public evidence today**: **Amazon's shortening of server useful lives (6→5 years, $920M accelerated charge)** — the only Big-4 accounting action taken *against* its own reported EPS, an irreversibility-weighted admission about GPU-era asset lives, standing in direct method-conflict with Meta's simultaneous extension to 5.5 years ([Footnote Brief](https://footnotebrief.com/hyperscaler-depreciation-ai-capex-circularity/); [National Law Review](https://natlawreview.com/article/deep-quarry-useful-lives-gpus-key-considerations)). It cuts both ways, which is why nobody trades it: it indicts the earnings quality funding the buildout (H2/H3), while simultaneously *raising the maintenance-capex floor* — if accelerators really live 3–5 years, the capex needed merely to stand still in 2027–28 is materially higher than consensus digestion math assumes, cushioning TSMC's revenue in the very scenario the market fears (H2 becomes shallower than Section II.3's 2023 analog) [agent-inferred]. Runner-up: ASML's bookings-disclosure withdrawal itself — a costly transparency reduction at the cycle's most information-hungry moment.

**Monitoring cadence for this report** (per [module: SOURCE_CADENCE_AND_LATENCY_MAP.md] — cadence, threshold, refuter, stop condition):

- **Daily/weekly (5 min):** neocloud credit tape; GPU-ABS spread widening >200bps in a month = escalate to precision mode.
- **Monthly, ~8th–10th:** Taiwan exports; TSMC + substrate/OSAT/ODM monthly matrix; trigger = TSMC 3-mo YoY <+15% or substrate names decelerating while TSMC still accelerates (order-cut sequencing).
- **Monthly, ~20th:** MOEA export orders, ICT line; trigger = ICT orders YoY <0.
- **Quarterly cluster calendar:** the §3 table is the calendar — the Jul 22–30 2026 guides (days away), late-Aug NVIDIA, Sep/Oct memory, mid-Oct ASML/TSMC, late-Oct first-2027-color, and the **late-Jan/early-Feb 2027 capex-guide cluster as the designated decision print**.
- **Every 10-Q/10-K:** footnote redline on depreciation lives, purchase obligations, supplier finance, backstop disclosures across NVIDIA + Big-4 + Oracle (compose the disclosure-redline workflow the library already owns).
- **Semiannual:** TSMC symposium/technical-paper review (April) for the duration leg.
- **Stop condition:** this loop retires when the Jan–Feb 2027 guide cluster prints — at that point probability mass collapses onto one branch and the report's live question becomes execution (Section II.7's kill-signal regime), not detection.

*Parameters log (per QUERY_LADDER): web queries run 2026-07-17 across ASML Q2'26 results, Big-4 earnings dates/capex, HBM sold-out status, lab ARR, circular-financing deals, depreciation debate, Taiwan MOEA orders, Dominion queue, ABF substrate, inference-economics debate; source families = company filings/IR, government statistics, market research, financial press, specialist substacks; known gaps = TSMC official 2Q26 transcript not yet posted (secondary quotes flagged in [E1]); ASML backlog now annual; lab revenue figures unaudited and disputed; all probability shifts are judgments, not fitted frequencies.*


## Section III.7 — Cycle Dating and Price-Move Forensics

**TSMC — Taiwan Semiconductor Manufacturing Company (NYSE: TSM, one American Depositary Share (ADS) = 5 common shares; TWSE: 2330). Module date: 2026-07-17.**

*Scope: cycle position ("year ~2.5") and the 2027 digestion scenario live in the normalization section; sentiment in the investor-lens module; the serial-beater signature in the valuation section. This module: (1) forensics on 2026-06-30 → 07-16; (2) cycle dating vs hard analogs; (3) the forensic-risk sweep. Prices are TSM ADS unless marked 2330. Abbreviations: GM/OM = gross/operating margin; NI = net income; P/E TTM/NTM = trailing/forward price-earnings; IV = implied volatility; ADV = average daily volume; EM = emerging markets; ETF = exchange-traded fund; HBM = high-bandwidth memory; CoWoS = TSMC's Chip-on-Wafer-on-Substrate packaging; N2/N3 = 2nm/3nm nodes; SOXX = iShares Semiconductor ETF (tracks the PHLX Semiconductor Index, "SOX"); TWD/NT$ = New Taiwan dollar; A/R = receivables; WIP = work in progress; ATH = all-time high.*

### 1. Price-move forensics: the 11-session, −14.2% event ledger

**Bounding** [skill: price-move-forensics / SKILL.md Stage 1]: ATH close **$477.57 on 6/30**; **$409.74 on 7/16** — **−14.20% in 11 sessions** (hfdt/Polygon daily bars, 7/17; [stockanalysis.com](https://stockanalysis.com/stocks/tsm/)). No day breached −7%: a drift-with-gaps, which per the skill's decision tree biases toward *flow + sector repricing*, not a single company shock.

Pre-window context: the semis complex had already cracked. SOXX peaked **6/22 ($655.01)**, fell **−7.88% on 6/23** in a global tech rout ([CNBC, 6/23](https://www.cnbc.com/2026/06/23/tech-stocks-sell-off-mag7-samsung-sk-hynix.html)); TSM fell −6.69% that day even as 2330 printed its own ATH (NT$2,535) in Taipei that morning ([TradingEconomics](https://tradingeconomics.com/2330:tt)). Quarter-end then lifted everything: TSM **+10.5% in two sessions (6/29–6/30)** vs SOXX +8.6% ([SOXX history](https://stockanalysis.com/etf/soxx/history/)) — into the close of the **best H1 on record for U.S.-listed EM ETF inflows ($38B)**, with TSM the largest EM index position (15.18% of the flagship EM ETF) ([SSGA, 6/30](https://www.ssga.com/library-content/pdfs/etf/us/monthly-flash-flows.pdf); [EEM holdings](https://stockanalysis.com/etf/eem/holdings/)). The 6/30 ATH was partly a flow-marked price.

**Ruled-out culprits** (dated evidence, per the post-hoc-rationalization check) [module: MOVE_INVESTIGATION.md]: **Tariffs** — the 25% Section 232 advanced-chip tariff has been in force since 1/15/26, TSMC insulated by the U.S.–Taiwan quota mechanism (2.5×/1.5× duty-free allowances for U.S.-building Taiwanese producers; [USTR, 2/2026](https://ustr.gov/about/policy-offices/press-office/fact-sheets/2026/february/fact-sheet-us-taiwan-agreement-reciprocal-trade)); the July 1 Commerce data-center report deadline passed without published action — a pending "Phase 2" overhang, not an in-window shock ([TariffLens](https://www.tarifflens.ai/blog/section-232-semiconductor-tariff-july-2026-phase-2-guide)). Tariff-driven looks like April 2025 (2330 limit-down −10%; [Bloomberg, 2025-04-07](https://www.bloomberg.com/news/articles/2025-04-07/tsmc-slumps-10-on-tariff-woes-as-taiwan-returns-from-holiday)); July resembled nothing of the sort. **TWD** — USD/TWD 32.21, TWD −9.5% YoY ([TradingEconomics](https://tradingeconomics.com/taiwan/currency)); a *weakening* TWD is a GM tailwind (+~40bp per 1%, CFO sensitivity, 2Q25 transcript) — exculpatory. **Downgrades** — none; only raises into the print ([stockanalysis forecast](https://stockanalysis.com/stocks/tsm/forecast/)). **Shorts** — 0.57% of shares out; nothing to cascade.

#### Decomposition: sector beta vs TSM-specific vs flow

[skill: price-move-forensics / module: MOVE_DECOMPOSITION_AND_THESIS_UPDATE.md; cross-checked with skill: macro-cycle-sensitivity / module: MACRO_BETA_DOMINANCE_KILL_SWITCH.md]

**(a) Sector beta — dominant.** SOXX −17.2% vs TSM −14.2%: naive 1:1 sector attribution *over-explains* the move by ~3pp. [Agent-inferred: regressing TSM's 11 daily returns on SOXX's gives in-window slope ≈ 0.66 (Σxy/Σx² = 115.8/175.8); 0.66 × −17.2% ≈ **−11.4pp sector**, residual ≈ −2.8pp.] The basket lost ~$2.1T from the 6/22 SOX peak, **median constituent −21%** ([Yahoo](https://finance.yahoo.com/markets/article/the-2-trillion-chip-sell-off-hits-a-make-or-break-level-chart-of-the-day-155000822.html)); Samsung −25%+, SK Hynix −30% from peaks. TSM at −14% was a *relative outperformer inside its own drawdown*. And the leg was not customer-led: NVDA — TSMC's largest customer (19% of 2025 revenue, [FY25 20-F](https://www.stocktitan.net/sec-filings/TSM/20-f-taiwan-semiconductor-manufacturing-co-ltd-files-annual-report-fo-e7792df70159.html)) — was **+3.65% over the identical window** (its own −13% drawdown from the 5/14 $235.47 ATH came earlier). July concentrated in memory, equipment, AI-cloud intermediaries, and late-peaking holdouts — TSM the largest of the latter.

**(b) Flow/positioning — the measurement-origin bucket.** [Agent-inferred, math shown:] from the *pre-markup* 6/26 base ($432.35), TSM fell **−3.0%** into the print (7/15) and **−5.2%** through print day — vs SOXX −5.9% / −10.1% from its 6/26 base. **~5pp of the "−14%" headline is the round-trip of the 6/29–6/30 quarter-end markup**, fully reversed by 7/2. Flow markers: heaviest non-print volume on the unwind days (18.4–18.6M vs 15.4M ADV); TSM flat on both big bounce days (7/9, 7/14); negative dealer gamma below $443; and an ADS premium of ~+7–9% over 2330 (derived in the market evidence file) that compresses when U.S. passive demand ebbs. This bucket changes path and entry, not earnings power [module: MOVE_DECOMPOSITION_AND_THESIS_UPDATE.md — mechanical: keep model unchanged].

**(c) TSM-specific — small and net-positive until the print.** June revenue +67.9% (7/10); target raises (7/14); one narrow negative (Intel tile insourcing, 7/15 — one customer's compute-tile share cut to 10–20% of total tiles (vs a planned 60–70% outsourced), against record 72.3% foundry share, [TrendForce, 6/12](https://www.trendforce.com/presscenter/news/20260612-13095.html)); print-day guide optics worth −1 to −2pp (§2). Residual ≈ zero — matching the Stage-2 rule: peers moved similarly or worse → default attribution external [skill: root-cause-diagnostic-loop / SKILL.md Stage 2].

**Ledger verdict** (mistaken/justified/mechanical): justified *sector* repricing (the assumed duration of excess AI demand shortened on real supply-side evidence) + mechanical quarter-end round-trip + a small justified company-specific margin-guide component. **No component impairs 2026 earnings power; the repriced object is the multiple's duration assumption.**

### 2. The beat-and-fall, 7/16: what −2.3% on a record print says

**The print**: revenue US$40.20B (+33.7% YoY in US$, +36.0% in NT$; top of guide); GM **67.7%** (above the 65.5–67.5% guided range); OM 60.3%; NI NT$706.6B (+77%) vs NT$632.6B consensus — **+11.7% NI beat** [computed]; EPS $4.31/ADS vs the options-market $3.88 line (+11%; ~+14% vs one aggregator's consensus) ([TSMC 6-K, 7/16](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1046179/000104617926000451/a2q26e_withguidancexfinal.htm); [BigGo](https://finance.biggo.com/news/44b11c82-84bf-446c-aa40-8ce8b2bc6554); [Alphastreet](https://news.alphastreet.com/tsmc-tsm-q2-results-beat-again-but-higher-expectations-still-drive-the-trade/)). FY26 guide raised to "slightly above 40%" USD growth; capex to $60–64B; +$100B U.S. investment ([Reuters via Yahoo, 7/16](https://finance.yahoo.com/technology/ai/articles/tsmc-invest-another-100-billion-100633089.html)). **Not a whisper miss on any reported line.**

**The tape**: pre-market −6% (~$422 → ~$397) ([Invezz, 7/16](https://invezz.com/news/2026/07/16/why-is-tsmc-stock-falling-despite-record-q2-earnings/)); opened $405.29, session low $403.50 early, then *climbed all day* to close $409.74 — upper half of range, above open and VWAP ($408.41) — on 24.9M ADS = **1.62× ADV** (hfdt/Polygon). That volume is elevated but sits below the 2–5× "informed" band (and far from the 5–10× forced band), not the 5–10× "forced" band; pattern = gap-down-then-absorb [module: MOVE_INVESTIGATION.md matrix]. Meanwhile **2330 closed >1% higher** on the same numbers in the next Taipei session ([Investing Engineer](https://investingengineer.com/tsmc-earnings-preview-july-16-2026/)) and **SOXX fell −4.46%**: on its own catalyst day TSM *beat its sector by >2pp and its home listing rose*. The U.S. close is the sector tape wearing a TSMC headline.

**Implied vs realized**: options priced **±5.6–6.7%** (~$150B of cap; [Benzinga, 7/14](https://www.benzinga.com/markets/options/26/07/60411633/tsmc-could-swing-150-billion-as-options-market-prices-big-moves-for-this-weeks-earnings)); realized −2.32% ≈ **0.35–0.41× implied** [computed]. Against the braced pre-event configuration (put/call 1.31, IV 96th percentile, negative gamma), the print resolved uncertainty *downward but mildly* and crushed vol [skill: price-move-forensics / module: PRE_EVENT_SENTIMENT_AND_OPTION_ANOMALY_PRIOR.md — defensive skew was the prior; the small move was its in-line resolution].

**What was actually sold — two forward-looking optics:**

1. **The first guided sequential GM decline of the cycle.** Q3 guide 65–67% vs 67.7% actual: **−1.7pp QoQ at midpoint while revenue is guided +12% QoQ** [computed from 6-K]. Under the hood it's worse: the guide embeds USD/TWD 32.0 vs 31.60 realized — a ~1.25% weaker TWD ≈ +50bp GM *tailwind* at the 40bp/1% sensitivity — so underlying dilution ≈ **−2.2pp** [computed], from the steep N2 ramp (2–3pp FY dilution guided in January, 2H-weighted; same-day coverage cited 3–4pp in 2H — [Investing.com highlights, 7/16](https://ca.investing.com/news/company-news/taiwan-semiconductor-manufacturing-co-ltd-tsm-q2-2026-earnings-call-highlights-strong--4737378)), overseas-fab dilution (2–3pp→3–4pp), and high-teens depreciation growth ([4Q25 transcript](https://investor.tsmc.com/english/encrypt/files/encrypt_file/reports/2026-01/51d09df96cd89ac19d65af39032b038dc2896a24/TSMC%204Q25%20Transcript.pdf)). For holders underwriting *margin momentum*, 67.7% is now officially the local top — a defensible, dated read, even though the fade is management-engineered growth dilution, not pricing erosion.
2. **The capex raise, read through a July lens.** $60–64B (from "high end of $52–56B" in April) would have been celebrated in January; in a week whose organizing fear was AI-capex sustainability, the supplier raising its own capex read as cost/duration risk — and the *sector* fell on it ([Stocktwits](https://stocktwits.com/news-articles/markets/equity/s-and-p-500-nasdaq-dow-end-lower-as-chip-stocks-slide-on-tsmc-capex-concerns-tsm-mu-nvda-unh-googl-in-focus/cZZEFzQR7s6); [Invezz](https://invezz.com/za/news/2026/07/16/micron-sk-hynix-other-memory-stocks-fall-as-tsmc-outlook-sparks-chip-selloff/)). Same signal, opposite sign vs January: a regime marker, not an information marker.

**Classification** [skill: price-move-forensics / module: PRICE_MOVE_FOUR_BUCKET_CLASSIFICATION.md]: **bucket 3 (narrative: "margin peak printed; buildout duration shortening") with a thin bucket-4 sliver** (the guided 2H fade is a real, dated input — update the H2 model, not the thesis) plus bucket-2 tape (sector, gamma). The bucket-2→4 promotion channel (cost-of-equity impairing the business model) is **dead** here: ~US$83B net cash (NT$3,518B cash/securities vs NT$864B LT debt, [2Q26 presentation](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1046179/000104617926000451/a2q26presentatione.htm)) and self-funded capex mean zero capital-markets dependence. The Samsung rhyme nine days earlier (−6.9% on a ~19x profit surge) confirms: in July 2026 the complex *taxes* records — this is the market's reaction function, not a TSMC-quality verdict.

### 3. Cycle dating: six downturns, and what 2026 does and doesn't look like

[skill: macro-cycle-sensitivity / SKILL.md Stage 2 + module: CYCLE_EXPOSURE.md through-cycle method]

| Cycle | Trigger | Revenue peak→trough | GM compression | EPS | ADS drawdown / duration | Lead indicators at the peak |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **2000–01** | Dot-com telecom/PC bust after double-ordered 2000 | FY01 ~−24% NT$; sales forecast cut −18% intra-year ([EDN, 2001](https://www.edn.com/tsmc-cuts-2001-earnings-estimate-by-nearly-75/)) | FY ~46%→mid-20s%; utilization <50% [agent-inferred from public annuals] | ~−75–85%; pretax target cut ~73% (EDN) | ~−75%, ~20 mo [agent-inferred] | Book-to-bill <1 late 2000; fabless inventory bulge; double ordering |
| **2008–09** | Global demand shock + inventory flush | FY09 −11.2% NT$; trough qtr 1Q09 **−54.8% YoY**, then +87.9% QoQ (V) ([TSMC 1Q09 release](https://pr.tsmc.com/english/news/1548)) | Quarterly ~47%→**18.9%** (1Q09), 46.2% by 2Q09 (same source) | 1Q09 EPS NT$0.06; FY09 ~−11% | ~−55%, ~16 mo [agent-inferred] | Orders stopped mid-quarter; utilization ~40%; capex slashed |
| **2011** | Euro-debt/inventory | FY11 ~flat NT$ | ~49%→~45% [agent-inferred] | ~−16% | ~−30%, <1 yr [agent-inferred] | Utilization dip only |
| **2015–16** | Handset inventory, China scare | **No annual decline** (FY15 +10.6%); 2H15 QoQ down-legs | Quarterly ~50%→~45% [agent-inferred] | Grew | ~−27% (Apr–Aug 15) [agent-inferred] | Handset channel inventory; top-customer digestion |
| **2018–19** | Crypto-ASIC collapse + handset plateau | FY19 +3.7% NT$/~+1% USD (4Q19 mgmt report); 1Q19 −16% QoQ | FY 48.3%→46.0%; 1Q19 qtr 41.3% [FY primary] | ~flat | ~−27% (Jun–Oct 18) [agent-inferred] | Crypto demand evaporating; 7nm inventory; **capex RAISED into the trough** ($14.9B, +42% — the 5nm bet) |
| **2022–23** | Double-ordered shortage unwind; consumer bust | FY23 **−8.7% USD/−4.5% NT$**; shipments **−21%** (4Q23 mgmt report) | FY 59.6%→54.4% (**−5.2pp**); qtrly ~62%→~53% [FY primary] | FY23 **−17.5%** | **−59%**, ~10 mo [agent-inferred]; year-end P/E 27.2→**11.2** ([companiesmarketcap](https://companiesmarketcap.com/tsmc/pe-ratio/)) | Record fabless inventory days; capex plan cut $44B→$36B→$30B; N7 utilization rolling 2H22; monthly YoY negative Feb-23 |

Readings: (1) **drawdown magnitude tracks whether revenue actually declines** — the growth-year corrections (2015, 2018) were −27% multiple events with flat-to-up EPS; the revenue recessions were −55% to −75%; 2022–23 was both. (2) **GM compresses every time (−2pp to −28pp)** and starts before the revenue trough. (3) Lead-indicator order: customer/channel inventory → foundry utilization cracks → capex cut → monthly revenue YoY rollover. (4) The one countercyclical capex raise (2019) preceded TSMC's best half-decade — capex direction alone is not diagnostic without a demand-side confirm.

**The 2026 marker checklist** [skill: macro-cycle-sensitivity Stage 2: late cycle = peak margins, tight capacity, over-earning]:

*Present — supply-side late-cycle signatures (8 fully present, 2 emerging, of 10):*
1. **Record margins**: GM 67.7%, an all-time high, **+8.1pp above the FY22 peak-year 59.6%** ([6-K](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1046179/000104617926000451/a2q26e_withguidancexfinal.htm)).
2. **Record share**: 72.3%, +~11pp in nine quarters ([TrendForce, 6/12](https://www.trendforce.com/presscenter/news/20260612-13095.html)).
3. **Multiple above the band**: TTM P/E ~33× vs 11.2–28.8× ten-year year-end range ([stockanalysis](https://stockanalysis.com/stocks/tsm/statistics/); companiesmarketcap, 7/16).
4. **Capex racing**: raised twice in six months; 2Q26 capex/revenue ≈ **39%** vs 33% FY24 [computed from 6-K].
5. **Allocation economics**: CoWoS sold out, one customer ~60% of allocations, prepayments standard — quantity rationing ([TrendForce, 6/15](https://www.trendforce.com/news/2026/06/15/news-tsmc-cowos-supply-demand-gap-reportedly-seen-narrowing-from-20-to-10-by-end-2026-as-capacity-expands/)).
6. **Working capital stretching**: inventory days **76→87**, A/R days 23→29 YoY ([2Q26 presentation](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1046179/000104617926000451/a2q26presentatione.htm)) — the odometer that read ~90+ before the 2023 downturn, though confounded by N2 WIP and overseas buffers.
7. **Utilization >100%** on N3 (call coverage, [BigGo, 7/16](https://finance.biggo.com/news/5784aaf1-fcbc-4f76-8856-491b9e7175f6)).
8. **Capacity response started**: CoWoS gap guided 20%→10% by end-2026 (TrendForce); Samsung's foundry at first monthly profit in ~3 years with a U.S. 2nm automotive tape-out ([Korea Times, 7/13](https://www.koreatimes.co.kr/business/tech-science/20260713/samsung-taylor-fab-enters-production-phase-for-teslas-ai5-chip)); Intel insourcing tiles (TrendForce, 7/15). Windfalls end by capacity response; it is now visible.
9. **Beat-and-fall regime** across the complex (TSM 7/16; Samsung 7/7) — the market's own late-cycle tell.
10. **Customer capex parabolic**: big-4 hyperscalers ≈ $700–725B 2026, **+77% YoY** ([Tom's Hardware](https://www.tomshardware.com/tech-industry/big-tech/big-techs-ai-spending-plans-reach-725-billion)).

*Absent — demand-side rollover signatures, ~1 partial of 6:*
1. **No demand deceleration**: June +67.9% YoY; FY guide *raised* to >40%; largest customer's data-center revenue +92% YoY ([NVIDIA Q1 FY27](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/0001045810/000104581026000051/q1fy27pr.htm)).
2. **No estimate cuts / book-to-bill rollover**: N2 tape-outs ~4× N3 at the same stage ([DigiTimes, 7/16](https://www.digitimes.com/news/a20260716VL228/tsmc-3nm-2nm-production-design.html)); consensus 2026 EPS +49% with raises into the print.
3. **No demonstrated double-ordering at the core**: management audits "customers' customers" and data-center progress ([call coverage, 7/16](https://www.investing.com/news/transcripts/earnings-call-transcript-tsmc-lifts-2026-outlook-as-ai-demand-stays-hot-in-q2-2026-93CH-4794777)); the one proven stockpiling channel (a sanctioned Chinese buyer's die bank) is exhausted ([SemiAnalysis](https://newsletter.semianalysis.com/p/huawei-ascend-production-ramp)). *Partial exception*: tariff-hedging consumer pull-ins (TrendForce Q1'26).
4. **End-demand funded**: top-10 customers = 78% of revenue (FY25 20-F), dominated by the largest cash generators in corporate history; financing strain only at the fringe (a reported $18B custom-chip financing snag at one AI lab, [5/2026](https://winbuzzer.com/2026/05/08/openais-ai-chip-deal-with-broadcom-hits-18-billion-xcxwbn/)) — watch it migrate inward.
5. **The foundry is not the over-builder**: TSMC capex +~50% vs customers' +77% ([Stratechery, 1/26](https://stratechery.com/2026/tsmc-risk/), updated for the 7/16 raise [computed]).
6. **No customer channel-inventory evidence** in-window; rising memory prices argue the opposite.

**Verdict: priced and margined like a peak, ordered like a mid-cycle** — supply-side markers ~8/10 present, demand-side rollover ~1/6 partial. Closest analog: **2015/2018 (multiple event in a growth year) carrying the *pre-echo* of 2022's working-capital and capex signatures** — not 2001/2009, not yet 2022. In every prior episode from this configuration, margin and multiple revert first, revenue last; −27% was the full extent of the 2015/2018 class. Whether 2027 converts this into the 2022 class is the normalization section's question.

---

### 4. Forensic-risk sweep

[skill: mean-reversion-and-forensic-risk / SKILL.md Stages 1–2; module: RISK_REGISTER_METHOD.md categories 1–6]

**Revenue quality (prepayments/take-or-pay).** Prepayments sit as liabilities; cash precedes revenue; recognition is on shipment — conservative, with no inflation channel. The real risk is informational: allocation-era deposits make demand look contractually firm, but FY23 proved customers pay to walk — shipments **−21%** while NT$ revenue fell only −4.5% (pricing/mix cushioned) [4Q23 mgmt report]. Deposits bound the P&L's downside, not the order book's.

**Margin sustainability (owned elsewhere — one paragraph).** The fade is management-guided and dated: N2 dilution 2–3pp FY26 (2H-weighted; 3–4pp in 2H per call coverage), overseas fabs 2–3→3–4pp, depreciation +high-teens, vs a stated "56%+ through-cycle" floor and 3–10% price hikes reportedly recurring through 2029 ([TrendForce, 12/29/25](https://www.trendforce.com/news/2025/12/29/news-tsmc-reportedly-to-raise-sub-3nm-prices-3-10-in-2026-plans-hikes-through-2029/)). One test belongs here because it *moves the reversion anchor* [module: INDUSTRY_WIDE_MARGIN_LIFT_IS_A_WINDFALL_NOT_A_MOAT.md]: the peer-universality check **fails** — UMC GM 29.2%, GlobalFoundries 27.6%, Intel's foundry −$10.3B FY25 operating loss, Samsung's foundry barely at break-even (citations in the market evidence file). A margin lift no peer shared is not a windfall; it is idiosyncratic (share, node monopoly, pricing). Anchor to the guided ~56–60% floor, not the pre-AI band — but do not capitalize 67.7% [module: PEAK_FACT_REVERSION_BURDEN.md — actor: TSMC's own ramp/depreciation/FX; timing: 2H26–2027; falsifier: GM ≥66% held through 2027].

**Accounting red flags — verified (one quality flag, two ambers, no red):**
- *Receivables vs revenue*: A/R days 29 vs 23 YoY — growing faster than +36% revenue [2Q26 presentation]. Absolute level still short; consistent with invoice concentration/quarter-end skew. **Amber-monitor.**
- *Inventory composition*: 87 vs 76 days — N2 ramp WIP + overseas buffers (benign) vs the 2022 analog of ~90+ days pre-downturn (malign). Watch a third consecutive rise. **Amber.**
- *One-offs inside the +77%*: non-operating items NT$95.8B (+232% QoQ) include a **NT$63.2B VIS (Vanguard International Semiconductor) disposal/mark-to-market gain — ~9% of quarterly NI** [2Q26 mgmt report; computed]. Use operating income (+65.4% YoY) as the run-rate line. **Disclosed, benign, but strip it.**
- *Related-party*: the VIS sale is a divestiture of an affiliate; no related-party revenue concerns. **Clean.**
- *Grant accounting*: CHIPS $6.6B is milestone-disbursed ($1.5B received); no equity conversion applied to TSMC (unlike the U.S. stake in a competitor); the risk is political, not accounting. **Clean.**
- *FCF vs NI*: 2Q FCF NT$287B vs NI NT$707B — the gap is disclosed capex (39% of revenue), not accruals; same pattern as the 2021 buildout. **Clean — but reported EPS carries a growing embedded depreciation claim.**
- *Leverage*: **net cash ≈ US$83B** [NT$3,518B − NT$864B at 32; computed]. No escalation rule triggers; the solvency leg is empty [skill: mean-reversion-and-forensic-risk escalation rules].
- *Regulatory*: owned elsewhere; in-window item = pending Section 232 "Phase 2" scope ([TariffLens](https://www.tarifflens.ai/blog/section-232-semiconductor-tariff-july-2026-phase-2-guide)), mitigated by the quota exemption.

**Mean-reversion candidates, ranked, with base rates from §3** [module: MEAN_REVERSION_MECHANISM_TEST.md — no mechanism, no reversion call]:

| Rank | Metric (2Q26) | Base rate | Mechanism & timing | Anchor |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **GM 67.7%** | 6/6 prior GM peaks reverted within 4–6 quarters (−2pp mild; −5.2pp FY22→23; −28pp quarterly 1Q09) | Named and dated *by management*: N2 + overseas fabs + depreciation, 2H26–2027; Q3 guide already −1.7pp (−2.2pp ex-FX [computed]) | Guided high-50s/low-60s floor — **not** the historical mid-40s (windfall test failed; lift idiosyncratic) |
| 2 | **Revenue growth ~+40%** | Of NT$ growth years ≥+30% (2020/2022/2024/2025), one completed sequence rolled negative (2022 +42.6% → 2023 −4.5%); management's own 2024–29 frame ~25% CAGR | AI-capex digestion (2027; owned elsewhere); coincident tell = first sub-30% YoY month | ~25% corridor; digestion year = negative possible |
| 3 | **ROE 45.9% annualized** (35.4% FY25) | FY band ~21–35% last decade; management through-cycle "high-twenties" | Arithmetic: equity compounding (+NT$3.0T since end-2023) + GM fade; no adverse event needed | High-20s through cycle |
| 4 | **P/E ~33× TTM / 20× NTM** | Ten-year year-end band 11.2–28.8×; 2022: 27.2×→11.2× (−59%) during a −4.5% NT$ revenue year — *the multiple, not EPS, does the drawdown damage* | Duration re-rating already underway: −14% price with rising estimates ≈ NTM ~23×→20× in 11 sessions [computed] | Owned by the valuation section |

---

### 5. Root-cause discipline: "record everything, stock falls"

[skill: root-cause-diagnostic-loop / SKILL.md + module: DIAGNOSTIC_FRAMEWORK.md — macro → competition → management, in that order]

**Stage 1 — anchor.** The fundamental surprise was *positive* (+11.7% NI beat, raised guide); the anomaly is the price (−2.3% day; −14.2% window). Per the framework's first pitfall, a −2.3% reaction to a +11.7% beat is a statement about *duration/discount rate*, not the quarter.

**Stage 2 — macro/external (first, mandatory).** Peers moved the same or worse: SOXX −17.2% same window; the closest comparable fell −6.9% *on its own record print* nine days earlier; basket median −21% from the 6/22 peak. Rule: peers within 1 SD → external unless company-specific cause is proven. Here **the external factor over-explains the move** (TSM outperformed the basket by ~3pp; +2pp on print day). *Attribution lands here.*

**Stage 3 — competition (run on the ≈0 residual).** The in-window facts point the wrong way for a competition story: record 72.3% share; competitor 2nm yields ~mid-50s% vs TSMC's reported 60–70%; the one adverse item (tile insourcing, 7/15) trims a single allocation, not the franchise. *Rejected as driver; logged as watch item.*

**Stage 4 — management (only now permitted).** No execution miss. The two punished actions — capex raise, margin guide — are *choices consistent with strong demand*; the sector fell on them, and an identical reaction hit a peer's record print the same week. Reaction function is regime-level. *Rejected as root cause; retained as print-day proximate trigger.*

**Stage 5A — cause-of-cause chain** ("sector selloff" is an outcome, not a cause):

```
record print, stock falls
→ proximate driver: multiple compression (NTM P/E ~23× → 20× in 11 sessions while estimates ROSE) [computed]
→ why the multiple moved: the marginal holder's assumed DURATION of excess AI demand shortened
→ underlying causal variable: first credible supply-catching-demand evidence, all dated in-window —
   a hyperscaler reselling surplus compute (7/1); a major AI lab designing in-house inference
   silicon (7/7); HBM shipment-pace doubt at a key memory maker (7/13); the packaging supply
   gap guided to halve by year-end (6/15)
→ durability class: cyclical-perception variable (duration re-rating), not current-earnings
   impairment — orders, pricing, and guidance all moved UP through the window
→ thesis-leg implication: hits the multiple leg and the year-3+ growth leg; the 2026 earnings
   leg is untouched
```

**Most probable root cause:** a crowded, quarter-end-marked-up AI-semiconductor complex re-rated the *duration* of the buildout on genuine supply-side inflection evidence; TSMC — the latest-peaking, highest-quality name in the basket — absorbed the basket derating at ~0.7 realized beta, plus a same-day expectations tax on the first guided sequential margin decline of its cycle. Confidence: high on external attribution (the peer test is decisive); moderate on the flow-vs-narrative split [agent-inferred, §1]. Displaced comfortable narratives: "the market rejected TSMC's quarter" (falsified — home listing rose, sector fell more) and "AI demand is rolling over" (falsified — every dated demand print in the window went up).

**Confirm/kill observations, next 90 days:**
1. **Hyperscaler Q3 reports (~late Oct)** — the 2027 capex frameworks. Growth (even decelerating) kills digestion-now; "optimize/digest" language confirms it and promotes bucket 3 → bucket 4 [module: PRICE_MOVE_FOUR_BUCKET_CLASSIFICATION.md promotion discipline].
2. **TSMC's own prints** — monthly revenue (~Aug/Sep/Oct 10): a first sub-30% YoY month is the coincident rollover tell (June: +67.9%); 3Q26 report (~mid-Oct): GM inside/above 65–67% with a ≥65% 4Q guide and *no capex trim* kills "margin peak = estimate peak"; GM <65% or a capex cut confirms — capex cuts led every genuine downturn in §3.
3. **Supply-gap telemetry** — CoWoS gap path (20%→10% guided), HBM contract-price direction, flagship GPU roadmap schedule ([TrendForce delay-risk, 4/8/26](https://www.trendforce.com/presscenter/news/20260408-13003.html)): oversupply prints or a material slip confirm 2027 digestion; allocation persisting into 2027 with wafer-price hikes sticking kills it. Tape check: >50% retracement of the 7/16 drop within five sessions reads it as flow; a sustained hold below ~$400 reads the duration repricing as persisting [module: MOVE_INVESTIGATION.md 5-day follow-through].

**Bottom line:** the −14% decomposes as ~−11 to −17pp of sector derating (≈5pp of it the round-trip of a quarter-end flow markup) against a ~zero-to-positive company-specific residual until the −1 to −2pp margin-guide nitpick on print day. The cycle map files the drawdown, so far, in the 2015/2018 "multiple event in a growth year" class — but with supply-side late-cycle markers at 8-for-10, the configuration in which margin and multiple revert first and the burden of proof shifts to the 2027 demand evidence above.

*Compliance note: internal research over public data; not investment advice. Flags are hypotheses to investigate, not accusations.*


## Section III.8 — Flow Architecture, Index Plumbing, and Instrument Selection

*As of 2026-07-17. TSM (NYSE American Depositary Share, "ADS"; 1 ADS = 5 common shares) closed $409.74 on 2026-07-16; TWSE 2330 closed NT$2,470 the same date (pre-print session). This module extends — and does not repeat — Section II.5 §1 (which owns the wrapper card and *why* the ADS premium exists) and Section II.4 §6 (which owns the crowding verdict and the premium-as-gauge headline). Here we build the quantified passive-flow map, the S&P 500 exclusion economics, the premium's regime history and thresholds, the holder-flow forensics, and the instrument-selection waterfall. Analytical only: this section ranks claims against thesis variants; it contains no trade, sizing, or execution instruction.*

---

### 1. The passive-flow map, quantified — the mandate stack that must hold TSM

*Method: [skill: index-reconstitution-and-forced-flows / modules: FLOW_SIZING_AND_ADV.md ("name the forced pool"; AUM × weight; days-to-clear), INDEX_MECHANICS.md (MSCI SAIR/QIR clocks, capping/reclassification flows)]. AUM = assets under management; ADV = average daily volume; EM/DM = emerging/developed markets; MSCI SAIR/QIR = semi-annual (May/Nov) and quarterly (Feb/Aug) index reviews.*

**The weight stack (all sourced):**

| Layer | Number | Source (date) |
|---|---|---|
| Taiwan weight in MSCI EM | **24.8%** — the largest country weight, above China for the first time since July 2007 | [MSCI markets-in-motion](https://www.msci.com/indexes/markets-in-motion/visualizations/taiwan-at-the-top-msci-em-weight-breakdown) (as of 2026-04-30); [Bloomberg](https://www.bloomberg.com/news/articles/2026-02-02/ai-fever-propels-taiwan-s-rise-over-china-in-em-stocks-benchmark) (2026-02-02) |
| May-2026 SAIR adjustment | Taiwan raised +0.30pp to **23.76%** of MSCI EM (announced pro-forma review weight; the 24.8% row above is the market-moved end-April weight, which the downstream flow math uses); TSMC raised +0.56pp to **58.33% of MSCI Taiwan** | [Taipei Times](https://www.taipeitimes.com/News/biz/archives/2026/05/14/2003857281) (2026-05-14); [Focus Taiwan](https://focustaiwan.tw/business/202605130009) (2026-05-13) |
| TSMC weight in MSCI EM | **>14%** (≈ 0.583 × 24%); TSMC alone now outweighs *all of India* in the index | evid-05 §6 (Lazard/Callan/Mitrade, 2026-03/07); [eworld substack](https://eworld.substack.com/p/emerging-markets-mirror-us-with-tech) (2026) |
| TSMC in the flagship EM ETFs | EEM **15.18% (#1 holding)**; VWO (FTSE benchmark) **8.97% (#1)** | [stockanalysis EEM holdings](https://stockanalysis.com/etf/eem/holdings/) (2026-07-16); [etfchannel VWO](https://www.etfchannel.com/symbol/vwo/) (retrieved 2026-07-17) |
| Assets benchmarked to MSCI EM indexes | **~$1.8T** (active + passive) | [MSCI](https://www.msci.com/indexes/group/emerging-markets-indexes) (as of 2025-12-31) |
| Big-3 US EM ETF AUM | IEMG **$160.7B**, VWO **~$123–146B**, EEM **$24.9B** | [trendspider](https://trendspider.com/learning-center/eem-vs-vwo-vs-iemg-which-emerging-market-etf-actually-fits-your-portfolio/), [etfdb](https://etfdb.com/tool/etf-comparison/IEMG-VWO/) (retrieved 2026-07-17) |
| TSMC + Samsung + SK Hynix | ≈ **30% of MSCI EM** | evid-05 §6 (Mitrade, 2026-07-13) |

**Passive-held dollars of TSM [agent-inferred, math shown].** Bottom-up: the three US flagship ETFs alone hold ≈ $160.7B×15% + $24.9B×15.2% + $135B×9.0% ≈ **$40B** of TSMC. Grossing to the full passive complex — non-US UCITS EM ETFs, EM index mutual funds, MSCI ACWI/FTSE All-World trackers (TSMC ≈ 1.5% of ACWI ≈ 24.8%×~10% EM share × 58% [agent-inferred]), dedicated Taiwan vehicles (EWT $6.1B at a 21% cap), and the newly unshackled local Taiwan funds (§3) — a defensible range is **$90–140B of TSMC held by pure passive**, i.e., **4.5–7% of the ~$2.0T local-listing market cap**. The wider *mandate-anchored* pool (anything benchmarked to MSCI EM, which behaves semi-forced at reviews) is ~$1.8T × ~13.7% ≈ **~$245B, or ~12% of the company**. Note the plumbing detail: index vehicles predominantly hold the **local 2330 line** through Taiwan FINI registration (Foreign Institutional Investor channel), not the ADS **[agent-inferred; verify against fund holdings files]** — which means the mechanical index bid props the *ord*, while the ADS float (20.5% of shares, 20-F FY2025) is where *discretionary, non-benchmarked* USD money lives. That asymmetry is why the ADS premium works as a US-sentiment gauge (§3).

**Routine flow arithmetic.** EM ETFs took in a record **$38B in H1-2026** ([SSGA flash flows](https://www.ssga.com/library-content/pdfs/etf/us/monthly-flash-flows.pdf), 2026-06-30; evid-05). At a ~14% weight that is ≈ **$5.3B of mechanical TSMC buying in six months** [computed] against a combined tape of roughly **$10B/day** (ADS: 15.4M ADS × $410 ≈ $6.3B/day, stockanalysis 2026-07-16; local 2330 turnover ≈ $3–6B/day [agent-inferred]). Days-to-clear ≪ 1× ADV: trivially absorbed on the way in — and, per the module's warning, exactly as *simultaneous and symmetric* on the way out [module: FLOW_SIZING_AND_ADV.md]. A −$20B EM-redemption quarter implies only ~$2.8B of forced TSMC selling — the passive anchor is a slow tide, not a cliff. The cliff scenarios are the *rule changes*, below.

**Where the cap rules bind — the "too big for EM" problem.** Three separate constraint regimes now interact with a 14–15% single-stock index weight:

- **UCITS 5/10/40** (EU Undertakings for Collective Investment in Transferable Securities rules: max 10% of a fund in one issuer; holdings above 5% may not sum past 40%). A UCITS EM fund **cannot hold TSMC at benchmark weight** — a *perpetual structural underweight* of 4–5pp that becomes a guaranteed performance drag whenever TSMC outperforms. A June-2026 sell-side note flagged managers "approaching or hitting their concentration ceilings" ([cryptobriefing](https://cryptobriefing.com/em-funds-max-out-tsmc-samsung-sk-hynix/), 2026; [Lazard AM](https://www.lazardassetmanagement.com/us/en_us/research-insights/market-insights/beyond-the-numbers/march-2026), 2026-03).
- **US RIC diversification** (Regulated Investment Company, IRC §851: at quarter-end, ≥50% of assets must sit in positions each ≤5%; no single issuer >25%). Broad EM funds can still carry 15% TSMC inside the non-diversified bucket, but single-country vehicles cannot: **EWT tracks the MSCI Taiwan 25/50 Index and holds TSMC at ~21% — the methodology maximum — versus a 58.33% uncapped weight** ([investing.com EWT holdings](https://www.investing.com/etfs/ishares-taiwan-index-holdings), retrieved 2026-07-17; [Callan](https://www.callan.com/blog/emerging-market-concentration/), 2026). A ~37pp forced structural underweight, mechanically re-trimmed at every rebalance TSMC outperforms into.
- **Index capping risk.** MSCI EM itself is uncapped today, but capped variants exist, and every incremental point of TSMC weight raises the probability that either the provider (a capped-index consultation) or the fund boards (more 25/50-style vehicles) impose one. A capping event converts the passive anchor into a **forced seller at AUM × Δweight** [module: INDEX_MECHANICS.md §4] — on $500–700B of MSCI-EM-tracking passive [agent-inferred], each 1pp of weight cap ≈ **$5–7B of forced supply**, 0.5–0.7 days of tape: absorbable, but a permanent demand-curve kink.

Net structural read: TSMC is simultaneously the EM index's anchor tenant *and* too big for a growing subset of its own holder base. The capped funds are forced sellers into strength; the uncapped active EM funds sitting at their 10% ceilings are *unable* marginal buyers. **The index door that made TSM crowded is starting to narrow mechanically at the margin — at exactly the weight where the company's fundamentals are pulling it higher.** [skill: index-reconstitution-and-forced-flows / module: INDEX_INCLUSION_FORCED_BUYING_VS_TRUE_FLOAT.md — the binding denominator is who *can* buy, not headline float.]

**The Taiwan→DM reclassification scenario.** The periodic debate — Taiwan is developed by income, size, and market infrastructure; MSCI and FTSE Russell keep it EM (FTSE has had Korea in DM since 2009; "Taiwan is the only Tiger still EM at FTSE" — [FTSE Korea whitepaper](https://research.ftserussell.com/products/downloads/FTSE_South_Korea_Whitepaper_Jan2013.pdf), 2013-01; [bogleheads discussion of Capital Group treating both as developed](https://www.bogleheads.org/forum/viewtopic.php?t=454294), 2024) — is worth pricing because TSMC *is* 58% of the country decision. Mechanics if MSCI ever migrated Taiwan EM→DM:

- **Forced sellers:** MSCI-EM-only passive ≈ $500–700B × ~14% ≈ **$70–100B of TSMC** [agent-inferred].
- **Forced buyers:** DM-only trackers. In MSCI World, Taiwan would enter at ~2.5% and TSMC at ~1.5%; in EAFE (DM ex-North America) TSMC would land ~6–8%, instantly a top holding [agent-inferred: EM ≈ 10% of ACWI; 24.8% × 10% ÷ ~90%]. Identifiable DM-tracker demand ≈ **$30–50B** [agent-inferred]. ACWI/All-World trackers are neutral (Taiwan stays inside).
- **Net mechanical effect: roughly −$30 to −$60B**, ~3–6 days of combined tape if unphased — but MSCI phases everything large (the China A-share precedent), so per the **MSCI-Review-Handicap discipline** the pre-announced, multi-step flow deserves a heavy decay haircut [module: INDEX_MECHANICS.md §3; SKILL.md §18].
- **Precedents cut both ways.** *Israel 2010* (EM→DM): went from ~3% of EM to ~0.4% of MSCI World; foreign fund involvement *fell*, and Tel Aviv volumes dropped ~44% from May 2010 to end-2012 per the Bank of Israel ([Bloomberg](https://www.bloomberg.com/news/articles/2013-08-22/israel-struggles-with-upgrade-from-emerging-to-developed-market), 2013-08-22) — the big-fish-to-plankton attention shock. *UAE/Qatar 2014* (Frontier→EM): net foreign inflows of ~$0.3B and ~$2.5B respectively — upgrades *into* a bigger benchmark pool attract money ([Marmore](https://www.marmoremena.com/en/insights/does-index-inclusion-in-ftse-msci-answer-kuwait-saudi-liquidity-needs/); [CNBC](https://www.cnbc.com/id/100809850), 2013-06). *Korea's perpetual candidacy*: sell-side estimated **+$30B (Goldman) to +$45B (KERI) net inflows** from a DM upgrade — yet MSCI again left Korea **off even the watch list in June 2026**, citing offshore won convertibility and short-selling mechanics ([Korea Times](https://www.koreatimes.co.kr/economy/20260624/msci-keeps-korea-off-developed-market-watchlist-but-global-analysts-stay-bullish), 2026-06-24; [Seoul Economic Daily](https://en.sedaily.com/finance/2026/06/24/korea-misses-msci-developed-market-upgrade-again-on-fx), 2026-06-24).
- **Taiwan-specific verdict:** Taiwan fails the same accessibility tests Korea fails — a managed New Taiwan dollar (TWD) with limited offshore convertibility (the May-2025 7%-in-two-sessions TWD episode and central-bank management are covered in Section II.5 §2), FINI registration frictions, and the 10% daily price limit. Taiwan is on no watch list; a migration requires a consultation cycle plus a multi-year phase-in. **Probability-weighted, this is a monitorable, not a thesis input** — but it is the single largest *rule-change* flow event that exists in this name ($70–100B gross), and unlike Korea, Taiwan's EM weight (24.8%) is now so large that its *departure* would also mechanically re-rate everything left behind in EM. Watch the June-2027 MSCI market-classification review as the standing calendar entry.

**Second-order flow worth one line** [module: FLOW_SIZING_AND_ADV.md §5]: TSMC's relentless weight *growth* forces EM trackers to trim a sliver of every other constituent at each review — TSMC is quietly the largest recurring "index-funding sell" inside EM, which is one mechanical reason EM breadth has been poor while the index headline is fine.

### 2. S&P 500 exclusion economics — the missing armature

*Method: [skill: index-reconstitution-and-forced-flows / module: SECURITY_ELIGIBILITY_EXPANSION_UNLOCK.md — name the exact filter, the pool it excludes, and the mechanical demand step]. Extends Section II.5 §1's one-line note that TSM is S&P-excluded.*

**The filter.** S&P's US index family requires a **US domicile** (10-K filer, US primary listing, plurality-of-assets/revenue tests per the S&P U.S. Indices methodology — structural fact; evid-05 §6 flags that the methodology document itself should be pulled if this becomes load-bearing). TSM is a Taiwan-incorporated foreign private issuer filing Form 20-F (CIK 0001046179) — **categorically ineligible**, despite being the 4th–5th largest company traded in the US.

**Sizing the missing bid [agent-inferred, math shown].** Assets tracking the S&P 500: conservatively **$7.5T+ directly indexed** (VOO+IVV+SPY alone >$2.6T — [Yahoo Finance](https://finance.yahoo.com/markets/stocks/articles/etf-tracking-p-500-just-171511223.html), 2026; [US News](https://money.usnews.com/investing/articles/best-s-p-500-index-funds-to-buy), 2026), with S&P DJI-attributed estimates as high as **~$13T passive of ~$20T benchmarked** as of Dec-2024 ([Morningstar](https://www.morningstar.com/funds/spacex-ipo-how-index-funds-are-adapting), 2026 — definitional variance across surveys is real; both bounds shown). A hypothetical US-domiciled TSMC at its ADR-implied cap (~$2.12T, evid-06) against an S&P 500 aggregate of ~$57T [agent-inferred] would carry a **~3.5–3.7% weight** → a mechanical passive holding of **~$260B (low bound) to ~$470B (high bound)**. Against the **$90–140B** of passive that actually holds TSMC today (§1), the domicile rule costs TSMC roughly **2–3× its current mechanical bid** — call it $150–350B of permanently forgone price-insensitive demand.

**The peer contrast makes it concrete.** NVDA (~$5.02T cap, ~8% of the index [agent-inferred]) enjoys roughly $600B–$1T of S&P-passive holding; AVGO (~$1.78T, ~3%) roughly $225–390B [same math]. Both also sit in the Nasdaq-100 and every US large-cap growth mandate. TSM's forward P/E of **20.0 vs NVDA 20.8 and AVGO 23.8** (stockanalysis, 2026-07-16/17, evid-05 §4) — for the business with the widest moat and 67.7% gross margins in the group — is partly the Taiwan tail (Section II.5 §3 owns that decomposition; do not double-count), but part of the residual is this: **TSM's marginal buyer must make a *decision*; NVDA's marginal buyer is a paycheck deduction.** The index-effect literature's finding that an addition's price level is largely *permanent* (awareness/monitoring, Chen–Noronha–Singal) [module: INDEX_MECHANICS.md §5] implies the mirror: permanent exclusion is a standing level discount, not a timing effect.

**Could eligibility ever change?** The only path is redomiciliation — the Broadcom route (Singapore→US in April 2018, followed promptly by S&P 500 inclusion **[agent-inferred from public record; verify dates before quoting]**). For TSMC this is a political impossibility, not a corporate-finance option: the largest shareholder is Taiwan's National Development Fund (6.38%, with a board seat — 20-F FY2025, evid-04 §5), the N-2 statute legally tethers the leading edge to Taiwan (evid-04 §5), and the "silicon shield" logic makes the Taiwanese domicile itself a strategic asset. S&P's 2026 listing-rule consultations concern private-market and dual-class names, not domicile ([Fortune](https://fortune.com/2026/06/02/spacex-index-funds-new-listing-rules/), 2026-06-02). **Treat the exclusion as permanent structure: a standing, quantifiable discount vs US-listed peers that no catalyst unlocks.** The eligibility-unlock module's screen — "which pool is excluded, and what would they underwrite when they arrive?" — returns: the pool is enormous, and it is never arriving [module: SECURITY_ELIGIBILITY_EXPANSION_UNLOCK.md, falsifier: the barrier is binding *and* permanent].

### 3. The ADS↔ord machine — premium history, regimes, and actionable thresholds

*Method: [skill: capital-structure-and-security-selection / module: CLAIM_SELECTION_AND_STUB_UNDERWRITING.md — same-economics class-spread discipline: economic equality, liquidity/listing, buyer base, spread catalyst]. Section II.5 §1 owns the premium's existence and creation-cap inference; this section adds the verified mechanics, the regime history, and thresholds.*

**Mechanics, verified.** Cancellation (ADS→ord) is free; **creation is quota-capped**: under standard Taiwan ADS terms, deposits of common shares to issue new ADSs are "permitted only to the extent that the total number of ADSs outstanding after an issuance does not exceed the number previously approved by the ROC Securities and Futures Commission" (plus dividends/rights adjustments) — the one-way valve Section II.5 inferred, confirmed in Taiwan-issuer offering documents ([Chunghwa Telecom F-1](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/0001132924/000114554903000847/u98438a5fv1za.htm), 2003 — the standard ROC ADS regime; TSMC's own deposit agreement should be pulled for the exact quota headroom). ADS float therefore grows mainly via approved offerings and National Development Fund sales-as-ADSs (20-F FY2025). One-way convertibility means the premium is **not an arbitrage — it is a bounded sentiment spread**: floored near zero by cancellation, uncapped above by the creation quota.

**The regime history (the part Section II.4's gauge needs):**

| Period | Premium | What was happening |
|---|---|---|
| 2004–2019 | low single digits (long-run dataset) | pre-AI; the wrapper convenience yield ([chungderson dataset](https://github.com/chungderson/tsmc-adr-premium), 2004–present) |
| Avg since 2024 | **~19%** | the AI bid concentrated in the USD wrapper ([KuCoin/BigGo](https://www.kucoin.com/news/flash/sk-hynix-adr-premium-surpasses-50-amid-structural-arbitrage-barriers), 2026) |
| Dec 2024 | **~26%** | US AI-crowding peak #1 ([TradingView/GuruFocus](https://www.tradingview.com/news/gurufocus:99508665c094b:0-tsmc-adr-premium-falls-to-two-year-low-as-taipei-shares-surge/), 2026-05) |
| Oct 2025 | **record — highest since 2002** (50-day MA basis); ADRs +54% YTD vs local +38% | "global buyers pile in" ([Bloomberg](https://www.bloomberg.com/news/articles/2025-10-15/tsmc-adr-premium-tops-two-decade-high-as-global-buyers-pile-in), 2025-10-15) |
| Dec 2025 → May 2026 | 26% → **13.7%**, five straight monthly declines, a two-year low | Taipei line +50% YTD vs ADR <40%: local investors out-bulling the US ([TradingView/GuruFocus](https://www.tradingview.com/news/gurufocus:99508665c094b:0-tsmc-adr-premium-falls-to-two-year-low-as-taipei-shares-surge/), 2026-05) |
| 2026-07-16 | **+6.9%** (closes; +9.4% on the same-information 7/15 basis) | evid-05 §1, DERIVED with the timing caveat |

**The April-2026 rule change is the punchline.** Taiwan's Financial Supervisory Commission (FSC) raised the single-stock limit for domestic equity funds and active ETFs **from 10% to 25%** in late April 2026 — unlocking an estimated **NT$200B of local institutional demand**, and TSMC (≈44% of the Taiex, the TWSE capitalization-weighted index) is the *only* stock the old cap was binding on ([MEXC news](https://www.mexc.com/news/1051254), 2026; TradingView/GuruFocus, 2026-05). This was the eligibility-expansion unlock [module: SECURITY_ELIGIBILITY_EXPANSION_UNLOCK.md] — **but operating on the ord line**, the opposite side from the usual ADR story. Local forced demand repriced the local line up to the wrapper, and the premium collapsed from record to trough *while the stock made all-time highs*. Whoever held the ADS for the 2025 run paid ~19pp of relative performance to the ord holder from the October record to now [computed: premium ~26%+ → 6.9%].

**Statistical character and thresholds.** The premium is *sticky*: unit-root tests on the 2004–present series fail to reject non-stationarity at short/medium horizons (ADF p≈0.28) while very-long-run stationarity holds (PP p≈0.01) — i.e., **10–20% divergences persist for months-to-years and revert on regime events, not on schedule** ([chungderson analysis](https://github.com/chungderson/tsmc-adr-premium), retrieved 2026-07-17). Both modern compression episodes (post-Dec-2024, post-Oct-2025) were triggered by *policy/local-flow* regime changes, not by arbitrage. Actionable reading grid, extending Section II.4's kill-signal row:

- **>20% and rising** — US-discretionary crowding regime (both 2024 and 2025 episodes); historically followed by multi-month compression; the *wrapper* is the crowded claim.
- **~5–12%** — the long-run convenience-yield band; gauge roughly neutral. **Current 6.9% sits at the bottom of it: the US-froth component is out, and the marginal crowded holder is now local Taiwan flow** — the fragility gauge points at Taipei, not New York. (Cross-check: SK hynix's new Nasdaq ADR trades at a **>50%** premium to Seoul under the same one-way mechanics — [BigGo Finance](https://finance.biggo.com/news/5e4a8c12-a166-45f8-8bd3-17168f065c07), 2026 — wrapper premia do not mean-revert on their own; they revert when the constrained side's rules change.)
- **<0% sustained** — distribution regime: US holders exiting through the only door they have while locals hold; a discount is *cancellable* (ADS→ord arb kills it), so persistence below zero signals stress in the arb chain itself — escalate to Section II.5's conversion-clock analysis.
- **Three failure modes of the gauge [agent-inferred]:** (i) an *orderly* risk-off compresses the premium (US sells first) — Section II.4's reading; (ii) a *Taiwan-specific* shock that halts or limit-downs the local line (10% daily limit; 2025-04-07 precedent, Bloomberg) **inverts** it — the ADS becomes the sole functioning price-discovery venue and the "premium" blows out to whatever the closed-end NY claim clears at (Section II.5 §2's blockade sequencing). A sudden premium *spike* on a bad-news day is therefore not bullish US demand; it is the local market failing to clear. Read the premium jointly with TWSE session state, never alone. (iii) The TWD channel also moves it mechanically: TWD appreciation raises the USD value of the ord and compresses the measured premium with no flow at all (May-2025: +7% in two sessions, CNBC 2025-05-06).
