### 4. Holder-flow forensics — the whale map, the Berkshire template, and the marginal-buyer ladder

*Method: [skill: holder-flow-and-crowding / modules: HOLDER_FLOW_METHOD.md (Stages 4–6: segment, measure, confound-check), SMART_HOLDER_VALIDATION_MUST_BE_REPRICED.md, HOLDER_BASE_PROOF_REQUIREMENT_MAP.md, CROWDING_POSITIONING_HEDGE.md]. Section II.4 §6 owns the crowding verdict; this section adds the position-level map and the exhaustion read. All 13F data carries the 45-day lag and aggregator caveats; EDGAR is the primary source before weighting any single row.*

**The register, one line (Section II.4 recap):** 20.5% of shares in the ADS; ~73–75% total foreign institutional ownership of 2330 [softer sourcing]; National Development Fund 6.38% (largest holder, board seat); directors/officers 0.23%; short interest 0.57% (evid-05 §6); ~3,800 13F institutions on the ADR (evid-04 §7).

**Q4'25–Q1'26 whale map, confound-checked:**

| Holder (public 13F filer) | Move | Confound check [module: HOLDER_FLOW_METHOD.md Stage 6] |
|---|---|---|
| Fisher Asset Management | +1.8% to ~28.35M ADS (~$5.6B) — largest tracked discretionary holder | genuine long-only conviction line ([MarketBeat](https://www.marketbeat.com/instant-alerts/filing-taiwan-semiconductor-manufacturing-company-ltd-tsm-stake-lifted-by-fisher-asset-management-llc-2026-05-25/), 2026-05-25) |
| Sanders Capital | **−12.2%** (−3.90M sh, ~$1.27B) Q1'26 | large fundamental holder trimming into strength — the highest-quality *bearish-at-the-margin* row (Quiver, evid-04 §7) |
| Capital-complex: Capital International Investors **−55.9%** (−14.35M) vs Capital Research Global **+210.7%** (+6.77M) and Capital World **+20.0%** (+5.48M) | net across the complex ≈ −2.1M sh [computed] | **intra-complex reallocation, not a conviction exit** — the apparent whale-exit headline dissolves when the three sleeves are summed ([MarketBeat](https://www.marketbeat.com/stocks/NYSE/TSM/institutional-ownership/), retrieved 2026-07-17) |
| UBS +51%; Susquehanna +1,341%; Jane Street (top-10 row) | large % adds | bank/market-maker inventory and options-hedging books, **not** directional conviction [agent-inferred; Section II.4 flagged the same] |
| Hedge-fund breadth | 158 → 186 funds long (Q3'25→Q4'25) | breadth up, but segment-weighted flow is what matters; count alone is not signal [module: HOLDER_FLOW_METHOD.md] |

Net forensic read: after confound checks, the Q1'26 tape shows **one real fundamental add (Fisher), one real fundamental trim (Sanders), and a lot of plumbing** — consistent with Section II.4's "mixed and second-order versus the index plumbing."

**The Berkshire 2022–23 round trip — the canonical flow event for this name.** The numbers: ~60.06M ADS bought in Q3-2022 for **$4.1B** (implied basis ≈ $68.3/ADS [computed]); disclosed 2022-11-14; **ADS +10.5% on 2022-11-15 — the best session since 2008** ([Asia Financial](https://www.asiafinancial.com/warren-buffetts-berkshire-reveals-4bn-stake-in-tsmc), 2022-11-15; [CNN](https://amp.cnn.com/cnn/2022/11/15/investing/tsmc-shares-berkshire-hathaway-stake-hnk-intl/index.html), 2022-11-15). Then: **−86.2%** revealed 2023-02-14 — ADS ~−4% after-hours, Taipei −3.3% next session ([CNN](https://www.cnn.com/2023/02/15/tech/warren-buffett-tsmc-chips-earnings-hnk-intl/index.html), 2023-02-15); full exit revealed 2023-05-16 ([CNN](https://www.cnn.com/2023/05/16/investing/berkshire-hathaway-taiwan-tsmc-stock-exit-hnk-intl), 2023-05-16). Stated reason: "I don't like its location, and I've re-evaluated that… I feel better about the capital that we've got deployed in Japan than in Taiwan" ([CNN](https://www.cnn.com/2023/04/12/tech/taiwan-buffett-tsmc-sale-hnk-intl/index.html), 2023-04-12). Three lessons, each attributable:

1. **Smart-holder validation must be repriced, not banked** [module: SMART_HOLDER_VALIDATION_MUST_BE_REPRICED.md]: the single highest-quality holder endorsement this company ever received was reversed within two quarters *on the jurisdiction, not the business* — the validation was never about the variable the bull case rests on.
2. **The market prices the validation itself as a ±10% state variable** — a ~$40B market-cap swing on the disclosure (+10.5% on ~$375B cap) and another ~$15–20B on the exit filing [computed], with zero fundamental content. Holder identity is part of this stock's price, which is exactly the second-order-pricing regime of [module: INVESTOR_BASE_AS_SECOND_ORDER_PRICING_VARIABLE.md].
3. **The proof-requirement map says why he left** [module: HOLDER_BASE_PROOF_REQUIREMENT_MAP.md]: a permanent-capital quality buyer requires a *jurisdictional* proof format that Taiwan cannot ever file. The buyer class that most "should" own the world's best manufacturing franchise is structurally unable to hold it — which permanently mortgages the multiple to buyers with shorter proof horizons (EM allocators, AI-theme money, locals).

**Marginal-buyer exhaustion: ATH vs the −14% pullback.** ADS peaked at $477.57 (2026-06-30) and sits −14.2% below at $409.74 [computed], after closing **−2.32% on a record beat-and-raise** (2026-07-16, evid-05) — the cleanest possible print that *positioning, not information, set the bar at the top*. Walk the buyer ladder [module: CROWDING_POSITIONING_HEDGE.md — audit the holder base before underwriting the dip]:

| Rung | State at −14% |
|---|---|
| EM passive | intact but **flow-dependent** — buys only what allocation flows deliver ($38B record H1 already printed; the comp gets harder) |
| Local Taiwan funds | the FSC unlock (NT$200B capacity) is the *freshest* bid, partially spent through the premium compression; retail AI enthusiasm extreme (TradingView, 2026-05) |
| Benchmarked-active EM | **capped** — UCITS 10% ceilings mean the natural dip-buyer class is structurally unable to add (§1) |
| US discretionary / AI rotation | **already left** — the premium's 26%→7% collapse *is* the exit tape of this cohort (§3) |
| Corporate | **absent** — no buyback program; dividends only, ~31% payout (evid-06); the company never supports its own tape |
| Short-cover | **absent** — 0.57% short interest is no fuel |

Honest synthesis: above the index bid, the ladder is thin, and the −14% pullback is being caught by *mechanical and local* demand, not by deep discretionary value buyers — the buyer class that would make the bid price-elastic (global quality/GARP mandates) is the one the S&P exclusion (§2) and the jurisdiction proof-requirement (Berkshire lesson) keep out. This is not a sell signal — it is a **fragility statement about the path**: shocks meet a demand curve with a mechanical core and thin discretionary edges, which is how a structurally under-shorted, index-anchored mega-cap can still print −10% air pockets (2025-04-07 limit-down precedent). Positioning risk here is a *sizing* input, not a thesis input [module: CROWDING_POSITIONING_HEDGE.md], and it hands off to the sizing section.

### 5. Instrument selection — which claim owns which thesis variant

*Method: [skill: capital-structure-and-security-selection / modules: DOCTRINE.md (claim-selection, one scenario set), CLAIM_SELECTION_AND_STUB_UNDERWRITING.md (ADR/local-line class-spread discipline; best expression before best story), OPTION_AND_CONTINGENT_PAYOFFS.md (options as bespoke claims), DYNAMIC_INSTRUMENT_RESELECTION_AFTER_THESIS_DRIFT.md]. TSMC has a single common class, no preferreds, no converts, net cash ≈ US$83B (evid-06) — the waterfall is trivial, so the entire selection question lives in **wrapper, venue, and derivative** space, plus the "adjacent claim" (ecosystem) alternative. Analytical ranking only; no trade instructions.*

**Claim inventory** [module: CLAIM_SELECTION_AND_STUB_UNDERWRITING.md Stage 1]:

| Claim | Economic exposure | Key frictions / terms | Liquidity |
|---|---|---|---|
| **2330 local ord** | 100% of the business, at NAV | FINI registration + local custodian; TWD settlement (managed currency); 10% daily price limit; thin local single-stock derivatives | ~$3–6B/day [agent-inferred] |
| **TSM ADS** | identical claim **+6.9% toll** (7/16 closes) | one-way conversion (cancel free, create capped); USD settlement; deepest options market of any non-US name; S&P-ineligible | ~$6.3B/day (evid-05) |
| **Listed TSM options** | convex claims on the ADS | 30-day implied volatility (IV) **56.7% = 96th percentile**, IV rank 79, vs realized 62.7%; put/call open interest 1.31; dealers short gamma below the $443 flip, put wall $420 / call wall $400 (evid-05 §6, pre-print) | deep |
| **Ecosystem claims** (ASML; US semicap) | TSMC's capex ($60–64B FY26 guide) is their revenue | different jurisdiction (Netherlands/US), different cycle position; ASML forward P/E **33.4×** vs TSM 20.0× (evid-05 §4) | deep |
| **No expression** | — | per the reselection module, always a candidate seat | — |

**One shared scenario set** (from Section II.6's expectations work; do not let each claim pick its own world): **Base** — FY26 >40% US$ revenue growth holds, 2nm ramps, stock re-rates on clean EPS. **Digestion** — AI capex pause late-2026/2027; HPC (61% of revenue) decelerates; forward P/E compresses toward ~24× on trimmed EPS (the reported ~$330–425 bear frames, evid-05 §7). **Tail** — blockade/invasion; Section II.5 §2's multipliers (≈0.22 trading floor in blockade; 0.02–0.06 in invasion).

**Payoff-by-claim, on that set:**

- **2330 ord.** Base: the strictly dominant economic claim — same cash flows 6.9% cheaper, *plus* it is the line the mechanical index bid and the new local-fund bid actually buy (§1, §3), *plus* it cannot suffer premium compression (which cost ADS holders ~19pp relative since Oct-2025). Digestion: falls with earnings; no wrapper cushion either way. Tail: **trapped** — 10% daily limits, plausible halt, TWD repatriation risk (Section II.5 §2); the 6.9% saving was the price of the exit door. Access is the gate: FINI plumbing, custody, and TWD exposure are real setup costs for a USD-based holder [agent-inferred; standard Taiwan access frictions].
- **TSM ADS.** Base: same upside minus the toll; the premium at 6.9% (bottom of the convenience band) means the *entry toll is currently cheap by this instrument's own history* — the 2024–25 froth tax is out of the price. Digestion: modestly worse than the ord if US sentiment leads the downgrade (premium compresses further), roughly equal otherwise; two-sided now that the marginal crowding is local. Tail: **strictly superior venue** — the ADS keeps trading in New York while the local line is halted (Section II.5 §2's central inversion); the closed-end claim discovers the tail price, ugly but *tradeable*. The 6.9% is best understood not as overpayment but as the **premium on venue-continuity insurance** — currently the cheapest it has been since early 2024 (§3).
- **Options on TSM.** The 96th-percentile IV is the market saying the quiet part: **convexity on this name is peak-priced in both directions.** What that pricing *means* analytically [module: OPTION_AND_CONTINGENT_PAYOFFS.md — options are bespoke claims, price the terms]: (i) optionality-based upside expressions pay ~57-vol for a stock whose base case is a grinding compounder — a payoff shape mismatch; (ii) downside convexity (the natural instinct on a Taiwan-tail name) is exactly what everyone else already reached for — put/call OI 1.31, IV rank 79 — so the tail is the *most* expensively insured part of the distribution; (iii) harvesting that richness by selling volatility is a claim that is short precisely the catastrophic, gap-across-strikes tail this name uniquely carries (limit-down/halt dynamics mean hedging assumptions fail exactly when needed). Conclusion: at this vol surface, **options are the worst-priced claim on the menu for every thesis variant except a dated, view-specific event** — and the crowding of tail-awareness is itself information: the market is not underpricing the risk everyone writes memos about. (Dealer positioning detail — short gamma below $443 — implies mechanically amplified moves near-term, a path note for whoever owns the common.)
- **Ecosystem claims (ASML / semicap) — the "same thesis, different jurisdiction" test, failed honestly.** The appeal: own the AI-silicon buildout through a Dutch or US claim that no PLA blockade can expropriate. The economics: ASML and the semicap group are claims on **TSMC's capex line, not its revenue line**. In *digestion*, TSMC defends utilization and cuts capex first (its own 2023 template: capex fell while margins held, evid-06 multi-year table) — the ecosystem claim is **higher-beta to the exact scenario you're diversifying**, and at 33.4× forward (ASML) vs 20.0× you pay a ~65% multiple premium for it. In the *tail*, ASML loses its largest EUV customer and semicap loses its largest WFE buyer — jurisdictionally clean, economically coupled; the claim survives (no expropriation) but the earnings crater. The ecosystem is a *different cycle exposure at a higher price*, not a laundered TSMC. It belongs in the report as a relative-value adjacency, not as a TSMC substitute. [module: CLAIM_SELECTION_AND_STUB_UNDERWRITING.md — "choosing the security closest to the exciting asset when an adjacent claim captures the same edge" fails here in reverse: the adjacent claim does *not* capture the same edge.]

**The waterfall verdict** [module: DOCTRINE.md — pick the best claim and prove the rejected ones worse]:

| Thesis variant | Best claim | Why the others lose |
|---|---|---|
| **Base-case compounder** (the report's spine) | **2330 ord** for a holder with Taiwan plumbing; **ADS** otherwise | Ord is the same claim 6.9% cheaper with the mechanical bids behind it; options overpay for convexity the thesis doesn't need; ecosystem is a different (capex) cycle at +65% multiple |
| **Base case, tail-aware** (own it, respect the jurisdiction) | **TSM ADS** | The 6.9% toll — at the bottom of its band — is the market price of venue continuity in the one scenario where venue is everything (Section II.5 §2); the ord's discount is the price of a door that shuts exactly when needed |
| **Digestion-led bear/underweight view** | **No long expression / common-line relative work** | Puts at 96th-%ile IV overpay for a slow-motion scenario; shorting the common fights a 0.57%-short, index-anchored tape; semicap is the higher-beta digestion claim (that's where the *short-side* of a digestion view lives, handed to the relative-value lane) |
| **Pure tail hedge** | **None cleanly priced** | Tail convexity is the most crowded claim on the surface (96th-%ile IV, P/C 1.31); Section II.5's honest answer stands — the tail is managed by *sizing the common*, not by buying the insurance everyone else already bid up |

**Wrong-security red-team** [module: DOCTRINE.md; DYNAMIC_INSTRUMENT_RESELECTION_AFTER_THESIS_DRIFT.md]: the wrong-security risk in TSMC is not seniority — there is no waterfall to misread — it is **venue and wrapper**. The two live failure modes: (1) owning the *ord* for the 6.9% saving and discovering in stress that the discount was the exit door's price; (2) owning the *ADS* through another US-crowding cycle and donating 15–20pp of relative return when the premium regime turns, as it just did. The reselection trigger is explicit: **any move in the FSC conversion quota (either direction), an FSC cap change, an MSCI capping/reclassification consultation, or a sustained premium outside the 5–12% convenience band (0–5% = watch zone) re-opens the instrument question** — the claim ranking above is regime-dependent and was materially different nine months ago.

---

#### (a) Key judgments

| # | Judgment | Confidence | Falsifier |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Passive/mandate-anchored ownership is ~$90–140B pure passive (~4.5–7% of cap) and ~$245B benchmark-anchored; the routine flow is a slow tide (<1× ADV events), and the real flow risks are rule changes (capping, reclassification, FSC quota), not redemptions | Medium (ranges are agent-inferred) | Fund-holdings audit shows passive >10% of cap; an EM outflow quarter moves TSM >2330 peers materially |
| 2 | The cap-rule complex (UCITS 5/10/40, RIC 25/50, capped single-country indexes) now makes marginal *forced sellers* of capped funds and *unable buyers* of at-ceiling active funds — a structural demand-curve kink at the top | High | Evidence of uncapped mandates replacing capped ones at scale; MSCI EM capped-variant AUM shrinking |
| 3 | Taiwan EM→DM migration is a low-probability, high-gross-flow ($70–100B sell / $30–50B identifiable buy) monitorable; Korea's June-2026 watch-list rejection marks the accessibility bar Taiwan also fails | High on mechanics, Low on event probability | MSCI adds Taiwan (or Korea) to the DM watch list at the June-2027 review; TWD offshore convertibility reform |
| 4 | S&P 500 exclusion permanently forgoes a $260–470B mechanical bid (2–3× the current passive base) and is a standing level-discount vs NVDA/AVGO with no unlock path | High on structure, Medium on sizing | S&P domicile-rule change; credible TSMC redomiciliation discussion (treat as near-zero) |
| 5 | The ADS premium is a bounded, sticky sentiment spread: >20% = US-crowding froth; 5–12% = convenience band (0–5% = watch zone); <0% sustained = arb-chain stress; sudden spike on a bad-tape day = local-market failure, not demand. At 6.9% the US-froth component is out and the crowded marginal holder is local | High | Premium re-widens past 20% without US inflow evidence; a discount persists >1 month with the conversion channel open |
| 6 | The marginal-buyer ladder above the index bid is thin (capped active, departed US discretionary, no corporate bid, no short cover): pullbacks are caught by mechanical/local money — a path-fragility input for sizing, not a thesis change | Medium-High | A broad global-quality/GARP 13F cohort initiates at scale in Q2'26 filings (due ~2026-08-14) |
| 7 | Claim ranking: ord for the pure compounder (access permitting); ADS for the tail-aware holder (venue-continuity toll at a cyclical low); options mispriced-rich for every variant (96th-%ile IV); ecosystem claims are a different cycle, not a jurisdiction-laundered TSMC | High (regime-dependent, see triggers) | FSC quota/cap changes; IV rank <40 (re-opens the optionality question); ASML/semicap multiple convergence to TSM |

#### (b) Monitoring lines (dated where possible)

1. **Quarterly (Feb/May/Aug/Nov MSCI reviews):** TSMC's MSCI EM weight and any capping consultation; TSMC crossing 60% of MSCI Taiwan; EEM weight >17% (Section II.4's line, reaffirmed).
2. **June 2027:** MSCI market-classification review — Taiwan/Korea watch-list status.
3. **Continuous:** ADS premium vs the 5–12% convenience band (0–5% watch zone), read jointly with TWSE session state and USD/TWD; alert at >12% (re-crowding), >20% (froth), <0% (distribution/arb stress).
4. **~2026-08-14 (Q2'26 13F window):** Fisher/Sanders/Capital-complex net; verify aggregator rows against EDGAR before weighting; watch for any first-time global-quality buyer cohort (would fatten the discretionary rungs of the ladder).
5. **FSC actions:** any further local fund-cap or ADR-quota change (each one has repriced the premium regime within weeks).
6. **NDF:** sales-as-ADSs (the one legal ADS-creation channel of size) — both a premium-supply event and Section II.4's state-monetization signal (>0.5%/6mo).
7. **Options surface:** IV rank normalization below ~40 with the P/C ratio unwinding — the tail-insurance crowd de-crowding — re-opens the convexity leg of the instrument ranking.

#### (c) Handoffs

- **→ Valuation (Section II.6):** carry the S&P-exclusion level discount as structural (do not model it closing); use the ord line (local cap ~US$1.99T) as the economic base and treat the ADS premium as a separate spread line; the passive-bid math above is the "who re-rates it" answer to Section II.6's multiple question.
- **→ Sizing/implementation:** judgment #6 (thin discretionary ladder) and the dealer short-gamma note are sizing inputs; the positioning audit here substitutes for a fresh one [module: CROWDING_POSITIONING_HEDGE.md].
- **→ Section II.5 (wrapper/tail):** the premium-regime grid (§3) plugs into Section II.5's conversion-clock scenarios; the "premium spike = local failure" failure-mode extends Section II.5 §2(i).
- **→ Relative-value lane:** the ASML/semicap adjacency (rejected as a TSMC substitute) is a live *pair* question — TSMC-vs-semicap on the digestion scenario — outside this module's scope.

*Compliance: all data from public filings, index-provider publications, and cited press/aggregators; 13F references are public disclosures by institutional filers. No material non-public information; no trade, sizing, borrow, or execution instruction; refuting evidence (decayed index-effect edge, crowded tail insurance, two-sided premium regime) surfaced with equal prominence.*


## Section III.9 — Investor Lenses and Analogy-by-Mechanism

**TSMC (Taiwan Semiconductor Manufacturing Company; NYSE: TSM, 1 ADS = 5 common shares; TWSE: 2330) — as of 2026-07-17.**
Anchors: TSM $409.74 (2026-07-16 close, print day); all-time closing high $477.57 on 2026-06-30 → −14.2% in ~two weeks (DERIVED); ~20× forward price-to-earnings (P/E, next-twelve-months), 33.25× trailing (stockanalysis.com, 2026-07-16); Q2'26 gross margin (GM) 67.7%, a company record (SEC 6-K acc. 0001046179-26-000451, 2026-07-16).

**Method note.** This section runs the lens library of `investor-lens-and-analogy` as *operators*: Stage 0 classification → Stage 1 lens runs, each with its failure mode checked → Stage 2 mechanism analogs with falsifiers → Stage 3 a `specialist-ledger` ADVANCE/PARK verdict (idea routing, never sizing). Three requested lenses (Marks, Soros, Fisher) are not among the library's ten cards; per the flywheel rule they run here as *candidate operators* distilled from their named public frameworks [skill: investor-lens-and-analogy / module: references/INVESTOR_LENS_LIBRARY.md §"Adding a new lens (flywheel)"]. Falsifier discipline (atomic, observable, time-bounded) follows `thesis-legs-and-redteam`. 2025–26 facts come from this report's evidence files E01–E06 (each carries its primary URL); pre-2025 historical numbers were web-verified this session (URL + date inline) or are marked **[agent-inferred]**.

**Stage 0 — classification and lens selection.** TSMC classifies as a *capital-intensive, sole-source, leading-edge logic foundry at a cyclical revenue/margin high*: 72.3% foundry revenue share in Q1'26 (TrendForce, trendforce.com/presscenter/news/20260612-13095.html, 2026-06-12); GM 67.7% vs UMC 29.2% and GlobalFoundries 27.6%; top customer 19% of revenue, top-10 ≈78% (FY2025 20-F, filed 2026-04-16). Selected: LENS-5 (capital cycle), LENS-1 (compounder), and the two mandatory gates LENS-10 (expectations) and LENS-9 (forensic). LENS-4/8 fold into LENS-1 — same moat, counted once [module: references/TRIANGULATED_KERNEL.md §4a]. LENS-2/3/6/7 misfit: the founder retired in 2018; TSMC banks scale gains as margin (3–10% price hikes into 2026, E03 §7) — the opposite of scale-economies-shared.

---

### 1. The capital-cycle lens (Marathon-style) — LENS-5

[skill: investor-lens-and-analogy / module: references/INVESTOR_LENS_LIBRARY.md §LENS-5; provenance: Marathon's published *Capital Returns*, ed. Chancellor]

The lens ignores demand forecasts and reads the supply side: capital flooding into high-return industries destroys the returns.

- **Industry capex is at all-time records.** Worldwide 300mm fab equipment spending: a record $133B forecast for 2026 (+18%), $151B in 2027 — first year ever above $150B (SEMI, semi.org/en/semi-press-release/semi-projects-double-digit-growth-in-global-300mm-fab-equipment-spending-for-2026-and-2027, retrieved 2026-07-17). Big-4 hyperscaler 2026 capex ≈ $700–725B, up ~77% from ~$410B in 2025 (E03 §6). TSMC raised its own 2026 budget to $60–64B (from $52–56B) on 2026-07-16, vs $40.9B in 2025 and $29.8B in 2024 (E01).
- **The lens's tell just flipped.** Through 2024–25 revenue growth ran roughly in line with capex growth; 2026 breaks the configuration decisively. 2026 inverts it: capex +47–56% year-over-year against "slightly above 40%" revenue guidance; capex/revenue is back from 33% (2024) to 35.2% in 1H26, ~39% Q2 run-rate (E06). TSMC's own history dates the lag: capex/revenue peaked at 53% in 2021 and the trough hit in 2023 (US$ revenue −8.7%, GM −5.2pp, shipments −21%) — about two years from capex peak to return trough (E01 §7).
- **Supply is responding everywhere — and failing at the leading edge.** Samsung's foundry turned marginally profitable (first monthly profit in ~3 years, June 2026; TrendForce's 1Q26 release already flagged a return to profitability); Intel is reportedly repatriating 80–90% of Nova Lake compute tiles from TSMC N2; SMIC targets 5nm in 2026 at ~20% yields (all E03). Yet TSMC's share *rose* from ~60% (4Q23) to 72.3% — the rare cycle where record capex has so far concentrated returns instead of competing them away.

**Lens read (falsifiable):** forward returns from record capex + record margins + a capex-over-revenue growth crossover are historically poor — unless the incremental capital keeps failing to become competing supply. The one metric this lens watches: **leading-edge supply growth versus AI-silicon demand growth**, operationalized as (a) TSMC capex growth minus revenue growth (now positive, first time since 2021–22) and (b) the CoWoS (Chip-on-Wafer-on-Substrate advanced packaging) supply-demand gap, already dated by TrendForce: ~20% undersupply mid-2026 → ~10% by end-2026 (trendforce.com/news/2026/06/15/, 2026-06-15). Surplus in 2027 = the downcycle on schedule. **Failure-mode check (secular-decline trap / premature timing):** demand is not structurally impaired, and the lens's doctrine forbids manufacturing a dated top — this is a regime warning, not a short signal.

### 2. The quality-compounder lens (Buffett/Munger) — LENS-1, gated by LENS-9/LENS-10

[skill: investor-lens-and-analogy / module: references/INVESTOR_LENS_LIBRARY.md §LENS-1; provenance: the Buffett–Munger public compounding doctrine]

**Return on incremental capital (the ROIIC check):** 2021–2025 TSMC deployed US$167B capex + US$30B research and development (4Q25 call, 2026-01-15) and grew net income NT$596.5B → NT$1,717.9B — crude incremental after-tax return ≈ 18%/yr, and *rising*: return on equity (ROE) 30.3% (2024) → 35.4% (2025) → 45.9% annualized 2Q26 (E01, E06). No ROIIC fade — the fade-blindness check passes; fade risk is exogenous (cycle, geopolitics, mandated overseas dilution), not competitive.

**The Berkshire round-trip — how a quality-first investor actually priced the Taiwan tail.** Verified: Berkshire Hathaway disclosed ~60.1M TSM ADS worth ~$4.1B in its Q3'22 13F — implied ~$68.5/ADS at the 9/30/22 mark ($4.1B ÷ 60.06M; a 13F carries quarter-end marks, so cost basis is near but not exactly this — DERIVED) (Forbes, forbes.com/sites/shanshankao/2022/11/15/, 2022-11-15). It sold ~86% by the Q4'22 filing and fully exited by Q1'23 (Tom's Hardware, tomshardware.com/news/berkshire-dumps-86-percent-of-tsmc-stock, 2023-02). Buffett, April 2023: geopolitics was "a consideration"; "I feel better about the capital that we've got deployed in Japan than in Taiwan" — while praising TSMC's management (Reuters via Yahoo, finance.yahoo.com/news/buffett-says-geopolitics-factor-berkshire-141522492.html, 2023-04-11). At ~$68.5 the stock traded ~10.5× the earnings it was about to print **[agent-inferred** from FY2022 EPS ≈ US$6.55/ADS, E01 §7**]** — Berkshire exited a business it called wonderful at a *cheap* price. What the round-trip proves is precise: **for an investor whose first rule is permanence of capital, the Taiwan tail is a veto, not a discount-rate item — no multiple is low enough**, because the tail destroys the asset, not the earnings path. The counter-ledger: the veto has so far cost ~6× ($68.5 → $409.74, ex-dividends, DERIVED). Both are true; that is what an uninsurable tail is — a risk whose price is unknowable, not necessarily one that pays to avoid.

**"Wonderful business, fair price" at $409.74:** the business half passes easily. The price half is the lens's third failure mode — *already-priced quality* — so the two gates:

- **LENS-9 (forensic):** largely clean. Margins are cash-backed (FY2025 operating cash flow NT$2,275B vs net income NT$1,718B); free cash flow NT$287B in 2Q26 despite record capex. Two flags: 2Q26 net income +77.4% is flattered by NT$63.2B of one-off Vanguard International Semiconductor disposal/mark-to-market gains in non-operating income (operating income +65.4% is the clean line); receivable days 29 vs 23 and inventory days 87 vs 76 YoY — modest, ramp-consistent (E06). Verdict: no earnings mirage — the long isn't killed on quality-of-earnings, and a short isn't handed a catalyst.
- **LENS-10 (expectations):** see §6. Preview: the quality read *is* the consensus.

### 3. The cycle lens (Marks) — candidate operator

[skill: investor-lens-and-analogy / module: references/INVESTOR_LENS_LIBRARY.md §"Adding a new lens (flywheel)"; provenance: Howard Marks's published memos and *The Most Important Thing* (pendulum framework), method only]

Pendulum readings off the 2026-07-16 tape:

- **Price/valuation:** −14.2% from the 6/30 all-time high into the print; 33.25× trailing vs a 10-year *year-end* band of 11.2–28.8× (companiesmarketcap.com/tsmc/pe-ratio/, retrieved 2026-07-16) — above the band on trailing, ~20× forward only because consensus embeds +49% EPS growth (E05).
- **Bullish pole:** 18 of 19 analysts Buy/Strong Buy, average target $516.76 (+26%), street high $700; record H1 emerging-market (EM) exchange-traded-fund inflows of $38B with TSM the largest EM index weight (~15% of the leading EM ETF) (E05 §3, §6).
- **Nervous pole:** the stock *fell 2.3% on a record beat-and-raise* (revenue above guidance, GM above the guided range, year raised to >40%, capex raised, +$100B US pledge) — "higher expectations still drive the trade" (Alphastreet, 2026-07-16); put/call open interest 1.31, 30-day implied volatility ~57% at the 96th percentile; short interest a negligible 0.57% — nobody is positioned bearish, they are hedged bullish (E05 §6).

**Lens read (falsifiable):** late-optimism, not euphoria — roughly the 7th inning **[judgment, agent-inferred]**. Euphoria tops carry triple-digit multiples and prices that rise on bad news; here the multiple is 20× forward and the price *falls on record good news* — the signature of a marginal buyer already fully invested. Dated observation: **watch the 3Q26 print (mid-October 2026). A second consecutive sell-the-news reaction to an above-guidance quarter confirms distribution; a rally on an in-line print falsifies the exhaustion read.** Failure-mode check (this lens's classic error is calling the top early): −14% after a +44% year-to-date run is also consistent with routine digestion — hence a marker, not a trade.

### 4. The reflexivity lens (Soros) — candidate operator: the AI-capex loop

[skill: investor-lens-and-analogy / module: references/INVESTOR_LENS_LIBRARY.md §"Adding a new lens (flywheel)"; provenance: Soros's published *The Alchemy of Finance*, method only; the supply half composes LENS-5]

The loop with 2026 numbers: NVIDIA data-center revenue ($75.0B in the April-2026 quarter, +92% YoY; "$1T cumulative Blackwell+Rubin visibility" through 2027 — sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1045810/000104581026000051/q1fy27pr.htm, May 2026) → validates hyperscaler capex ($700B+ guides) → becomes TSMC revenue (HPC 66% of wafer revenue) → funds TSMC capex ($60–64B) and CoWoS expansion (~35K wafers/month 2024 → 120–140K end-2026) → enables the next accelerator cycle → re-validates the next capex round. The loop is reflexive in the strict sense because the financing leg depends on rising asset prices — and **vendor financing has now entered it**: NVIDIA committed $40B+ of equity into its own customer ecosystem in four months of 2026, led by a $30B OpenAI stake (cut from an announced $100B), plus CoreWeave structures where GPUs collateralize debt that buys more GPUs (TechTimes, techtimes.com/articles/317839/20260605/, 2026-06-05; io-fund.com, retrieved 2026-07-17). Circular vendor-financing sits at the loop's weakest joint: it converts demand signal into a function of the vendor's own balance sheet — the mechanism by which a self-reinforcing process manufactures its own confirming evidence.

**TSMC's position:** the highest-collateral node — paid cash by the most solvent buyers on earth, financing no one, its CEO explicitly auditing the loop ("customers and customers' customers… to make sure the chips are not going into inventory," 2Q26 call, E01). But TSMC's capex *is* the loop's supply response, and 67.7% GM is its pricing expression.

**Crossover (falsifiable):** self-reinforcing turns self-defeating when underlying cash flows must service the financing. Markers: (a) the widening AI capex-to-revenue gap at the application layer (Forbes, 2026-06-02, E03 §6); (b) the first financing-leg crack is already visible — the $100B→$30B OpenAI cut. **Atomic marker: any big-4 hyperscaler guiding 2027 capex flat-or-down versus 2026 = the loop has crossed; TSMC's order book and CoWoS allocations would show it within two quarters.** Failure-mode check (reflexivity's error is seeing a bubble in every boom): the fundamentals leg is real and enormous; a loop is falsified by crossover markers, not by being a loop.

### 5. The scuttlebutt lens (Fisher) — candidate operator: the three highest-value checks

[skill: investor-lens-and-analogy / module: references/INVESTOR_LENS_LIBRARY.md §"Adding a new lens (flywheel)"; provenance: Philip Fisher's published *Common Stocks and Uncommon Profits* ("scuttlebutt"), method only; source-plan mechanics defer to `variant-source-map-and-research-edge`]

Ranked by information value, each resolving a model field filings cannot:

1. **Supply-chain check — OSAT (outsourced assembly-and-test) allocation behavior.** TSMC outsources ~240–270K CoWoS wafers/yr to Amkor/SPIL as overflow (E03 §6). Ask the channel: are 2027 OSAT CoWoS bookings being extended or trimmed? Overflow is the shock absorber cut *first*, quarters before TSMC's own utilization moves. Resolves: the real-time slope of AI packaging demand (`cowos_utilization_2027`).
2. **Customer-silicon-team check — hyperscaler custom-chip attrition and second-sourcing intent.** For design-services engineers (ex-Broadcom/Alchip class): which announced ASIC (application-specific integrated circuit) programs have *signed* 2027 wafer commitments, and is Microsoft's reported Maia 2 move to Intel 18A-P (Tom's Hardware, Jan 2026, E03 §2) an outlier or dual-sourcing as policy? Resolves: pricing-power durability into the 2027 hike schedule (`advanced_node_asp_trajectory`) — a monopoly customers must pay versus one they're quietly engineering away from.
3. **Tool-vendor / construction check — Arizona execution reality.** From equipment and construction vendors: actual tool move-in cadence at Fab 21 P2/P3 versus the $265B announcement (E02 §3). Resolves: whether the US buildout is near-term real capacity (then the 2–4pp overseas GM dilution guidance is real, possibly light) or a politically sequenced placeholder (then margins hold but policy risk is bought with promises) (`overseas_dilution_pp`).

Failure-mode check (scuttlebutt's error is anecdote promoted to thesis): each check is a disconfirmation probe against a named model field; none touches material non-public information — allocation and staffing cadence are standard channel work.

### 6. The margin-of-safety lens (Graham/Klarman) — run through LENS-10

[skill: investor-lens-and-analogy / module: references/INVESTOR_LENS_LIBRARY.md §LENS-10 (expectations/reverse-DCF); provenance: Graham's and Klarman's published margin-of-safety doctrine, method only; replacement-cost logic composes `external-mark-and-replacement-cost-triangulation`]

What is the margin of safety (MOS) in a ~$2.1T company (DERIVED: 25,932M shares ÷ 5 × $409.74) at 3.7–5.1× replacement cost (this report's §Section II.3 triangulation: ~$410–570B like-for-like rebuild, cross-checked against net property, plant and equipment of NT$4,303B ≈ US$134B and the $265B price tag of TSMC's own US replication — [report-internal derivation])?

- **Asset floor: none — worse than none.** Graham's MOS must be independent of the thesis. The replacement-cost cushion is negative (price is a multiple of it), and the irreplaceable assets sit overwhelmingly on the island whose tail is the dominant bear case — the asset floor is the first thing the tail vaporizes. A safety that evaporates in precisely the scenario it insures is not safety (Klarman's test).
- **Earnings floor: real, but far below.** Management's through-cycle guide: GM "56% and higher," ROE "high-twenties" (4Q25 call, 2026-01-15). Illustrative stress (DERIVED): GM to the 56% floor plus a 10% revenue haircut (2023-style digestion) → earnings power ≈ $11.5–12/ADS vs 2026 consensus $15.91; at the 11–15× multiples TSM actually bottomed at in 2019–2023 (year-end 2022: 11.2×), the cyclical floor is ~$130–180/ADS — **−56% to −68% from $409.74**. Every prior cycle offered Graham buyers that price, including Berkshire's ~10.5× in 2022.
- **What the safety actually is: duration.** At ~20× forward and ~20.5× the 2027 consensus of ~$20/ADS (DERIVED from NT$128.80 at 32.2 FX), the price embeds roughly a decade of persisting monopoly economics. Protection at $409.74 is moat quality — a LENS-1/LENS-4 argument, and the kernel forbids letting a quality argument masquerade as a safety argument [module: references/TRIANGULATED_KERNEL.md §2].

**Lens read:** in the Graham/Klarman sense, **no margin of safety at $409.74 — a margin of quality instead.** The LENS-10 reverse-solve: the price already assumes ≥40% growth 2026, ~28% 2027, GM ≥60%, no Taiwan event — which is the published consensus verbatim (E05 §3). Failure-mode check ("priced-in but still an idea"): an ADVANCE requires a defensible *duration* variant. Candidate evidence exists — N2 tape-outs ~4× N3 at the same stage (DigiTimes, 2026-07-16), the A14/A16 roadmap to 2028–29, demand "strong through 2029–2030" per management — but all of it is public, said on earnings calls, and reflected in +26% average targets. The variant fails the gate: PARK.

---

### 7. Analogy-by-mechanism: the analog set

[skill: investor-lens-and-analogy / module: references/ANALOGY_BY_MECHANISM_OPERATORS.md — AM-1 (mechanism-first framing), AM-3 (analog-set triangulation with failure cases). Each analog passes the gate: mechanism named, falsifier stated, disanalogy checked.]

**AM-1 framing (no industry nouns):** TSMC is a *sole-source toll collector on the conversion of capital-expenditure booms into physical compute, paid cash at shortage-priced record margins, whose toll plaza sits in a contested border zone.* Analogs must share that chain, not the sector.

| # | Analog | Shared mechanism | What happened next | Sharpest disanalogy | Dated marker → this analog is operative | Fit |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Intel, 2000** | Genuine monopoly (x86) priced at the peak of an infrastructure boom; the monopoly survived, the earnings didn't | Peak cap $509B Aug-2000; −50.9% in the year after the Nasdaq top; Sept-2000 revenue warning; processor revenue −23% YoY by Q3'01; ~26 years to reclaim the peak (finance.yahoo.com/news/heres-much-investing-1-000-142048982.html; Intel FY2001 10-K; techrevolt.news, retrieved 2026-07-17) | Intel's moat was being breached (AMD) and demand was consumer-PC; TSMC's share is *rising* and its buyers are cash-rich corporates. Peak multiple ~40–50× trailing **[agent-inferred]** vs TSM 20× fwd | First TSMC *guidance cut* while the stock still holds >$400 — the Intel-Sept-2000 configuration | **Moderate** — "monopoly" is zero protection against a demand cycle |
| 2 | **Cisco, 2000** | Arms-dealer whose revenue is a *derivative of customers' financed capex*; orders evaporate faster than end demand (internet traffic kept growing; the stock still lost 80%) | +236% from Jan-1999 to a $555B cap Mar-2000 at ~201× earnings; −80% in two years, $431B erased; never regained the peak (libertythroughwealth.com/2022/03/22/cautionary-tale-of-cisco-systems/, 2022-03-22) | **The multiple:** 201× vs 20× forward — an order of magnitude. Cisco's marginal buyers were junk-debt-financed carriers; TSMC's are the most solvent firms on earth. But the vendor-financing echo is live (§4) | CoWoS order book: customers pre-booking >100% of expansion (NVIDIA already holding the largest share of 2026 allocations, E03 §6) followed by allocation *give-backs* — the 2000-style air pocket forms in the order book first | **Mechanism strong / valuation weak** — indicts the demand *shape*, not the price |
| 3 | **ASML, 2018–2024** (the bull analogy) | Irreplaceable process-chokepoint monopoly gets *permanently re-rated* as scarcity is recognized; compounds through capex wobbles | P/E ~22× (Dec-2018) → ~33× (end-2024) → ~60× trailing mid-2026; annual returns 2019–24: +93/+66/+64/−31/+40/−8% — a ~5× outcome with two −30%+ episodes en route (companiesmarketcap.com/asml/pe-ratio/; slickcharts.com/symbol/ASML/returns, retrieved 2026-07-17) | ASML has no domicile tail — its scarcity is priced *up* (≈33× fwd vs TSM 20×, E05 §4). TSMC's discount to its own customers exists *because* of Taiwan; the ASML path needs the tail untriggered for a decade | TSM forward multiple holding ≥18× *through* the next capex-digestion year confirms the re-rate; a round-trip to 11–15× (as 2022) says still cycle-priced | **Moderate-strong** — the best case, capped by the domicile disanalogy |
| 4 | **Standard Oil (1911) / US railroads (1890s)** | Essential-infrastructure monopoly rents attract the *state*; returns get capped politically, not competitively | Railroads: overbuild → ~25% of mileage in receivership in 1893, ~33% by 1897, then rate regulation (en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Panic_of_1893, retrieved 2026-07-17). Standard Oil: broken into 39 parts — which *doubled* within a year; Rockefeller got richer (en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Standard_Oil, retrieved 2026-07-17) | No antitrust case exists; the modern intervention is *industrial policy taxing margin*: the $265B US buildout, 25% Section 232 tariff architecture with investment-linked quotas, Taiwan's N-2 statute, a state fund (6.38%) on the board, 2–4pp permanent overseas GM dilution — rate regulation by other means (E02 §3, E04). Bullish branch: Standard Oil says political endgames for essential monopolies can be shareholder-*positive* | Overseas-dilution guidance drifting above 3–4pp; or any US equity/golden-share demand reaching TSMC (the Aug-2025 Intel precedent was explicitly *not* applied — E04 §3) | **Strong but slow** — grinds the GM floor down over a decade; not a 2026 event |
| 5 | **Samsung memory, 2017–18** | Shortage-driven record margins at a capacity chokepoint are *the cycle's message, not the new normal* | Record ₩58.9T operating profit 2018 (record quarter Q3'18) → −29% QoQ in Q4'18 → −60% Q1'19; semis revenue −25% in 2019 (tomshardware.com/news/samsung-financial-results-q1-2019-profits-fall,39210.html; tadviser.com Samsung financials, retrieved 2026-07-17) | Memory: fungible commodity, three suppliers, zero design lock-in. Leading-edge logic: sole-source, 2–3-year design-win lock-in — and the supply response keeps *failing* (Samsung 2nm yields ~mid-50s vs TSMC's reported 60–70%; Intel 14A: zero committed external customers, Jan-2026 — E03) | Dated supply-response test: Intel said 14A commitments land 2H26–1H27; Samsung Taylor targets AI5 volume 2027. A first credible external anchor at either starts the clock on the 67.7% | **Moderate — applies to the margin, not the franchise.** Management's own 56%+ floor concedes the direction |

**AM-3 triangulation:** three failure cases (Intel, Cisco, Samsung — real businesses, peak configurations, poor forward returns) and one success (ASML). What distinguished the winner: an unstretched starting multiple, demand that proved secular rather than a financed build-out, and a supply response that physically couldn't arrive. TSMC-2026 holds the winner's supply-side card (rivals keep failing) and a mid-pack multiple (20× forward — far from Cisco, near ASML-2019), but carries the failure cases' margin configuration (record GM at record industry capex) and Cisco's demand *shape* (a derivative of financed capex). Which analog is operative is an empirical question with 2026–27 resolution dates — the markers above are the assignment mechanism.

### 8. Synthesis — where the lenses agree, and the verdict

**Heaviest lenses here:** LENS-10 (expectations), LENS-5 (capital cycle), and the Berkshire-calibrated LENS-1 — because the controversy is not quality (undisputed) but price-versus-cycle-versus-tail, exactly their jurisdiction.

**Where the lenses agree (the strongest signal):**
1. *The business is real and still strengthening.* Every lens — including the bearish ones — concedes rising share, rising ROIIC, hikes sticking, and a failed competitive supply response.
2. *67.7% GM is cyclical, by the company's own testimony.* LENS-5, LENS-9, the Samsung analog, and management's "56%+ through cycle" plus 2–4pp overseas-dilution and 2–3pp N2-dilution math all point one way: the record margin is shortage pricing above 100% utilization, not the new baseline. Models capitalizing 67.7% are capitalizing a cycle top.
3. *No margin of safety at $409.74 — the safety is quality and duration.* Graham/Klarman, LENS-10, and the Berkshire round-trip agree: prior cycle floors sat at 11–15× on much lower margins (−56% or worse from here, §6), and the asset floor is negative *and* concentrated in the tail scenario.
4. *No lens can name a live variant perception at this price.* The AI-monopoly thesis is the consensus (18/19 buys, +26% average target, >40% growth guided and believed).

**The one genuine cross-lens tension** (reported, not averaged, per the kernel): LENS-1 reads a runway possibly *longer* than even 20× implies (N2 tape-outs 4× N3, A14/A16 roadmap, demand visibility to 2029–30); LENS-5 reads a capex-over-revenue crossover at record industry spend — the configuration that preceded every prior foundry margin trough. That duration-versus-supply-cycle tension is the next piece of research, not a trade: it resolves on the dated markers, primarily hyperscaler 2027 capex guides (Jan–Feb 2027) and the CoWoS gap through end-2026.

**Does any lens argue for acting NOW?** Both directions, honestly:
- *Buy now* fails the LENS-10 gate: the only available variant (duration) is publicly articulated by management and embedded in +26% consensus targets. Buying here pays a full multiple for the consensus view, above a −56% cyclical floor, under an uninsurable tail — the exact configuration the already-priced-quality failure mode exists to block.
- *Short/underweight now* fails three ways: LENS-5's doctrine says the capital cycle is un-timeable and punishes manufactured catalysts; LENS-9 found no accounting break to date a de-rating; and the franchise evidence is still improving — shorting an executing monopoly on cycle grounds alone is the trade that was a year early against Samsung in 2017 and deeply underwater first **[agent-inferred]**.

**Net lens-library read: the DO-NOTHING/WATCHLIST house view is confirmed — a PARK with teeth, not a shrug.** Stage-3 `specialist-ledger` entry:

Mechanism analogs: ASML-2018 (permanent re-rate, bull) vs Intel-2000/Samsung-2018 (peak-configuration mean-reversion, bear); assignment resolves on dated markers, not taste. PARK is a first-class result; this one comes with six alarm clocks.

## Section III.10 — Scores, Monitoring State Machine, and Implementation Discipline

**TSMC — Taiwan Semiconductor Manufacturing Company Limited (NYSE: TSM, 1 ADS = 5 common shares; TWSE: 2330). Module date: 2026-07-17. Reference price: US$409.74/ADS (2026-07-16 close).**

*Role: closing operational module. It scores, grades, and operationalizes the analytical sections' conclusions; it re-argues none of them. Facts carry section/evidence citations; judgments are marked **[agent-inferred]**; methods are attributed [skill: slug / module: FILE]. Acronyms: ADS = American Depositary Share; GM = gross margin; EPS = earnings per share; IRR = internal rate of return; MOIC = multiple on invested capital; KPI = key performance indicator; YoY = year-over-year; D&A = depreciation & amortization; P/E = price-to-earnings; HPC = high-performance computing; CoWoS = Chip-on-Wafer-on-Substrate advanced packaging; OSAT = outsourced assembly & test; N2/A16/A14 = TSMC 2nm/1.6nm/1.4nm nodes; IV = implied volatility; EM = emerging markets; EEM = the largest MSCI EM exchange-traded fund; NDF = National Development Fund (Taiwan); CBC = Taiwan's central bank; TWSE = Taiwan Stock Exchange; FINI = Foreign Institutional Investor registration (Taiwan); §232 = Section 232 of the U.S. Trade Expansion Act; ART = U.S.–Taiwan Agreement on Reciprocal Trade; N-2 = Taiwan's two-generations-behind overseas-deployment statute; BIS = U.S. Bureau of Industry and Security; CCG/PLA = China Coast Guard / People's Liberation Army; TWD = New Taiwan dollar; FX = foreign exchange.*

**Standing rule, stated up front and repeated where it matters: the scores below are an encoding of verdicts the analytical sections already reached — they annotate the DO-NOTHING-WATCHLIST call, they did not produce it, and every conclusion in this report reads identically with the scorecard deleted** [skill: conviction-scoring-and-calibration].

### 1. Conviction scorecard

Scale conventions: **magnitude** facets (0–1000) are polarity-free property measurements; **signed** facets (−1000…+1000) are directional and, for anything security-level, expectations-relative. Three readings, none new to the prose: (i) the **gap between thesis_support (+620) and security_pressure (−120) is itself the finding** — the evidence strongly supports the declared thesis ("a genuinely improved monopoly printing cyclically unrepeatable margins at a price that pays for both" [II.7 audit trace]), and that thesis, being about the *price*, yields no directional edge; (ii) research_readiness at 730 rather than higher is an honesty mark — the pricing schedule is unofficial press, the official 2Q26 transcript was pending at compile, and 2027 revenue consensus was premium-gated [II.1 §2.1; E01 gaps; E05 gaps]; (iii) the 0.40 confidence on the one decision-relevant headline is the L3 UNKNOWN wearing a number — and it decays further (catalyst-path half-life ~45 days) unless refreshed, which is what forces the re-underwrite date in §3.

Docked one: the adverse case's central variable (hyperscaler budgets) can only be *monitored*, not analyzed — the boundary is a calendar, not a model |

**Weakest-link facet: 2 × 200 = 400 (medium).** The screen-result-vs-idea gate passes — the name carries a variant perception, dated catalysts, and falsifiers, so this is an *idea*, not a screen result — and the thematic-idea gates were actually run *against the bull case* (the price-implied conjunction is probability-multiplied to ~25–33% joint odds [II.6 §1.2]: conjunction discipline pointed at consensus). Verdict in the skill's vocabulary: **watchlist only** — precisely what the synthesis-and-decision section issued, and for the same reason: proof-chain craft is high while the evidence-*edge* leg cannot support a directional position at this price.

**What would raise the grade** (each converts assembly-edge toward access-edge): (i) demand-side primary work — hyperscaler procurement/data-center channel checks and CoWoS 2027 booking verification to resolve L3 *before* the January–February 2027 guides — the single highest-value item; (ii) pricing verification via customer cost disclosures/teardown economics to replace the unofficial hike schedule; (iii) the four flagged 20-F items (prepayments/contract liabilities, grant accounting, VIS treatment, depreciation split) [II.4 §(d)]; (iv) official-transcript reconciliation of the 2–3pp vs 3–4pp N2-dilution discrepancy [E01 gaps]; (v) statutory-text work on N-2 and the Arizona-2nm seam [E04 §5]. Items (i)–(ii) would move evidence edge to 3–4.

### 3. Monitoring state machine

[skill: thesis-drift-monitor / modules: THESIS_HEALTH_STATE_CONTRACTS.md, CATALYST_AND_DECAY_TRIGGERS.md, SEQUENTIAL_MONITORING.md, VARIANT_CONVERGENCE.md]

Monitoring here is a state-transition system, not a dashboard: every KPI row names the leg it tests, its bands, and the transition it can force; every trigger maps to a concrete action (anti-noise rule). Current state: **WATCHLIST**. Reachable states: **ACT-BUY-REVIEW**, **ACT-SELL/AVOID-REVIEW**, **SHELVE / RE-UNDERWRITE**, plus **REPAIR** if state contracts go malformed. No transition executes anything — each emits a decision-log draft into the decision framework; any implementation re-cut routes to §4, never inline.

#### Transition rules

- **WATCHLIST → ACT-BUY-REVIEW:** trigger 1 fires (KPI-3 ≥+20%) **and** no red stands on KPIs 2, 5, 6, 8, 10 (L1/L2/L4 intact). Emits a decision-refresh draft; the synthesis-and-decision section's execution note applies — if the tape has re-rated above ~$470 before firing, re-run the reverse-DCF before any packet is drafted [II.7 §6].
- **WATCHLIST → ACT-SELL/AVOID-REVIEW:** trigger 2 fires (KPI-1 red), or KPI-2 and KPI-5 go red together (the margin-normalization section's combination that creates the *dated* short on consensus FY27 [II.3 §(d)]). For fresh money: avoid-and-study-short; for a holder: trim/exit review draft.
- **Any KPI-10 red → immediate exit-class action** on any live exposure and suspension of BUY-flip eligibility regardless of KPI-3 — the gate overrides the aggregate, in monitoring exactly as in scoring.
- **Multiple-looks discipline:** KPI-1 is checked twelve-plus times against a fixed threshold; before declaring L3 BROKEN on marginal readings, run the anytime-valid e-process monitor (and the conformal band for GM-vs-guide surprises) so the false-alarm rate does not silently inflate [module: SEQUENTIAL_MONITORING.md]. A fire escalates the leg; a single dip is a reading.
- **Variant-convergence check (quarterly):** if Street FY27 estimates converge onto the 57–60% band without prints forcing it, the margin variant has become consensus — the bear edge is gone and the BUY-flip bar effectively cheapens; log it [module: VARIANT_CONVERGENCE.md].
- **Catalyst-role-migration tripwire:** if the long case is ever restated with the $265B U.S. program, the replacement-cost floor, or "sovereign-proofing" doing load-bearing work the wafer economics used to do, that is thesis creep — force a zero-base re-underwrite [module: CATALYST_AND_DECAY_TRIGGERS.md].

#### Stale-thesis escalation (the expiry date)

The file converts from unresolvable to resolvable between 2026-10-15 and 2027-02-28 [II.7 §6]. Accordingly: **if neither flip trigger has fired by 2027-03-15** (two weeks after the last big-4 guide), trigger 1 has expired by its own deadline and the thesis state is stale by construction — the name moves to **RE-UNDERWRITE**: refresh the reverse-DCF at the then-price off FY27 actuals, re-derive both flip triggers, re-register the calendar, re-score. The April-2027 window (1Q27 print + FY2026 20-F + technology symposium) is the outer bound: **no version of this thesis file may be cited after 2027-04-30 without re-underwriting.** The confidence mechanics force the same conclusion: the 0.40 headline confidence decays through the ~45-day catalyst-path half-life to sub-floor well before then, firing the stale-evidence gate on its own [module: CONFIDENCE_DEFINITION_AND_DECAY.md].

---

### 4. Implementation discipline — review documentation only

[skill: position-sizing-and-implementation-discipline] — everything below is **review-discipline documentation** of what the skill's checklist would demand *if* a trigger fired. Nothing here is a trade instruction; today's live output remains DO-NOTHING (the upstream call is DO-NOTHING-WATCHLIST, so per Stage 1 there is no live packet to prepare).

#### 4a. If FLIP → BUY fired (trigger 1)

**Conviction-tier mapping** [module: CONVICTION_AND_SIZING_LADDER.md]. The ladder's product test — conviction quality × variant edge × catalyst proximity, each 1–5, product not sum — maps this name at trigger-firing to roughly **4 × 3 × 4 ≈ 48 → BASE tier** [agent-inferred]: conviction quality 4 (primary-document triangulation with killed refuters; docked for no channel checks per §2); variant edge capped at ~3 because the trigger is *public* the moment it exists — post-firing you are buying confirmed excellence, and the residual variant is duration plus the pricing ratchet; proximity 4 (quarterly confirmation milestones). **HIGH CONVICTION is structurally hard to reach** — it needs ≥61 plus an arena proof card, and the edge factor cannot honestly exceed 3 once the flip condition is on every earnings call. Two overlays bind *before* the tier default: (i) **size off the lower bound, not the point estimate** — the Section II.6 case set (MOIC 1.49/1.05/0.67/0.46 plus a separated tail [II.6 §(a)]) has a deeply negative scenario lower bound, and a tier that clears on expected value but not on the lower bound is over-sized by definition; (ii) **the frozen-state track** [module: ABSORBING_TAIL_OUTCOMES_AND_FROZEN_STATE_SIZING.md]: the worst case is a frozen claim at 0.02–0.22×, not −30% [II.5 §(b)], so the weight is min(normal cap, frozen-state cap), the frozen-NAV budget is a *country-level* Taiwan cap including supply-chain and index look-through (TSM alone is >14% of MSCI EM / 15.18% of EEM [E05 §6]), and the absorbing-tail margin-of-safety reset (200–300% upside vs probability-weighted absorbing loss) applies. All percentage scaffolds are illustrative and yield to firm policy.

**Vehicle review** [modules: VEHICLE_SELECTION_MATRIX.md, THEME_TO_VEHICLE_SELECTION.md] — candidate plus rejected alternatives, as the skill requires:

- **ADS cash equity (candidate default):** liquidity a non-issue (~15.4M ADS/day ≈ $6.3B [E05 §1]); SEC-grade disclosure; dividend capture (~0.67% [E05 §4]). Cost: the ~7% wrapper premium [E05 §1] — tolerable *because* the claim-enforceability section finds the ADS is the stronger claim in the blockade state (New York becomes price discovery; converting to ordinaries is value-destructive, the inverse of the 2022 Russia precedent [II.5 §3]). A rare case where the premium buys something real.
- **Local 2330 line (rejected for a U.S.-based book):** avoids the premium but adds FINI registration and TWD settlement/custody, and loses the Section II.5 blockade asymmetry; same jurisdiction tail either way.
- **Options (rejected as primary expression):** IV sat at the 96th percentile pre-print (30-day IV ~56.7%, IV Rank 79 [E05 §6]) — buying calls at near-record vol for a *soft-window, confirmation-milestone* thesis is the matrix's textbook mismatch: indeterminate dates pull away from options, theta and rich vol eat the edge. If vol stays rich, defined-risk call *spreads* selling the expensive wing are the reviewable variant; long-dated puts remain the only defined-risk tail expression, with the module's own caveat that in the true absorbing state the derivative may freeze with the underlying.
- **Ecosystem basket (rejected, test named):** the default for a theme-level edge is a basket until one security clears the single-name superiority hurdle. TSMC clears it — deepest margin layer, 66% HPC exposure, >90% CoWoS share [II.6 §2] — *except* that the single name concentrates the Taiwan tail; a basket dilutes the jurisdiction risk only by diluting the moat economics being bought. Named and rejected, as the review requires.

**Catalyst-window review plan** [module: CATALYST_WINDOW_SCALING.md]: indeterminate-date form, confirmation-milestone ladder — milestone 0: trigger 1 fired clean (initial tranche 25–50% of the approved cap, review-proposed); milestone 1: 1Q27 print holds the band (GM ≥60%, monthly prints ≥+15%) → add review; milestone 2: April-2027 symposium cadence intact and the 14A window closes (2027-06-30) with zero top-10 commitments → peak review. Carry on cash is ~neutral; milestone failure defaults to down-tier one tier, not exit. One entry unique to this name: **a scheduled de-risking review at the 2028 second-source window** — the moat is dated (~2030 fade start [II.2 §8b]), so a pre-committed harvest/re-tier review must ensure the position does not outlive its own moat fade schedule.

**Correlation / book-context flags — the one-bet-held-N-times problem** [module: COMPOUNDER_THEME_CROWDING_AS_A_CORRELATED_FACTOR_EXPOSURE.md; Section II.3 §5.1]: the normalization section's single-factor-dominance flag says hyperscaler AI capex controls TSMC's revenue, margin, *and* exit multiple simultaneously. A typical book already holds that factor several times: the merchant accelerator designer, the equipment monopoly, memory names, any AI-thematic sleeve — and *passively* via EM exposure, where TSM is the #1 index weight and TSMC+two Korean chip names ≈30% of the EM index [E05 §6]. The review must tag the position with three factor labels — **hyperscaler-capex, Taiwan-jurisdiction, compounder-archetype premium** — sum each across the book, and size against the *residual* factor budget, not the single-name cap. A book long the AI complex four ways that adds TSMC at BASE weight has made one bet five times.

**Final DO-NOTHING gate** [module: THIN_EVIDENCE_DO_NOTHING_GATE.md], run deterministically even after the trigger: triangulation ≥3 source classes (pass — filings, transcripts, market data, policy documents, industry trackers); pre-registered dated falsifiers (pass — the kill-signal registry); catalyst inside carry budget (pass — quarterly cadence); promotional construction (watch — post-trigger evidence skews company-and-Street-confirming; re-consult the file's adversarial sources, not just the beat-and-raise tape); edge at realized cap (the live test — **if the price has re-rated above ~$470 before the packet is drafted, the bull-cell math is spent and the gate fails** [II.7 §6]); path-pain vs return (pass at entry near current levels). Any single failure → DO-NOTHING with the criterion recorded and the clearing condition named; a DO-NOTHING with no recorded reason is a silent pass, which the skill forbids.

#### 4b. If FLIP → SELL/AVOID fired (trigger 2) — short-side path-pain notes

[module: SHORT_PATH_PAIN_MODELING.md] For fresh money the firing means avoid; the *short* study it dates must first survive the path-pain ledger. **Borrow is trivial** — short interest 0.57% of shares outstanding, mega-cap liquidity [E05 §6] — so the classic squeeze categories are absent. The pain is monopoly-compounder carry: (i) the "E" grows against the short ~7–10% per quarter while the thesis waits [II.7 §3.2]; (ii) the sandbag regime means the short must beat *guidance floors*, not consensus — guidance moves last, monthly prints move first [II.7 §4 entry 9]; (iii) a structural, valuation-indifferent EM-index bid (record $38B H1'26 inflows [E05 §6]); (iv) the H-3 pricing ratchet cushions the very EPS crack being shorted (2023 analog: shipments −21%, revenue only −9% [II.7 §1]), shrinking the punchline from ~−40%-vs-consensus toward −20–25% [agent-inferred]; (v) multiple-expansion drag at a 20× forward entry that quality peers span 20.8–33.4× forward (NVDA at parity with TSM; AVGO/ASML at 23.8–33.4×) [E05 §4]; (vi) dividend carry, small but real. The one great absence: **takeout risk ≈ 0** — no one acquires TSMC — removing the short's usual worst single-day gap. Ledger verdict, pre-committed: a *standing* short fails the expected-pain-vs-expected-return test at any plausible sizing; the only reviewable expression is the **dated** post-trigger version — entered after KPI-1/KPI-2/KPI-5 confirm, sized for 1–2 masked quarters against consensus FY27 [II.3 §(d)], with defined-risk put spreads competing against short stock given still-elevated IV (sell the far wing: the target is the mediocrity mark, not zero), and a pre-committed harvest rule at the consensus-capitulation point per the variant-convergence check in §3. Even then, path-pain is debited before edge is credited, in writing.

### 5. If you remember nothing else

1. **The scorecard says exactly what the report says, on purpose:** research readiness high (730), thesis support strongly positive (+620), security pressure inside the no-edge dead-band (−120 at 0.40 confidence, capped by the standing AI-demand UNKNOWN) — the work is done, the business is proven, the price removed the edge; the scores annotate that verdict and had no vote in it.
2. **This name converts from unresolvable to resolvable between 2026-10-15 and 2027-02-28**, and the whole posture is two atomic triggers: big-4 hyperscaler 2027 capex guides ≥+20% aggregate → BUY review; two consecutive monthly revenue prints <+15% YoY → SELL/AVOID review — with the December-2026 pricing round as the cheapest early kill on the margin leg.
3. **The watchlist entry has an expiry date, not a lease:** if neither trigger fires by 2027-03-15 the thesis is stale by construction and must be re-underwritten from FY27 actuals at the then-price; no version of this file may be cited after 2027-04-30 without that re-underwrite.
4. **Any future long is BASE-tier, cash-ADS-shaped, and Taiwan-country-capped** — sized off the scenario lower bound and the frozen-state cap (the worst case is a frozen claim at 0.02–0.22×, not a −30% drawdown), reviewed as one more expression of the hyperscaler-capex factor a book already owns several times, and it must fail cleanly through the DO-NOTHING gate if the tape re-rates above ~$470 before the packet exists.
5. **Any future short is dated or it is nothing:** borrow is trivial but the carry pain — compounding EPS, sandbagged guidance floors, a structural index bid, and a pricing ratchet that cushions the crack — makes a standing short on a monopoly-compounder an unpayable path; the only reviewable short is the post-trigger cyclical version, sized for one to two masked quarters with the pain ledger debited first.

Probabilities and unpublished mechanics are labeled [agent-inferred].*


## Section III.11 — The Evidence Matrix, Hypothesis Arena, and Variant-Perception Map

**TSMC — Taiwan Semiconductor Manufacturing Company Limited (NYSE: TSM, 1 ADS = 5 common shares; TWSE: 2330). Module date: 2026-07-17. Reference price: US$409.74/ADS (2026-07-16 close).**

*Role: formalizes what the red-team/decision section adjudicated narratively — a claim-level, credibility-weighted evidence matrix for the four thesis legs; a hypothesis arena with registered prediction locks and a disagreement register; and the variant-perception card. Consumes the normalization, moat, valuation/expectations, red-team/decision, 20-F redline, guidance-archaeology, and product-cycle/non-AI-engine sections. Facts carry source + date; judgments are **[agent-inferred]**. Acronyms: ADS = American Depositary Share; GM/OM/NPM = gross/operating/net margin; EPS = earnings per share; P/E = price-to-earnings; NTM = next twelve months; YoY/QoQ = year-over-year/quarter-over-quarter; HPC = high-performance computing; ASIC = application-specific integrated circuit; CoWoS = Chip-on-Wafer-on-Substrate advanced packaging; N2/N3/A16/A14 = TSMC 2nm/3nm/1.6nm/1.4nm-class nodes; SF2 = Samsung Foundry 2nm; 18A/14A = Intel 1.8nm/1.4nm-class nodes; wpm = wafers per month; FX = foreign exchange; BIS = U.S. Bureau of Industry and Security; §232 = Section 232, Trade Expansion Act; ART = U.S.–Taiwan Agreement on Reciprocal Trade; N-2 = Taiwan's two-generations-behind overseas-deployment statute; EM = emerging markets; A/R = accounts receivable; 6-K/20-F = SEC foreign-issuer filings; IR = investor relations; ADIZ = air defense identification zone; OSAT = outsourced assembly and test. Sell-side and buy-side institutions are unnamed per section rules.*

---

### 1. Method and credibility rubric

[skill: evidence-matrix-engine / module: CLAIM_EXTRACTION.md, module: CREDIBILITY_WEIGHTING.md]

Each leg is decomposed into discrete, dated evidence claims — observations, not opinions — classified **S** (supports), **R** (refutes/weakens), or **N** (neutral/informs), and weighted:

| Tier | Wt | Definition |
|---|---|---|
| P5 | 5 | Primary filing (6-K, 20-F, competitor 10-K, government proclamation) |
| Section II.5 | 4 | Company statement (primary transcript, press release, IR data; a customer CEO on the record) |
| V3 | 3 | Reputable data/market-research vendor; think-tank monitoring series |
| P2 | 2 | Journalistic press |
| R1 | 1 | Supply-chain report / rumor |

Conventions: company statements known only via same-day secondary coverage are down-weighted to **Section II.2** (official 2Q26 transcript pending on IR at compile time — evidence file 01, gaps); self-interested forward assertions keep their tier but are flagged in the read. Net posture per leg = weighing of S-mass vs R-mass → **well-supported / contested / thin**. Accounting: **34 rows, 16 R rows (47%)** — a two-sided matrix, not a support summary [skill: evidence-matrix-engine / Stage 5].

### 2. The evidence matrix, leg by leg

#### L1 — Moat: process + packaging leadership defensible to ~2030

| # | Evidence claim (dated, sourced) | Stance | Wt |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Foundry share 72.3% in 1Q26, up from ~60% end-2023 (research vendor, 2026-06-12) | S | V3 |
| 2 | Intel 14A: zero committed external customers; FY2025 10-K keeps may-pause language absent a significant external customer (filed Jan 2026) | S | P5 |
| 3 | Rival economics: Intel Foundry FY25 operating loss −$10.3B on $307M external revenue (10-K, Jan 2026); Samsung foundry+LSI ≈ −KRW 6T FY25 (press, 2025-11-13) | S | P5/P2 |
| 4 | N2 in volume production since 4Q25 "with good yield"; first revenue 2Q26 at 3% of wafer revenue (4Q25 call, 2026-01-15; 2Q26 6-K, 2026-07-16) | S | P5 |
| 5 | N2 tape-outs ~4× N3 at same stage; ~15 committed N2 customers, 10 HPC (press, 2026-07-16; vendor, 2025-09-22) | S | P2/V3 |
| 6 | Maia 2 (a hyperscaler's next-gen AI ASIC) reported placed on Intel 18A/18A-P (press, labeled claims-report, Jan 2026) | **R** | R1 |
| 7 | Intel reported insourcing 80–90% of Nova Lake compute tiles to 18A, scaling back the planned TSMC N2 allocation (vendor news repost, 2026-07-15) | **R** | R1 |
| 8 | Tesla AI5/AI6 dual-sourced TSMC + Samsung (customer CEO, 2026-04-15); AI5 taped out, Texas fab in production phase on SF2 (press, 2026-07-13); SF2 yields reported mid-50s–60% vs TSMC N2's 60–70% (press, 2026) | **R** | Section II.5/R1 |

**Read.** Support is heavy and high-tier — the two most damaging-to-rivals facts (14A pause language; the loss economics) are primary filings — while all three defection rows are R1-grade. But the refutes cluster in the last six months and share a mechanism: deliberate second-sourcing by customers who own a fab, own the architecture, or don't need the frontier (red-team ledger, entry 5). Posture: **well-supported through ~2030, with dated erosion markers**. The load-bearing refuter is scheduled: the 14A commitment window closes 2027-06-30 [skill: evidence-matrix-engine / Stage 3, refuter-attempt requirement].

#### L2 — Normalized mid-cycle GM 57–60%

| # | Evidence claim | Stance | Wt |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Through-cycle GM floor raised: "53% and higher" (2Q25 call, 2025-07-17) → "56% and higher" (4Q25 call, 2026-01-15) — brackets the band from below | S | Section II.5 |
| 2 | Guided dilution stack: overseas fabs 2–3pp GM → 3–4pp later; FY26 depreciation +high-teens %; N2 dilution 2–3pp full-year (4Q25 call, 2026-01-15) | S | Section II.5 |
| 3 | Historical anchors: FY24 GM 56.1% under adverse FX; 2023 trough 54.4% on shipments −21% (company reports, 2024–26) | S | Section II.5 |

| 5 | GM printed 67.7%, above its own guide top; OM 180bp above (2Q26 6-K, 2026-07-16) — economics keep outrunning the normalization timetable | **R** (above) | P5 |
| 6 | Reported pricing ratchet: +3–10% on advanced nodes for 2026, annual hikes through ~2029; ~$30k N2 tier (vendor/supply-chain, 2025-12-29; 2025-10-09) — if value-priced, the band sits higher | **R** (above) | R1 |
| 7 | Same-day coverage cites N2 dilution "3–4pp in 2H26" vs 2–3pp full-year on two prior primary calls (secondary, 2026-07-16; official transcript pending) | **R** (below) | Section II.2 |
| 8 | Customer prepayments ("temporary receipts") NT$291.1B → NT$189.9B FY24→FY25; noncurrent tail −74% off the 2022 peak (FY2025 20-F Note 22, filed 2026-04-16; via the 20-F redline section) — shortage-era co-funding expiring as capex re-accelerates | **R** (below) | P5 |

**Read.** The healthiest kind of matrix: refutes attack from *both directions*, as a band-claim should. The center rests on Section II.5 (management's own arithmetic); the strongest refute (#8, P5) says the *risk-sharing* under the band is thinning — customers no longer pre-fund capacity, so the next downturn is defended by fewer contractual cushions than 2023's [agent-inferred]. Posture: **contested within a supported frame** — 57–60% survives, but where within it, and how fast the print descends, is open. Cheapest discriminator: the December-2026 pricing-round reports (normalization section, kill signal 5).

**Read.** Support mass is enormous but *short-dated* — every S row proves 2026 and most of 2027; none reaches 2028. Refute mass is individually light (one P5 pair, the rest V3/R1) but aimed at one seam: commitment duration, financing quality, end-demand breadth beneath the AI layer. Row 11 is a missing-source task, not a conclusion. Posture: **contested — supported to ~2027, structurally unprovable beyond**, matching the red-team ledger's standing UNKNOWN (entry 10); hence this leg carries both house flip triggers rather than a verdict.

No leg's weighted refute mass exceeds its support mass today; L3's support decays fastest with horizon [agent-inferred].

### 3. The hypothesis arena

[skill: investment-hypothesis-arena / Stages 0–7; module: HYPOTHESIS_GRAMMAR.md]

#### 3.1 Question of record

**Q-E1:** *Does the credibility-weighted evidence, as of 2026-07-17, support the four legs strongly enough that the registered BUY flip trigger (big-4 hyperscaler aggregate CY2027 capex guidance ≥ +20% YoY by 2027-02-28) can fire alone — or does some leg carry refuting evidence heavy enough that the flip needs a companion condition?* **Falsifier:** any leg whose weighted refute mass ≥ support mass on evidence in hand. **Decision dependency:** flip-trigger specification — trigger design, not the call, which the decision section owns.

Preview: no leg fails today, but L2's two-directional contest means a capex guide alone leaves the margin question open into 2027. The BUY flip should carry the **December-2026 pricing-round confirmation as a companion condition**, promoting the decision section's execution note to an explicit conjunct [agent-inferred].

#### 3.2 The slate

The decision section's slate carried a pricing-ratchet third alternative. This arena keeps that slate live and forces a **different, orthogonal third alternative** — time-based rather than mechanism-based [skill: investment-hypothesis-arena / Stage 1].

| ID | Claim | Mechanism | Falsifier (observable, dated) | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| H-L (long) | Monopoly compounds through 2030; GM ≥63%; the 2030 buyer pays ≥22× | Sold-out leading edge + annual hikes + packaging attach; second sources fail | Two consecutive monthly prints <+15% YoY; GM <64% at the 3Q26/4Q26 prints; or a committed marquee 14A customer by 1H27 | active; not underwritable at $409.74 (valuation section) |
| H-S (short) | FY27 EPS breaks to ~$11.5–12.7/ADS vs consensus ~$20 as depreciation wave + rent reversion + digestion coincide | 5-year equipment lives meet the $60–64B capex vintage exactly as demand decelerates | Big-4 CY27 capex guided ≥+20% (Jan–Feb 2027); or FY27 guided ≥+20% revenue with GM ≥63% | active, undated — first observable crack 2026-10-15 |
| H-C (consensus) | Market roughly right; base IRR ≈ +2%; whisper bar already above published consensus | Serial-beater saturation: −2.32% close on an +11.7% net-profit beat (2026-07-16) | Either house flip trigger firing | provisional survivor (decision section) |
| **H-RB (forced third: range-bound compounder)** | **EPS compounds ~15–20%/yr through 2027–28 while the NTM multiple compresses ~15–20% (20× → ~16–17×); ADS roughly flat ±10% for ~2 years, then re-rates on the 2028 echo/A14 cycle** | Expectations saturation does the work the cycle never does: beats get sold, estimates rise, the multiple bleeds — TSMC's own record shows compression *through* EPS growth (2022: EPS +70%, year-end P/E 27.2× → 11.2×; 10-yr band 11.2–28.8×, retrieved 2026-07-16) | NTM P/E ≥24× sustained a full quarter (re-rate refutes compression); NTM P/E <15× (that is H-S's break); FY27 EPS growth <10% (compounding half fails) | **live; nothing in the matrix refutes it; most consistent with the print-day tape** [agent-inferred] |

H-RB needs no demand break (contra H-S), no margin miracle (contra H-L), and unlike H-C makes a testable *path* claim — flat price on rising earnings, resolved by watching the multiple. If H-RB is right, DO-NOTHING is not a compromise but alpha-neutral by construction.

#### 3.3 Refuter-first assignments

[skill: investment-hypothesis-arena / Stage 3 — what each PROPONENT must explain away, from the matrix]

- **H-L:** prepayments −35% YoY through a supercycle (P5); inventory/A-R drift (P5); the −2.32% close on a record beat — who is the incremental buyer; end-device unit collapse beneath the AI layer (V3); a reverse-DCF needing ~17–19% EPS CAGR to 2030 at a 20× terminal just to earn the discount rate (valuation section).
- **H-S:** June +67.9%; a twice-raised guide and $8B mid-year capex raise; CoWoS booked out; *zero* Tier-1 cut-risk language markers on the 2026-07-16 call (guidance-archaeology watchlist: current-demand "inventory adjustment/correction," capex "lower end"/"tighten up," "tool delivery schedule changes"); the sandbag ledger (guides are floors within regime); the 2023 analog (shipments −21%, revenue −9% on +16% revenue/wafer).
- **H-C:** published FY26 consensus 4–7% below the guide-anchored build ($15.91/$15.35 vs ~$16.5 core) — survivable only via the whisper distinction; consensus FY27 implying ~51% NPM against management's own dilution stack — a "roughly right price" carried by wrong line items.
- **H-RB:** the momentum regime — total returns +92.6% (2024), +55.9% (2025), ~+44% YTD (market-data pages, 2026-07-16) rarely die into a flat channel without an event [agent-inferred]; the mechanical EM-index bid (~15.2% weight in the leading EM fund; record $38B H1-26 inflows) that can re-rate the multiple; the risk the Jan–Feb 2027 capex guides fire and the re-rate front-runs the compression.

Cross-examination [Stage 4]: cheapest discriminators — H-C vs H-RB, the *multiple path through beats* (a 3Q26 beat with NTM P/E slipping below ~19× favors H-RB); H-L vs H-S, the December-2026 pricing round; H-S vs all, the monthly print cadence. No pair is left at "both plausible, no next test."
