### 4. The variant-perception map

[skill: variant-perception-and-catalyst-engine / Stages 1–7; module: VARIANT_PERCEPTION_CARD_SPEC.md]

#### 4.1 Price-implied consensus (the anchor)

What $409.74 must believe (valuation section reverse-DCF, r = 10%): **~18.4% EPS CAGR 2026→2030 at a 20× terminal** (band 15.7–21.2% across r = 8–12%); implied 2030 revenue **$325–401B**; roughly **a decade of above-market growth**; joint probability of the full conjunction ≈ **25–33%**. The sell side sits above the price: average target $516.76, 12 of 19 at the top rating (aggregator, 2026-07-16). Published FY26 EPS consensus $15.91/$15.35 vs the guide-anchored ~$16.5 core; FY27 consensus NT$128.80/common ≈ $20/ADS derived — implying ~51% NPM, above the 2026 record. The worry the multiple encodes: the Taiwan tail (~15% probability-equivalent) plus digestion anxiety (put/call open interest 1.31; −2.32% close on a record beat, 2026-07-16).

#### 4.2 Where the variant lives — both directions

- **Near-term, consensus too low (fast-decaying):** published FY26 numbers lag the "slightly above 40%" guide by 4–7% on EPS; mechanical upward revisions follow (six up, zero down in the 90 days post the April print — guidance-archaeology section). Real, but closes by 2026-10-15; an observation, not an edge.
- **Medium-term, consensus too high (slow-building):** FY27 at ~51% implied NPM embeds *none* of management's own dilution stack (overseas 2–3→3–4pp; N2; depreciation +high-teens compounding off a $60–64B vintage on 5-year lives). The normalization section's FY27 paths run $11.5–17 vs consensus ~$20 — the mean-reversion exposure sits entirely on consensus's side.
- **Unnoticed engine — the non-AI content ladder:** smartphone platform revenue grew ~+13% in FY2025 on +1.5% global units (product-cycle section, computed); GenAI attach (36%→45%→52% of shipments) monetizes through die size and node mix with *no unit growth required*; the non-AI stack adds ~$20B of revenue by 2029 — comparable to all 2025 AI-accelerator revenue — on demand nearly orthogonal to hyperscaler capex. Consensus files 22%-and-falling smartphone share as decay; the dollar line says content ladder.
- **The tail:** priced thin-to-fair; not part of the variant in either direction.

#### 4.3 Upside priced at ≈0, with the cheapest disproof for each

[skill: variant-perception-and-catalyst-engine / Stage 3; module: SHOTS_ON_GOAL_OPTIONALITY_LADDER.md — numbered shots; base case credits zero]

| Shot | Priced-at-≈0 upside | Cheapest disproof that it is >0 | Dated falsifier (metric + threshold + by-when) |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **2028 memory-deferral replacement echo.** The 2026–27 memory shock (bill-of-materials +8–15%; 2026 units −13.9%) defers, not destroys, replacement; the cohort retires into N2/A14-generation, GenAI-default devices in 2028, on the same calendar as A16/A14 ramps (product-cycle section; vendor forecasts: 2027 −1.1%, 2028 +5.5%) | Already on record cheaply: the vendor's 2028 +5.5% rebound forecast, plus one quarterly ratio — TSMC's smartphone-platform dollar line vs the unit tracker | 2028 unit forecast + platform revenue YoY; kill if 2028 revised below ~+5% *and* platform dollars negative YoY two quarters; by the ~Jan-2028 tracker |
| 2 | **Smartphone content ladder.** Consensus models the 22% platform as decay; the die-size/node-price/GenAI-attach ladder is a low-volatility annuity that pays for N2 first (the largest smartphone customer reportedly took over half of initial N2 capacity; press, 2025–26) | One public table per quarter: TSMC's platform-mix table vs unit data — no model needed | Smartphone platform revenue ex-FX; kill if negative YoY across FY2027 while units are flat; by the 4Q27 print (~Jan 2028) |
| 3 | **Take-rate expansion on the ASIC shift.** Per AI end-dollar migrating from ~$30–40k merchant GPU systems to ~$3–8k hyperscaler ASICs with similar TSMC content, TSMC's share of the end-dollar *rises*; every major production ASIC except one reported program is TSMC-fabbed (evidence file 03; direction affirmed, magnitude struck — ledger entry 8) | The customers' own disclosures: the lead ASIC design partner's ~$73B AI backlog (company statement, May 2026) growing while TSMC HPC revenue outgrows the merchant-GPU vendor's data-center line — a two-line ratio from public prints | TSMC HPC growth minus lead GPU vendor's data-center growth, trailing 4 quarters; kill if persistently negative while the ASIC backlog grows; by the 2Q27 print |

Every shot carries its falsifier — a card without one is a story, not an idea [skill: variant-perception-and-catalyst-engine / Validation]. All three are non-conjunctive — each pays independently — and all are the free optionality *behind* the DO-NOTHING call; shot 1 is the long-side symmetry item the product-cycle section already flags for the 2027 shopping list.

#### 4.4 Upstream catalysts that confirm the variant before TSMC's P&L shows it

[skill: variant-perception-and-catalyst-engine / Stage 4 — TSMC is itself the chokepoint, so confirmation runs through its customers' order books and the layers ahead of its revenue recognition]

| Big-4 hyperscaler CY2027 capex guides | Jan–Feb 2027 | L3 direction — the flip variable, before any TSMC print can |

| Lead GPU vendor's data-center prints + $1T-visibility restatement | Aug/Nov 2026, Feb 2027 | The largest customer's demand, one layer downstream of its customers' budgets |
| ASIC partner backlog (~$73B) and hyperscaler ASIC roadmap cadence (four generations through 2027, announced Mar 2026) | Quarterly | Shot 3 — take-rate migration landing at TSMC |
| Memory price rollover (memory +40% through 2Q26) | 2H26–2027 | Dates the shot-1 echo: bill-of-materials relief restarts the replacement clock |
| Unit-tracker revisions (2027 −1.1% / 2028 +5.5% baselines) | Quarterly | Shots 1–2 calendar |
| Lithography-vendor bookings (baseline **pending**; register at first check per the monitoring section) | Quarterly | Independent upstream read on 2027 equipment intent; corroborates, never triggers |

#### 4.5 Counterweight gates

[skill: variant-perception-and-catalyst-engine / Stage 7 — verdicts recorded so generation isn't cheerleading]

**Screen-result-vs-idea:** pass — variant + dated catalysts + falsifiers present; but the *long at today's price* stays a screen result until a flip trigger fires (the price already owns the variant's good half). **Weak-link causal chain:** theme-to-P&L is proven (HPC 66% of 2Q26 revenue, 6-K); the weak link is *duration* — named, dated, monitored, not assumed. **TAM stock-vs-flow:** flagged live — the bear's best structural point is that part of 2024–26 AI capex is a one-time training-buildout *stock* priced as perpetual *flow*; the 25–33% joint-probability math discounts it and lock 8 tests it. **Asymmetry-by-conjunction:** the bull case is a conjunction (persistence AND margin AND terminal multiple AND quiet tail) priced as one; the variant shots are deliberately non-conjunctive. Card valid; hands off to exactly the posture the house holds [Stage 8].

---

**Bottom line.** The matrix shows a thesis whose support is top-tier but time-boxed, and whose refuting evidence — prepayments, working-capital drift, financing seams, end-device weakness, second-source clocks — is low-tier but all aimed at the 2027–28 seam the price insists on owning. The arena converts that shape into eight pre-registered locks and one specification improvement (a pricing-round companion on the BUY flip). The variant map locates the durable non-consensus material in unglamorous places — the content ladder, the 2028 echo, the take-rate migration — each priced at ≈0 and each falsifiable for the cost of reading public tables on a calendar. Nothing here changes DO-NOTHING/WATCHLIST; everything here makes it faster to change when the evidence does.

*Compliance: analytical research over public data and the cited evidence pack; no position-sizing or execution instruction; no material non-public information; probabilities and unpublished mechanics are labeled [agent-inferred]; institutions unnamed by design.*


## Section III.12 — Earnings-Cycle Card, the Management Agenda, and Ownership Signals

**TSMC — Taiwan Semiconductor Manufacturing Company Limited (NYSE: TSM, 1 American Depositary Share (ADS) = 5 common shares; TWSE: 2330). Module date: 2026-07-17. Reference price US$409.74/ADS (2026-07-16 close, print day).**

*Role: the recurring-workflow layer — the 2Q26 REVIEW card and 3Q26 PREVIEW card [skill: earnings-cycle / modules: REVIEW_MODE.md, PREVIEW_MODE.md, ESTIMATE_RECONCILIATION.md], the management meeting agenda [skill: meeting-prep / module: MEETING_TIERS.md, standard tier], the Form 4 / 13F resolution [skill: insider-and-13F-signals / modules: FORM4_SUBSTANCE.md, ROLE_SPECIFIC_INSIDER_SIGNAL.md], and the cross-name snapshot [skill: sector-dashboard]. It consumes — never re-derives — the guidance archaeology (Section III.3), flow architecture (Section III.8), and thesis legs/thresholds (Section II.7, Section III.10). Evidence files cited [E01]–[E06]. Acronyms: GM/OM/NPM = gross/operating/net margin; EPS = earnings per share; D&A = depreciation & amortization; FX = foreign exchange; YoY/QoQ = year-over-year/quarter-over-quarter; HPC = high-performance computing; N2/N3 = TSMC 2nm/3nm nodes; A16/A14 = 1.6nm/1.4nm-class nodes; CoWoS = Chip-on-Wafer-on-Substrate packaging; wpm = wafers per month; kpcs = thousand 12-inch-equivalent wafers; ESPP/LTI = Employee Stock Purchase Plan / Long-Term Incentive; FPI = foreign private issuer; SEC/EDGAR/CIK = U.S. Securities and Exchange Commission / its filing system / Central Index Key; ADR = American Depositary Receipt; NTM = next twelve months; P/E = price-to-earnings; EM = emerging markets; IV = implied volatility; OI = open interest; A/R = accounts receivable; LTA = long-term agreement; VIS = Vanguard International Semiconductor; N-2 = Taiwan's two-generations-behind overseas-deployment statute; BIS/VEU = U.S. Bureau of Industry and Security / Validated End-User; HBM = high-bandwidth memory; OSAT = outsourced assembly & test; EUV = extreme-ultraviolet lithography; ASIC = application-specific integrated circuit; 13F = quarterly institutional holdings report (45-day lag).*

### 1. 2Q26 REVIEW card (quarter ended 2026-06-30; reported 2026-07-16)

[skill: earnings-cycle / REVIEW_MODE.md — guide-vs-actual on every line; quality adjustments; leg-reconciliation hard gate]

#### 1.1 Guide-vs-actual: every guided line beaten

Guidance set 2026-04-16 at FX 31.7 NT$/US$ [E01 §2]; actuals per the 6-K (accession 0001046179-26-000451, 2026-07-16) [E01 §1; E06]:

| Line | Guide (2026-04-16) | Actual 2Q26 | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|
| Revenue (US$) | $39.0–40.2B | **$40.20B** (+33.7% YoY, +12.0% QoQ; NT$1,270.4B, +36.0%) | top of range |
| Gross margin | 65.5–67.5% | **67.7%** | **above the band** (+20bp) |
| Operating margin | 56.5–58.5% | **60.3%** | **above the band** (+180bp) |
| FX | 31.7 assumed | 31.60 realized | slightly stronger TWD than assumed — a small headwind absorbed (1% TWD appreciation ≈ −40bp GM; CFO, 2Q25 call, 2025-07-17 [E01 §6]); the margin beat is not an FX gift |
| Net income | consensus NT$632.6B (18 analysts, pre-print [E05 §3]) | **NT$706.6B** (+77.4% YoY) | **+11.7% beat** |

| Alongside | — | FY26 growth guide raised to "slightly above 40%" US$ (from "above 30%"); capex to **$60–64B** (from $52–56B); +$100B U.S. commitment ($265B total) | double raise [E01 §1–2; E02 §3–4] |

#### 1.2 Quality adjustments [composes quality-of-earnings]

Non-operating items of NT$95.83B include **NT$63.20B of disposal/mark-to-market gains on VIS shares** (8.1% stake sold 2026-05-15) [E01 §1] — NT$2.44/share, so **clean EPS ≈ NT$24.81 ≈ US$3.93/ADS** vs the $4.31 headline; operating income (+65.4% YoY) is the cleaner run-rate line [E06]. Minor same-direction items: NT$5.27B other operating income "mainly due to insurance claims from the April 3, 2024 earthquake"; tax rate 18.1%, up QoQ on the recurring undistributed-retained-earnings tax [E01 §1]. YoY margin optics also got a weaker-TWD assist (31.60 vs 31.05 in 2Q25 [E01 §6]).

#### 1.3 Estimate reconciliation — the mechanical street impact

[ESTIMATE_RECONCILIATION.md; whisper/echo mechanics consumed from Section III.3 §4]

Pre-print FY26 consensus: EPS US$15.91 / $15.35 (two aggregators), NT$100.40/common share; revenue NT$5.21T (+36.75%) [E05 §3]. The new guide ("slightly above 40%" on FY25's $122.42B) implies **FY26 revenue ≥ ~US$171.4B ≈ NT$5.42–5.49T at FX 31.6–32.0** [computed] — consensus sits **~4–5% below guide** and marks up near-mechanically (the lowball-anchor configuration, Section III.3 §4). Proposed entries (approval_status: proposed):

```yaml
- {period: FY26, kpi: revenue_usd, current_internal: ">=171.4B (guide-through)", actual: "1H26 $76.10B",
   street: "NT$5.21T pre-print", guide: "slightly above +40% (2026-07-16)",
   revision_cause: "second intra-year raise + 1H actuals", source_refs: [E01 §1-2], linked_legs: [L3]}
- {period: FY26, kpi: eps_ads_usd, current_internal: "16.5 core ex-VIS; ~17.0 headline", actual: "1H26 $7.79 incl. VIS",
   street: "15.35-15.91 pre-print", revision_cause: "guide-through build; Section II.7 entry 6 (core $16.5, consensus floor $15.5)",
   source_refs: [E06, Section II.7 §4], linked_legs: [L2, L3]}
- {period: FY27, kpi: eps_ads_usd, current_internal: "unchanged - print raises the base, not the 2027 margin answer",
   street: "NT$128.80 ≈ ~$20/ADS derived", revision_cause: "none mechanical; the $60-64B capex vintage raises FY27 D&A +22-28% [agent-inferred, Section II.3]",
   source_refs: [E05 §3, Section II.3], linked_legs: [L2]}
```

In words: FY26 street EPS marks up **+4–7%** as the guide propagates; FY27 street numbers *rise on base effect* while still embedding **no normalization** — consensus FY27 implies ~50–51% NPM under any plausible revenue denominator (Section II.7 ledger entry 3, REAL). The print widens the 2027 consensus-vs-house gap; it does not close it.

#### 1.4 Market-reaction read — what got priced

Options priced a **±5.62–6.65%** earnings move (~$150B of market cap); put/call OI 1.31; 30-day IV ~57%, rank 79 [E05 §6]. Delivered: **−2.32%** to $409.74 (after-hours −0.83%) on a beat-every-line, double-raise print [E05 §1, §3] — ~0.4× the implied move, in the adverse direction. Read (Section III.3 §4; Section III.8 §4): the operative bar was the *whisper* — guide plus the historical +6–12pp sandbag — which sat at or above the new guide before it was announced. Spot closed below the $443 dealer gamma-flip (negative-gamma regime) and through the $420 put wall [E05 §6], meeting a thin discretionary bid above the index rung (Section III.8 §4). The −2.3% is expectations saturation, not information — the key input to §2.

|---|---|---|---|

### 2. 3Q26 PREVIEW card (print expected ~2026-10-15 — estimated from the mid-Jan/Apr/Jul/Oct cadence, not yet confirmed)

[skill: earnings-cycle / PREVIEW_MODE.md]

#### 2.1 Line-item expectations

Guidance (2026-07-16, FX 32.0): revenue **$44.6–45.8B** (+12.4% QoQ at mid); **GM 65–67%**; OM 56–58% [E01 §1]. Guide-through [computed, agent-inferred]: mid revenue $45.2B ≈ NT$1,446B; the GM band embeds the 2H-arriving N2 dilution (2–3pp full-year guided Jan/Apr; "3–4pp in 2H26" per same-day secondary — transcript reconciliation pending [E02 §1]); clean EPS builds to roughly **NT$27.5–28.5 ≈ US$4.30–4.45/ADS**, +9–13% over 2Q's clean $3.93. The monthly tape frames it: June NT$442.7B (+67.9% YoY), 1H +35.6% [E01 §4]; the guide midpoint implies ~NT$482B average months, first checkpoint ~Aug 10 [computed]. Seasonality (smartphone ramp) stacks on the "steep ramp-up of our 2-nanometer technology" (CFO, release [E06]); expect N2 to step from 3% toward mid/high-single-digit % of wafer revenue and HPC to hold ≥64% [agent-inferred].

#### 2.2 Beat/miss asymmetry — what the whisper already assumes

The serial-beater problem (Section III.3): every guided line has been beaten through this regime, EPS surprises are *widening* (Q1 +4.8% → Q2 +14.3%), whisper ≈ guide + sandbag — and July proved **an +11.7% beat plus a 10pp raise clears consensus but not the whisper** [E05 §3; Section III.3 §4]. Into October: (i) revenue at the *top* of the range is already assumed — in-range is a push at best; (ii) published consensus will have marked to guide by September, so "beat vs consensus" carries near-zero information; (iii) the only live upside channel is a **third intra-year FY26 raise** (October 2025 precedent [III.3 §1]) and/or an above-seasonal 4Q guide; (iv) downside is the fat side — a Tier-1 language marker moves the stock more than a headline beat can, into negative gamma and a thin discretionary bid (Section III.8 §4). Net [agent-inferred]: October is a listen-to-the-language event; the words will price before the numbers.

#### 2.3 Three things that matter more than the headline

1. **GM vs the 65–67% band, with the margin-ledger read-through** (Section II.3): decompose the print against the depreciation holiday (FY26 D&A guided +high-teens; 2Q D&A intensity ~15.5% of revenue vs 22.9% FY24), N2 dilution, overseas dilution (2–3pp early-stage), FX at 32.0. A 66–67% print with N2 accelerating = the rent holds; 65.0–65.5% at >100% utilization = the dilution stack arriving faster than guided [agent-inferred].
2. **The 4Q26 guide: ≥$48B is the line** (Section II.7). Check: "slightly above 40%" ⇒ ≥$171.4B; 1H $76.1B + 3Q mid $45.2B ⇒ implied 4Q ≈ **$50.1B** [computed]. A $48–50B guide keeps the FY guide honest; below $48B (+7% QoQ) cracks momentum even if 3Q beat.
3. **Any FY27 capex/depreciation language — the archaeology tells** (Section III.3 §2–3). Capex language led every modern cut by 1–2 quarters (3 of 3). Tier-1 markers, verbatim families: customer **"inventory adjustment/correction"** applied to current AI/HPC demand; capex **"closer to the lower end" / "tightening up" / "pushed out"** (any tool-delivery framing); a first FY-guide trim (never the last, 2 of 2). Their absence — plus whether FY27 D&A framing steps from "high-teens" to "+20s%" — is the real content. "Prudent in our business planning" alone is boilerplate; it fires in both regimes [III.3 §3].

#### 2.4 Decision-linked thresholds (house monitoring plan, Section III.10/Section II.7 — restated)

- **GM <64% at the 3Q26 print (or 4Q26 GM guide <63%) breaks the normalization floor** → mid-cycle point ≤57%, base→mediocrity re-weight; any quarter <60% before 2027-06-30 → band cut to 55–58%.
- **Monthly-revenue two-stage rule:** two consecutive months **<+15% YoY = deceleration alarm** (flip trigger); two consecutive **<+10% = air-pocket confirmation** (2023 playbook: volume −20%, price holds).
- Working capital at the print: inventory ≤95 days; A/R ≤32 days.
- FY27 D&A guided >+25% *with* revenue framed <+15% (Jan-2027 call) = compression arithmetic locks.

Pre-print stance: the October event is a monitoring gate, not a catalyst to front-run [agent-inferred; consistent with Section II.7's do-nothing lane].

### 3. Management/IR meeting agenda — standard tier

[skill: meeting-prep / MEETING_TIERS.md Tier 2; evasion-tells per [skill: management-communication-quality / COMMUNICATION_QUALITY_TESTS.md — Q&A behavior, topic avoidance, cost-framing tell, KPI continuity]]

**What's changed since last interaction** (the mandatory section): second FY26 raise to "slightly above 40%"; capex $60–64B; +$100B/$265B U.S. commitment; 2nm first revenue; HPC 66%; VIS sale gain NT$63.2B; A16 volume production re-dated to 2027; N-2 statute in force; Nanjing on annual export licenses [E01–E04].

Thirteen questions by thesis leg — each with why / good vs bad answer / evasion tell:

**Margin & normalization (L2)**

1. **FY27–28 depreciation by quarter, given $60–64B on 5-year equipment lives.** Why: the mechanical driver of the 2027 margin debate (FY26 +high-teens guided; house math FY27 +22–28% [II.3, agent-inferred]). Good: a quarterly D&A path plus the revenue level that keeps intensity flat. Bad: "high-teens for now; too early for 2027." Tell: **metric switch** — answering a D&A-dollars question with GM-floor language ("56% and higher") or capex ratios.
2. **Scope of the 2027 price-hike round — nodes, applications, magnitude.** Why: H-3 (pricing ratchet) is the live third alternative; reports say 3–10% annual hikes through ~2029, ~15% on 3nm in 2H26 [E03 §7]. Good: annual value-based repricing confirmed with tiering. Bad: "strategic, not opportunistic" with no forward frame. Tell: retreat to the stock phrase; external check = December-2026 supply-chain reports (the cheapest L2 kill-signal, Section II.7 §5).
3. **Reconcile 2nm dilution: 2–3pp full-year (Jan/Apr) vs "3–4pp in 2H26" (July coverage).** Why: ~50bp of FY26 GM path; first test of N2 ramp economics. Good: both statements bridged on basis. Bad: neither confirmed nor denied. Tell: **a bridge that only closes under management's chosen framing** [cost-framing tell].
4. **When does the overseas-dilution "latter stage" (3–4pp) begin, and how much does Arizona premium pricing offset it?** Why: the glide path (2–3pp early → 3–4pp later; CFO, 4Q25 call [E02 §5]) is undated; Arizona wafers quoted ~25–30% above Taiwan vs ~10% true cost (TechInsights, 2025) [E03 §7]. Good: dated stage boundaries + a premium-capture number. Bad: the range re-quoted undated. Tell: offset framed against an unrepresentative base.
5. **What will the FY26 20-F show on customer prepayments/temporary receipts, and the LTA renewal terms for 2027 capacity?** Why: prepayment-funded capacity is a balance-sheet substitute that unwinds pro-cyclically; quarterly accounts don't break it out (accrued liabilities +NT$48.4B QoQ) [II.3]. Good: balance, tenor, take-or-pay share of 2027 leading-edge/CoWoS. Bad: pointing at "accrued liabilities" without the note. Tell: **legalistic answer to a cash question**.

**Demand (L3)**

6. **What did the latest "customers' customers" verification cycle actually check — and what would make you flag inventory accumulation?** Why: the CEO's stated loop ("make sure chips are not going into inventory" [E01 §1]) is the company's own named falsifier; Section III.3's verdict requires it to keep returning clean answers. Good: named observables (data-center power-on rates, deployment lags). Bad: re-assertion that "demand is real." Tell: **adjectives without numbers; new vocabulary without a metric**.
7. **Will FY27 capex again grow slower than revenue — and what triggers restraint?** Why: the crossover discipline (CFO: revenue growth outpaced capex growth [E05 §7]) is the bull case's discipline pillar, and capex language is the proven lead indicator (Section III.3 §2). Good: an explicit rule tied to committed demand. Bad: "we invest for the long term" — and any "lower end / tighten / pushout" phrasing is itself the datum. Tell: watch the capex paragraph, not the revenue sentence.
8. **CoWoS exit-2027: capacity vs demand, and does the >90% share hold as OSATs add ~50–60K wpm?** Why: packaging is the scarcity rent's sharpest edge — gap ~20%→~10% by end-2026, ~90K→120–140K wpm, top-3 customers ≈85% of allocation [E02 §2; E03 §6]. Good: a 2027 exit-capacity figure and booked ratio. Bad: "working closely with customers." Tell: **omitting the OSAT variable** the supply chain discusses loudly [topic avoidance].
9. **Your largest customer is 19% of revenue with a reported ~60% of CoWoS — what is the reallocation mechanic if its build pauses?** Why: HPC at 66% makes the next turn single-factor; fungibility (node conversion, CoWoS reallocation lead-time) decides whether a pause is an air pocket or a repricing [E03 §5; E02 §2]. Good: conversion lead-times and waiting-list depth. Bad: "demand is broad-based." Tell: concentration never acknowledged in the answer.

**Moat & roadmap (L1)**

10. **A16: the April symposium moved volume production to 2027 — anchor customers and tape-out count?** Why: first node where the ramp is explicitly "customer-dependent"; lead customer reportedly the largest AI-accelerator vendor, while the largest smartphone customer reportedly skips to A14 [E02 §1]. Good: tape-out count + first-revenue quarter. Bad: "on track" without reconciling the 2026→2027 change. Tell: **KPI-continuity break left unaddressed**.
11. **How does the N-2 statute interact with Arizona Fab 3's 2nm/A16 timeline?** Why: the statute (in force end-2025) keeps overseas deployment two generations behind; Arizona 2nm by ~2027 requires Taiwan A14 ramping 2028 — a seam the report dates to 2027-06-30 [E04 §5; Section II.7 §5]. Good: the statutory mechanics (what counts as a "generation") plus a consistent tool-in date. Bad: "we comply with all applicable regulations." Tell: **long legalistic answer to a simple dated-timing question**.

**Wrapper & policy (L4)**

12. **Nanjing: status of the annual BIS license (post-VEU expiry 2025-12-31), and is the Nov-2024 advanced-AI shipment suspension for China customers still in force?** Why: China is 6% of revenue; the license renews annually; no report shows the suspension lifted [E03 §4; E01 §1]. Good: license dates + contingency framing. Bad: "we comply." Tell: categorical brevity on a dateable factual item.

**Capital allocation**

13. **With net cash ~US$78–83B and ~31% payout, under what conditions does a buyback enter the hierarchy?** Why: cash accretes even at $60–64B capex; the dividend path (NT$7.00/quarter declared 1Q26; ≥NT$23/share cash guided for 2026 [E01 §5]) is a floor, not a policy answer; there is no buyback program and the company never supports its own tape (Section III.8 §4). Good: an explicit hierarchy with a repurchase trigger. Bad: the "sustainable and steadily increasing dividend" sentence, full stop. Tell: **talk-without-action** — return enthusiasm with no dated mechanism.

*(Handoffs: a new margin mechanism surfaced by Q1/Q3 → re-underwrite; external attendees → compliance-logging [skill: meeting-prep].)*

### 4. Ownership signals: the July 2026 Form 4s resolved, and the 13F posture

[skill: insider-and-13F-signals / FORM4_SUBSTANCE.md — economic-substance filter; Stage 6 grade]

**What the filings are.** Investigated live on EDGAR (CIK 0001046179, 2026-07-17). These Form 4s are **not** ordinary Section 16 obligations: as an FPI, TSMC's registered securities are generally exempt from Section 16 (Exchange Act Rule 3a12-3(b)), and EDGAR shows **zero Form 4s in TSMC's entire history before 2026-03-24** (1998–2011 archive: 0; none in the recent file before that date). Since then: **136 Form 4s (2026-03-24 → 2026-07-16)**, a steady ~32–35/month — a new, company-administered program. From the filings themselves:

- **Filers are TSMC executive officers** — Chairman & CEO Wei Che-Chia (C.C. Wei), senior/vice presidents, the Controller. No 10% owners; no independent directors in the July sample.
- **The July wave is the monthly ESPP cycle:** on 2026-07-09, ~29 near-identical filings reported the plan administrator's 2026-07-07 purchase "pursuant to terms predetermined by the issuer" at NT$2,462.64 avg (US$76.62/common at FX 32.143) — CEO Wei 146 shares (~US$11K), SVPs 44–54 shares (~US$3.4–4.1K) (accessions 0001046179-26-000435 → -000445), several with LTI-bonus trust purchases. The 2026-07-16 filing (-000449, the Controller) pairs a 50-share buy with a 50-share sale (~US$3.7K) — plan mechanics.
- All are signed by one attorney-in-fact (Jen-Chau Huang) — centrally administered — and none are marked Rule 10b5-1 transactions.
- The program's opening filings include one genuine discretionary open-market buy: a VP purchasing 1,000 common shares at US$55.93 (~US$56K) on 2026-03-22 (accession -000122); officer equity-linked-note holdings are also disclosed.

**Why they exist [agent-inferred]:** the filings state no reason; the March-2026 start coincides with the January-2026 U.S.–Taiwan agreement and the enlarged U.S. footprint, making voluntary adoption of U.S.-style insider transparency (or a counsel-driven precautionary election) the most consistent reading. Nothing else in TSMC's reporting profile changed (still 20-F/6-K — still an FPI).

**What they do and don't signal** [FORM4_SUBSTANCE.md]: the July activity is **pre-programmed, administrator-executed, payroll-scale** — 40–150 shares/month against officer-and-director aggregate ownership of 0.23% [E04 §7]. On the substance score: size 0, predetermined, and the "cluster" is one payroll batch, not independent conviction — **noise by construction**. The lone discretionary buy (~$56K) is directionally positive but small; there is **no substantive insider selling** on record either. **Grade: N — no meaningful insider signal in either direction** [Stage 6]. The real content is *governance-procedural*: TSMC voluntarily put its officers' monthly dealings on the U.S. public record for the first time in 28 years — a transparency ratchet consistent with the U.S.-integration posture; one line for the governance file (Section II.4), not a conviction input. Ownership *state* ≠ transaction *signal* [Stage 2]: nothing here confirms or contradicts the thesis.

**13F posture, one paragraph** (depth owned by Section III.8 §4): ~3,800 ADR-holding institutions [E04 §7]. The confound-checked Q4'25–Q1'26 tape shows one genuine fundamental add (the largest tracked discretionary long-only holder, ~28.35M ADS), one genuine fundamental trim (a large value manager, −12.2%), an apparent whale exit that dissolves into an intra-complex reallocation when sibling funds are summed, and large bank/market-maker adds that are inventory, not conviction (Section III.8 §4; aggregator data — EDGAR primary before weighting any row). Hedge-fund breadth rose 158→186 (Q3'25→Q4'25) [E04 §7]. Net: institutional positioning is **mixed and second-order next to the index plumbing** (EM-benchmark weight ~15%; record $38B H1'26 EM inflows [E05 §6]). Next checkpoint: the **Q2'26 13F window ~2026-08-14** — a first-time global-quality cohort initiating would fatten the thin discretionary rung of the buyer ladder (Section III.8 §6).

## Section III.13 — The Controlled Ecosystem and the Substrate/Overhang Split

**TSMC (NYSE: TSM, 1 ADS = 5 common shares; TWSE: 2330) — wave-3 module. As of 2026-07-17; reference price $409.74/ADS (2026-07-16 close [E5 §1]).**

*Method note.* Three L1 lenses not yet run as primary instruments: the economic-entitlement map over what TSMC monetizes without owning (the Open Innovation Platform), the formal substrate-vs-overhang split with a bounded-overhang ledger, and the rulebook-as-asset read of Taiwan's franchise. Facts carry source + date; [E1]–[E6] = evidence pack; analytical estimates are **[agent-inferred]**. Accretive to Section II.1 (switching costs), Section II.2 (fab-replication ladder), Section II.5 (wrapper/tail), Section II.6 (valuation), Section III.1 (PDK/EDA edge): those priced the moat and the tail; this maps *who owns the moat's parts* and separates what the price pays for from what it endures.

*Acronyms:* OIP = Open Innovation Platform (TSMC's partner-ecosystem program, launched 2008); EDA = electronic design automation (chip-design software); IP = (semiconductor) intellectual-property blocks; PDK = process design kit; ADS = American Depositary Share; GM = gross margin; EPS = earnings per share; NPV = net present value; HBM = high-bandwidth memory; CoWoS = Chip-on-Wafer-on-Substrate (TSMC 2.5D packaging); OSAT = outsourced semiconductor assembly and test; ASIC = application-specific integrated circuit; DTCO = design-technology co-optimization; N-2 = Taiwan's two-generations-behind rule for overseas deployment; BIS = U.S. Bureau of Industry and Security; ART = U.S.–Taiwan Agreement on Reciprocal Trade; NDF = Taiwan National Development Fund; MOEA = Taiwan Ministry of Economic Affairs; NSTC = National Science and Technology Council; LY = Legislative Yuan; wpm = wafers per month.

---

### 1. The controlled ecosystem: what TSMC monetizes without owning

[skill: economic-entitlement-and-controlled-ecosystem / modules: ECOSYSTEM_PAYMENT_FLOW_MAP.md, BORROWED_MOAT_AND_CLAIM_RIGHTS.md, CLAIM_PIPE_AND_TRAPPED_VALUE_TAXONOMY.md]

The entitlement skill's default case is a listed toll-collector on someone else's assets. TSMC is the inverted case the payment-flow module is built to catch: it looks like a wafer vendor but sits at the chokepoint of an ecosystem in which nearly every other participant — EDA vendors, IP vendors, design-service houses, packaging-material and memory partners, the Taiwan state — invests its *own* capital to make TSMC's nodes the default destination for new silicon. TSMC legally owns fabs, process recipes, masks, packaging lines. It does not own the design tools, the third-party IP catalog, the design-service benches, the HBM stacks, the substrate/OSAT capacity, the co-located supplier web, or the university pipeline — yet it extracts rent from all of them, because each is calibrated to TSMC first. The OIP spans 100+ partners across six alliance classes — EDA, IP, Design Center, Value Chain, Cloud, 3DFabric [SemiWiki OIP overview, retrieved 2026-07-17].

**The entitlement map** (legal owner → entitlement extracted → self-reinforcement → contest point):

| Layer | Legal owner | TSMC's entitlement | Self-reinforcement | Contestable where |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| EDA reference flows | Cadence, Synopsys, Siemens (certification joint) | First-node readiness: certified flows on A16/N2P announced 2025-04-23 (Synopsys PR) and N2/A16 with A14 PDKs underway (Cadence PR, 2025–26) — ~2 years before A16 volume | Designs closed in TSMC-certified flows train customer CAD teams on TSMC assumptions; vendors prioritize the node with the most seats | PDK-parity clock: Intel 14A PDK 0.5 shipping (Jan-2026 call), 0.9 PDK due Oct-2026, Cadence–Intel DTCO deal 2026-06-08; Samsung SF2/SF2P certified by Synopsys 2025-06-16, Cadence 2026 |
| Third-party IP catalog | Dozens of vendors (Arm, Rambus, Alphawave, Silicon Creations, Analog Bits…) | Industry's largest silicon-verified foundry-specific catalog: ~93,000 certified IP items in 2025 vs ~3,000 in 2010 [SemiAnalysis via SemiWiki, retrieved 2026-07-17 — REPORT; no comparable Intel/Samsung count] | Vendors fund per-node verification only where design starts are expected; each start deepens the catalog that attracts the next | A customer-funded IP port at scale — the reported Maia 2 move to Intel 18A-P is the live test [Tom's Hardware, Jan-2026 — REPORT] |
| Design-service alliance | Alchip (TWSE 3661, independent; Trainium3 partner [E3 §5]); GUC (TWSE 3443 — **TSMC-affiliated: TSMC largest shareholder ~35%, from 52% acquired Jan-2003; sole-foundry** [GUC profile, retrieved 2026-07-17]) | The hyperscaler ASIC on-ramp: houses owning the foundry interface default to TSMC; GUC co-detailed the HBM4/HBM4E roadmap with TSMC [Tom's Hardware, retrieved 2026-07-17] | Every ASIC win staffs more TSMC-flow engineers; the bench is the barrier | Broadcom-class aggregators are portable in principle; watch second-gen hyperscaler ASICs (OpenAI gen-2 planned on A16 [TrendForce, 2026-01-15 — REPORT]) |
| 3DFabric/CoWoS alliance | 19 partners at launch (2022-10-26): memory Micron/Samsung Memory/SK hynix; substrate Ibiden/Unimicron; OSAT ASE/Amkor/SPIL; test Teradyne (2025 OIP award) [TSMC PR 2968, 2022-10-26; Teradyne PR 2025] | HBM co-qualification: SK hynix HBM4 base dies on TSMC N12FFC+/N5 (agreed Apr-2024); Micron handing TSMC HBM4E base/logic dies (~2027) [Tom's Hardware, retrieved 2026-07-17]; OSATs absorb overflow (~240–270k wafers/yr 2026 [E3 §6 — REPORT]) while TSMC keeps >90% of CoWoS [BigGo, 2026-07-16 — REPORT] | Memory partners get early spec alignment; TSMC gets HBM roadmaps optimized for *its* interposers — the accelerator bill-of-materials is co-designed around TSMC packaging | Samsung's memory+foundry turnkey pitch; the panel-level (CoPoS) transition 2028–30 re-opens the stack [E2 §2] |
| Supplier/materials web | Hundreds of chemicals/gases/parts/equipment-service firms in the science parks | The "one-hour ecosystem": ~NT$2.4T (~US$74B)/yr procurement; local sourcing 46% (2024) → 60% target 2030; parts-localization with 12 suppliers cut validation lead times ~50% [CommonWealth, 2026-06-18; DigiTimes, 2026-03-23] | Suppliers build capacity next to the fabs that buy from them; proximity compounds yield-learning speed | Physically non-portable — cannot be contested abroad, only *left behind* (§3) |
| University/talent pipeline | Taiwan's national universities + the state | Four semiconductor academies (NTU, NTHU, NCKU, NYCU) opened end-2021 under the May-2021 Training Act; 13 academies at 12 universities since; TSMC pledged NT$4B to schools (2024-06) [CommonWealth, 2024-02-01; Taipei Times, 2024-06-07] | Academies exist *because* TSMC's hiring anchors them; graduates default to the employer the curriculum was built around | Taiwan's shrinking demographic cohort; overseas fabs bidding for the same engineers |

**The chokepoint read.** The payment-flow grid's strongest tell — *supposed competitors pay the company* — fires here: Samsung Memory and SK hynix, arms of TSMC's foundry rivals' parent groups, sit inside the 3DFabric Alliance aligning HBM4 criteria with TSMC's packaging roadmap [TSMC PR, 2022-10-26]; Intel buys TSMC N3/N2 wafers for its own tiles while building 18A [E3 §2]. When rivals' affiliates calibrate products to your interposer, you occupy a deeper layer than "wafer vendor."

**Quantifying design-start gravity.** The filed proxies: in 2025 TSMC manufactured **12,682 products for 534 customers on 305 process technologies** [20-F filing press release, 2026-04-16]; N2 tape-outs run ~4× N3 at the same stage [DigiTimes, 2026-07-16 — REPORT], with ~15 committed N2 customers, 10 in HPC [TrendForce, 2025-09-22 — REPORT]. Each product is a design closed and signed off in TSMC flows against TSMC-verified IP — 12,682 annual votes for the default, each deepening the catalog the next vote consults.

**What the fab-replication ladder does not capture — explicitly.** Section II.2's ladder prices *capex and yields*: ~half a trillion dollars, a decade, defect-density parity. The OIP layer adds an axis money does not directly buy: **third-party investment allocated by expected volume**. Certified EDA flows are near-parity items now (Intel and Samsung both hold Synopsys/Cadence/Siemens certifications — the tool-level parity clock has largely run), but the ~93,000-title verified-IP catalog and the design-service bench cannot be subsidized into existence: each IP title is a per-node test-chip and characterization cycle an independent vendor funds only against expected design starts, and design starts flow to where the catalog already is. That circularity adds **~2–4 years to any challenger's replication timeline beyond capex-and-yields parity** and makes the challenger's first-node fill problem structural: Intel's external 14A commitments stand at zero with two prospective customers on test chips [Intel Jan-2026 call, E3 §2] not because tools are missing but because no rational customer starts a flagship where the IP catalog is thin **[agent-inferred]**. State money compresses the ladder's clocks (yield learning, capacity); the catalog clock compresses only with customer commitments that the catalog's absence itself deters — which is why Section II.2's ~2030 moat window is conservative on this axis.

**Where the entitlement is contestable.** (1) Tool-level PDK parity has converged (14A production PDKs optimized for Cadence tools contracted 2026-06-08; Siemens runsets in 14A-E; SF2P certified) — what remains un-ported is the catalog and the muscle memory. (2) A repeatable customer-funded port: Maia 2 on 18A-P is strike one; a second hyperscaler production generation outside TSMC by end-2028 (Section II.2 kill signal 6) would prove the port cost fundable and repeatable, and IP vendors would begin verifying elsewhere in parallel — the flywheel in reverse **[agent-inferred]**.

**Entitlement classification** [module: CLAIM_PIPE_AND_TRAPPED_VALUE_TAXONOMY.md]: the OIP layer is *controlled-but-not-owned capacity* with a named, proven pipe to the listed security — design-start gravity → node pricing power (reported +3–10%/yr hikes through ~2029 [E3 §7 — REPORT]) and structural utilization → the 67.7% GM print and the "56% and higher" through-cycle floor [E1 §1; 4Q25 call, 2026-01-15]. Credit it as moat *duration*, not a separate valuation line: it is already capitalized inside Section II.6's ~$1.5–1.6T moat-layer residual. The borrowed-moat test [module: BORROWED_MOAT_AND_CLAIM_RIGHTS.md] passes for the commercial layers — the counterparty-inversion question ("what would Cadence/Arm/SK hynix do if they fully believed our bull case?") answers *deepen TSMC coverage*, since their P&Ls monetize TSMC volume — with one exception: the deepest layer TSMC rents is the **Taiwan state's franchise stack**, and that counterparty has already repriced once (N-2). Section 3 takes that layer.

### 2. The substrate/overhang split, formally

[skill: substrate-vs-overhang-underwriting / modules: ASSET_QUALITY_VS_SECURITY_QUALITY_FRAMEWORK.md, OVERHANG_DECOMPOSITION_AND_BUCKETING.md, RISK_HALF_LIFE_CLASSIFICATION.md, BEARS_RIGHT_THESIS_STILL_LONG.md, REASON_TO_EXIST_TEST.md]

#### 2.1 The substrate charter (single verifiable paragraph, per the Stage-1 gate)

TSMC operates the world's only at-scale leading-edge foundry: 72.3% of global foundry revenue (1Q26 [TrendForce, 2026-06-12]), >90% of CoWoS-class packaging [REPORT, 2026-07-16], 305 process technologies serving 534 customers and 12,682 products (2025 [20-F PR, 2026-04-16]), every major merchant and hyperscaler AI accelerator except Huawei's fabbed on its nodes [E3 §5]; customers cannot replace it on any commercial timeline (rivals' foundry units lost ~US$10.3B — Intel, FY25 — and ~KRW 6T — Samsung, FY25 — while TSMC earned a 59.9% FY25 GM [E5 §5; E1 §3]); the franchise self-funded a 2019–25 buildout (US$167B capex) from retained earnings with zero dilution and a rising dividend (NT$4.00→NT$7.00/quarter across 2024–26 [E1 §5]); normalized earning power — stripping cyclical peak, FX, and one-offs — is a **57–60% through-cycle GM on management's "56% and higher" floor [4Q25 call, 2026-01-15; house band per Section II.6/Section II.7] and clean EPS in the mid-teens US$/ADS (core FY26E ≈ $16.5 with the NT$63.2B Vanguard disposal gain stripped [E1 §1; Section II.6])**. Every clause is verifiable from filings and cited data; none relies on management credibility. The reason-to-exist test passes on mechanism, not adjectives: absent TSMC, ~$700B/yr of hyperscaler capex [E3 §6] has no leading-edge logic supply at any price on a five-year horizon.

#### 2.2 The overhang ledger

[module: OVERHANG_DECOMPOSITION_AND_BUCKETING.md — classes exclusive; per SKILL.md, one unbounded overhang gates the whole view and cannot be averaged away]

| # | Overhang | Bucket | Bounded? | Dated | Sized | Removal / monitor |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| (a) | **Taiwan-jurisdiction tail** (the claim runs through a Taipei-incorporated parent) | *Known-priced* in class but **fails the bounded test** | **NO — absorbing, unbounded** | No — open-ended | Invasion recovery ≈0.02–0.06× on the ADS; blockade ≈0.22× [II.5]; expected-loss drag ≈1.5–2.5%/yr vs ~1.0–1.25%/yr priced [C4; Section II.6 §7] | **None exists** — no calendar, no cleanup path, no counterparty who can sign it away; deterrence decays ~5–8yr half-life [II.5 j.7] |
| (b) | **Cycle/normalization** (AI-capex digestion; dilution stack: overseas 2–3→3–4pp, N2 2–3pp, depreciation +high-teens% [E1 §1; E2 §5 — COMPANY]) | *Temporary* | Yes | **2027–28** (capex cohort hits depreciation as hyperscaler second-derivative resolves) | **−30–40% EPS in a digestion year** (house; Section II.6: bear $12.8 vs base $21.0 = −39%; mediocrity $16.5 = −21%), GM → 57–60% | Calendared: big-4 Jan–Feb-2027 capex guides; TSMC Jan-2027 FY27 guide; first clean post-digestion quarter (Section II.6 kills 1–3) |
| (c) | **Policy/toll** ($165B→**$265B** U.S. commitment 2026-07-16 [E4 §3]; Section 232 25% tariff with 2.5×/1.5× quota exemptions, ART signed 2026-02-12 [E4 §1]; BIS matter [E4 §2]) | *Known-priced / governance-discount* (recurring implicit tax) | Yes per cycle; **recurring** | Rolling: quotas repriced each investment cycle; ART LY ratification by 2026-12-31 [II.5] | **~1–3% of market-cap NPV**: overseas GM drag 2–4pp ≈ $3–6B/yr pre-tax at FY26 scale **[agent-inferred]** + BIS ≤~$1.5B (~0.07% of cap) | Not removable but cappable: each ratchet turn is disclosed, sized, absorbed (56%+ floor reaffirmed *after* the $265B announcement [E1 §1]) |
| (d) | **Governance/second-source window** (Intel 14A commitments due 2H26–1H27, production 2029 [E3 §2; Tom's Hardware, retrieved 2026-07-17]; Samsung profit target 2028 [E5 §5]; ASIC re-bid cadence 12–18mo [III.1]) | *Curable / monitorable* | Yes | **2028 window** — every dated challenger clock lands 2028±1 | Erosion, not breach: ~1–2% revenue per defection (Nova Lake precedent [E3 §2]); pricing-power haircut if the 2027 hike round fails [II.2 kill 7] | Fully monitorable: Section II.2 kill signals 1–6 |

**Why (a) caps the multiple permanently.** The half-life lens [module: RISK_HALF_LIFE_CLASSIFICATION.md] makes the asymmetry exact: (b), (c), (d) are short-to-ambiguous half-life risks with named mechanisms and calendars — the material this skill exists to buy through. (a) is the textbook long-half-life risk: no resolution mechanism, an absorbing residual, and non-stationary probability (rising as deterrence decays [II.5]). The skill's scoring surface is explicit that overhang-boundedness is a **conjunctive gate** — one unbounded overhang caps the verdict regardless of substrate quality. Translation: TSMC can never trade at the multiple its substrate would command in a clean jurisdiction; the ~13-turn forward-P/E gap to ASML (20.0× vs 33.4× [E5 §4]) is the standing rent on this ledger line, and it is *correct in kind* while thin-to-fair in size (~1.0–1.25%/yr priced vs ~2.3%/yr warranted [II.6 §7 j.6]). Bears-right discipline [module: BEARS_RIGHT_THESIS_STILL_LONG.md]: concede the tail is real and unbounded; contest only its pricing — and the pricing is not generous enough to be paid for holding it. That is a statement about the security, not the asset.

#### 2.3 Asset quality vs security quality

[module: ASSET_QUALITY_VS_SECURITY_QUALITY_FRAMEWORK.md]

| Question | Asset-quality answer | Security-quality answer |
|---|---|---|
| What does it own? | The only at-scale process+packaging+ecosystem stack; 72.3% share; §1's controlled ecosystem on top | An ADS wrapper on a Taipei parent at a ~7% premium to the local line [E5 §1]; realizable-return multiplier ≈0.90 current / 0.22 blockade / 0.02–0.06 invasion [II.5] |
| Why does it exist? | Reason-to-exist passes on mechanism (§2.1) | The wrapper exists at the pleasure of two states' policy stacks (N-2 + §232 quotas), both executive-discretionary and renewal-shaped [II.5 §5] |
| Why is it (not) cheap? | Nothing about the asset is broken; fairly-to-fully priced at cyclical peak [II.6 §7] | The 13-turn gap to clean-jurisdiction peers is permanent rent on overhang (a) — structural haircut, not mispricing |
| What has to go right? | Nothing heroic: hold share, normalize to 57–60% GM, keep cadence | Duration: the price needs excellence through 2030; evidence proves ~2027 [II.6 §7] |
| What can kill it? | Only the tail — no commercial actor can | Digestion (−30–40% EPS × 4–6 multiple turns), the ratchet hardening, or the tail |

**Verdict on the split: world-best asset, structurally haircut security** — the haircut permanent (unbounded line), currently thin-to-fair (no stigma premium to harvest; the stock is ~14% off its high, not in revulsion [II.5 §6]), and therefore paying nothing extra for bearing it at $409.74.

#### 2.4 Multiple independent paths to value — the honest count

[SKILL.md Stage 5: a buy requires **≥2 independent paths that survive the ledger**, base case not dependent on speculative upside]

| Path | Value source | Survives ledger? | At $409.74 | At $280–330 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1. Earnings compounding + dividend growth | Mid-teens clean EPS on the ~25% 2024–29 revenue-CAGR guide; dividend NT$4→7/q since 2024, ≥NT$23/share committed 2026 [E1 §5] | **Yes** — survives (b) with a 1–2yr delay, (c) by absorption, (d) by erosion; discounted by (a)'s 0.90 multiplier | **Fails the hurdle**: base IRR ≈ +2%/yr [II.6 §a] — the path exists but is pre-paid | **Clears**: ~17–20× clean mid-teens EPS, ~13.5–16× the 2028 base path ($21); compounding + ~1% yield reaches a mid-teens IRR **[agent-inferred arithmetic]** |
| 2. Multiple re-rating **if** the tail thins via US/Japan scaling | ~30% of 2nm+ capacity in Arizona once scaled [E2 §3] reduces warranted tail drag | **Partially** — blocked by the N-2 cage (§3): the statute keeps the leading edge home, so the tail cannot thin faster than Taiwan's own roadmap; (a) stays unbounded regardless | Minimal: 20× NTM already paid; headroom ≈2–4 turns, not demonstrable before ~2030 **[agent-inferred]** | Real but capped; free option, never base case |
| 3. 2028 replacement-echo demand leg | The 2024–26 AI installed base hits its refresh window (5-yr equipment lives [E2 §5]) as N2/A16 and 14-reticle CoWoS land 2028 [E2 §1–2] | **Yes** — the recovery side of (b); independent of (c), (d) | Already in the price: 20× capitalizes an unbroken growth path — the echo is what consensus assumes | **Clears**: at the band the echo is optionality you were not charged for |
| 4. Ecosystem-rent deepening via packaging share | Advanced packaging >10% of 2025 revenue → low-teens % 2026, growing faster than corporate average [4Q25 call, 2026-01-15]; CoWoS ~90K→120–140K wpm, >90% share, ASP nearing 7nm-wafer levels [E2 §2 — REPORT]; Chiayi AP7 P1 producing Jun-2026, Phase II broke ground Jul-2026 [Focus Taiwan, 2026-07-12] | **Yes** — most ledger-robust: packaging rent grows even under wafer-share erosion (§1: the accelerator BoM is co-designed around TSMC interposers); (d) does not touch it before the CoPoS contest 2028–30 | Modest: real but a low-teens-% revenue line cannot carry a $2.1T valuation | **Clears** as the kicker that raises the band's floor |

**The honest count: at $409.74, only path 4 both survives the ledger and offers uncharged return content — and it is too small to carry the case. Path 1 survives but is pre-paid (+2%/yr); paths 2 and 3 are capped or already capitalized. One < two: the skill's own gate returns DO-NOTHING at this price, independently confirming Section II.6/Section II.7. At $280–330, paths 1, 3, and 4 all clear with path 2 as a free option — three surviving paths, and the base case requires no speculative upside.** That is the skill-native statement of the house view: strongest substrate charter in coverage, all bounded overhangs datable and survivable — but one unbounded overhang plus a full price leaves fewer than two paid paths to value.

### 3. The regulatory franchise: Taiwan's rulebook as asset and cage

[skill: regulatory-franchise-and-local-market-power / modules: NATURAL_LEGAL_SCARCITY_STACK.md, POLICY_INCENTIVE_STABILITY_TEST.md]

**Stage 1 — name the rulebook.** Four privileges, each with a named rulemaker: (i) **science-park infrastructure** — land, permitting, administration via the NSTC's park administrations, with expansion pre-cleared for TSMC's roadmap (Hsinchu/Baoshan, Kaohsiung, the Chiayi packaging hub, an approved 1nm-class hub [DigiTimes, 2024-11-29; DCD, retrieved 2026-07-17]); (ii) **water and power priority** — in the 2021 drought (Taiwan's worst in decades) the government curtailed agricultural irrigation and trucked water while fabs ran [IndustryWeek, 2021; The Diplomat, 2024-09]; the state built reclaimed-water plants for the southern park (from 2022), and President Lai pledged in June 2026 that industry's water and electricity needs "will be met" [Taipei Times, 2026-06-13]; TSMC used ~8.4% of Taiwan's electricity in 2023, projected toward ~24% by 2030 [DCD/TrendForce, 2024-10]; (iii) **the N-2 statute** — the late-2025 amendment to the Statute for Industrial Innovation (Art. 22): overseas deployment must trail Taiwan's leading edge by two generations, outbound investment approval-gated; in force end-2025 [E4 §5]; (iv) **the talent regime** — the May-2021 Training Act's semiconductor academies purpose-built around industry hiring [CommonWealth, 2024-02-01]. Rulemakers: LY (statute), MOEA/Investment Commission (outbound approvals), NSTC (parks), the water/power bureaucracies — and, as shareholder, the NDF (6.38%, board seat [E4 §5]).

**Stage 2 — franchise type.** A *natural-plus-legal scarcity stack* [module: NATURAL_LEGAL_SCARCITY_STACK.md]: the natural constraint (a four-decade agglomeration of fabs, suppliers, engineers existing nowhere else) compounded by legal permission (priority claims on water, power, land, graduates no ordinary industrial user receives) — and simultaneously a *local must-have network*: Taiwan's system cannot exclude TSMC (57% of the index weight [E5 §6], anchor of the "silicon shield") any more than TSMC can exit Taiwan. **Nobody else gets this stack**: no other jurisdiction suspends farm irrigation to keep a private company's fabs wet, builds university colleges around its hiring plan, and writes its process-node lead into national statute.

**The dual read — asset and cage.** As *asset*: the franchise subsidizes the moat — infrastructure priority compresses time-to-yield, the academies underwrite labor supply, and N-2 is a **state-enforced guarantee that the leading edge stays agglomerated**: no board, activist, or foreign government can force TSMC to strand its densest asset, because Taiwanese law forbids it. As *cage*: the same statute is the formal proof of Section II.5's judgment 5 — the franchise is **location-locked by construction**. The $265B U.S. program buys tariff quotas, political cover, and N-2-compliant trailing capacity; it cannot move the franchise, because the franchise *is* the location — the one-hour supplier web (46%→60% local procurement [DigiTimes, 2026-03-23]), the water/power priority, the academies, and the statutory fence all stop at the shoreline. TSMC's filings quietly concede the tension: the FY2025 20-F deleted "in the R.O.C." from its ecosystem-synergy sentence, twice [III.4 §1.2] — de-anchoring the *filed* moat from Taiwan even as the statute anchors the real one harder. The valuation consequence was drawn in §2: the cage is why path 2 cannot run ahead of Taiwan's own roadmap, and why overhang (a) stays unbounded — **the rulebook that makes the asset irreplicable makes the security's tail unhedgeable**.

**Stage 3 — political economy and stability** [module: POLICY_INCENTIVE_STABILITY_TEST.md]. Underwrite the government's incentive, not the current rule. The franchise exists because TSMC solves Taiwan's *durable* political problem — relevance and deterrence — and the state is rewarded by the company's success (jobs, tax, the shield): solution-aligned, the stable kind. The module still demands the reversal question — what pain would make repricing rational? Three scenarios, dated:

| Reform scenario | Mechanism | Who pushes | Dated markers |
|---|---|---|---|
| **N-2 loosening toward N-1** (cage opens; tail thins; Taiwan's leverage erodes) — Section II.5 case weight 25% | MOEA implementing-rule change or LY re-amendment under U.S. trade pressure (the 2.5×/1.5× quotas reprice each cycle) | Washington; TSMC quietly (the 20-F redline); the Arizona Fab-3 2nm/A16 target (~2027) colliding with Taiwan's A14 ramp (~2028) | **2027-06-30**: the Section II.5 seam — Fab 3 still targeting 2nm/A16 with no statutory accommodation → statute fight or slip; MOEA "times have changed" signaling (2025 N-1-loosening precedent [E4 §5]); ART ratification riders through 2026-12-31 |
| **Rent-capture reframing** (resource priority loses legitimacy as TSMC's grid share heads toward ~24% by 2030) | Tariff resets, green-power mandates, water pricing, park-expansion blocking — the reflexive-rent pattern: the thesis's success recruits claimants | Local politics (Nanzih science-park energy pushback, Apr-2026 [New Bloom, 2026-04-27]); the agricultural south in the next drought | Taipower industrial-tariff decisions (semiannual review **[agent-inferred cadence]**); next drought's irrigation-vs-fabs allocation; reclaimed-water share; any windfall-levy or "chip dividend" bill in the LY **[agent-inferred marker]** |
| **Controller inversion** (the state monetizing while restricting) | NDF has "from time to time" sold TSMC shares as ADSs [E4 §5]; a disposal program concurrent with N-2 tightening = Section II.5's controller-incentive inversion | Fiscal politics (the NDF stake ≈ US$135B at market **[agent-inferred: 6.38% × $2.12T]**) | Any NDF disposal announcement read jointly with N-2 posture and Investment-Commission decisions on Arizona — event-driven (Section II.5 kill trigger) |

**Stability verdict:** the franchise passes the incentive test on a 3–5 year view — deterrence value, employment, and index weight make TSMC's priority claims politically self-enforcing, and the observed direction (N-1→N-2, i.e. *tightening*) shows the state treats the franchise as strategic property. Instability is second-order and slow: legitimacy erosion as resource claims scale (the ~24%-of-grid trajectory eventually forces a public bargain), and the structural irony that both reform directions hurt the security — loosening N-2 thins the tail but erodes the cage that guarantees agglomeration; tightening deepens the moat while welding the tail on. **No Taiwan policy path reforms the franchise in shareholders' favor on both axes at once [agent-inferred].**
