To Section III.10: the three §3 reform markers (2027-06-30 N-2 seam; Taipower/drought cadence; NDF-disposal-plus-tightening inversion). Evidence gaps: the 93,000-IP count and 100+-partner figure are REPORT-grade (SemiAnalysis/SemiWiki, not company-published); alliance member counts beyond 3DFabric's launch 19 are not publicly quantified — verify against TSMC's next OIP Ecosystem Forum disclosures (Sep–Oct 2026 cadence).

*Compliance boundary: analytical research over public filings, company releases, and cited press/market-research sources; press and market-research figures are labeled and are not certified data; no investment advice, price target, or trade instruction.*


## Section III.14 — Relative Value: The Spread Book Around TSMC

*As of 2026-07-17. TSM (NYSE American Depositary Share, "ADS"; 1 ADS = 5 common shares) closed $409.74 on 2026-07-16; TWSE 2330 (the Taiwan-listed common) closed NT$2,470 the same date — an ADS premium ≈ +6.9% on non-synchronous closes [E5 §1]. Forward multiples (next-twelve-month price/earnings, "fwd P/E"; stockanalysis.com 2026-07-16/17): TSM 20.0×, NVDA 20.8×, AVGO 23.8×, ASML 33.4×, AMD 56.7× [E5 §4]. Evidence keys: [E3]/[E5]/[E6] = the competition, market-valuation, and SEC-primary evidence files; Section II.6/Section III.8/Section III.6/Section III.7 = this report's valuation, flow, theme-loop, and price-forensics sections. Section III.8 §5 handed the "ecosystem basket" question to this lane. Method: [skill: relative-value-and-pair-trade-underwriting / SKILL.md] — every candidate must name a retained exposure, a reduced exposure, two independently underwritten legs, a convergence mechanism, and a basis-risk kill switch, or take the honest no-pair verdict. Acronyms defined at first use. Analytical review only — not a trade, sizing, borrow, or execution instruction.*

---

### 0. Why a spread book, and the gate every candidate must pass

The finding set is awkward for an outright book: Section II.6's verdict is that $409.74 underwrites *excellence* (base-case internal rate of return, IRR, ≈ +2.1%/yr; probability-weighted ≈ −2% total) on evidence that runs only to ~2027 [II.6 §7, (a)]; Section III.6 puts the artificial-intelligence (AI) demand loop at 60/30/10 across healthy/digestion/credit-break hypotheses [III.6 §5]; the house call is DO-NOTHING/WATCHLIST outright. The natural question: is there a *spread* that monetizes what the report established — monopoly durability, a higher floor in digestion, a venue-priced jurisdiction tail — without paying the entry price the outright long fails on?

The skill's core principle governs everything below: a pair is a single spread thesis with two legs, one shared factor, and one expected convergence mechanism — with no named factor to reduce, no independent long-leg edge, no convergence path, or no basis-risk kill switch, it is two theses taped together [skill: relative-value-and-pair-trade-underwriting / SKILL.md — core principle, Stages 1–5]. Stage-0 classification of the five candidates:

| Candidate | Spread shape [SKILL.md Stage 0] | First routing check |
|---|---|---|
| 1. TSM vs NVDA | Quality spread inside one theme chain | Is sector beta the risk to reduce, or the thesis itself? |
| 2. TSM vs ASML | Cross-node "two irreplaceables" comp spread | Is the 13-turn gap a factor or a fair price? |
| 3. TSM ADS vs 2330 | Same-claim venue/wrapper spread | Routed through the claim-selection discipline Section III.8 §5 already ran |
| 4. TSM vs UMC/GF | Monopoly-vs-commodity sector spread | Do the legs share *any* factor after de-labeling? |
| 5. TSM vs SOX/SMH | Index hedge | Is the ETF exposure cleaner than a curated peer subset? |

---

### 1. TSM vs NVDA — the intra-AI-chain quality spread

**Edge-isolation sentence** [skill: relative-value-and-pair-trade-underwriting / SKILL.md Stage 1]: *the spread intentionally keeps exposure to the relative durability of TSMC's input-priced manufacturing toll versus NVIDIA's price-times-share merchant-silicon rent, plus the asymmetry in customer concentration, and reduces exposure to the AI unit-volume cycle that both legs are long.*

**The oddity that creates the candidate.** The market charges 20.0× and 20.8× fwd P/E for two claims on the *same* physical unit stream with different capture mechanics [E5 §4]. TSMC is paid per wafer regardless of whose logo wins the accelerator socket: every major merchant and hyperscaler AI accelerator except Huawei's Ascend (fabbed at SMIC) and the *reported* Microsoft Maia 2 (Intel 18A-P) is fabbed at TSMC [E3 §5, net]. NVIDIA is paid price × share of the accelerator layer — currently spectacular (Data Center revenue $75.0B in the April-2026 quarter, +92% year-over-year; $1T cumulative Blackwell+Rubin visibility claimed [E3 §6, SEC press releases 2026-02-25/05]) but contested by every customer large enough to design an ASIC (application-specific integrated circuit).

**Leg theses** [SKILL.md Stage 2]. *Long TSM:* an input-priced toll at 67.7% gross margin (GM) with 72.3% foundry share, raising prices 3–10% annually with hikes reportedly planned through 2029, and a customer book where the top name is 19% of revenue and falling relative to the ASIC cohort growing inside it [E6; E3 §1, §5, §7]. *Short NVDA:* not "overvalued" on the print — the same ~20× on faster current growth — but *more exposed to the shared factor*: its rent requires holding share and price simultaneously while the customer set visibly builds the substitute. Dated ASIC markers, all fabbed at TSMC: Google Ironwood deployed Nov-2025; AWS Trainium3 generally available Dec-2025, >1M chips deployed; Microsoft Maia 200 deployed Jan-2026 (TSMC 3nm); Meta's MTIA roadmap of four generations through 2027; the OpenAI–Broadcom chip on TSMC N3 targeted for end-2026, second generation planned on TSMC A16; Broadcom AI backlog ~$73B [E3 §5]. Every ASIC dollar displacing a merchant-GPU (graphics processing unit) dollar moves gross profit *out* of NVIDIA's layer while leaving wafer volume — TSMC's claim — intact or larger. The pair isolates exactly that migration.

**What the spread has done, 2024–26.** TSM ADS returned +92.6% (2024), +55.9% (2025), ~+44% year-to-date 2026 [E5 §2]; NVIDIA over the same arc became TSMC's largest customer (19% of 2025 revenue, 20-F-anchored [E3 §5]) and a $5.02T cap [E5 §4]. The terminal state is the headline: *multiple parity* — the same forward multiple now paid for the toll and the merchant rent, versus the historical wide merchant premium [agent-inferred from the E5 §4 snapshot; the pack carries no continuous NVDA return series]. In the July-2026 drawdown the legs decoupled measurably: TSM −14.2% while NVDA was +3.65% over the identical eleven sessions [III.7 ledger] — the pair is genuinely two-legged, not one trade twice.

**Convergence mechanism and dated markers** [SKILL.md Stage 4]. The mechanism is earnings-mix migration, not sentiment: ASIC share of AI compute rises → NVIDIA's price×share rent compresses at the margin while TSMC bills the wafers either way. Dated markers: OpenAI N3 deployment (end-2026); MTIA generation cadence (through 2027); Trainium ramp disclosures (Dec-2026); NVIDIA's reconciliation of the $1T visibility claim (Q4 FY27 print, late Feb-2027 [III.6 §3]); TSMC's AI-accelerator revenue-CAGR (compound annual growth rate) guide update (~Jan-14-2027 call [III.6 §3]).

**Basis risks — stated bluntly** [SKILL.md Stage 3]. (i) *NVIDIA's software/ecosystem rent is real and unhedged inside this pair*: CUDA (NVIDIA's proprietary compute software stack), networking ($11.0B in a single quarter [E3 §6]), and system integration can defend the rent even as ASIC units grow — the short leg can keep beating (Q1 FY27: +85% revenue, guide ~$91B [E3 §6]). (ii) **The Taiwan tail is NOT hedged by this pair.** Both legs load on the same causal variable — NVIDIA's products are made at TSMC — so a cross-strait event impairs both in unknowable relative proportion; pairing a thesis with a security that shares its dominant variable is the classic hedge failure [skill: relative-value-and-pair-trade-underwriting / module: HEDGE_CAUSAL_VARIABLE_NOT_SECTOR_LABEL.md]. Section II.6 prices TSM's conflict residual at $40–80/ADS [II.6 (a)]; no NVDA short sized off normal-state betas covers that. (iii) An AI-capex bust hits the shared factor — that part *is* hedged; Section III.6's credit-break variant, forcing NVIDIA backstops, could briefly hit TSM harder via cancellation optics. Kill switch: a second flagship accelerator committed to a non-TSMC node (the Maia 2 report is the template), or ASIC deployments slipping en masse (the reported ~$18B OpenAI financing snag is the live warning [E3 §5]).

**Verdict: PAIR — the only candidate here that passes all five gates** [SKILL.md Stage 5], with the no-pair red-team answered: own-outright fails on Section II.6's entry-price math; short-NVDA-outright fails because the merchant rent is not yet cracking on any dated evidence. Honest job description: long pricing-power durability and concentration asymmetry, flat the AI cycle, *still long the island*.

---

### 2. TSM vs ASML — the two irreplaceables, thirteen turns apart

**Edge-isolation sentence:** *the spread would keep exposure to TSMC's earnings durability versus ASML's equipment-cycle earnings volatility, and reduce exposure to the shared leading-edge semiconductor buildout — if a convergence mechanism existed.*

ASML (the Dutch monopoly in extreme-ultraviolet (EUV) lithography, ~100% of supply) and TSMC are sequential monopolies on the same chain: ASML sells the only tools that make the leading edge possible; TSMC is the only foundry that reliably yields it. The market pays 33.4× forward for the tool monopoly, 20.0× for the fab monopoly [E5 §4] — 13.4 turns. The work is to *name* the factor behind the gap before trading it [skill: relative-value-and-pair-trade-underwriting / module: CROSS_COMP_THE_IMPLIED_DURABILITY_SPREAD.md].

**Decomposition: is the gap a Taiwan-tail price or an earnings-cyclicality price?** The cyclicality evidence runs the *wrong way* for the premium: ASML is the more cyclical stream. TSMC defends utilization and cuts equipment purchases first — it cut capex ~$6B intra-year in 2022→23 [III.6 §3 kill-column; E1 §7 via Section III.6] — making wafer-fab-equipment revenue higher-beta to exactly the digestion scenario the market fears [III.8 §5, ecosystem test]. ASML's near-term visibility has also *narrowed*: quarterly bookings disclosure ended with Q1-2026, leaving backlog an essentially annual (January) print, even while it guides FY26 €43–45B, calls 1H26 ordering "very strong" and itself "close to receiving all the orders it needs" for 2027, and plans +30% EUV capacity versus 2026 [III.6 §1, ASML press release Jul-2026]. And TSMC's High-NA (high-numerical-aperture, next-generation EUV tooling) deferral — low-NA multipatterning through A14/A13/A12 on the April-2026 roadmap [E2 via TrendForce/Tom's Hardware, 2026-04-23] — trims ASML's per-node tool intensity: the premium leg just lost a purchase cycle its largest customer was supposed to anchor. What remains to explain 13 turns is mostly *jurisdiction*: ASML carries zero conflict-site risk, TSMC the absorbing tail — Section II.6 §6 read the same gap as "the irreplaceability-premium vs Taiwan-site-discount tug-of-war made visible."

**Why it still fails the pair gate.** A long-TSM/short-ASML construction has a nameable factor (the tail-vs-cyclicality mispricing) — but **no convergence mechanism** [SKILL.md Stage 4: "eventually" is not a mechanism]. Nothing dated forces the Taiwan discount to close (that requires tail resolution, unknowable and mostly discontinuous), and nothing dated forces ASML's 33× to de-rate — with bookings now annual, the short leg cannot even be *monitored* at quarterly cadence [SKILL.md Stage 6]. Shorting the higher-multiple leg of a duopoly-of-monopolies without proving the premium unearned is the named anti-pattern [module: CROSS_COMP_THE_IMPLIED_DURABILITY_SPREAD.md — "short leg has no edge; you're just paying borrow on a fair price"; cf. module: WITHIN_DUOPOLY_PICK_LEGACY_LIABILITY_TIEBREAKER.md, which picks a *better leg to own*, not a spread to force].

**Verdict: NO PAIR.** What *would* create one, with dates: (i) the ~Jan-27-2027 annual backlog print falling >20% while TSMC holds its FY27 capex guide (≥$70B per Section III.6's healthy branch) — a dated short-leg catalyst with a protected long leg [III.6 §3]; (ii) a High-NA insertion decision at an A14 roadmap revision (restores ASML intensity — arguing the *opposite* pair); (iii) a formalized cross-strait modus-vivendi event (Section II.6 judgment #6's falsifier) compressing TSM's discount against every clean-jurisdiction comp at once — at which point the trade is really just long TSM. Until then the spread is a *diagnostic*: the 13 turns are the market's live quote on the Taiwan tail — worth tracking, not trading.

---

### 3. TSM ADS vs 2330 — the same-claim venue spread

**Edge-isolation sentence:** *the spread keeps exposure to the price of venue continuity (the New York wrapper's value in states where Taipei cannot clear) and reduces exposure to the business entirely — the legs are the same cash-flow claim.*

Stage-0 routing sends share-class spreads to the claim-selection discipline first [SKILL.md Stage 0]; Section III.8 §5 ran it. What remains for this lane: is the +6.9% premium a *tradable spread* or a gauge? The mechanics decide it. Creation of new ADSs is quota-capped under the standard ROC (Republic of China) ADS regime — deposits permitted only up to the previously approved ADS count — while cancellation (ADS→ordinary) is free [III.8 §3, Chunghwa F-1 template; TSMC deposit agreement flagged for pull]. One-way convertibility means the premium is **not an arbitrage: it is a bounded sentiment spread**, floored near zero by cancellation, uncapped above by the quota [III.8 §3].

**The regime history is the two-leg thesis.** Premium ~low-single-digits 2004–2019; average ~19% since 2024; ~26% Dec-2024; a two-decade record Oct-2025; then five straight monthly declines to 13.7% by May-2026, and +6.9% at the 7/16 closes (+9.4% on the same-information 7/15 basis) [III.8 §3 table; E5 §1]. The collapse's proximate cause was a *rule change on the other leg*: Taiwan's Financial Supervisory Commission (FSC) raised the domestic-fund single-stock cap from 10% to 25% in late April-2026, unlocking an estimated NT$200B of local demand for the one stock the old cap bound (TSMC ≈ 44% of the Taiex) — local forced buying repriced the ordinary line up to the wrapper [III.8 §3, MEXC/GuruFocus 2026-05]. The two legs, then: *long 2330* = long the local-bid regime (the mechanical index bid lives on the ordinary line [III.8 §1]); *short ADS* = short the US-crowding premium — i.e., short venue-continuity insurance.

**Why the current level is not a trade.** At 6.9% the spread sits at the *bottom* of its 5–12% convenience band — the US-froth component is already out [III.8 §3]. The series is statistically sticky (unit-root test on 2004–present fails to reject non-stationarity at short horizons, ADF p≈0.28): 10–20% divergences persist for months-to-years and revert on *regime events*, not schedules [III.8 §3, chungderson dataset]. There is no convergence mechanism at 6.9% — the quota blocks creation-arb from above, and the residual premium is the market-clearing price of the one wrapper that keeps trading if Taipei halts (10% daily limit; the 2025-04-07 limit-down precedent [E5 §2]). Worse, the short-ADS leg is *short the tail*: a Taiwan-specific shock inverts the spread violently as the ADS becomes the sole functioning price-discovery venue [III.8 §3, failure mode (ii)]. Selling that insurance at its cheapest price since early 2024, into the risk this entire report is about, fails the wrapper-choice test on its face — same engine, two wrappers: the comparison is holder-specific realized outcomes, not price-versus-price, and a wrapper that functions in the halt state can *justify* its premium [skill: relative-value-and-pair-trade-underwriting / module: SAME_ENGINE_TWO_WRAPPER_AFTER_TAX_ARBITRAGE.md].

**Access reality.** Even the mechanically clean direction (long-ord/short-ADS above ~20%) requires FINI (Foreign Institutional Investor) registration, a local custodian, TWD (New Taiwan dollar) settlement in a managed currency, and tolerance of the 10% daily limit [III.8 §5] — the runnable population is small and already structurally long Taiwan plumbing. The comparator says the rest: SK hynix's Nasdaq ADR sits >50% above Seoul under the same one-way mechanics — these spreads revert when the constrained side's *rules* change, not because someone arbitrages them [III.8 §3].

**Verdict: NO PAIR at current levels — a monitoring gauge**, with re-open thresholds inherited from Section III.8: >12% re-crowding; >20% froth (the only level where the short-wrapper leg has historically had a repeatable edge, and even then with months-long holding periods); <0% sustained = arb-chain stress; any FSC quota or cap change re-opens the question [III.8 §3, (b)].

---

### 4. TSM vs the foundry floor (UMC/GlobalFoundries) — monopoly vs commodity as a cycle instrument

**Edge-isolation sentence (attempted):** *the spread would keep exposure to TSMC's leading-edge rent and reduce exposure to generic foundry-industry conditions.* It fails at Stage 3, honestly.

De-label first [SKILL.md — lookalike sector de-labeling]: the shared label is "foundry"; the real risk sources are disjoint. TSMC's marginal dollar is AI/high-performance-computing (HPC) leading-edge — HPC is 66% of wafer revenue, ≤7nm is 77% [E6]. UMC (United Microelectronics) and GlobalFoundries (GF) sell mature-node capacity into consumer/industrial/auto: 3.9% and 3.3% revenue share, 29.2% and 27.6% gross margins versus TSMC's 67.7% [E3 §1; E5 §5]. In the very quarter TSMC grew +36% year-over-year, UMC's revenue fell −3.2% quarter-over-quarter and GF's fell −11% [E3 §1, TrendForce 2026-06-12]. On an exposure-ranked table [skill: relative-value-and-pair-trade-underwriting / module: EXPOSURE_RANKED_PAIR_CONSTRUCTION.md], the fraction of value tied to the AI leading-edge factor is roughly: TSM high-double-digit, UMC/GF ≈ 0 [agent-inferred from the mix data above]. A short leg with ~zero loading on the factor hedges nothing; a long leg in the floor recycles no beta. The quality gap, meanwhile, is *earned* — 38-point GM spreads, rivals losing money at scale [E5 §5] — so there is no durability mispricing to fade [module: CROSS_COMP_THE_IMPLIED_DURABILITY_SPREAD.md, falsifier branch: the premium twin's durability is real].

**The cycle test — "in digestion, does the floor fall less because it never had the rent?"** The 2022–23 record in the pack: TSMC's FY23 USD revenue fell −8.7%, GM compressed 59.6%→54.4%, EPS −17.5%, and the *stock* fell −59% peak-to-trough as the year-end P/E went 27.2×→11.2× — the multiple, not the earnings, did the damage [III.7 §cycle table, TSMC management reports + companiesmarketcap]. The pack does not carry the matching 2022–23 UMC/GF margin and drawdown series — an explicit evidence gap; any leg-level claim built on it would be `INSUFFICIENT EVIDENCE` [SKILL.md — Output]. What the pack *does* establish is the structural answer: the floor never had the rent, but it also never had the multiple — TSMC's drawdown risk is compression from 20× NTM, the floor's is utilization/ASP erosion; different state variables, different clocks. Indeed the floor's cycle is currently *turning up from below* — China mature-node utilization 95.8–100%+, SMIC/Nexchip/Hua Hong raising prices ~10% into 2026, top-10 8-inch utilization ~90% versus ~80% in 2025 [E3 §4] — while TSMC prints peak margins.

**Verdict: INFORMATION PAIR — watch, don't trade.** The floor is a *cycle-dating instrument*: mature-node pricing and utilization are the broadest read on semiconductor demand ex-AI, and a rollover there while TSMC still accelerates would date the digestion clock better than any model. The legs are also the wrong vehicles for their own signal — UMC is itself Taiwan-domiciled (the "hedge" imports the jurisdiction), and GF's US trailing-edge franchise is partly a policy asset. Log the monitoring line (quarterly TrendForce share/pricing tape; UMC/GF margin prints) and decline the position.

---

### 5. TSM vs SOX/SMH — the index-carve

**Edge-isolation sentence:** *the spread keeps exposure to TSMC's idiosyncratic monopoly economics and reduces exposure to semiconductor-sector beta.* (SOX = the PHLX Semiconductor Sector Index; SOXX and SMH = the iShares and VanEck exchange-traded funds (ETFs) tracking semiconductor baskets [III.7 header].)

**What it actually isolates.** The naive version — dollar-neutral long TSM, short the index — mis-sizes on the report's own forensics: TSM's realized slope to SOXX through the June–July 2026 drawdown was ≈ 0.66 (regression over the 11-session window, computed in Section III.7: Σxy/Σx² = 115.8/175.8), so a 1:1 short over-hedges by a third and leaves an accidental net-short-semis residual [III.7 §attribution (a), agent-inferred there with math shown]. Properly beta-trimmed, the carve is still not "TSM minus the market": the index *is* TSMC's ecosystem — NVDA (its largest customer, 19% of 2025 revenue [E3 §5]), AVGO, AMD, the equipment complex, and TSM itself sit inside SOXX/SMH [III.7 header; composition detail agent-inferred — verify weights and strip the TSM self-position from the hedge notional]. The honest description: **long the idiosyncratic monopoly, short everything-else-in-the-chain** — including the long leg's own customers, the same-causal-variable trap in index form [module: HEDGE_CAUSAL_VARIABLE_NOT_SECTOR_LABEL.md — sector ETFs frequently contain the thesis; remove or cap].

**When it pays.** In digestion, TSMC's floor is higher — demonstrated twice in the pack: July 2026 (TSM −14.2% vs SOXX −17.2%, median basket constituent −21%; on its own print day TSM −2.32% vs SOXX −4.46%) [III.7 §1–2], and 2023 (revenue −8.7% while two rivals lost $10B+/yr and the floor earned 27–29% GM [E5 §5]). The module built for this logic also bounds it [skill: relative-value-and-pair-trade-underwriting / module: SHORT_THE_INDEX_TOO_REBUTTAL.md]: an index short neutralizes *multiple compression* the whole complex shares — and Section II.6's mediocrity case is substantially a multiple event (20×→16× on flattish earnings [II.6 (a)]) — but it defends nothing against an *earnings* or *company-specific* impairment, and TSMC's two named idiosyncratic risks (margin normalization off 67.7%, the Taiwan jump) are exactly the alpha-side risks the rebuttal cannot launder. The carve converts Section II.6's mediocrity cell from ~−15% IRR toward the spread of floors (≈ +3pp relative in the live 2026 sample) [agent-inferred from the Section III.7 numbers above].

**Carry and crowding costs.** The short leg pays away the sector's drift in the 60%-probability healthy world [III.6 §5] — and this sector's drift is violent (Q2-2026 alone: Micron +240%, Intel +216%, AMD +186% [E5 §4 momentum note]); ETF borrow is cheap but the carry is negative-expectancy whenever the boom persists. Crowding: hedging this complex is the consensus reflex — TSM's options surface shows 30-day implied volatility at the 96th percentile with put/call open interest 1.31, against *cash* short interest of 0.57% [E5 §6]. Verdict: **not a pair — a positioning/beta hedge** [SKILL.md Stage 5, basket/ETF class]: legitimate only as an overlay on Section II.6's multiple-compression scenario, at ~0.66 beta with the TSM self-weight stripped, and a pre-committed retirement trigger (the Jan–Feb-2027 hyperscaler capex-guide cluster [III.6 §3, §5] resolving healthy retires the hedge before it bleeds).

---

### 6. The spread-book verdict

| Candidate | Verdict [SKILL.md Stage 5] | One-line reason |
|---|---|---|
| TSM / NVDA | **Pair** (the only one) | Same 20× for toll vs merchant rent; dated ASIC migration markers; cycle genuinely shared and reducible |
| TSM / ASML | No pair — diagnostic | 13 turns = the live Taiwan-tail quote; no forcing event; short leg now reports its best leading indicator annually |
| ADS / 2330 | No pair — gauge | +6.9% = bottom of the convenience band; one-way quota kills arb; shorting the wrapper = selling tail insurance cheap |
| TSM / UMC-GF | Information pair — watch | Legs share a label, not a factor; the floor is a cycle clock, not a hedge |
| TSM / SOX-SMH | Basket hedge, not a pair | Valid only against shared multiple compression, at ~0.66 slope, net of the index's TSM/customer content |

**Which expression dominates outright DO-NOTHING?** For a book that wants *some* exposure to the finding set, the TSM/NVDA quality spread is the only construction that expresses the report's differentiated content — monopoly durability and concentration asymmetry priced at parity with a contested merchant rent — without underwriting the two things the outright long fails on (entry multiple, cycle timing). It dominates DO-NOTHING only under that mandate; against the house call it is an *alternative researched expression*, and the decision belongs to the decision framework, with the spread card, exactly two flip triggers (a second flagship accelerator committed off-TSMC; the Jan–Feb-2027 capex cluster resolving to digestion), and the no-pair red-team attached [SKILL.md — Handoffs; this section authorizes nothing].

**The standing basis-risk warning, bluntly:** *no pair in this book hedges the Taiwan absorbing tail.* Every candidate short leg — NVDA, the index, the floor (UMC is Taiwanese), even ASML — loads on the same island through the same fabs, and the venue spread is *short* the one instrument that functions in the halt state. A hedge object that shares the causal variable is not a hedge [module: HEDGE_CAUSAL_VARIABLE_NOT_SECTOR_LABEL.md]; the tail's convexity is also the most expensively insured part of the options surface [III.8 §5; E5 §6]. The only tool that addresses an absorbing state priced at a $40–80/ADS residual [II.6 (a)] is exposure size — owned by the sizing discipline downstream, not by any spread constructed here.

*Compliance: analytical review over public filings, index/ETF publications, market-research and press sources cited with dates; derived numbers labeled with math or [agent-inferred]. No trade, sizing, borrow, hedge-ratio, or execution instruction; expression verdicts are research classifications handed to the decision and implementation disciplines.*
